# KB: File structure — per-file operating essays

Moved VERBATIM out of CLAUDE.md (tier-2 pass). Each `### path` section is that file's full operating essay — invariants, incidents, gotchas. **READ the entry BEFORE modifying the file; UPDATE it in the same commit when behavior changes.**

### server.js

server.js              — BOOTSTRAP + WIRING ONLY (~1910 lines since the 2.325.0 拆分; the arch suite RATCHETS it ≤2100). Everything mechanical lives in src/server/* `create(deps)` factory modules: usage-pool-engine (pool auto-switch/estimator/anchors/sealed orders), session-stdout (setupSessionPty = caps.streamProtocol → src/server/stdout/ consumer lookup + attach since S5, plus onExit death classification / attachToDtach / session-meta store), boot-restore (migrations+restoreSessions+R6 reopen+keeper re-adopt), session-brain (parity comparator+sbSeenFirst+claudeSideEffects), account-usage-routes, mounts-plugins-wiring, cli-env (X display/adapters/CLI probes/model registry), dial-pairing, exit-routes, ops-routes (version/self-update/maintenance), incident-wiring, sysinfo-wiring, goal-sync, agent-tool-generators. server.js destructures each factory's exports under the SAME names, so grep-by-name still lands. Late-boot singletons cross as lazy getters; mutable `let` state as explicit `get*()`.

### data/bin/editor/code

data/bin/editor/code   — Fake "code" script for Ctrl+G (generated by server; moved OUT of data/bin in 2.267.0 — data/bin is PATH-prepended into every local session and the fake `code` shadowed real VS Code there; EDITOR= stays the absolute path, boot unlinks a stale data/bin/code; remote layout was already ~/.vibespace/editor/). **ANCHOR RULE (2.267.2 regression, self-caught): ONLY `code` lives in EDITOR_DIR — every OTHER generator (vibespace-status/vibespace-hook.mjs/hook-register) and USAGE_STATUSLINE_CMD anchors on AGENT_BIN_DIR (= data/bin, the PATH dir); the 2.267.0 move dragged them all into editor/ because they shared the constant (tools off PATH + statusline pointing at a missing file + global hook registration rewritten). Before changing a shared path constant, grep every consumer. data/bin/editor/ is gitignored (generated; tracked generated files block self-update — 2.111.26 class).**

### data/bin/vibespace-hook.mjs

data/bin/vibespace-hook.mjs — Dual-harness hook (SessionStart→task context OR a baseline tools intro for no-task sessions, UserPromptSubmit→first-prompt context + status-override notices + per-turn micro-reminder, Stop→bookkeeping nudge (2.79.0, CLAUDE-ONLY registration): calls GET /api/agent/stop-check and emits {decision:'block', reason} when the server says the board state is stale — the agent gets ONE follow-up mini-turn to set status/mirror asks/log progress, gated by stop_hook_active (never loops), server-side staleness+cooldown thresholds (agents.stopNudgeStaleMinutes/CooldownMinutes, defaults 10/30min, clamped 1-240/2-720 — 2.89.0; an EXPLICIT 0 = every-stop mode: 0 stale = always stale, 0 cooldown = none — 2.210.0 user request; stop_hook_active/nudgeTurnActive still cap it at ONE mini-turn per user turn); codex's equivalent rides codex-chat-wrapper's turn/completed (maybeStopNudge → synthetic <vibespace-reminder> turn, nudgeTurnActive guard) since the app-server has no blockable Stop in JSON-RPC mode; generated at startup; env-gated no-op only when VIBESPACE_API/TOKEN are absent — a MISSING VIBESPACE_TASK_ID no longer no-ops (2.38.0), so any VibeSpace session still learns `vibespace-status`; emits ONLY nested hookSpecificOutput). Registered idempotently for BOTH events in ~/.claude/settings.json + ~/.codex/hooks.json by ensureAgentHooks — only the vibespace entry is ever touched. **TEMP-SERVER GUARD (2.225.1, real incident): a server running from under os.tmpdir() skips ALL global hook writes (register AND strip; hookRegistrationSafe) — a /tmp worktree smoke server once rewrote the global hook command to its throwaway path, and after cleanup every hook errored MODULE_NOT_FOUND for two days (the CLI snapshots hook config per session, so healing the file doesn't reach running sessions). Any test harness that boots server.js should also set VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS=1 (all four worktree smokes do); VIBESPACE_FORCE_AGENT_HOOKS=1 overrides.** **INTEGRATION MASTER SWITCH (2.190.0, settings category "Integration"): `agents.vibespaceIntegration` default ON; OFF = the model gets a pristine CLI. SCOPE = AGENT-VISIBLE integration ONLY (2.190.1, user decision): model-invisible plumbing is EXEMPT — the usage statusline, billing/account env, Ctrl+G editor, keeper transport all keep working either way. PER-FEATURE toggles under it (2.211.0, user request '注入context但不给ask/progress'): `agents.contextInjection` (group payloads/diffs; per-group injectContext stays the finer grain) + `agents.toolStatus`/`toolAsk`/`toolTask` — a disabled tool is neither TAUGHT (sessionToolsIntro()/renderContext's _toolsSectionParts/per-turn reminder/stop-nudge steps all compose from enabledTools(); teaching a refusing command trains dead ends) nor SERVED (its write endpoints 403 with skip-and-continue-do-not-retry guidance); toolStatus off also silences the stop-check arbiter (it is keyed on status staleness); contextInjection off falls back to the baseline tools intro; reads (GET /api/agent/task = `vibespace-task show`) stay governed by the master, not toolTask. Route smoke: scripts/test-tool-toggles.mjs (21 asserts).** Hook entries stripped live on toggle (persistence writeSettings' `onSettingsWrite` callback → stripAgentHookEntries; boot re-checks the setting so a flip-while-down converges; the boot ensureAgentHooks call is DEFERRED until after setupPersistence so the setting is readable), new spawns get NO VIBESPACE_API/tools-PATH/remote-prelude (keeper still ships; VIBESPACE_SESSION_TOKEN stays for Ctrl+G editor auth, inert alone since every consumer guards on api+token), and agent-routes gates every model-visible CONTENT response for pre-toggle sessions too: the three deliveries return empty (prompt-context also consumes-and-DROPS pending status-override notices — deferring them injected stale reminders on re-enable) and GET /api/agent/task 403s (it returns the same steering substance; `vibespace-task show` must not bypass the pristine state) while WRITE endpoints stay live. Both Install routes (local /api/agent-hooks/install AND host /api/hosts/:id/agent-tools/install) refuse while OFF; the Manage-Agents host row shows the residue + keeps Remove as cleanup. Dial spawns drop the `$HOME/.vibespace/bin` PATH segment too (leftover tools from earlier ON spawns must not be name-resolvable). Distinct from the data/.agent-hooks-optout marker (manual Manage-Agents Remove) — the setting never writes the marker, and re-enable does NOT override it. E2E: scripts/test-integration-toggle.mjs. vibespace-hook-register.mjs = self-locating installer distributed to remote hosts (P3); its `--uninstall` mode (2.129.0) strips ONLY our entry from the remote configs (foreign hooks untouched) — the Manage-Agents remote Remove path. **SSH→DIAL GRADUATION (2.248.0, B-6640; **AUTOMATIC since 2.311.0** — `hosts.onSshConnected` fires after every successful ssh device connect and the server graduates in the background. Gates, each of them a way to do something the operator did not ask for: setting `agentd.autoGraduate` (default on) + an operator-declared `agentd.publicUrl` (it must NEVER self-publish this instance to the relay — that side effect is the operator's choice) + per-machine `autoGraduate:false` + one attempt/machine/6h; failures serverNotice and leave the machine on ssh. The install half is `graduateHostToDial()`, shared by the button and the auto path. Test: scripts/test-auto-graduate.mjs): an ssh machine can install the daemon as a persistent service and DIAL BACK over ws — `POST /api/hosts/:id/graduate-dial` (`{serverUrl|viaRelay}`, `{remove:true}` rolls back). The record keeps `transport:'ssh'` and gains `deviceId`+`dialTokenHash`+`dialRoot`; `_deviceConnect` prefers the live dial link and falls through to ssh on ANY failure (never worse than before), `findByDeviceId` falls back to non-dial records so dial-in auth resolves, `onDialIn` evicts a cached ssh dm, `list()` exposes `graduated`/`dialLive`. INVARIANTS: ssh stays the bootstrap+rescue channel (never removed); the base URL is explicit>publicUrl>relay and the MACHINE is asked whether it can reach it BEFORE anything installs (unreachable ⇒ abort, stay pure-ssh — a daemon that can never dial in is worse than no daemon); `dialRoot` must mirror the installer's own derivation byte-for-byte (hostname-only + `tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9.-'`) or removal tears down the wrong directory; removal always clears the local record even if the host is dead. E2E: scripts/test-graduate-dial.mjs (10 asserts against a real host; needs an ssh reverse tunnel so the host can reach the throwaway server). REMOTE-TEST GOTCHAS the harness encodes: server.js honours NO data-dir env (a worktree's own data/ IS the isolation), an inherited VIBESPACE_PASSWORD turns auth on and 401s everything, a worktree is at HEAD so uncommitted changes must be copied in, and `pkill -f vibespace-agentd` over ssh matches its OWN command line and kills the session (use `pgrep -f "[v]ibespace-agentd" | xargs -r kill`).** **BOUNDED DEVICE CONNECT (2.246.2/2.247.0, B-fa6f — userN's flapping H200s): `hosts.deviceBounded(id, connectMs=6000)` races device() against a deadline for every READ-ONLY/interactive data-plane consumer (~26 sites: discovery/RemoteFs/dirComplete/slab sync/usage harvest/mount heal/ports/exit) — the connect ladder runs up to ~2.7min and on a lossy path where TCP opens but the ssh banner hangs (ConnectTimeout only bounds the TCP connect, verified live) every surface sat on it BEFORE its own ssh fallback. THREE INVARIANTS (adversarial-review-hardened, do not regress): ① session-ESTABLISHING consumers keep the FULL ladder or ≥15s — findKeeperFor (adopt-vs-respawn: a deadline reads as 'no live keeper' → writer sweep SIGTERMs the healthy claude), _hostShell (accountsStatus rides it inside CREATE's linked/host-held rescue), homeDir (feeds the create cwd default, B-0d70 shape); ② the race OBSERVES the loser promise (p.catch(()=>{})) — an abandoned ladder's late rejection is an unhandledRejection = process crash on Node≥15; ③ device() DEDUPES in-flight connects per id (_deviceInflight → _deviceConnect) — without it every timed-out probe stacked another 13×ssh ladder under a 5s-polling sidebar. Any hosts-like TEST MOCK must also implement deviceBounded (two suites broke on the missing method). **B-218d FIXED in 2.247.1-2.247.3** (2.247.2: EXPLICIT-host ssh resumes probe findKeeperFor too — before, only host-inference and dial did, so a sidebar resume with the host selected always swept; 2.247.3: findKeeperFor rewritten — the ssh leg scanned only legacy run/ and NEVER saw agentd sessions, and grep-in-state-json only matches RESUMED spawns (--resume embeds the cid) — now ONE scan over both stores + a childPid→~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json lock leg for fresh spawns, returning {sid, kind} so the consumer routes to the right attach binary): findKeeperFor's agentd pipe sids are tagged `keeperKind:'agentd'` and the ssh branch adopts them via the attach-cli with a NO-spawn-spec cfg (the dial branch's contract) — before this, they were fed to the legacy keeper binary (which only reads ~/.vibespace/run) so the 2.218.0 keeper-attach optimization silently never worked over ssh and every such resume degraded to sweep+respawn. Legacy keeper sids (client discovery K lines) keep the old runTail; provisioning failure degrades to it too.** **NODE RESOLUTION (2.244.2-2.244.4, userN's Novita chicken-and-egg): hook entries register with the ABSOLUTE process.execPath (hooks run via /bin/sh with claude's PATH — nvm-managed node never resolves there, and claude is a native binary), and ALL FOUR register invocation sites (ssh prelude / dial install / manual Install / Remove) run a POSIX node finder (PATH → newest nvm → common paths) then EXPORT the found node dir onto the session PATH — a bare `node` invocation of the register silently no-op'd on dash hosts, so the fix could never apply itself AND every #!/usr/bin/env node agent tool was dead there. Any new remote code path invoking node must use the finder, never bare `node`.**

### data/bin/vibespace-task

data/bin/vibespace-task — Agent task-reporting CLI (STATIC tracked file, not generated — inline template-in-template escaping was unmaintainable): show [--full]/progress "summary" [--detail]/backlog/backlog-add/backlog-done/backlog-drop → /api/agent/task* (scoped to the session's own context task). **progress SUCCESS OUTPUT reminds the agent (2.210.0, the vibespace-ask 2.111.21 pattern): the activity log is agent-facing — deliverables (URLs/paths/results) must ALSO go in the chat reply (real pattern: agent logged a URL via progress and ended the turn without saying it).** **CHECKLIST REMOVED (2.121.0, user decision):** `plan-check/plan-uncheck/plan-add` are gone — a group-level list of agent WORK ITEMS never made sense (those live at the SESSION level = the agent's own todo list, surfaced as the card's Steps). The subcommands now print a redirect; `/api/agent/task-plan` answers old CLI copies (remote hosts) with 410 + guidance; stored `plan` arrays stay DORMANT in data/task-groups.json (never rendered/written; update() ignores plan patches; config-bundle import passes them through). **BACKLOG ADDED (2.122.0; CLAIM MODEL 2.123.0 — a DIFFERENT concept on the same shelf):** the group's PARKING LOT for NON-immediate items (deferred user decisions, "later" work; item = {**id: stable `B-xxxx`**, text, detail?, status open|done|dropped, **claimedBy: [session keys]**, addedBy/At, resolvedBy/At}); agents park via `backlog-add` when the user defers something (parking AUTO-CLAIMS for the caller) and are taught to NEVER start parked items unasked. Refs everywhere = id / 1-based OPEN-list index / unique substring; **the id travels** — the user copies it from the viewer and hands it to ANY member agent ("看一下 backlog B-ab12"), which runs `backlog <id>` (full item view, works on resolved items) and `backlog-claim <id>`. **INJECTION IS SUMMARY-ONLY + TARGETED (user directives): the hook never dumps the backlog** — renderContext gets `sessionKey` and emits a compact reminder block ONLY for open items THAT session CLAIMED (+ a one-line "N open — `vibespace-task backlog`" pointer otherwise); change events (PARKED/RESOLVED/DROPPED/CLAIMED/UNCLAIMED/reworded/REMOVED) ride the diff updates but reach ONLY sessions that created or claimed the item — diff matching is by the stable id (text edit ⇒ "reworded", not REMOVED+NEW). TASK.md/`show` carry the full open list with ids+claimants. One-time seed: dormant plan's unchecked items → open backlog items; id + creator-auto-claim backfill for pre-2.123.0 items (constructor, idempotent). **SUMMARY+DETAIL split (2.69.0):** progress entries carry a one-line `note` (what everyone sees inline — board rows, injected context marked `†`) + optional `detail` (≤6000 chars; TASK.md renders it as an indented blockquote, `show --full` prints it, the board's Activity rows expand on click) — this keeps the byte-budgeted injection dense without losing information. session-status records gained the same: `reason` = one-liner on chips, `detail` (≤2000) shown in Session Properties + `vibespace-status show`

### data/bin/vibespace-ask

data/bin/vibespace-ask — Agent CLI for the GLOBAL USER-TODO inbox (2.65.0, STATIC tracked): files items the USER must act on (decision/input/review) → POST /api/agent/user-todo (vsst_ scoped to own session). `"text" [--detail] [--urgency]` / list / resolve <id|text>; `help|-h|--help` and any flag-looking first arg print usage instead of filing (2.76.0 — a real agent filed the literal item "--help"); idempotent re-file by same text; the SUCCESS OUTPUT reminds the agent (2.111.21) that the inbox is only a notification mirror — the full content must ALSO go in the chat reply (real pattern: agents filed and ended the turn silently). **Semantics (2.76.1, user directive): MIRROR every chat question** — whenever an agent asks the user anything in chat / ends a turn waiting on them / sets needs-input|blocked|review, it must ALSO file the item (the user is often not watching that window), and the moment the user answers ANYWHERE (chat counts) the agent resolves it itself — taught in all three surfaces (SESSION_TOOLS_INTRO, renderContext tools section, the CLI's own usage text); taught in SESSION_TOOLS_INTRO + renderContext; distributed to remote hosts like the other tools

### data/bin/vibespace-status

data/bin/vibespace-status — Agent status tool (GENERATED at startup like data/bin/code — NEVER git-track it: tracking it dirtied the tree every boot and blocked `git pull --ff-only` in self-update (real incident 2.111.26); gitignored (NOTE: .gitignore has NO inline comments — a `path  # comment` line becomes the whole pattern and never matches, which silently re-tracked it once) and update.sh resets any regenerated tracked data/bin file before pulling. GENERATOR-TEMPLATE GOTCHA (P0 2.111.29): a shell example ending a single-quoted JS template string with `\\` rendered as `\'` → escaped the closing quote → every generated file was a SyntaxError and the whole CLI crashed on 2.111.24-28 — never end a JS template string with a bare backslash. WAITING STATES REQUIRE COMPLETE REASONS (2.111.24): blocked/needs-input/review need BOTH --reason (board chip) AND --detail (full context) — CLI pre-flight (show-lookup grace for same-state urgency tweaks) + agent-route both reject otherwise; done/working exempt; setting a waiting state prints a point-of-use reminder to also say it in chat + mirror via vibespace-ask)

### data/bin/vibespace-usage

data/bin/vibespace-usage — PASSIVE usage capture (STATIC tracked file, not generated; 2.60.0). A `statusLine` hook injected via `--settings` into LOCAL CLAUDE TERMINAL sessions only (2.62.0: the ws-handler injection is gated `backend==='claude'` — only the claude CLI understands `--settings`; un-gated it rode on `zsh -l` and codex spawns too, making every shell terminal (incl. the Manage-Agents Update/Login helpers) and local codex terminal exit instantly as "terminated"; NOT gated by the Integration master switch — 2.190.1 user decision: the switch scopes to AGENT-VISIBLE integration only, and the statusline is model-invisible plumbing) — the CLI feeds it the payload's `rate_limits` (5h/7d used_percentage+reset, "only after first API response" — and NOTHING else: model-scoped buckets like the Fable weekly cap are NOT in the statusline payload, verified vs the 2.1.206 payload builder) which it writes to data/usage-cache/<accountKey>.json (throttle 8s; **PRESERVES `scopedWeekly`/`scopedFetchedAt` from the existing file** — 2.70.0, clobbering them to [] every write is what made Fable vanish from the quota popup) + passes through the user's own statusline. Also harvests `model.id` → data/usage-cache/__models__.json (passive model discovery). ZERO Anthropic calls — this is why VibeSpace makes no background /api/oauth/usage poll by default (that off-CLI fixed-cadence sub-token call is the #1 ban trigger — see §ban-safety on accounts.js). Chat/stream-json has no statusline → those accounts show last-known. **THE ACCOUNT KEY IS THE LINK, RESOLVED PER WRITE, ONE REQUEST BEHIND IT (2026-09-07 + r3).** `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_KEY` is fixed at SPAWN, so a hot-switched pooled session filed every later reading under the member it STARTED on; ws-create also ships `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_LINK` (the credential SYMLINK the CLI reads) and `accountKey()` readlinks it. But the payload's `rate_limits` is the CLI's LAST API response — made with the credentials the link pointed at BEFORE a re-point — so resolving the link alone honoured a switch one request EARLY and the old member's numbers landed on the new one, stamped `fetchedAt: now`, where the anchor sweep turns any usage-cache write into ground truth (the B-b3cd odometer-flap class on the write path). The server's twin pins the slot for the TURN; a statusline has no turn, so it pins on the NUMBERS: a `.slot-<session_id>` sidecar beside the cache (deliberately no `.json` — every usage-cache scanner filters on that extension) holds `{key, fp}` for the last payload OBSERVED (updated even when the 8s throttle skips the write — a payload seen under the old target is still evidence about which credentials produced it), and while the link has MOVED but the fingerprint has NOT, the reading is left where it is. The first differing payload is the first one the new credentials produced. A session with no link writes no state file at all (it cannot switch under itself).

### data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan

data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan — REMOTE-side usage ledger scanner (2.127.0, STATIC tracked; **PARITY-GUARDED since 2.275.0: it is a shipped SINGLE FILE (a host has no checkout ⇒ it cannot require the local walker), so it silently lagged its twin twice — the 2.265.0 subagents/workflows walk took 6 weeks to reach it. scripts/test-usage-walk-parity.mjs runs THE THREE walkers (local UsageHistory walk + this file + src/usage-walker.js) over one fixture and demands identical coverage + incremental-cursor behaviour; negative-controlled against the pre-2.271.0 walk. Any one-sided change fails it.**; shipped over ssh stdin per harvest, never argv): walks the host's ~/.claude/projects with remote byte cursors (~/.vibespace/usage-cursor.json), emits per-request NDJSON events (claude-only v1); cursor persists ONLY after stdout fully flushed (interrupted transfer ⇒ re-emit, rid-dedup absorbs). **R4 step 1 (2.286.0): daemon hosts prefer the `usage-scan` OP instead — src/usage-walker.js is the walk as a module BUNDLED into the daemon, run in a child process (single-artifact rule: the daemon re-execs its own bundle with `--usage-scan-child`), events streamed count-gated; the walker NEVER persists its cursor — hosts.harvestUsage commits it back over the device link (fsWrite+fsRename) only after the full transfer landed (two-phase; the script's flush-then-persist model LOST events on relayed dial paths when the link died mid-transfer with the cursor already advanced). Per-op capability gating: the daemon hello-ack `capabilities` array (['probe','transcript-op','usage-scan']) — consumers gate on it and throw fast, NEVER parse daemonVersion (unknown ops on old daemons get no reply = a hang until timeout). Fallback ladder: op → script-ship over the device link → ssh. E2E: scripts/test-usage-scan-op.mjs (13 asserts, real daemon).** **R4 step 2 (2.287.0): ①PUSH-triggered harvest — the daemon's discovery watch (now also `.codex/sessions`) pushes debounced `discovery-dirty`; hosts._armDirtyPush (armed at every _deviceConnect; client re-arms on reconnect like reverse forwards) fans it to discovery cache invalidation + server-side per-host debounced harvest kick (60s floor unchanged) — remote ledger/billing-popup freshness ≈1min for ANY activity incl. mid-turn/codex/external; the dirty signal previously had NO consumer. ②CODEX ROLLOUTS in all three walkers (synthetic rid = cumulative total, turn_context model/cwd persisted in cursor, input−cached split, heartbeats skipped, CODEX_HOME honored); ingestRemoteEvents honors `be` — remote codex usage lands in the ledger. Codex parity leg in test-usage-walk-parity (21).** Harvest chain: hosts.harvestUsage (15min/host idle throttle; **EVENT-DRIVEN since 2.284.3: a remote session's `result` kicks a prompt harvest with a 60s/host floor** — the ledger/billing-popup lag is ~1min, not 15; the pool control loop never depended on this cadence, it eats relayed stdout live via noteLive) → usageHistory.ingestRemoteEvents (**PER-ACCOUNT since 2.294.0**: resolves the real account by the SAME by-time attribution walk local events use — VibeSpace records the spawn account for remote sessions too, the ingest just discarded it; pool tag included. Only sessions VibeSpace never spawned (external terminals, pre-attribution) keep acct=<hostId>/atype='host'/billing 'remote-host', which now honestly means 'that machine's own login'. No back-billing past the grace window; `host` field always kept so the device dimension still works; rid namespaced `h:<id>:`; host-bucket events stay EXEMPT from _loadEvents re-attribution — the local log would re-bucket them to global) → POST /api/usage-stats/harvest-hosts (kicked on Usage-window open, reload on added>0). Quota side: /api/usage/refresh {host} = on-demand ⟳ with hosts.readRemoteOAuth (READ-ONLY remote token peek, never refresh, §ban-safety) → usage-cache/host-<id>.json → GET /api/usage `hosts` field (every configured host listed) → popup "Remote hosts" section (bars + per-host ⟳, class usage-host-refresh). **Walker v3 (codex per-message meta, 2026-09-06; mirrored line-for-line from src/usage-walker.js): codex events ADD `mid` = the `response_id` of the 0.153 `token_usage_record` that PRECEDES the token_count (parked in the CURSOR as `pendMid`/`pendTotal` so a harvest landing between the pair still pairs on the next run; adopted only when its `usage.total_tokens` equals `last_token_usage.total_tokens`; pre-0.153 rollouts get no mid — never invented) and `effort` (turn_context.effort, persisted like model). `rid` is UNCHANGED — it is the dedup key already baked into every permanent ledger; changing it would double-count every scanned rollout. Parity leg 21→32 incl. the scan-boundary case.**

### data/bin/vibespace-remote-keeper

data/bin/vibespace-remote-keeper — REMOTE-side persistence for remote CHAT sessions (2.124.0, STATIC tracked; distributed to remote ~/.vibespace/bin with the other tools): claude runs setsid-DETACHED on the host under a keeper daemon (stdout→buffer file, stdin←unix socket) so an ssh drop kills only the pipe; the local chat-wrapper reattaches with a byte offset (`run <sid> <offset> -- cmd`; stdout carries ONLY raw buffer bytes — diagnostics on stderr), `_remote_exit` sentinel = claude really ended (run never restarts an exited sid); stop mode = the kill path. See the 远程session稳定性 bullet + scripts/test-remote-keeper.mjs

### transcript-service.js

  transcript-service.js — ONE interface over the transcript parse stack (R3 of docs/design-three-tier.md; 2.283.0 step 1 = server-hosted, HTTP read family rerouted through it). **SWITCHED LIVE in 2.292.0** — remote page/turnmap/searchIndexed/status/taskState run as `transcript-op` on the owning machine (parsed KBs on the wire, no whole-file pull). LADDER not replacement: device op → transcript-cache pull + local parse → honest error; capability-gated (an old daemon is never asked — unknown ops hang), failures log throttled and degrade. TWO CARVE-OUTS, both load-bearing: ① a LIVE session never reads from the device (the server owns the stdout buffer overlay + live normalizer — the device would drop the un-flushed tail); ② the huge-file SEEK family switched ATOMICALLY in 2.293.0 once the daemon gained a `searchFull` op — `gapInfo` returns an OPAQUE HANDLE (`\0device:<host>` or the local path) that every follow-up (slab/search/turnmap) honours, so line numbers never come from two sources (the route already treated fp as opaque, so nothing above the service changed); device streaming search degrades honestly to one batch. Ladder test: scripts/test-transcript-switchover.mjs (7). **2.285.0 step 2: the DEVICE DAEMON hosts the SAME service** — agentd's `transcript-op` op (lazy-constructed, no buffer overlay device-side — the stdout overlay stays a server concern) runs page/turnmap/searchIndexed/status/taskState/gapInfo/gapSlab/fullTurnmap next to the bytes and streams JSON over a count-gated byte channel (read-range contract); client = DeviceManager.transcriptOp. DARK: no production consumer until the parity substrate soaks (retirement-order law). scripts/test-transcript-parity.mjs (19 asserts) pins BYTE-IDENTICAL results across in-process vs real-daemon — incl. >½MB multi-window payloads, a ~36MB fixture's real seek family, codex, and honest unknown-method/missing-file shapes. Its first run caught the tool-card ts bug (below). SafeFs/transcript-worker degrade INLINE inside the bundle (worker file absent next to the bundle → spawn fails → sync fallback, by design). **B-21e4 item 5:** `view()` warms through `harnessOf(backend).store.warmTranscript(id, cwd, {remote})` (claude parse-cache warm as before; codex thread/read fallback for a missing rollout, local only) — no backend ternary.

### session-store.js

  **NO claude model reader lives here (B-6b6d round 2)** — and the long comment where one used to be is the reason. Round 1 shipped `lastClaudeTurnModel` (tail scan for the last MAIN-THREAD assistant record's `message.model`) as the claude descriptor's `store.lastTurnModel`; it is REMOVED, because that field answers *which model served a turn* and the ladder asks *what should this resume command*. Two measurements decided it: (a) across the whole local corpus (2650 transcripts / 3.5 GB) **zero** `message.model` values carry a `[…]` context-variant suffix while the CLI's own `model_refusal_fallback` records prove conversations running `claude-fable-5[1m]`, so commanding the served id silently turns a 1M conversation into a 200k one on every resume; (b) a classifier reroute is an ASSISTANT record already ON the fallback model carrying a `{type:'fallback',from,to}` block, with the `system/model_refusal_fallback` record written 2–10 lines AFTER it — round 1's ≤20-line backward look-back matched 1 of 139 reroutes, and 16.8% of main-thread assistant records sit inside a reroute run. So a claude resume commands NEITHER model nor effort and the CLI's own session record (variant-exact) decides. Re-adding a reader is one hook in `src/harnesses/claude.js` if a future CLI ever records the commanded value.

  session-store.js     — SessionMessages + JSONL parsing + session discovery helpers. **THE LOCAL SWEEP'S PID-REUSE RUNG IS A RECORDED TWIN OF THE SWEEP'S IDENTITY RULE (B-3185 r3, RADIUS CORRECTED IN r4):** `isLockClaude` prefers B-2104's `procStart` match (no fork) and falls back to `isProcessClaudeAsync` = `ps -o comm=` + `.includes('claude')`. **r3 recorded this twin as "the local sweep's PID-reuse fallback" and that described only HALF of it: the SYNC `isProcessClaude` had one caller — `/api/kill-pid`'s local branch — so the substring rule GATED A SIGTERM. r4 deleted the sync half (that route now asks `isCliProcess` for both CLI names, like its own remote branch) and what survives is DISCOVERY-ONLY: one caller, `isLockClaude`'s no-`procStart` fallback (macOS locks write `procStartFt`; Linux answers with the file read first), deciding whether a card reads RUNNING and nothing else.** It is not an executable test the way it looks, either: `comm` is 15 bytes the process may set for itself (node renames its main thread to `MainThread`, so an npm-installed `node …/claude-code/cli.js` answers NO) matched as a SUBSTRING (`claude-keeper` answers YES) — the "4277 processes, same 16, zero divergence" measurement was of THIS box's native install, not of the rule. It stays un-ported because the rung that reaches it is macOS, where `isCliProcess` is SYNCHRONOUS and a per-lock blocking fork is the 2.242.0 event-loop stall this async family exists to avoid; adding a `comm` rung to the shared predicate instead would widen a KILL path. **It must never move back onto one** (test-writer-sweep §14 asserts the single caller and the sync twin's absence). It has a live parity gate too — scripts/test-local-discovery-device.mjs asserts device-facts sweep === local sweep, which compares the two rungs on every run (its fixture must therefore be a REAL binary named `claude`, never a `#!/bin/sh` script: the kernel gives that argv[0] `/bin/sh` while `comm` stays `claude`). **Merge is POSITION-PRESERVING (2.74.0):** surviving buffer records interleave at their true chronological spot (anchor = jsonl index of the last buffer record that deduped; a rotated buffer's unmatched head anchors just before the first match) instead of all appending at the end — end-append made an EARLIER turn's stream-json-only `result` replay AFTER a still-running tool_use, flushing the live pending tool card to ✗ Interrupted on every server restart (real report), and scrambled turn boundaries in the replay. **`chatStatus()` IS THE ATTACH/HTTP TWIN OF THE LIVE COMMAND-LIST OP (§2.6), AND ITS RULE IS AN ORDERING (round 3):** the tail scan records the POSITION of the newest `commands_changed` and of the newest `init`, and the push replaces the frame's `slashCommands` only when it is genuinely newer (plus the degradation branch: an init that declares no list cannot erase the push before it — live, `_emitSlashCommands` returns early on such a record, so the last thing that SPOKE stands). Applying the push unconditionally was actively harmful, not merely absent: a buffer holds one init per SPAWN (33 in one real conversation here), so `[…, commands_changed, …, init]` is the ordinary shape of a resume or wrapper respawn, and `applyStatus()` runs AFTER `loadHistory`'s render loop — the pre-restart list OVERWROTE the correct one the slab's own init-card side effect had just set, and was then mixed with the newest frame's `terminalSlashCommands`. The invariant the twin exists for is that **a window that opens after the push agrees with one that watched it happen**, so the gate drives the SAME records through both paths (scripts/test-init-frame.mjs `chatStatus twin`, with the pre-fix rule kept as the negative control).

### codex-chat-wrapper.js (P2 verbs, 2.369.20)

  data/bin/codex-chat-wrapper.js — **USER-RECORD PRODUCER RULES (round 3-4, 2026-09-07): our copy is spelled `userInputToContent(encodeUserInput(text, attachments))` — the exact inverse of what we SEND, so it matches codex's rollout copy block for block (a hand-rolled attachments-first array doubled every image message on reload); EVERY submission that will not be committed AS WRITTEN is withdrawn with `retractUserRecord(id, reason)` → `event_msg webui_user_retracted` — `turn/start`/`thread/queue/add` threw, the queued item was removed by the user or by Stop before it ran, the wrapper ANSWERS it itself (a slash command: ONE gate `isWrapperSlashCommand`, retracted BEFORE `applySlashCommand` runs because `/compact` takes minutes), or **the `edit` verb REWROTE its text while it waited** (the sixth path, merge review 2026-09-07: the submission really does RUN, just with different words — codex commits the NEW text and can therefore never retire the claim our PRE-EDIT bubble made; gated on `queuedFullText(item.input) !== text` because a no-op save still commits as-written, on the SUCCESS branch only because a failed update leaves the queued text untouched, and by the row's own `cid` so an INHERITED row — which has no record of ours yet — is a no-op). **There is no write-time marker (round 4 deleted `webui_no_commit`): a typed message also has the SERVER's preview copy (`CodexAdapter._buildUserPreview` → `session.buffer`, ws-handler) under the same `webui_msg_id`, it lands FIRST and is the copy that CLAIMS, so anything the wrapper writes on its own record is unreachable — the retraction names the ID, which both copies carry.** The retraction makes the merge withdraw that record's twin CLAIM (an unconsumable claim deletes an unrelated message later), never removes the bubble, and an item the app-server had already drained (`{deleted:false}` = it RAN) is never retracted. The queued peer copy carries its cid as `webui_queue_id`.** chat-input: slash commands are served by the WRAPPER (`applySlashCommand`: /compact → `thread/compact/start` (real compaction; `context_compacted` from the contextCompaction item or the RPC), /review → review/start uncommittedChanges, /model + /effort → the set-* verbs, `command_applied` notice), then **queue-while-busy**: `meta.activeTurnId` set ⇒ `thread/queue/add` + `queued_input` event, never `turn/start` (codex steers a regular turn / rejects review+compact turns). Both `wrapper_meta` records advert `slashCommands`; CodexMessageManager stores them in `_status.slashCommands`, fills the init card and patches it in place (edit op) when they arrive after the card. Live item visibility: mcpToolCall/dynamicToolCall/webSearch/imageView/contextCompaction are recorded as function_call/function_call_output twins (names chosen for collapseKindOf: `mcp__<server>__<tool>` → mcp fold; web_search → search fold, view_image → image fold since 2.369.33/34). The imageView stub records `{path}` only while codex's own rollout copy of the same call carries `{path, detail}` and an `[{input_image, image_url:data:…}]` output twin — the normalizer's call_id dedupe + input_image lifting (2.369.43) is what keeps that ONE card; the stub contract itself is pinned by test-codex-p2-wrapper and test-image-cards.. **Race guard**: `turn/completed` can be processed before the `turn/start` reply's promise resolves (same stdout chunk) — `completedTurns` makes startTurn skip re-marking a finished turn active. Pins: scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs (30); test-chat-frame-guard's stub completes turns at once. **Fork echo (B-21e4):** the wrapper's session_meta record dedupes `forked_from` (`[...new Set(chain)]`, own id dropped) and echoes `forked_from_id` from the thread/start|resume|fork response (`Thread.forkedFromId`, 0.153.4 bindings); the fork BOUNDARY ordinal is not on the wire — only the rollout's own session_meta carries it, and extractCodexThreadMeta reads it from the file. **webSearch completion = codex's OWN rollout shape (2.369.43, owner: every card read `{"query":"","action":null}` / "(empty)"):** the v2 WebSearchItem is an EMPTY stub at item/started (query '', action null — the function_call recorded there is the pending card and stays so) and complete only at item/completed, which the wrapper records as `event_msg web_search_end {call_id, query, action, results?, error?}` (key order mirrors codex-rs; `results` omitted when the item has none, `error` only when present) — NOT a function_call_output of raw JSON. The normalizer renders it (src/search-card.js), so the live record and the rollout's own web_search_end are one renderer and one card (the two copies fingerprint-dedupe on re-attach or edit the same call_id in place). Pin: test-codex-p2-wrapper (stub emits the real started-stub + completed pair; asserts the event shape, key order, no output twin, ONE complete card with the rendered result list). **imageView parity (2026-09-06):** a real `ImageView` item carries a `file://` URL (48/48 in the 0.153.4 corpus) and the normalizer strips it when it routes the rollout's own `item_completed` copy — the wrapper's live `view_image` twin strips it too, or the ONE card (both copies use the item id) rewrote its own text on re-attach. Pin: the same suite asserts the stripped path in the live `function_call` arguments + output AND that feeding the rollout copy afterwards leaves one card with unchanged text. **The 0.153.4 rollout writes NO `web_search_end`** (it persists the search as `item_completed` `Extension web.search`) — the wrapper's `web_search_end` is a LIVE-only relay of the same v2 item under the same `exec-…` id; see the cli_version table in the codex-message-manager.js essay before changing either side. **THE INPUT QUEUE — QUEUED vs STEERED (2026-09-06, owner ask; §queue-steer):** the wrapper OWNS the queue but does not KEEP it — `thread/queue/list` is the truth, re-read on every `thread/queue/changed` (the notification carries only `{threadId}`) and published as ONE typed event `queue_changed {items:[{id, msgId, preview≤120, ts, kind, from}], turn_id}` (fingerprint-deduped; also at boot and at each `turn/started`, so a client attaching mid-turn can rebuild the strip from the buffer). `queueMeta` (clientUserMessageId → {kind, msgId, ts, from}) is registered BEFORE the `thread/queue/add` RPC — the change notification's refresh can beat the add's own reply, and an item whose msgId we learn late renders with no bubble chip. The `queue-op` stdin verb serves `steer` / `remove` / `steer-all`. **MEASURED ON A LIVE 0.153.4 app-server, all four load-bearing (the design assumed a `thread/queue/remove` that does not exist):** ① the removal verb is `thread/queue/delete {threadId, queuedSubmissionId}` → `{deleted:true}`; the ClientRequest enum (printed verbatim by the server's own unknown-method error) is add/list/update/delete/reorder/start — **no `remove`** ② `turn/steer {threadId, input, expectedTurnId, clientUserMessageId}` → `{turnId}`, and it does **NOT** dequeue the item even when it carries that item's own clientUserMessageId ⇒ the wrapper deletes it or the message runs twice ③ several `turn/steer` calls inside ONE turn are accepted ⇒ `steer-all` is sequential steers in queue order, each keeping its own input (images + per-message ids survive), never one concatenated blob ④ a `thread/queue/add` on an IDLE thread starts a turn by itself — the app-server drains its own queue, we never call `turn/start` for a drained item. **ORDER IS STEER-FIRST, DELETE-ON-SUCCESS:** a steer failing is a DESIGNED path (`cannot steer a review turn` / `cannot steer a compact turn` = CodexErrorInfo::ActiveTurnNotSteerable, and `expected active turn id \`X\` but found \`Y\`` when the turn ended between the click and the RPC), and on that path the item must stay queued IN PLACE — which delete-first could not restore (re-adding appends). The wire carries only `{code:-32600, message}` (the typed CodexErrorInfo variants never reach the client), so `classifySteerFailure` discriminates on the message TEXT — deliberately, with the strings quoted from the binary's own table. Every outcome rides `queue_op_result {op, id, msg_id, ok, reason, detail}`; a steer that succeeded but whose delete failed reports `ok:true, reason:'steered-not-dequeued'` so the user is warned about a possible double run instead of it happening silently. A REFUSED `steer-all` emits ONLY the per-item result (which carries the real reason AND the real turn kind) and logs the abort to the wrapper journal — the `{op:'steer-all', ok:false, reason}` summary it used to emit as well rendered a SECOND card from the same failure, and, carrying no `kind`, made the normalizer name a compact turn "review" (round-1 review). The `ok:true, done:N` summary stays: the normalizer renders no card for a successful op. **STOP CLEARS THE QUEUE (owner decision 2026-09-07 — the ACP divergence is closed):** the `interrupt` verb runs `clearQueueForStop()` FIRST and only then `turn/interrupt`. Order is load-bearing twice: ① the app-server drains its own queue when a turn ends **including a turn ended by an interrupt** (measured; the stub keeps draining on interrupt on purpose, so a delete-after-interrupt implementation fails the suite for the RIGHT reason — the negative control leaves the session streaming a drained turn), and ② each `queue_op_result {op:'remove', ok:true, msg_id, reason:'stopped'}` is emitted BEFORE the emptied republish, because the normalizer clears the chip of anything that left the queue with no result (= "it ran") — the ACP round-1 lesson, same frame, same reason. `queueSweepActive` latches **`publishQueue` — the ONE choke point**, not `refreshQueue` (round-2 review: `turn/started` re-publishes the CACHED item list with NO RPC at all, so a turn starting mid-sweep resurrected the already-reported bubbles as `Queued`, carrying their real msgIds; `queueSweepSeq` additionally discards a `thread/queue/list` that was already in flight when the sweep began and re-lists instead of returning, or the coalesced closing refresh would publish nothing at all); the sweep ends with its own `refreshQueue()` = THE one truthful publish, so an item whose delete FAILED is still listed — that failure is reported (`ok:false, reason:'error', detail`) and journalled, never a silently "cleared" queue. ③ **the app-server's own verdict is READ** (`deleteQueuedItem`, shared with the explicit `remove`): `thread/queue/delete` answers `{deleted:false}` — not an error — for an item it DRAINED between the sweep's list and the delete, i.e. a message that is RUNNING; it is reported `ok:false, reason:'gone'` ("no longer queued — it already ran"), never a `Removed` chip, and a PEER entry on that path is NOT handed back to the delivery ladder (it WAS delivered; a re-stash would deliver it twice). A missing flag still means acked — only an explicit `false` is a refusal. ④ **Stop is budgeted like the safety control it is**: `STOP_SWEEP_RPC_MS` 2500 per RPC, `STOP_SWEEP_TOTAL_MS` 6000 for the whole sweep (the closing refresh included), and when the cap expires the items it never reached are reported `ok:false, reason:'timeout'` and `turn/interrupt` goes out AT ONCE — a wedged app-server used to hold the user's Stop for 15s per RPC per item (measured in the suite: 15.0s / 25.1s before, 2.5s / 6.0s after). A dropped PEER/job entry gets the explicit-remove path's treatment verbatim (`peer_message_result ok:false` ⇒ the delivery ladder re-stashes it). ⑤ **BOTH HALVES OF STOP ARE SINGLE-FLIGHT (round-3 review)**: stdin dispatches `handleInput` WITHOUT awaiting it, so two `interrupt` frames (a double-click, or a second attached client) really do run concurrently — the second sweep listed the queue the first was still deleting and reported those items `ok:false reason:'gone'` ("it already ran") right after the first had reported them `stopped`, i.e. the round-2 falsehood re-created by the round-2 fix's own honesty, and on the `gone` verdict a queued peer entry is deliberately NOT re-stashed, so the duplicate also lost a promised message. `clearQueueForStop` is now a single-flight wrapper around `_clearQueueForStop` (a second Stop awaits the running sweep; what it clears is what that sweep LISTED — anything queued afterwards is still listed truthfully by the closing publish), and `interruptTurn(turnId)` coalesces `turn/interrupt` per TURN while its RPC is in flight — the key is the turn PLUS a live call, never a time window, so an answered interrupt whose turn is still running still accepts a real retry (a duplicate that slipped through anyway is harmless: the app-server rejects an interrupt for a finished turn and the rejection is logged). The sweep's own throw may never eat the interrupt (Stop is a safety control: logged VERBATIM, interrupt anyway). ⑥ **`steerOne` reads the delete's verdict too**: after a landed steer, `{deleted:false}` means the app-server DRAINED the item — it now runs twice (injected into the current turn AND as its own) — so it returns `ok:true, reason:'steered-not-dequeued', detail:'it had already left the queue (it may run a second time)'`, the verdict the normalizer already renders; r2 read the flag in the sweep but not here and answered a bare `ok:true`. Deliberate codex-side difference from ACP: no extra "Stop also dropped N queued messages" notice — every dropped codex item owns a bubble whose chip flips to `Removed`. Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper (a stub app-server implementing the measured queue: two queued → steer #2 → turn/steer carries expectedTurnId + only #2's input, #1 keeps its place, the steered copy is DELETED; remove; steer-all in order; a review turn refuses and the item stays; a turn ending ON ITS OWN drains and the chip clears; then Stop with two queued (one user, one peer) → exactly two deletes, both BEFORE the single turn/interrupt, two `reason:'stopped'` removals ahead of the empty republish, the peer re-stash frame, `queueState` `removed` through the real normalizer, and NOTHING running afterwards; plus the round-2 legs ②d, each against its OWN stub because the main one cannot be those app-servers: a delete answering `{deleted:false}` ⇒ chip NOT `Removed` + no peer re-stash, a `turn/started` arriving mid-sweep ⇒ no stale republish, and a stub that never answers `thread/queue/list` / `thread/queue/delete` ⇒ the interrupt still goes out inside the cap with every uncleared item reported `ok:false`; plus the round-3 legs ②e/②f: a SLOW stub (every queue RPC answers 250ms late — the only way to hold two sweeps open at once, and it logs how many enumerations saw a non-empty queue) driven by two `interrupt` frames 50ms apart ⇒ exactly ONE enumeration, ONE delete per item, ONE `turn/interrupt`, every removal reported `stopped` and never `gone`, the coalescing decision in the journal, and the dropped peer message handed back ONCE; and a landed steer whose delete answers `{deleted:false}` ⇒ the `steered-not-dequeued` warning through the real normalizer. Negative control: reverting the single-flight reproduces the paired `[q1 stopped][q1 gone]` results verbatim) + test-queue-steer (the framework rows + both wrappers' Stop pins). Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper (a stub app-server implementing the measured queue: two queued → steer #2 → turn/steer carries expectedTurnId + only #2's input, #1 keeps its place, the steered copy is DELETED; remove; steer-all in order; a review turn refuses and the item stays; the turn ends and the app-server drains) + test-queue-steer (the framework rows). **RESPONSE STYLE / PERSONALITY (2.369.54, the 2026-09-07 harness survey):** the wrapper used to write `personality:'pragmatic'` into BOTH `thread/start` and `turn/start`, silently overriding whatever the user's own `~/.codex/config.toml` said. The value now comes from the per-session response-style slot (env `CODEX_WEBUI_PERSONALITY`, set by CodexAdapter.buildSessionArgs from the same `outputStyle` option claude's `--settings outputStyle` uses) and **the UNSET rule is absolute: an empty value means the key is not put on the wire at all** — `'none'` is a REAL codex value ("no persona") and can only arrive from an explicit pick. `PERSONALITY_VALUES = ['none','friendly','pragmatic']` is codex's own `Personality` enum, read out of `codex app-server generate-json-schema --experimental --out DIR` on 0.153.4 (never remembered); an out-of-enum value is dropped at the adapter AND refused loudly at the stdin verb. `thread/fork` has no `personality` field in the schema, so a fork never sends one. NEW stdin verb `set-response-style {style}` → `thread/settings/update {threadId, personality}` ("Override the personality for subsequent turns") — a LIVE switch, no restart; clearing sends `personality: null`, which is the only way back to the config-file default on a thread we already styled. The sidecar adverts `caps.responseStyle:true` and reports the EFFECTIVE `personality` (the 2.361.1/2.364.1 rule: a capability is read from the file the PROCESS writes). **SERVER REQUESTS: ONE EXPLICIT BRANCH PER METHOD (2.369.54).** `SERVER_REQUEST_SPEC` is the table; each of the eleven ServerRequest methods has its own reply schema and they share NO vocabulary — `item/commandExecution/requestApproval` {decision: accept|acceptForSession|{acceptWithExecpolicyAmendment}|{applyNetworkPolicyAmendment}|decline|cancel} · `item/fileChange/requestApproval` {decision: accept|acceptForSession|decline|cancel} (**no** amendment variants) · `item/permissions/requestApproval` **{permissions: GrantedPermissionProfile, scope, strictAutoReview}** — a GRANT, not a decision, so approving echoes the requested profile and denying grants `{fileSystem:null, network:null}` · `item/tool/requestUserInput` **bare `{answers: {<question id>: {answers:[…]}}}`** (no `decision` field; cancel = an empty map) · `mcpServer/elicitation/request` `{action: accept|decline|cancel, content?}` · the v1 pair `applyPatchApproval`/`execCommandApproval` `{decision: ReviewDecision}` = approved | approved_for_session | {denied:{rejection}} | abort. Until 2.369.54 exactly ONE method was matched by name and everything else was answered `{decision:…}` — a result the server cannot deserialize for four of them, i.e. a hung turn nobody could explain, plus a bogus "Permission" card for requests no human should see. Classification now happens at ARRIVAL (`handleServerRequest`): **auto** (`currentTime/read` → `{currentTimeAt:<unix seconds>}`, answered instantly, never a card), **unsupported** (`item/tool/call`, `attestation/generate`, `account/chatgptAuthTokens/refresh`, and any unknown method — refused with a JSON-RPC **error** −32601 exactly like the ACP wrapper, so the call FAILS CLEANLY instead of hanging, plus an `event_msg client_request_unsupported` the normalizer renders as a system line and fires `codex-unknown-record:server_request:unsupported:<method>` for), and **ask** (a card). `normalizeNestedAnswers(value, questions)` fixes two silent answer-eaters at once: the shared AskUserQuestion UI sends ONE PLAIN STRING per question (a non-array value used to be skipped, the map came out empty and the caller reported "answered nothing") and keys its map by the question TEXT while codex routes by question ID. MCP elicitation rows are derived ONCE (`elicitationQuestions`, from `requestedSchema` — enum/oneOf/boolean become options, `mode:'url'` becomes a plain approve card) and travel WITH the `server_request` record, so the card and the reply mapping can never drift; `elicitationContent` types each accepted property per the schema (a boolean property arrives as a JSON boolean, not "true"). **imageGeneration + sleep (2.369.54):** neither had a branch, so a generated image was invisible while streaming (it only appeared after a re-attach merged the rollout) and a 20-minute `clock.sleep` looked exactly like a hang. `item/started` opens a PENDING card (`image_gen {prompt, path}` / `sleep {durationMs}` — the SAME input keys the completion carries, so the merge cannot drift) and `item/completed` is recorded in codex's OWN rollout spelling, `event_msg item_completed {item:{type:'Extension', kind:'image_gen.generation'|'clock.sleep', …}}` — the same record the rollout persists and codex-thread-read synthesizes, so THREE producers hit ONE card path and the live copy converges with the rollout copy on the item id. The item's `result` (a 3 MB base64 PNG in the real records) is deliberately NOT carried: the card names `savedPath` and the browser draws the file (2.369.35 law). Gate: scripts/test-harness-honesty.mjs. **THE EFFORT A TURN IS RUNNING AT (2.369.62, owner "我刚才把那个van的对话调成了 ultra, 但我看它回复怎么都 metadata 显示的是 xhigh?"):** the wrapper synthesizes the `turn_context` every reader takes a turn's reasoning effort from — 0.153.4's `TurnStartedNotification` is `{threadId, turn:{id,status,startedAt,items…}}`, **no effort, no model** (schema-checked), so the value can only come from our own bookkeeping, and the two turn ORIGINS have different answers. THREE named facts replace the one `effort` var: `effort` = the COMMANDED value for the next turn (spawn env / set-effort), `threadEffort` = the app-server's own word for the thread (`thread/start|resume|fork` reply `reasoningEffort`, `thread/settings/updated`, and our own accepted commands), `turnEffort` = what the ACTIVE turn runs at. At `turn/started`: a turn WE started (`turnStartsInFlight > 0`) runs at what we put on `turn/start` (TurnStartParams.effort is documented "Override the reasoning effort for this turn **and subsequent turns**" ⇒ commanding one re-points the thread, so `startTurn` moves `threadEffort` BEFORE the RPC and reverts on refusal); a turn the APP-SERVER started (queue drain, resume auto-continue, goal continuation) runs at the THREAD's effort and never sees our pending value. **The resume race is the incident**: the app-server pushes `turn/started` for an auto-continued turn BEFORE the `thread/resume` reply that carries `reasoningEffort` (measured order in the owner's own buffer: goal_cleared, turn_context, task_started, session_meta, wrapper_meta — all inside 07:06:44.565-.566Z), so the first copy can only quote the spawn env; `noteTurnEffort()` then RESTATES the same turn id once the reply lands (same id ⇒ no reader sees a new turn — the normalizer bumps turnIndex only on a CHANGED id and mergeCodexRecords folds the repeat into the first copy). `buildTurnContext` carries `wrapper:true` (this copy is SYNTHESIZED — codex's own outranks it in the fold) and states `effort_next` only when a re-pick is waiting. `recordWrapperMeta()` is the ONE writer of the sidecar's `effort` (= `liveTurnEffort()` = turnEffort ‖ threadEffort ‖ effort) and of the record's `{effort, effortNext}` pair — a client attaching mid-turn reads both without a rollout re-read; its fingerprint carries `activeTurnId` so a per-turn refresh survives a rebuild instead of collapsing onto the boot copy. **`set-effort` now tells the app-server too** (`thread/settings/update {threadId, effort}` — "Override the reasoning effort for subsequent turns"; same verb the response-style switch already used): before this only `turn/start` carried our value, so a turn the app-server started on its own still ran at the OLD effort while the UI claimed the new one. A refusal is logged and leaves the per-turn path as the fallback it always was. The change also records `event_msg thread_settings_applied` in codex's OWN rollout spelling — **only when the RPC was ACCEPTED, and MARKED `wrapper: true` (r2 review)**: emitting it after a refusal wrote a record, in codex's spelling and unattributed, describing a setting codex had just rejected, and our 5-key `thread_settings` can never dedup against codex's real 9-key one (`model_provider_id`, `approvals_reviewer`, `collaboration_mode`, `permission_profile`…) so a rebuilt history kept it forever; a refusal now says nothing about the THREAD and the pending pick still rides `wrapper_meta.effortNext`, which is a statement about US. The same marker goes on the `thread/settings/updated` transcription (`{type, thread_id, wrapper, thread_settings:{model, approval_policy, cwd, reasoning_effort, personality}}`, snake_case, read verbatim off a real 0.153.4 rollout) so the live twin and the rebuilt history feed the normalizer one shape. Gate: scripts/test-codex-effort-meta.mjs (64). **THE VERB TABLE — reorder / edit / run-now / run-all (2026-09-07, design-harness-features §2.1, owner decision 1):** the ws frame speaks RELATIVE user intent (`{op:'reorder', id, afterId}` with `afterId:null` = the front; `{op:'edit', id, text}`; `{op:'run-now', id}`; `{op:'run-all'}`) and the app-server's ABSOLUTE shapes are synthesised HERE, one RPC before they go out — the two layers deliberately share no vocabulary, because this queue has more than one writer (the peer lane rides it: backend-caps `peerDelivery:'rpc-queue'`). ① **`listQueueAll()` is the only reader a full-order verb may use**: `thread/queue/list` paginates (`{cursor, limit}` → `nextCursor`, measured), and the pre-verb-table `refreshQueue` threw the cursor away — so a queue longer than one page was PUBLISHED short (the strip, the sidecar mirror and the Stop sweep all saw a truncated queue), and a reorder computed from it is REFUSED by the app-server (`queue reorder must include every queued submission exactly once`, measured — it does NOT silently delete the unread tail, which corrects the design doc's assumption). It returns `{rows, complete}`; a partial read still publishes (a degraded view beats none) but `reorder` refuses with `reason:'incomplete'`, the Stop sweep says what it never enumerated, and `refreshQueue` skips the `queueMeta` prune (a partial list would evict the kind/msgId of items on the pages it never reached). A repeated cursor or 50 pages stop the walk as INCOMPLETE rather than looping. ② **the order is computed from a FRESH list at landing time**: `reorderedIds(ids, id, afterId)` (PURE) moves the item inside the server's current order, so an id the client never saw — a peer `queue/add` between the render and the drop — keeps its server-side place simply by being in that list. An anchor that has left answers `reason:'anchor-gone'` and sends NOTHING (a guessed position is worse than a refusal). ③ **`replaceQueuedText(input, text)` (PURE) preserves BY EXCLUSION**: the first `text` element takes the new words with its `text_elements` CLEARED (those byteRanges index the old buffer), later text elements are dropped, and everything else — image / localImage / **audio** / **localAudio** / skill / mention, and whatever an eighth `UserInput` variant turns out to be — is carried over byte for byte, in place. A whitelist would delete attachments the day the protocol grows; `queuePreview`'s `[image]`/`[audio]`/`[skill …]`/`[@…]` markers are what the user can see of them. ④ **peer items are not editable** (`queueMeta.kind==='peer'` ⇒ `reason:'not-editable'`) — rewriting another agent's words is a misattribution; removing and reordering them stays allowed. ⑤ **run-now / run-all are `thread/queue/start` WITH and WITHOUT an id**, and they are SEPARATE verbs everywhere (the adapter throws when `run-now` has no id) because an id-less start drains the whole queue — one lost id must never become a drain. Both refuse with `reason:'busy'` while `meta.activeTurnId` is set, saying the queue runs when the turn ends, instead of waiting (accept-and-ignore in a different hat). They matter on a RESUMED thread, which can come back idle with items already queued — measured 2026-09-07: a `thread/queue/add` on an idle thread is drained by the app-server at once, so an idle non-empty queue is otherwise rare. ⑥ every op ends with `refreshQueue()` — **the closing publish is the truth** — and every `ok:false` carries `reason` AND `detail`; `classifyQueueMutationFailure` maps the server's own words (`not found` ⇒ `gone`, the exactly-once complaint ⇒ `stale-order`). ⑦ each queue item now carries its FULL `text` (kind 'user', ≤20000 chars) so the client's edit control opens the MESSAGE and not the 120-char preview — an item over the cap simply carries none and shows no edit control. ⑧ the verbs this build serves are advertised twice from ONE list (`QUEUE_VERBS_SERVED`): sidecar `caps.queueVerbs` (what a LOCAL server reads) and `verbs` on EVERY `queue_changed` (the only advert a REMOTE session's orchestrator can see). Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper ⑦ (a PAGINATING stub with items injected behind the wrapper's back: the whole queue published across 3 pages, the merge of an unknown id, an anchor-gone refusal with no RPC, all seven `UserInput` variants surviving an edit with `text_elements` cleared, a peer refusal, busy-vs-idle run-now/run-all, and run-all's id-LESS params) + test-queue-steer ⑩ (the same builders lifted out of this file and driven against a REAL 0.153.4 app-server on a throwaway logged-out CODEX_HOME — nothing billed, and the leg asserts it never sends turn/start or thread/queue/start). **NOTIFICATIONS STEER, HUMANS QUEUE (2026-09-07, owner decision; the `peer-message` verb):** the delivery ladder now TYPES its frame (`kind:'notification'` | `'peer'`; anything else, including an older server that sends none, is a PEER — the conservative lane). A `'notification'` arriving while `meta.activeTurnId` is set is **steered** into that turn instead of queued behind it: `steerInput(encodeUserInput(text,[]), 'notif-…')` → `peer_message_result {ok:true, mode:'steered'}` and the same `recordPeerMessage()` the other two lanes write, so the labelled peer card renders live and on rebuild exactly as before (its rollout twin fingerprint-dedupes through `webui_peer` stripping, as ever). **INTEGRATION with the inherited-queue rule (2026-09-07): the id is a NAME, not a throwaway** — `notifCid` is minted first, registered with `noteRecordedUserCid()` BEFORE the record and carried as the record's `webui_queue_id`, because the steered submission now enters the turn under an id the app-server will hand back in its own `item/completed {userMessage, clientId}` at the next turn boundary: without the registration that carrier would write a SECOND, anonymous bubble beside the labelled card (exactly the branch round 1 added), and without the id two identical notifications in one turn would collide on the content key. The incident: a session holding 20 "[VibeSpace Background Work] … done" items as 20 queued submissions = 20 billed turns behind the running one. `steerInput` is the ONE `turn/steer` call site (extracted from `steerOne`, which now calls it and keeps its own delete-on-success half) — it sends the caller's input and NOTHING else, and it never reads or writes the queue, which is why a notification cannot disturb what the user has queued: items already queued keep their place AND their order (the suite's negative control for the "carry the queue along" variant, which would have had to delete them). That is upstream behaviour, not our design: `turn/steer` maps `params.input` into ONE `TurnInput::UserInput` (codex-rs v0.153.4 `app-server/src/request_processors/turn_processor.rs`:1023-1039) and core drains every pending steer wholesale before each model request (`core/src/session/turn.rs`:312-323 → `session/input_queue.rs get_pending_input`, `pending_input.items.split_off(0)`), so consecutive notifications merge by themselves. A REFUSED steer is a designed path — the turn ended between the `activeTurnId` check and the RPC (`no active turn to steer` / `expected active turn id …`), or the turn is a review/compact one — and it FALLS BACK by re-reading `meta.activeTurnId`: still busy ⇒ `thread/queue/add` (`mode:'queued'`), idle ⇒ `startTurn` (`mode:'turn'`), both carrying `steerFailed:<reason>` + `steerDetail` so the server can log the lane the message actually took (stdout/codex-events prints it) — a queued notification is never a silent divergence from the documented rule. HUMAN peer messages are deliberately unchanged (`thread/queue/add`, their own turn): a person's message is its own task, and folding it into someone else's running turn would change what the agent was asked to do mid-answer. The ACP wrapper serves the same typed frame but ACP v1 has no steer method at all (`session/prompt` is one-at-a-time; opencode's `queueVerbs` omit `steer` for that structural reason), so a mid-prompt notification queues there and SAYS so on the wire — `peer_result {ok:true, mode:'queued', steer:'unsupported'}` — rather than accepting-and-ignoring the tag. Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper ②f (own stub app-server, three steer modes: one steer + zero queue/add on an empty queue; one steer + ZERO queue/delete with three items queued, order intact; a human frame and an untyped frame both queueing; the turn-ended race falling back to its own turn; an unsteerable turn falling back to the queue; the labelled card live + rebuilt + merged with codex's own rollout copy; an XSS notification carried as DATA) + test-peer-delivery (the typed frame + every wiring pin). **THE INHERITED QUEUE / `userMessage` (2026-09-07, owner "我刚才在那个codex session里全给插入了，但是我只能看到我最后插入的一条消息"):** a user bubble is written by the record the wrapper makes as the text passes THROUGH it (handleInput chat-input / the peer lane) — but the app-server's queue belongs to the THREAD and outlives us, so a resumed thread hands the NEW wrapper a queue it never filled (measured on the owner's session: `queue_changed n=25` two seconds after boot, every `clientUserMessageId` minted by the wrapper this one replaced). Steering that queue put 25 messages into the turn and produced ONE bubble. The app-server's own carrier for "this submission is now in the turn" is `item/completed {item:{type:'userMessage', id, clientId, content:UserInput[]}}` (0.153.4 `UserMessageThreadItem`; the rollout persists it as `item_completed` / `UserMessage` / `client_id`), and it fell off the end of `_handleItemCompletedInner` with no branch. Now: `recordInboundUserMessage(cid, userInputToContent(content))` writes the bubble for any submission ENTERING the turn whose id we have not already recorded — from the item/completed twin (a DRAINED item, or one steered by a second client) and, for immediacy, the moment a `turn/steer` LANDS (the twins arrived 42s later on the owner's session, at the model's next turn boundary; a refused steer records nothing — that message is still queued). `recordedUserCids` remembers every id whose bubble already exists (chat-input msgId, the generated `queued-…` id, the peer cid), and a **clientId-less** item is ours by construction (only `turn/start` carries none). The record carries THREE markers, all out-of-band in the normalizer's `recordKey` (so both copies of one message mint the same id): `webui_queue_id` = the submission's own clientUserMessageId, which the merge KEYS the record by (round 2, 2026-09-07 — keying it on CONTENT instead deleted the second of two same-text steers on reload); `webui_queue_via` = 'steered' | 'drained', forensics for a buffer dump; and `webui_after_commit: true` on the drained one — **the reader's contract**, meaning "the app-server had already persisted its own copy when this record was written", so the merge lets that copy stand instead of doubling the bubble. The idle peer path (`recordPeerMessage(true)`, after `turn/start` has committed) is the OTHER producer that declares it; a wrapper producer of a user record answers that one question before it ships (scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑨(j6) counts them). The queue ROW advertises the same clientUserMessageId as its `msgId` (`queueItemsFrom` / `steerOne`) so the strip row and the bubble's `Steered` chip join. **NO SILENT DROPS on the live side either:** the tail of `_handleItemCompletedInner` is `noteUnhandledItem(type)` — deliberate no-ops are NAMED (`NO_RENDER_COMPLETED_ITEMS`: hookPrompt/functionCallOutput/plan/entered+exitedReviewMode), anything else is logged once verbatim and counted in `meta.unhandledItems` (its absence is exactly how `userMessage` stayed lost). Gates: scripts/test-codex-p2-wrapper.mjs ⑥ (an inherited-queue stub + steer-all + drain + the PRE-FIX control run from `git show master:` — 12 steers, 1 bubble) and scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑨.


  **THREAD GATE (B-7473, the owner's "根本没区分出这是subagent消息"):** the app-server relays notifications for EVERY thread it hosts, and the old `item/started|completed` handler took `params.item` and IGNORED `params.threadId` — a CHILD's `agentMessage` was recorded as a ROOT `message role=assistant` (forensics: production buffer `sess-4-1788639964629.buf` line 414 is `/root/usecases_v4`'s FINAL_ANSWER; the root rollout holds the same text as an `agent_message` from that child ⇒ the owner also saw it TWICE). Now: **the gate is INVERTED (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06)** — the question is not "is this method in my list of thread-scoped methods" (a method whitelist goes stale: `error`, `thread/compacted`, `thread/queue/changed` and `turn/diff/updated` all carry `threadId` in the 0.153.4 bindings and were ALL missing from the first cut, so a CHILD's error notification became the ROOT's `task_failed` + error card + turn end) but "does this notification NAME a thread, and is it mine": any `params.threadId !== meta.threadId` is FOREIGN unless the method is in the (today empty, documented) `THREAD_ID_NOT_SCOPE` allowlist; `thread/started`/`thread/resumed` are handled ABOVE the gate because they are what TEACHES us our id, and a notification arriving before `meta.threadId` is known cannot be judged and passes. A foreign `error` is recorded as the CHILD's `sub_agent_activity {kind:'errored', detail:<message>}` — a collab activity row, never this conversation's failure. Every foreign notification passes `foreignThreadOf(params)` FIRST; a foreign-thread `agentMessage` becomes an attributed `agent_message` record (`{type, id, thread_id, author, recipient, content:[output_text], phase, delivery, msg_type}`, author from `meta.subagents` = the agentPath→threadId map learned from `subAgentActivity`, else the thread id), every other foreign kind is DROPPED with a once-per-thread log + a `meta.foreignDrops{total, threads{<tid>:{dropped, agentMessages, agentPath}}}` counter. An OWN-thread `agentMessage` whose text starts with the inter-agent envelope (`Message Type: … / Task name: … / Sender: /root/x`) is ALSO an `agent_message` (author = the Sender line) — it was written FOR another agent, never a reply to the user. **The ENVELOPE is the only signal; `delivery === 'async'` is NOT (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06, MAJOR):** on an own-thread `agentMessage` that flag marks a question/async reply the USER is meant to read, and treating it as inter-agent DROPPED the text from the transcript entirely (an asked question simply vanished). Pinned with the stub: an own-thread `delivery:'async'` message with no envelope must appear as an assistant message. **`meta.agentPath`** (this thread's own path, the reference point that makes a message inbound or outbound) is ASSIGNED from the thread/start|resume reply when it carries one and echoed in the wrapper's `session_meta` record — it used to be READ in three places and never written; 0.153.4's `Thread` struct carries agentNickname/agentRole/parentThreadId but NO agentPath (checked against the installed binary's serde field list), so the '/root' default is what a VibeSpace-spawned thread actually is, and a RESUMED sub-agent conversation gets its real path from codex's own `session_meta` at line 0 of its rollout. `subAgentActivity` items fill the map and emit the live `event_msg sub_agent_activity` (the 0.153.4 rollout carries the same fact as `item_completed/SubAgentActivity`; the normalizer routes both to ONE path). Every item record now rides `recordItem()`, which stamps the notification's `thread_id`/`turn_id` — **both are stripped from `recordFingerprint` (codex-session-store) and `CodexMessageManager.recordKey`, or every buffer record becomes a stranger to its rollout twin (double cards on each attach)**. `caps.threadScoped: true` adverts it. `AGENT_ENVELOPE_RE`/`parseAgentEnvelope` are MIRRORED in src/codex-message-manager.js (a shipped single file cannot require it) — change one, change both. Pins: test-codex-p2-wrapper ④ (stub pushes a child agentMessage / an own-thread envelope one / a subAgentActivity / a plain root reply ⇒ exactly ONE assistant record). RESIDUE: records a PRE-B-7473 wrapper already wrote keep their root-assistant shape (the normalizer cannot tell them from a real reply) — they age out with the buffer; only NEW traffic is gated, and a restart of that session is the immediate cure.

### plugin-loader.js / plugin-manifest.js / plugin-client.js (Plugin Ph2 minimal, 2.369.24)

  The plugin system's first runtime slice (docs/design-harness-plugins.md §3.2–3.3). **Trust model as shipped**: iframe tier = untrusted UI (opaque origin, the published-pages CSP posture — NEVER add allow-same-origin; the bridge exposes only namespaced localStorage, toasts, close); server tier = a separate PROCESS with an IPC-only API (`route`/`tool`/`shutdown`) — plugin code is never require()d into the orchestrator; agent tools = generated shims that carry the calling session's vsst_ token to `/api/agent/plugin-tool/<id>/<name>` (cookie-exempt prefix ⇒ the route validates the bearer itself; unknown token = 401). Invariants: an invalid manifest is LISTED with its errors (never silently skipped) and cannot be enabled; enable/disable is atomic + broadcast; a crashing plugin restarts with backoff and is parked after 5 crashes/10min (never a crash loop against the box); shims are regenerated on every change and removed on disable; `hostVersion` is read from `<rootDir>/package.json` (never a relative require — the 2.341.1 lost-binding class). Trusted client modules, contributed settings/themes/keybindings, install UI, capability enforcement and remote shim shipping are the next slices (B-bb56/B-93f9). **SECURITY POSTURE after the 2.369.43 review (kb-bugfix-invariants “PLUGIN SECURITY BATCH”): a manifest is untrusted input — its free text may reach generated code only as a JSON literal (jsLiteral) and is sanitized once in the validator (cleanText); a declared fs path is collapsed — and its `*` wildcard MODELLED as the string prefix node actually matches (2.369.44) — before the forbidden-root test; contributed agent tools NEED consent (a shim is a program on every session PATH, outside the sandbox — the shipped hello-plugin example is consent-gated now); consent is recorded per PACKAGE (contentHash + source) and the reset is keyed on the REGISTRY RECORD, not on a folder having been replaced (2.369.44 — consent outlives a hand-deleted plugin dir), so replacing the code behind a trusted id disables it and re-prompts; proxied /x/* replies are data (nosniff + sandbox CSP, no document content-type from an untrusted plugin).** Pins: scripts/test-plugin-security.mjs (62) + scripts/test-plugin-loader.mjs (43: validator matrix + the shipped docs/examples/hello-plugin driven end to end — assets/CSP/traversal, IPC routes, tool + shim + 401, lifecycle).

### window-types.js (client window-type registry, Plugin Ph1)

  window-types.js — the first client contribution-point registry (2.369.19, docs/design-harness-plugins.md §3.2 `windows[]`): `registerWindowType({ type, icon (svg), label, replay(app, spec) → winInfo|null, persist?, singleton? })`, `getWindowType`, `windowTypeIcon`, `replayOpenSpec(app, spec)`. replayOpenSpec used to be an 18-case switch with no default — an unknown `openSpec.action` was silently dropped; now it warns + emits telemetry `openspec-unknown`. Core registers every existing kind at module load in the module that owns the window (session-lifecycle chat/terminal, file-viewer/editor, usage/settings/task/workflow/session-props/browser/jobs/desktop…), byte-identical behaviour; `TYPE_ICONS` (tab-group.js) is now a live view over the registry so existing importers keep working. A plugin's `windows[]` contribution will call the same registerWindowType. Pins: scripts/test-window-types.mjs (53: DOM-free node import, fake type dispatch, unknown-action warning, the exact core type/action sets, no switch/TYPE_ICONS literal left).

### contributions.js / gear-menu.js (client commands + menus + keybindings registry, Plugin Ph1)

  contributions.js — the second client contribution-point registry (docs/design-harness-plugins.md §3.2 `commands[]` + `menus[] {where, when, group}` + `keybindings[]`), built to window-types.js's rules: DOM-free at import (only telemetry-client), node-imported directly by its gate. `registerCommand({ id, title (string | ctx→string), run(ctx), when?(ctx), icon?, signal? })`, `registerMenuItem({ menu, command | label+run, id?, group?, order?, when?, label?, labelHtml?, disabled?, style?, tooltip?, children?, expand?, separator?, kind?, icon?, danger?, keepOpen?, decorate?, signal? })`, `menuItems(menu, ctx)` → showContextMenu-shaped items, `registerKeybinding({ key, command, when?, inTerminal?, signal? })` + `installKeybindings(document, { signal, getCtx })` (ONE bubble-phase dispatcher, app-lifetime AbortController in app.js), `runCommand(id, ctx)`. **Semantics (do not regress):** items sort by group ('navigation' first, then lexicographic), then `order`, then registration; `when` gates SURFACES (menus/keys) — `runCommand` never consults it (VS Code executeCommand); **SEPARATORS ARE EXPLICIT contributions** (VS Code's automatic between-group rule cannot reproduce the hand-built window menu, which joins Minimize and Close with NO rule when the session block is absent) and leading/trailing/doubled ones collapse; an EMPTY `children` result drops the item (the legacy `if (groups.length)` guards); `mod` = ctrl OR meta (the app's `(e.ctrlKey || e.metaKey)` convention), unlisted modifiers must be up; keys inside `.xterm` never match unless `inTerminal`; later bindings win. **Loud:** duplicate/malformed registrations THROW at module load; `runCommand` of an unknown id THROWS + telemetry `command-unknown`; an unknown command named by a menu item / keybinding warns once + telemetry (`menu-unknown-command` / `keybinding-unknown-command`) and is skipped; a throwing `when()` hides the item and warns once; a throwing keybound command is logged + `command-failed`.
