Every register* returns dispose() and honours `signal` — the plugin host API (plugin-client.js `api.registerCommand/registerMenuItem/registerKeybinding/runCommand`, ids namespaced `plugin:<id>:<slug>`) binds all three to the plugin's AbortController so contributions leave with the plugin. **Core dogfoods it, byte-identical:** the sidebar card menu = `session.*` commands + `registerSessionCardMenu()` in session-card.js (ctx `{ app, state, settings, s, card, event, displayName, customName, originalName, onRename, agentOpts }`); the window title-bar/taskbar/window-list menu = `window.*` commands + `registerWindowMenu()` in taskbar.js (ctx `{ app, id, win, s, switchSubmenu, closeLabel }`; `showWindowContextMenu`'s wrapAct fires `onAction(kind)` after items AND submenu children — Task Groups / Move to Desktop — never the Switch-window submenu; the session block REUSES `session.restart` (Restart / Resume labels via the item label override), `session.locate`, `session.properties`); the ⚙ menu = gear-menu.js `registerGearMenu()` (groups 0_prefs/1_admin/2_maint/2p_plugins(expand: plugin windows)/3_help, `decorate(el, ctx)` for the Language sub-menu row and the two-line Update row, `buildGearMenu(app, pop)` renders with escHtml'd labels; mobile-nav's gear shares it via `_showGlobalSettings`). Command mode registers every single-key action as `activeWindow.*`/`layout.*`/`desktop.*`/`session.new`/`sidebar.toggle`/`browser.open`/`explorer.open`/`commandMode.toggle`; the palette registers `session.palette`. **Their KEY DISPATCH deliberately stays where it was** (NOT registerKeybinding): the legacy checks are modifier-LENIENT (Ctrl+K opens on Ctrl+Shift+K too; Ctrl+\ ignores shift) and run in the capture phase in a load-bearing listener order, while the registry matcher is strict and bubble-phase — migrating them would change behaviour. Each register block is self-contained (`export function registerX() {…} registerX(); // end registerX`) so the gate can extract + replay it with its closed-over names injected. **THE FIRST CORE `registerKeybinding` CHORD (2026-09-07): `chat.steerNow` / `alt+enter`** (chat-view.js — see its essay). The pattern a future core chord copies: the COMMAND is registered ONCE at module scope behind `hasCommand()` (a duplicate id THROWS by design, so a per-instance registration would die on the second window), while the KEYBINDING is per instance, carries that instance's AbortSignal and a `when` that scopes the chord to the window the keystroke happened in — which is also what makes N simultaneous registrations of one chord legal (an unconditional duplicate pair throws; conditional ones are VS Code's own model). The dispatcher ctx is app-wide (`{ app, event }`), so the command resolves its target from `ctx.view` (an in-process caller handing itself in) or from the mounted view whose container holds `event.target` — never a "last focused" guess. The composer's own keydown routes through `runCommand` rather than calling the handler, so keyboard, button and plugin all run ONE verb. File-explorer menus (file-explorer-ops.js) are NOT migrated (out of the slice). Gate: scripts/test-contributions.mjs (functional in node + the three menus diffed against VERBATIM legacy builders over a state matrix + the REAL ws-handler default + wiring pins).

### ws-handler.js (heartbeat + attach rebuild)

  ws-handler.js — **`default:` case (Plugin Ph1): the `switch (data.type)` had NO default, so an unknown type vanished — no log, no telemetry, no reply, the sender waited forever.** Now `noteUnknownWsType(telemetry, type)` logs once per type per 10 minutes with the running count (a chatty stale client cannot flood the journal), records telemetry `ws-unknown-type` per occurrence, and replies `{ type:'error', code:'unknown-type', message, unknownType, reqId? }` — **deliberately WITHOUT sessionId**: ws.js routes session-scoped handlers by `d.sessionId`, and every client `error` consumer (chat-view's branch, terminal's per-session handler, session-lifecycle's create/attach/view handlers) keys on `msg.sessionId`/`msg.reqId` — a session-scoped error frame is what flips a live window into the exited/read-only path, and an unknown TYPE is not a dead session. `reqId` IS echoed so a `ws.request()` caller fails fast. `telemetry` joined WS_CTX_CONTRACT for it.

  ws-handler.js — **2.369.16 (userW inc-mtndq0vb): the first-attach history rebuild is TIME-SLICED and single-flight (`normalizers.rebuildHistory` → `convertHistoryAsync`, ~25ms slices yielding to the loop; concurrent attaches await one promise; live records arriving mid-rebuild queue on `session._rebuildQueue` and replay in order; `_historyLoaded` set after success). Every live record enters a session normalizer through `normalizers.feedLive(session, msg)` — a direct `session._normalizer.processLive` call is a gate bypass (test-attach-rebuild counts them = 0). Rebuilds are FIFO (one at a time — fair slicing landed every window at the total time) and the attach re-acks every 10s with `{progress}` while waiting/converting (client ladder extends on fresh acks, 15min bound), then re-checks the session is still live before sending `attached`. The heartbeat lives in src/server/ws-heartbeat.js and is STALL-AWARE with TWO detectors: tick lateness AND a 1s loop-gap pulse (a 30–35s block starting right after a ping ends with the tick only seconds late but the pong unread — the review's blind band); a tainted round terminates nobody, bounded at 6 consecutive pong-less tainted rounds; terminations are logged + telemetered. The old inline interval terminated a live client after a 4-minute rebuild stall and dropped its queued kills. `kill` replies `killed {sessionId: REQUESTED id, resolvedId, ok, reason}` to the requester in both outcomes (`exited` only reaches attached clients) — the client's `killSession` sends the backendSessionId fallback frame as a one-shot and resend-chases only the plain kill (a resent fallback would kill the resumed successor), with its watchdog disarming the request.**
  ws-handler.js — **B-21e4 item 5:** the first-attach warm and the view-only path both call `harnessOf(backend).store?.warmTranscript(id, cwd, {remote: !!host})` — the descriptor's pre-read hook (claude: worker parse-cache warm, unchanged; codex: the 0.153 thread/read fallback when the rollout file is missing, local only). The view-only path now warms claude too (a cache warm before the same sync parse — the 2.235.0 optimization, not a behaviour change).

### message-manager.js

  message-manager.js   — MessageManager (Claude stream-json → normalized messages with stable IDs). **Tool messages take `ts` from `_currentTs` (the record's own timestamp), never bare Date.now() (2.285.0, caught by the transcript parity suite's first run): the hand-rolled tool-message path bypassed _create's ts ladder, so every REBUILD-rendered tool card (restart/view-only/resume) was timestamped with the rebuild time — msg-meta popup showed the wrong time for all historical tool calls.** **convertHistoryAsync (2.369.16) = the time-sliced twin used by the ws attach path (both normalizers; parity-pinned byte-identical to convertHistory); callers hold live records back while it runs (feedLive gate).** **convertHistory is per-record isolated + _historyLoaded set AFTER successful rebuild (2.89.2)** — a single throwing record amputated every restart-rebuilt view at that point and the flag-before-work made it permanent (real fleet-wide incident: the 2.80.0 `output`→`raw.output` hook-card typo threw on every buffered hook_response; live path swallowed it per-line so it hid for 9 releases). **queued_command attachments = mid-turn user messages (2.88.0)**: the JSONL's ONLY record of a message sent while the agent works — normalized into a real user message (typed-flagged, echo-deduped by exact text vs the last 12) or the user's words vanish from every JSONL-rebuilt history (restart/resume/view-only; real incident, 211 records). msg.typed (promptSource/_fromWebui) beats notification text-shape in renderUserMsg; msg.synthetic (isSynthetic) forces it **任务生命周期独立索引(2.368.15): 一切task closer不得经pendingToolCalls找卡——tool_result秒删表项而完成通知晚数分钟(每对话都攒永久running卡的根因); taskMsgByToolUse/taskMsgByTaskId整会话存活, _taskMsgFor三级解析; test-task-wakeup-card钉真实record顺序(tool_result在通知之前)。** **历史可用的生命周期真源(2.368.30, owner: c1206711很多subagent任务没识别): task_started/task_progress/task_notification系统subtype只在活流上存在——602MB现场转录里为0条——所以resume后一切派生自它们的状态全灭; 落盘的是启动ACK与<task-notification>用户记录, parseBackgroundLaunch(PURE导出, session-store taskState扫描共用, 无twin)从ACK合成taskInfo(Agent的agentId行/Workflow的Run ID/后台Bash的Command running行), 通知记录关闭并带summary(卡片优先显示); 幽灵裁剪=合成running且启动时间早于当前wrapper startedAt者删除(OS任务活不过CLI进程, 现场16个僵尸watcher); test-task-lifecycle(19, 真实转录fixture)。**通知有三条运输(2.368.31, owner复报'又大量running'): 空闲=user记录, 忙碌=queue-operation(enqueue/remove)+attachment(queued_command)——重工作流会话几乎全走后者, closer只认user记录时17个phantom; _closeTaskFromNotification统一三运输(normalizer+scan), queued投递渲染notification卡而非'You'XML气泡; resumeFromRunId同id再启动supersede原卡; taskState()=scan打底+wrapper覆盖MERGE绝非fallback(1个活条目曾顶掉全部41个, 双形态载荷律第二击)。** 2.369.35 LAW: binary never enters a normalized message — splitToolResultContent lifts image blocks out of tool results as {mediaType, bytes} (a Read of a PNG returned ~600KB base64 per card; stringified into `output` it made a 12MB attach payload and froze the renderer — two windows 'stuck opening'); chat-renderers shows the file from disk (/api/file/raw) or a size chip. **2.369.48 (owner: "view image 能不能也多媒体化"): the lifted `images` now drive a MEDIA CARD** — `imageMediaHtml` in chat-renderers renders every image a tool looked at (claude Read of a png/jpg — whose branch had returned BEFORE the generic thumbnail splice, so the exact case 2.369.35 was built for never showed a thumbnail, and the splice's URL carried a stray `''` — codex view_image, MCP tools naming an image file) as an OPEN `<details class="chat-media">` with the `/api/file/raw` thumbnail (lazy; `&host=` from the renderer's `_sessionCtx()` for remote sessions) + the standard .chat-img zoom; no path on disk = size chip; a failed load flips `.chat-media-broken` through ChatView's capture-phase error delegate ("Image not available on this machine"). **THE LIFTED BLOCKS DECIDE, NEVER THE EXTENSION** (a refuted first cut classified a `Read` by `IMAGE_EXT_RE` and dropped the source silently): `viewsImage` for a Read is `images.length > 0` and nothing else — claude returns numbered TEXT for a text-source image format, measured for .svg (real fleet shape, cli 2.1.85: all 10 local `Read *.svg` tool_results are plain strings starting `"1\t<svg …"`; .ico is the same class, unmeasured), which keeps its ordinary code-block card and only gains a thumbnail appended BELOW it because the browser can draw that type. `IMAGE_EXT_RE`/`isImagePath` answer ONE question — can the browser decode this as an `<img>`? — so they gate thumbnail-vs-chip only: a tiff/heic Read that DID return image blocks renders the image card with the type/size chip and NO `<img>` (an unrenderable format must not become a broken thumbnail). The SAME evidence rule governs the fold: `messageKind` (src/lib/chat-run-summary.js) calls a Read 'image' only when `content[0].images` is non-empty, so a text `.svg` read is counted and folded as an ordinary file read (image-card review round 2, 2026-09-06). Gate: scripts/test-image-cards.mjs against the REAL renderer module in node (both directions pinned, negative-controlled). **THE INIT FRAME IS KEPT WHOLE (§2.6 of docs/design-harness-features.md): `_processSystem`'s init branch used to keep three fields (`model` / `permissionMode` / `slash_commands`) and drop the rest of a record that also carries `tools` / `mcp_servers[{name,status}]` / `agents` / `skills` / `plugins[{name,path,source,version}]` / `plugin_errors` / `plugin_warnings` / `mcp_server_errors` / `terminal_slash_commands` / `output_style` / `memory_paths{auto,team}` / `betas` / `claude_code_version` — every name VERBATIM from the 2.1.257 binary's own zod schema (`initFrameFacts`, PURE + exported; scripts/test-init-frame.mjs re-greps each one out of the installed binary and SKIPs with evidence when there is none). The facts ride `initData.frame` on the same card, so no consumer of model/permissionMode/slashCommands changes. **Absent ≠ empty**: a key the CLI did not send is DROPPED, never zero-filled — an old CLI must not read as a session with zero skills. (Which keys can actually be absent is a MEASURED fact, not an assumption: only `agents` / `betas` / `terminal_slash_commands` / `plugin_errors` / `plugin_warnings` / `mcp_server_errors` / `memory_paths` are `.optional()` in the schema; the rest are required, which is why the card's gate is not a version test — see chat-renderers.js.) **`initData.frameRepeat` (round 2)**: the branch fingerprints each frame and marks one identical to the PREVIOUS init as a repeat, so the renderer draws one card per distinct frame instead of one per spawn (33 in one real conversation here at the time of measurement — see chat-renderers.js for the two snapshots). The fingerprint is snapshotted at init time, BEFORE `commands_changed` patches `_initFrame.slashCommands` in place — reading it back off that object would make the next identical init look changed and silently disable the dedup (pinned). `commands_changed` (the CLI's fire-and-forget full-list push, whose rows are RICH `{name, description, argumentHint, aliases?}` objects, NOT init's plain strings) is now HANDLED: it REPLACES the list (upstream's own word), patches the init card in place for a later history rebuild, and re-emits the ONE command-list op. **That op is a `meta` op (`subtype:'slash-commands'`, `{commands, terminal}`), not an `edit`** — an `edit` on a COMPLETE system card is never re-rendered by ChatView, so its side effects never re-run and the composer would keep the boot list for the whole session (the ACP and codex normalizers emit the same op for the same reason: ONE client path, no local/remote twin).**
  ws-handler.js — **`queue-op` (2026-09-06, the ONE new case for QUEUED vs STEERED):** `{op:'steer'|'remove'|'steer-all', id}` validated against `capsOf(session.backend).inputModes` — a CAPABILITY, never a backend id (the frame-file-by-backend-id incident's rule). `queueOps:false` ⇒ "<Harness> owns its own input queue"; `steer` on a `steer:false` harness ⇒ "<Harness> cannot steer: a message sent during a turn runs after it. You can remove it instead." `adapter.formatQueueOp` is the second line of defense (its throw becomes the same refusal). **A SECOND GATE, the RUNNING WRAPPER:** `wrapperCaps(BUFFERS_DIR, id, socketPath).inputQueue` (the sidecar advert the wrapper itself writes) — or, for a REMOTE wrapper whose sidecar lives on ITS OWN machine, the in-band proof `session._normalizer.queuePublished()` — must also be true — the caps row describes a KIND of agent, while a dtach session started before this release satisfies the row and would have its `queue-op` frame dropped SILENTLY. Its refusal names the wrapper's start time and says "Terminate + Resume the session to get the controls"; the verdict is re-read every time (never cached negative — a wrapper resuming a huge thread writes its sidecar late; the 2.364.1 law). Every refusal is `{type:'error', code:'queue-op-unsupported', scope:'action', sessionId, error, message}` — and the CLIENT rule is an EXPLICIT SET, not "anything coded": **a per-session `error` is a scoped refusal (renders in chat, window untouched) when it carries `scope:'action'` or one of `SCOPED_REFUSAL_CODES` (input-rejected, not-codex-chat, queue-op-unsupported); everything else is the attach failure it used to be read as** (inc-mt2arppw was `input-rejected`; `not-codex-chat` had been read-only'ing live windows the same way ever since — but `ended-during-attach`, raised right here after the history rebuild, is CODED AND FATAL and must keep taking the view-only rescue; round-1 review caught the over-generalization). The attach payload gained `queue` (`session._normalizer.queueState()`) + `queueSupported`, and `created` carries `queue: []` + `queueSupported: false` (a just-spawned wrapper has reported nothing yet; its own baseline `queue_changed` turns the controls on) — the 2.368.4 enumerate-every-window-birth rule. **THE VERB TABLE (2026-09-07):** the gate is now `capsOf(backend).inputModes.queueVerbs` — no verbs ⇒ "<Harness> owns its own input queue", an undeclared verb ⇒ its OWN sentence from `queueVerbRefusal(op, label)`, each of which says what happens to the message anyway (a refusal that only says "no" leaves the user wondering whether it is lost). The frame grew `afterId` (carried ONLY when the client sent one, because `null` MEANS the front) and `text`; every refusal also carries a machine `reason` beside the sentence. The wrapper gate became a VERB gate too: `wrapperCaps().queueVerbs` (sidecar) or the normalizer's in-band `queueVerbsPublished()` (remote), with a wrapper that adverts `inputQueue` and no list mapped to `LEGACY_QUEUE_VERBS = remove/steer/steer-all` — an old build must neither be asked for a verb it would drop nor lose the verbs it does serve; a verb it does not serve is refused by NAME, listing what it does serve. The attach payload gained `queueVerbs` next to `queueSupported`, so the client renders exactly the controls this process will honour — **`null`, never `[]`, when the server knows nothing** (`created` always, `attached` when neither sidecar nor in-band list is readable): an empty ARRAY is a real answer the client INTERSECTS with, and sending it for "unknown" hid the entire strip from a session the server was serving the legacy three for (round-2 review). **ROUND-2 SERVER HARDENING (2026-09-07):** ① every refusal ECHOES `op` and `id` — this reply never becomes a `queue_op_result`, so the id is the only way the strip can find the row it marked pending (those rows spun forever); ② `edit` is the one verb carrying USER TEXT into a RAW PTY STDIN write, so it is bounded twice, both loudly: `QUEUE_EDIT_MAX_CHARS` (20000 — the wrapper's OWN cap, the one above which it publishes no `text` and therefore offers no edit control; parity-pinned) and, after the frame is built, `QUEUE_OP_MAX_BYTES` (64 KiB, the same threshold the `input` case treats as the shredding line — JSON escaping can multiply a control-char string sixfold, so the char cap does not imply the byte one). Both constants live in src/server/wrapper-files.js beside `LEGACY_QUEUE_VERBS`, which itself moved to the PURE `src/backend-caps.js` because the CLIENT applies the same mapping.

  codex-message-manager.js — CodexMessageManager (Codex JSON-RPC/rollout → normalized messages). **2.368.15审计(真实582记录会话): custom_tool_call_output从未被路由(function_call_output的孪生; 84输出全丢⇒52工具卡永久pending)——已路由+块数组输出({type:input_text,text}[])扁平化为文本; sub_agent_activity(codex子代理线程, event_id=call_id/agent_thread_id指真实rollout)started时出系统行, interacted不刷屏, 无终态kind故绝不造task chip(永久running类); token_count的usage meta携带contextWindow(live context%此前要重attach); rollout历史reasoning只有encrypted_content+空summary=上游加密, 历史无思考可显示(live流的delta照常渲染)。test-codex-history(7)进gate。** **2.368.16: codex并发流式消息——collab/子代理回合把多个message item的delta逐字符交错, delta处理器切换key时不得finalize其它open流(曾把两条消息切成逐段碎片"断 AA 边"等, owner原话复现于真实buffer回放); 每条流由自己的response_item:message按key原位收尾, turn结束仍全体finalize。账号面: billing chip与switcher全局行对codex显示ChatGPT login且不挂claude机器配额chips。** **P1 peer/通知卡片(design-harness-plugins §1): 此前无injectPeerCard⇒normalizers.feedPeerCard对每个codex会话静默返回false(auto-resume公告/Background Work drain/vibespace-msg卡片全部丢失), rpc-queue通道wrapper记的user消息渲染成匿名"You"气泡。现在: ①injectPeerCard=claude同形卡(role user+status complete+originKind 'peer-message'+peerFrom, 内存态不落记录, renderer后端中立) ②rpc-queue帧带fromName+cardText, wrapper把它们写成自己记录上的`webui_peer:{name,body}`标记(仅buffer副本; 模型看到的文本不变), recordKey与codex-session-store的mergeCodexRecords指纹都剥掉该字段⇒buffer副本与codex自己rollout副本的同一条user消息仍碰撞去重(两种时间序都钉: idle路径rollout先, queued路径wrapper先) ③无标记副本(rollout-only重建/老wrapper)按**锚定**的服务器frame形状(`Message from session "…" (via vibespace-msg` / `[VibeSpace Background Work] `)识别, 取名用message-manager导出的peerDisplayName(claude重建parity); webui_msg_id(用户手打/auto-resume续跑行)永不成卡 ④peer记录不调_finalizeStreaming(queued路径是活turn中途记录, 关流会把回复切碎), peer卡不是turn边界(task_complete回扫越过它). 未选"deliver通道对codex也feedPeerCard": 记录+内存卡=活流双渲染, 且stdin写成功≠投递成功(ok:false再入stash后drain再渲染=幽灵卡). Gate: test-peer-msg-card 19→52, test-peer-delivery 21.** **0.153 RECORD TOLERANCE (B-21e4 item 2):** every rollout line / response_item / event_msg type is HANDLED, explicitly SKIPPED (exported sets `SKIPPED_RECORD_TYPES` world_state/inter_agent_communication_metadata/compacted (token_usage_record moved to HANDLED with the per-message meta work below), `SKIPPED_RESPONSE_ITEM_TYPES`, `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` — twins of rendered records, tool-lifecycle noise, wrapper/engine side channels; names from codex-rs protocol.rs + every local rollout since 0.142) or UNKNOWN ⇒ telemetry `codex-unknown-record:<type>` ONCE per process (`_noteUnknown`, the cli-unknown-system-subtype precedent) then skipped — never a system card (splits the surrounding fold) and never a dropped turn. Newly RENDERED: `agent_message` items (0.153 multi-agent v2 sub-agent↔root chatter: author/recipient + the visible envelope, `[encrypted payload — withheld upstream]` named) and `sub_agent_activity` (started|interacted|interrupted|completed) are COMPLETE tool cards in the **'agent' fold kind** (the old sub-agent system line split every run; completed/interrupted edit the one card in place; no task chip — nothing to close on); old Responses-API items `web_search_call` (1.5k in local rollouts, invisible before) / `image_generation_call` render complete cards, `tool_search_call`/`tool_search_output` + `local_shell_call` pair by call_id. `thread_settings_applied` (typed model/reasoning_effort) and `turn_context.effort` feed `status().effort` — no card. Gate: test-codex-0153 ② + test-codex-history (sub-agent card). **PER-MESSAGE META (owner 2026-09-06 "codex会话是不是依然看不到每条消息的详细信息、计费账号、使用模型"; design-harness-plugins §1 P1 row):** `_create` threaded no `meta`, so the left-strip popup showed Role/Time/uuid only and the Billing row fell to a (dead, see chat-view.js) session fallback. Codex stamps usage per RESPONSE, never per item — real 0.153.4 rollouts go `reasoning/message/function_call items → token_usage_record{thread_id,turn_id,response_id:'resp_…',usage,turn_token_usage,thread_token_usage} → tool outputs/item_completed → event_msg token_count` (surveyed 79 local rollouts / 830 pairs: the record PRECEDES its token_count by 1–5 lines, same turn_id, `usage.total_tokens === last_token_usage.total_tokens`, `thread_token_usage.total_tokens === total_token_usage.total_tokens`, never a response item in between; 65 token_counts had no record = resume/fork heads). So `_threadUsageMeta` (called from the token_count branch) attaches ONE meta object to every assistant/tool message created since `_usageMark` (the index the previous usage-bearing token_count closed at — the codex twin of claude's recMeta-per-block), then advances the mark; user/peer/system records never get it; heartbeats (info:null/empty) attach nothing. Meta shape = claude's + honest kind markers: `requestId` = `cx:<threadId>:<cumulative total_tokens>` derived EXACTLY as usage-walker.js mints the ledger rid (the dedup key already baked into every permanent ledger — the only join old rollouts have; `requestIdKind:'ledger'`, never passed off as a vendor id), `msgId` = the `resp_…` response_id from the preceding `token_usage_record` (`_processTokenUsageRecord` parks it; consumed by the next token_count after a total_tokens check; `msgIdKind:'response'`; NULL on pre-0.153 rollouts and on the LIVE v2 stream, which has no such record — never invented), `usage` {input_tokens = FRESH = input−cached (the ledger's `i`), cache_read_input_tokens = cached, cache_creation_input_tokens AND codex's own cache_write_input_tokens, output_tokens, reasoning_output_tokens}, `model`, `effort` (turn_context), `stopReason:null`. **Thread id = the conversation being RENDERED, mirroring the walker's per-FILE sid** (adversarial verifier 2026-09-06, real data: a codex SUB-AGENT rollout carries its OWN session_meta at line 0 and the PARENT's at line 1 — 29/79 local rollouts carry two, 79/79 have their own FIRST; "last session_meta wins" keyed 11 rollouts / 2320 messages `cx:<parent>:…` and 64 of those keys COLLIDED with the parent conversation's real ledger events, so the billing row named the wrong conversation's account). **ROUND 3 (2026-09-06, the verifier refuted the absolute reader pin with byte copies of two real rollouts): the ledger thread id is a fact about the RECORD's FILE, never about the reader.** A merged read (CodexSessionMessages prepends fork ancestry — native `forked_from_id`+`forked_from_ordinal_exclusive` parents cut at the boundary, the wrapper `forkedFrom` chain whole) tags every record with the rollout it came from (`__threadId`, NON-enumerable, codex-session-store.tagRecordThread — survives the sort copy, never reaches JSON.stringify/fingerprints/wire) and `_threadUsageMeta` keys by `record.__threadId` first (`_recordThreadId`, captured per record in `_processRecord`): parent-half records key `cx:<parent>:…` exactly as the walker keys the parent FILE (the absolute pin keyed them `cx:<child>:<parent cumulative>` — keys the ledger never minted for that thread, 0/30 parent messages matched the parent's ledger, two collided with real child events). The constructor `{threadId}` is the DEFAULT for provenance-less records only (gap slabs, tail-only reads, the live stream and the wrapper buffer; normalizers.rebuildHistory passes session.backendSessionId||claudeSessionId, ws-handler's view-only attach backendSessionId, transcript-service view()/gapSlab() r.sessionId — a gap slab carries NO session_meta at all), and `_adoptThreadId` is: ① wrapper_meta.threadId REPLACES the default, constructor id or not (the wrapper's OWN record naming the file it writes NOW — after a mid-life thread/fork codex-events re-points session.backendSessionId on that same record and the normalizer follows it IN-STREAM through feedLive, ordering-correct across a rebuild queue where a direct re-pin from the consumer is not; a pinned manager kept minting `cx:<old>:…` while the walker keyed the new file) ② the FIRST session_meta fills a void only (a prepended fork-ancestry session_meta or the copied parent-provenance one never overwrites) ③ token_usage_record.thread_id fills a void only. Same round, adapters/codex.extractCodexThreadMeta: `forked_from_id` is read from the thread's OWN session_meta only, like the boundary ordinal — "last wins" took the copied parent meta's `forked_from_id` (= the GRANDPARENT) and paired it with the child's own boundary, so the merged read cut the wrong file. **DUPLICATE token_counts** (codex re-emits an identical record after later items — 369/8490 in the local corpus; real window: token_count → item_completed → assistant message → identical token_count → token_usage_record → next token_count) return on `ridKey === _lastRidKeys.get(threadId)` (PER thread since round 3 — the walker's cursor is per file and a merged read interleaves files) BEFORE the pending record or the mark is touched — the walker's `rid === cur.lastRid` twin; the first cut stamped the PREVIOUS response's key/numbers onto the NEXT response's messages and advanced the mark past them for good (45/1143 wrong). `_ledgerKeys` records every minted {rid, mid, n, tid} (tid = the file the key belongs to; n=0 = a CARDLESS response: back-to-back token_counts with no item, an agent_message event without a response_item twin — 676/4344 in the local corpus; a per-message check alone can never see them). Both token_count shapes handled (rollout snake_case; the wrapper's relay of thread/tokenUsage/updated keeps v2 camelCase inside `last_token_usage`/`total_token_usage`). Live emits the SAME `{op:'edit', fields:{meta}}` claude uses so an open window's popup refreshes. Gate: test-codex-history (88: real-rollout-head fixture, walker-rid equality, live camelCase, heartbeat, wiring pins; the real sub-agent head (two session_metas, child first) + default/precedence semantics + the reader wiring pins; the real duplicate-token_count window on rebuild AND live; the rid-info model/effort consumer pin; a CORPUS SMOKE over ≤30 local ~/.codex/sessions rollouts — every meta.requestId starts with the file's own uuid, the MINTED key set EQUALS the walker's rid set per file, mids agree — printed skip where the directory is absent (CI); the round-3 MERGED-READ leg over scripts/fixtures/codex-native-fork/ — verbatim anonymised cuts of two real 0.153.4 rollouts (a sub-agent and its own sub-agent, each carrying the copied parent meta at line 1) with `forked_from_ordinal_exclusive: 53` INJECTED into the child's own session_meta (marked in the record), copied into a temp CODEX home bound BEFORE the first require of adapters/codex: parent-half minted keys ⊆ the walker's rids of the parent file and = exactly the pre-boundary ones, child-half == the child file, zero parent→child collisions, the wrapper-chain whole-file shape, a provenance-less gap read, and the LIVE RE-POINT sequence (wrapper_meta{old} → token_count → session_meta+wrapper_meta{new} → token_count ⇒ cx:old:4242 then cx:new:4242, cross-checked with the walker on the two files, pinned and unpinned) — negative-controlled: the absolute pin scored parentCorrect 0/5 with every parent key "nowhere", the old extractCodexThreadMeta resolved the ancestry to the grandparent) + test-usage-walk-parity (32) + test-codex-p2-wrapper (33: real wrapper → token_count → normalizer chain). Gate: test-peer-msg-card 19→52, test-peer-delivery 21.** **0.153 RECORD TOLERANCE (B-21e4 item 2):** every rollout line / response_item / event_msg type is HANDLED, explicitly SKIPPED (exported sets `SKIPPED_RECORD_TYPES` world_state/token_usage_record/inter_agent_communication_metadata/compacted, `SKIPPED_RESPONSE_ITEM_TYPES`, `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` — twins of rendered records, tool-lifecycle noise, wrapper/engine side channels; names from codex-rs protocol.rs + every local rollout since 0.142) or UNKNOWN ⇒ telemetry `codex-unknown-record:<type>` ONCE per process (`_noteUnknown`, the cli-unknown-system-subtype precedent) then skipped — never a system card (splits the surrounding fold) and never a dropped turn. Newly RENDERED: `agent_message` items (0.153 multi-agent v2 sub-agent↔root chatter: author/recipient + the visible envelope, `[encrypted payload — withheld upstream]` named) and `sub_agent_activity` (started|interacted|interrupted|completed) are COMPLETE tool cards in the **'agent' fold kind** (the old sub-agent system line split every run; completed/interrupted edit the one card in place; no task chip — nothing to close on); old Responses-API items `web_search_call` (1.5k in local rollouts, invisible before) / `image_generation_call` render complete cards, `tool_search_call`/`tool_search_output` + `local_shell_call` pair by call_id. `thread_settings_applied` (typed model/reasoning_effort) and `turn_context.effort` feed `status().effort` — no card. Gate: test-codex-0153 ② + test-codex-history (sub-agent card). **WEB SEARCH (2.369.43):** a rollout persists a search ONLY as `event_msg web_search_end {call_id, query, action, results}` (35/35 in 2026-08 0.153 rollouts; NO web_search_call item, NO web_search_begin anywhere) — it sat in SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES, so a rebuild rendered no search at all and the live path showed the wrapper's empty item/started stub. `_processWebSearchEvent` now lands the three carriers of one search on ONE card keyed by call_id: ① the wrapper's function_call at item/started (pending, empty input) ② `web_search_end` — from the wrapper at item/completed AND from the rollout (same shape) — `_finalizeToolCall` merges `{query, action}` into the input (mergeToolInput) and renders results via `renderSearchOutput` (src/search-card.js: "title — url\nsnippet" blocks, `opened <url>` / `found '<pattern>' in <url>` heads, 20 results / 4 KB caps, `error` ⇒ error card); a second copy edits the same card, never a second create ③ the id-less Responses-API `web_search_call` item that 0.14x rollouts write right AFTER the end for the same search (action only) — `_processWebSearchCallItem` recognises it by `searchActionKey` (action-only key; `_lastSearchEnd`/`_lastSearchCall` pair in either order), while an orphan call (April rollouts, no end) still renders its `status: …` card and `action` now stays the OBJECT (the client's title chip reads `action.queries/url`). `web_search_begin` (never persisted; live-stream tolerance) = pending card if none, else a query patch that keeps it pending. Gate: test-codex-history (web search block: rollout-only rebuild, live stub+end+twin = one card/one create, begin tolerance, error, 0.14x pairing both orders, wrapper pin). Gate: test-peer-msg-card 19→52, test-peer-delivery 21.** **0.153 RECORD TOLERANCE (B-21e4 item 2):** every rollout line / response_item / event_msg type is HANDLED, explicitly SKIPPED (exported sets `SKIPPED_RECORD_TYPES` world_state/token_usage_record/inter_agent_communication_metadata/compacted, `SKIPPED_RESPONSE_ITEM_TYPES`, `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` — twins of rendered records, tool-lifecycle noise, wrapper/engine side channels; names from codex-rs protocol.rs + every local rollout since 0.142) or UNKNOWN ⇒ telemetry `codex-unknown-record:<type>` ONCE per process (`_noteUnknown`, the cli-unknown-system-subtype precedent) then skipped — never a system card (splits the surrounding fold) and never a dropped turn. Newly RENDERED: `agent_message` items (0.153 multi-agent v2 sub-agent↔root chatter: author/recipient + the visible envelope, `[encrypted payload — withheld upstream]` named) and `sub_agent_activity` (started|interacted|interrupted|completed) are COMPLETE tool cards in the **'agent' fold kind** (the old sub-agent system line split every run; completed/interrupted edit the one card in place; no task chip — nothing to close on); old Responses-API items `web_search_call` (1.5k in local rollouts, invisible before) / `image_generation_call` render complete cards, `tool_search_call`/`tool_search_output` + `local_shell_call` pair by call_id. `thread_settings_applied` (typed model/reasoning_effort) and `turn_context.effort` feed `status().effort` — no card. Gate: test-codex-0153 ② + test-codex-history (sub-agent card). **WEB SEARCH — WHICH SHAPE DOES WHICH VERSION WRITE (2.369.43; the first cut targeted a shape the INSTALLED CLI does not write and was refuted on real data 2026-09-06 — re-cut from a READ-ONLY fleet scan of every local rollout keyed on `session_meta.cli_version`, 82 files):** **view_image = ONE card per call_id (2.369.43):** a real rollout carries the triple `function_call view_image {path, detail}` → `event_msg view_image_tool_call {call_id, path}` → `function_call_output [{input_image, image_url:data:…}]`, and a live session ALSO has the wrapper stub's `{path}` twin + `viewed <path>` output — the merge fingerprint keeps all of them (different payloads), and a second card for a known id used to leave the first pending forever. `_absorbTwinCall` (function_call/custom_tool_call for a known call_id merge input, no new card), `view_image_tool_call` is ROUTED (left SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES: the ≤0.130 engine's own record, and the ONLY trace when the call pair is absent), and `_processFunctionCallOutput` lifts `input_image` blocks to `images:[{mediaType, bytes}]` + a `[image …]` marker (the 2.369.35 law's codex twin — base64 never enters a card). **…but on 0.153.4 NONE of those shapes is what a rollout persists.** CORPUS FACT (82 local rollouts, measured 2026-09-06): the 25 files whose session_meta says cli_version 0.153.4 hold 48 image views and every one is an `event_msg/item_completed {item:{type:'ImageView', id:'exec-…', path:'file:///home/…/studio.png'}}` — ZERO `function_call view_image`, ZERO `view_image_tool_call` (those live only in the ≤0.130 rollouts, 98 calls + 15 events, and in the wrapper's LIVE stream). **ONE ROUTER, ONE DECODER:** the ImageView case of `_processItemCompleted` (the item_completed ALLOWLIST above) and the `view_image_tool_call` branch both call `_processViewImageEvent`, the single image-view card path — a KNOWN call_id only learns the path, an unknown one becomes a complete `view_image` card a later output twin edits in place — and `fileUrlToPath` is applied THERE and nowhere else (file:// → plain path, percent-decoded ONCE; a plain path, which is what the live RPC hands the wrapper, is never touched, so a literal `%` in a filename survives). LIVE + ROLLOUT CONVERGE ON ONE CARD because the wrapper's live stub uses `call_id = item.id` — the same `exec-…` (verified against a live buffer: call_id `exec-a0e11416-…` is the id of the ImageView item the rollout wrote for that view). Gate: test-codex-history (real-order fixture + the ImageView pin) + test-image-cards ⑧ (14 ImageView records cut VERBATIM from the 0.153.4 root rollout → 14 complete cards, none pending; negative control: every other item type still produces no card and no unknown-record telemetry). **QUEUE STATE IS SESSION STATE, NEVER A MESSAGE (2026-09-06):** `queue_changed` → `{op:'meta', subtype:'queue', items}` + `queueState()` for the attach payload; nothing about the queue ever enters the transcript. The user bubble a queued message came from wears the state instead: `_stampQueueChip(msgId, 'queued'|'steered'|'removed'|null)` joins through `userMessageIds` and rides the ordinary `edit` op, so an open window updates in place. Three rules that took a chip's lifecycle to get right: ① `queued_input` stamps the chip and returns — the old system card is gone, and survives ONLY as the fallback when there is no bubble to stamp (a peer/job message has no `webui_msg_id`), because a queued message must never be invisible ② an item that leaves the queue with no explicit steer/remove was drained by the turn ending, so its chip CLEARS — a bubble that keeps claiming to be queued forever is a lie ③ a failed `queue_op_result` becomes a VISIBLE notice, and `queueOpFailureText` (PURE, exported for the suite) words every branch in terms of what happens to the message NOW ("stays queued and runs when this turn ends" / "will simply run next" / "already ran"). The ACP normalizer carries the byte-identical trio. Two more, from the round-1 review: ④ the user bubble is stamped with **`webuiMsgId`** where `userMessageIds` is written — the map is server-side only, and the CLIENT chip has to join a rendered bubble back onto its queue row (without the stamp every chip click answered "That message is no longer queued") ⑤ `queuePublished()` — a wrapper that has never emitted a `queue_changed` (i.e. one that predates this release, but still emits `queued_input` as it has since 2.369.20) gets the OLD system card instead of a chip, because a chip there could never clear and never be acted on; the meta op carries `supported:true` so the client learns the same fact in band. **image_gen / sleep / elicitation card paths (2.369.58):** `_processImageGenEvent` and `_processSleepEvent` are the ONE renderer each fact funnels through — `item_completed Extension image_gen.generation` (rollout, the live wrapper's mirrored copy, and codex-thread-read's synthesis) and the ≤0.130 `response_item image_generation_call` all route into the first; `Extension clock.sleep` into the second. Canonical cards: `image_gen` with input `{prompt, path}` and output `status: <status>` + `saved <path>` (base64 `result` NEVER enters a card — the 2.369.35 law), `sleep` with input `{durationMs}` and output `slept 30s` (`formatSleptMs` is the ONE server-side duration formatter; the renderer's countdown formats a DIFFERENT thing, time remaining). Both are `collapseKind: null` — VISIBLE work, because a 20-minute wait folded into "N tool calls" is precisely how a deliberate pause reads as a hang. `_processServerRequest` also renders `mcpServer/elicitation/request` through the existing AskUserQuestion family (`kind:'user_input'`, rows from the wrapper's `payload.questions`; `mode:'url'` falls back to the plain approve card), and `isAllowedServerDecision` spans all five reply vocabularies. **TWO EFFORT FACTS, NEVER ONE (2.369.62):** `_status.effort` = what the CURRENT (or last) turn is running at — the value `_attachUsageMeta` bakes onto every message of that turn — and `_status.effortNext` = the pick that applies from the next turn. Conflating them is how a turn codex ran at 'ultra' reported 'xhigh' on every message: the live buffer held exactly ONE `turn_context` (the wrapper's synthesized copy, quoting a stale spawn env) for a session whose whole life was one app-server-started turn. `_noteEffort(live, next, emit)` is the ONE writer, so every carrier lands the same way — `turn_context` (a record that names an effort also SETTLES the pending question: `effort_next` present = a re-pick is waiting, absent = nothing is), the wrapper's `wrapper_meta` pair (the FALLBACK that makes a mid-turn attach honest when no turn_context is in range), and codex's own `thread_settings_applied` (the THREAD's effort = the NEXT turn's; it must NOT relabel the turn in flight, and only answers "now" before any turn has named one). It also EMITS `{op:'meta', subtype:'effort', data:{effort, effortNext}}` — chat-view routes it to `ChatStatusBar.setEffort`, so a `/effort` typed into the chat or another client's pick moves the chip live instead of waiting for a re-attach. Gate: scripts/test-codex-effort-meta.mjs. **`webui_queue_id` — the SECOND-CLASS user identity (2026-09-07):** the wrapper stamps it on the bubble it writes for a submission it never typed (an INHERITED queue item entering the turn). `_processResponseMessage` reads it into the same `webuiMsgId` field the chip joins on (`_stampUserIdentity`, the ONE writer of the bubble-id ⇄ userMessageIds pair), but it is deliberately second class: it does NOT suppress peer detection (most of the owner's 25 inherited items were agent-to-agent messages and must keep their labelled card, which a `webui_msg_id` would have turned into an anonymous "You" bubble), and `recordKey` STRIPS it (with `webui_queue_via` / `webui_after_commit`) exactly like `webui_peer` so the buffer copy and codex's rollout copy of that message mint the SAME message id — either may be the one that survives the merge. **Round 2:** such a bubble is also marked `midTurn` on the MESSAGE, and `_finalizeStreaming`'s backward scan treats it like a peer card (`m.role === 'user' && originKind !== 'peer-message' && !m.midTurn` is the boundary). It deliberately does not CLOSE the streams it landed among (finalizing there fragmented the reply — the 2.368.16 class), so it must not HIDE them from the scan either: without the exemption a reply left open when the turn ended stayed `status:'streaming'` forever — a phantom spinner on a dead, read-only conversation, on every `convertHistory` attach. `item_completed:UserMessage` stays an explicit member of `ITEM_COMPLETED_SKIPPED_TYPES` (its response_item is the bubble).

| cli_version | how a search persists | `results` |
|---|---|---|
| 0.120 / 0.125 / 0.130 | `event_msg web_search_end {call_id:'ws_…', query, action}` **+ an id-less Responses-API `web_search_call {status, action?}` on the next line** — the TWIN | never (1698 ends, 0 with the key) |
| 0.149.1 | `web_search_end` only (`call_id:'exec-…'`), no `web_search_call` anywhere | always (35/35) |
| **0.153.4 (installed)** | **no `web_search_end` at all** — only `event_msg item_completed {item:{type:'Extension', kind:'web.search', id:'exec-…', query, action, results}}` | always (245/245, `[]` = really none) |
| live (wrapper) | `function_call web_search` at item/started (EMPTY stub = the pending card) + `web_search_end` at item/completed (the v2 item relayed) | as the item carries |

  Three 0.153.4 rollouts with 20/43/30 searches rendered ZERO cards because `item_started`/`item_completed` sat in `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` — and a skipped type emits no telemetry, so nothing said so. `_processEvent` now dispatches `item_completed` to `_processItemCompleted` BEFORE the generic skip (which the type stays in, for every other kind). **All carriers land on ONE card keyed by the item/call id** (the Extension id IS the exec-… id the wrapper's live card uses, so live + rollout edit the same card): `_finalizeToolCall` merges `{query, action}` into the input (mergeToolInput) and renders results through `renderSearchOutput` (src/search-card.js). **Dispatch is an explicit ALLOWLIST on `item.type`** (`ITEM_COMPLETED_SKIPPED_TYPES`, exported + pinned), never a fall-through: an unrecognised TOP-LEVEL type fires `codex-unknown-record:item_completed:<type>` once per type, an unknown Extension kind `…:item_completed:Extension:<kind>`, an unknown sub-agent kind `…:item_completed:SubAgentActivity:<kind>` — the telemetry means "never seen upstream", so a type we deliberately do not card is in the allowlist instead. **0.153.4 `item_completed` census (all 25 rollouts, snapshot 2026-09-06 — the corpus is LIVE: two scans an hour apart differed ~4%, so the RATIOS are the claim, not the totals; `item_started` is NEVER persisted — 0 records):** RENDERED ELSEWHERE ⇒ skipped by name — Reasoning 1480 / AgentMessage 84 carry an id that IS a response_item id (1480/1480, 84/84); CollabAgentToolCall 5 carries the function_call's call_id (5/5); CommandExecution 588 + FileChange 135 have no id twin but each is the RESULT of the `exec` custom_tool_call in front of it (876 `exec` calls, the only shell tool name in the corpus), whose card already shows the command and its output — **0.153.4 has NO standalone `apply_patch` tool call at all (0 in the corpus; the earlier "apply_patch exec count == FileChange count" claim was false): a patch rides the SAME `exec` call, whose input text carries the `*** Begin Patch` payload, and 135/135 FileChange items follow an exec naming the changed file**; UserMessage 31 is the already-rendered prompt; SubAgentActivity started 18 + interacted 214 carry the id of the tool CALL that caused them (spawn_agent 18/18, send_message 197 + followup_task 17), which renders its own card; ContextCompaction 1 is deliberately unrouted — the live wrapper emits its own `context_compacted` for the same item and the two spellings do NOT fingerprint-dedupe in the buffer⇄rollout merge, so routing it would print the notice (and bump turnIndex) twice on every live session; **known cost: a rollout-ONLY rebuild shows no compaction notice.** **ONLY CARRIER, so routed here: `Extension web.search` 245 → the search card, `Extension image_gen.generation` 3 → the existing image_gen card path (`revisedPrompt` in, `status` + `savedPath` out — the 3 MB base64 `result` NEVER enters a message, the 2.369.35 binary law), `ImageView` 54 → the view_image card path with `file://` stripped (the wrapper strips it too, so the live and rebuilt copies of that one card are byte-identical), `SubAgentActivity` kind `completed` 32 (over 18 distinct threads) → the `sub_agent_activity` card path.** The sub-agent completion is the second carrier this CLI MOVED: 0.153.4 persists no standalone `sub_agent_activity` event at all while 0.149.1 persists 349 of them (started 64 / interacted 277 / interrupted 8 / completed 0), and the completed records' id `subagent-completed-<uuid>` twins nothing — a sub-agent finishing was recorded NOWHERE else, so `_processSubAgentActivity` (extracted from `_processEvent`, now shared by the standalone event, the thread/read mapper and item_completed) CREATES the card when no 'started' opened one and is keyed by THREAD so any repeat is idempotent. Terminal kinds only: routing 'started'/'interacted' would double-render against their spawn/send cards. Old-vs-new whole-corpus replay: 0.153.4 gains exactly 18 Sub-agent cards and changes nothing else (search 246 / image 56 / bash 911 identical), 0.149.1 is card-for-card identical, telemetry 0 in both. **Twin pairing (0.120-0.130) is POSITIONAL:** one slot holds the immediately preceding SEARCH record (end or id-less call — intervening records never touch it, and both handlers overwrite it), so an orphan call later in the file with the same action never pairs with a stale end. Whole-file replays: the 0.125 file = 96 orphan calls then 104 end+call twins ⇒ 200 cards; the 0.130 file (2,119,710,127 bytes = **2.0 GiB**, 1,116,410 lines — `ls -l` on the named rollout; the first cut said "512 MB") = 1524 ends + 1324 calls (every call an adjacent twin) ⇒ 1524 cards, 0 empty, 0 'no results'. An end with action `{type:'other'}` is twinned by a call carrying NO action key at all — keyed apart they rendered the owner's exact second empty card (`{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed'), so `searchActionKey` puts `{type:'other'}` and no-action in ONE bucket. An orphan call still renders its `status: …` card and `action` stays the OBJECT (the client's title chip reads `action.queries/url`); an orphan carrying NO action either (exactly ONE such record in the whole local corpus, 0.120.0) is LABELLED — head `web search (no details recorded)`, input `{note:'no details recorded'}` from the shared `NO_SEARCH_DETAILS` — so no card can reproduce the owner's reported empty shape `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' byte for byte (old-vs-new replay of the 0.120.0 corpus: that shape 1 → 0, card count unchanged); `web_search_begin` (never persisted; live-stream tolerance) = pending card if none, else a query patch that keeps it pending. Gate: test-codex-history's web-search block — VERBATIM 0.153.4 records (search / other / openPage / findInPage-with-url-null / results:[] / image_gen / ImageView, cut from named rollouts + ordinals, anonymised) + verbatim 0.120.0 twin records + PATTERN replays of three whole rollouts (scripts/fixtures/codex-web-search-patterns.json: the real record order and action identity; `expectedCards` is ground truth computed OUTSIDE the normalizer) + the live+rollout one-card check + the unknown-kind telemetry control. **Regenerate the fixture** by re-walking ~/.codex/sessions READ-ONLY, grouping by `session_meta.cli_version`, and emitting E/C tokens per search record (`E~` = end with `{type:'other'}`, `C-` = action-less call).

  **MULTI-AGENT VISIBILITY (B-7473; supersedes the two card shapes above):** `item_completed` LEFT the skip set — corpus-scanning every local 0.153.4 rollout showed it is the ONLY carrier of sub-agent activity there (214 `item_completed/SubAgentActivity`, ZERO `event_msg sub_agent_activity` — that shape exists only on the wrapper's live stream). **ONE router, ONE dispatch (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06 integration):** `_processItemCompleted` is the ALLOWLIST router described above — it runs FIRST in `_processEvent` (the type stays in the generic `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` as the fall-through, and test-codex-history pins the ORDER, not the membership), skips the `ITEM_COMPLETED_SKIPPED_TYPES` twins by name, routes Extension web.search / image_gen.generation / ImageView to their card paths, and sends EVERY SubAgentActivity kind to `_processSubAgentActivity`; anything else fires `codex-unknown-record:item_completed:<type>` (Extension kinds namespaced as `…:Extension:<kind>`) once per type.
  **SUB-AGENT LIFECYCLE — ONE implementation, ONE shape (the merge rule; state it before changing either side):** `_processSubAgentActivity` ① ALWAYS learns `agentPath → agent_thread_id` into `status().subagents` (that map is what makes an agent NAME clickable; it is learned even when no row is drawn), ② renders the fact as a one-line collab ACTIVITY row — **the old standalone "Sub-agent" tool CARD is RETIRED** (a card per lifecycle event buried the conversation; the row carries who/kind/thread plus the click-through), ③ draws NO row when the record's own id IS a rendered call: on 0.153.4 `started`'s id is the `spawn_agent` call_id (18/18) and `interacted`'s the `send_message`/`followup_task` call_id (197+17), and that call already drew its own collab row — the durable `_collabSeen` marker `call:<id>` (set when the collab call renders, NOT the `_collabByCall` entry, which is deleted when the call's output arrives) makes the suppression order-independent. Terminal records synthesise `subagent-completed-<uuid>`, twin nothing, and always draw. Dedupe keys `sa:<thread>:<kind>:<id>` and `sa:<thread>:<kind>` (a repeated 'started' is idempotent; 32 'completed' records over 18 threads ⇒ 18 rows). Three carriers, one path: the 0.149.1 standalone `event_msg sub_agent_activity` (349 records) and the wrapper's LIVE stream, the thread/read mapper (src/codex-thread-read.js), and 0.153.4's `item_completed`. A FOREIGN thread's `error` arrives here too, as kind `errored` with the message in `row.detail` (hover) — a child's failure is never the root's task_failed. Sub-agent traffic is a first-class `collab` shape on the message — `{dir:'in'|'out'|'spawn'|'wait'|'activity', agentPath, agentName, nickname, msgType, encrypted, target, cellId, threadId, kind, detail, rows:[…]}`: an inbound PLAINTEXT payload becomes an ATTRIBUTED **sub-agent report** (role 'tool', **collapseKind 'report'** — its OWN fold kind since B-7473 integration 2026-09-06, deliberately NOT in the default `chat.collapseKinds`: a child's written answer is CONTENT, and folding it under 'agent' hid the thing the owner opened the window to read; the rows stay 'agent' — envelope stripped, body rendered as markdown by the renderer), while encrypted inbound, outbound `send_message`/`followup_task`, `spawn_agent`, `wait` and lifecycle events are ONE-LINE rows that COALESCE into a single message (edit-in-place live, same on rebuild). The collab tool family never becomes a tool CARD: its `message` argument is a fernet blob upstream, and `collabDetailOf` only ever reads plaintext keys (prompt/description) — **no encrypted blob may reach a rendered string**. Twin dedup: live (wrapper, id `msg_…`) vs rollout (id `amsg_…`) by id, else by the CONTENT leg within the turn — the two ids differ, so the (author, text) leg is what actually kills the owner's duplicate. **That content leg only exists where there IS content (B-7473 integration 2026-09-06, MAJOR, measured on the owner's real root rollout):** an ENCRYPTED inter-agent message has `body === ''` and `msgType 'MESSAGE'` for EVERY message, so the `(turn, author, msgType, body)` key degenerated to one key per (turn, author) and SILENTLY DROPPED distinct messages — the verifier measured 101 of 140; re-measured on the same (still-growing) rollout at 3648 records, 120 of 168. Every one carries a distinct id AND a distinct blob, so the id leg never fired. Now: the (author, msgType, body) leg fires only when `body` is non-empty; an encrypted message dedupes by ID plus a discriminator taken from the fernet blob's first 32 chars (version + timestamp + IV). Pinned with three VERBATIM encrypted records from one agent in one turn ⇒ 3 rows coalesced into ONE "3 messages · water_research" item, never 1 (negative-controlled: the old key scores 1). `session_meta.agent_path` (own meta only) sets `status().agentPath`, the reference point that makes a message inbound or outbound. Labels/HTML come from the PURE src/collab-row.js. Gate: test-codex-0153 ⑥ (fixtures verbatim from the real 0.153.4 root rollout) + test-codex-subagents. **ONE IDENTITY PER SUB-AGENT (round-5, MAJOR, measured on the owner's real root rollout at 4807 records):** codex names the same child TWO ways — the OUTBOUND legs (`spawn_agent`/`send_message`/`followup_task` arguments) carry a BARE `task_name`/`target` ("interior_research"), while the spawn OUTPUT (`{"task_name":"/root/interior_research"}` — the proof of the intended shape), every inbound `agent_message` author and every SubAgentActivity record carry the absolute agent path. Keying rows on the raw value split each agent in two: **36 distinct row identities for 20 real children**, the fold header drew the same agent as two chips, the "N sub-agents" count over-reported, and the `agentPath → threadId` map (absolute keys) never matched an outbound row — **98 of 409 rows carried no thread id and therefore had NO click-through at all**. `absAgentPath(name, ownPath)` normalises any value WITHOUT a '/' to `(status().agentPath || '/root') + '/' + name` at the ONE place each row is built (collab tool call, agent-message envelope leg, spawn output, SubAgentActivity), so chips / map lookup / back-fill / summary all see one identity: after = **20 identities, 20 map entries, 408/409 rows with a thread id** (the 409th is the agent-less `wait` row) and the message/report/row counts UNCHANGED. `_noteSubagentThread` carries the BELT: its back-fill also matches a row whose own path has NO '/' against the key's last segment — deliberately one-directional, because two parents may each own a child named `research` and mapping one onto the other's thread would open the WRONG conversation (a wrong answer is worse than a missing one; negative-controlled). **ERRORS ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE (round-5, MINOR):** every other lifecycle kind repeats the same fact, so `(thread, kind)` is the right coalescing for them — but an `errored` record carries a MESSAGE, and that key silently dropped every error after a child's first, differently worded or not. `errored` now keys on `sa:<tid>:errored:<id>|<detail>`: a genuine re-read of one record collapses (both legs equal) while two differently-worded failures never do, EVEN IF their ids collide — the wrapper's synthesized `foreign-error-<tid>-<Date.now()>` was not unique for two failures inside one millisecond (it now appends a counter too, but the key does not depend on that). No eviction on that lane on purpose: `_collabSeen` also holds the durable `call:<id>` double-render markers and an FIFO sweep would drop one while its twin is still to come. Pins: test-codex-subagents ④ (real-shaped fixture: one identity, every row's thread id, one map entry, the same-leaf-name negative control, three distinct errors ⇒ three rows, the id-collision case, and 'completed' still coalescing).

### turn-state.js / rewind-ops.js (B3 turn truth — design-harness-features §2.5 / §2.10 / §2.11 / §3.5)

  **src/turn-state.js — PURE authoritative turn state.** "Is a turn running right now" backs the Stop button, auto-resume's *it is already working* skip, the attach payload's `isStreaming` and the session-card chips — and every one of them used to read a flag we INFERRED from record shapes (a `result` ends the turn; a `user` record starts one), the fuzzy area both 2.339.2 (stuck thinking) and 2.369.16 (attach storm) landed in. Claude Code 2.1.257 publishes the fact itself: `{type:'system', subtype:'session_state_changed', state:'idle'|'running'|'requires_action', uuid, session_id}` — its own `.describe()` reads *"'idle' fires after heldBackResult flushes and the bg-agent do-while exits — authoritative turn-over signal"*, i.e. strictly LATER than the `result` we used to end turns on. **The record only exists when `CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS` is in the CLI's env** (`if (a.CLAUDE_CODE_EMIT_SESSION_STATE_EVENTS) mu({type:"system",subtype:"session_state_changed",state:e})`, read out of the binary), so `src/adapters/claude-code.js` SETS it on every **chat** spawn (owner decision 8(c): the ONE spawn default this batch changes, pure observability; NOT on terminal spawns — the stream-json parse is its only reader and nothing verified what the TUI's yield sink does with an extra record). `agentEnv()` is a DROP table, so the env passes through with no allowlist edit — never add it to AGENT_ENV_KEEP. Exports: `isTurnState` (unknown state ⇒ NEVER coerced to idle), `turnStateEffect(state,{hasLabel})` (what a state means for `_isStreaming` + the spinner label — `requires_action` is a LIVE turn paused on the user, and a bare `running` does not stomp a live tool label). **A turn state may only write a spinner line that stays TRUE for the rest of the turn (round 8, reproduced defect):** the wire's resume record is a bare `{state:'running'}` carrying no tool name, so nothing can RETRACT a line whose truth ended with the pause — the round-7 shape wrote `'waiting for you'` at `requires_action` and the very next `running` (whose job is "leave the derived label alone") faithfully preserved it for the whole tool run, contradicting a chip that had already flipped back. So `requires_action` writes **no line at all** (its voice is the status-bar chip), leaving exactly the two writes that cannot go stale: `''` on `idle` and `'thinking...'` onto an empty line. That is also what makes the consumer's `hasLabel: !!session._streamingLabel` sound — whatever is on the line when a `running` arrives is still true, `reconcileAttachStreaming({turnStateSeen,turnState,isStreaming,sidecar})` (ONE attach decision: rung ① the harness's own last state wins in BOTH directions; rung ② the wrapper sidecar stays the 2.339.2 backstop but is a DERIVED observer, so under authority its settle window is 30s instead of 3s and a heal that fires anyway is flagged `staleAuthority` → telemetry `turn-state-stale` + `session._turnState='idle'`, because a lost `idle` record must never wedge a session on "thinking" forever; a sidecar can only END a turn, never start one; an unreadable sidecar heals nothing). **PURE because the decision is made in TWO places** — the live consumer (`src/server/stdout/claude-stream-json.js`) and the ws attach path — and a twin between those two is the whole stuck-thinking family. Session fields: `_turnState` (last observed), `_turnStateSeen` (the latch that stands the derived writes down — false forever on an old CLI, which is the DEFAULT path, not an error path), `_inProgressTools`. Caps: `turnState:'authoritative'|'derived'|null` + `inProgressTools:bool` in src/backend-caps.js, mirrored in BACKEND_META (test-harness-contract deep-compares both keys in both directions). The tool-granular twin is claude's `set_in_progress_tool_use_ids {op:{action:'add'|'remove', ids}}` (*"Surfaces use this to show which tools are running"*) — consumed card-less in the same consumer, resolved to a SET, pushed as `tools-in-progress`, and carried on the attach payload; a PENDING card is not a RUNNING one (between parse and execution a tool can sit on a permission prompt). **That whole lane is DORMANT and `inProgressTools` is `false` everywhere (round 4, measured on the WIRE): 2.1.257 hands the record to a host callback and returns (`n.onInProgressToolUseIDs?.(e.op); return`), so it never reaches a stream-json consumer — a probe in the wrapper's exact spawn shape saw 6 tool_use / 6 tool_result and 0 records while `session_state_changed` arrived on the same stdout, and 24 production buffers hold 212 tool_use blocks and 0 records.** The consumer, broadcast, attach field and `.chat-tool-inflight` dot are kept as written-and-pinned code; the ONLY thing that may flip the cap is test-stdout-registry's wire-reachability leg (`scripts/probe-claude-stdout.mjs`, one read-only turn, loud SKIP without a CLI) observing one — it asserts caps⇄wire agreement in BOTH directions and its failure message names the two files to edit. Client: `chat-status-bar.setTurnState` draws a chip ONLY for `requires_action` (idle/running are already said by the composer's spinner — one fact must not have two voices), `chat-view._onToolsInProgress` toggles `.chat-tool-inflight`. **Session death retires BOTH, through ONE named owner** (round 8): `chat-view._retireLiveClaims()` — called from the `exited` branch — clears the compaction stage, `setTurnState(null)` (nobody reports a state any more; never `idle`, which would be a claim about a dead process) and `_onToolsInProgress([])`. It exists as one method because the previous version retired only the compaction stage from that branch, so "which claims does session death retire" had no home and a terminated session kept a pulsing "waiting for you" chip forever; a NEW live claim is added there and gets a row in test-turn-truth-ui ⓪b's CLAIMS table. **Gates:** test-stdout-registry (real-shape fixtures + the env-absent degradation leg + the pure reconciler table), test-attach-rebuild (reconciliation table + wiring pins), test-harness-contract (caps rows + client mirror + "the 'authoritative' claim is backed by the consumer actually driving _isStreaming"), test-turn-truth-ui (375×667 chrome).

  **src/rewind-ops.js — PURE retraction semantics.** Two harnesses tell us on the live stream that part of the conversation has been TAKEN BACK, and both were dropped on the floor. claude: `{type:'tombstone', message:<the retracted Message>, uuid, session_id}` — *"Emitted when a previously-yielded message is superseded or removed from the transcript (e.g., streaming→non-streaming fallback removes a partial orphan). Consumers that render or persist the stream should remove the referenced message"* — and we do BOTH, so a retracted half-message lived in the transcript forever. codex: `{type:'event_msg', payload:{type:'thread_rolled_back', num_turns:N}}` — `ThreadRolledBackEvent` has exactly ONE field (0.153.4 serde dump) and `thread/rollback`'s param doc defines it: *"The number of turns to drop from the end of the thread"*; it sat in `SKIPPED_EVENT_TYPES` since the codex normalizer existed, so a rollback made from the codex TUI left ghost turns on screen. The **rollout carries the identical payload** the live stream does (verified on the owner's two real rollouts that contain it: num_turns 11 → 45 messages struck, num_turns 1 → 2), so ONE handler covers both reads — the §6 union-survey rule. Both land on ONE normalized op `{op:'meta', subtype:'rewound', data:{harness, toMessageId, numTurns, ids, kind, ts}}`; exactly one of toMessageId/numTurns is non-null and BOTH keys are always present (a null is a statement, an absent key is a gap). **MARK, NEVER SPLICE:** removing entries would shift every index under `slice(offset,limit)` and change `total` under a client mid-pagination — the machinery three paging incidents came out of. `kind` decides the treatment: `'superseded'` (claude) is HIDDEN, following the CLI's own instruction, because the canonical copy is right beside it; `'rollback'` (codex) is STRUCK IN PLACE with a "rewound" tag, because hiding it would silently rewrite what someone remembers reading. **HONEST STATUS (round 4): the codex half is VERIFIED (`thread_rolled_back` exists in 3 real local rollouts) and is the retraction lane that actually runs today; the claude half is UNVERIFIED — 0 `tombstone` records in 24 production buffers, 0 in the live wire probe, and 0 of 7450 files under `~/.claude/projects/`, so the persisted path cannot produce one either.** It is NOT disproven the way `set_in_progress_tool_use_ids`/`compact_progress` are (those go to host callbacks; this one is `yield`ed onto the query stream at 185068785 / 185075330) — it simply needs a server REFUSAL-FALLBACK (`ks.type==="refusal_no_fallback"` / the `server_fallback` + `api_refusal_category` branch), which is not deterministically triggerable and which we will not provoke to satisfy a test. `_processTombstone` and the `superseded` rendering stay as dormant, test-pinned code. A codex turn is counted by USER messages, not by our `turnIndex` (that counter also advances on task_started and on compaction — one codex turn spans up to two of them: 24 user records under 30 turnIndex groups on a real rollout); already-rewound messages are skipped so two rollbacks in a row each take N LIVE turns; `num_turns` beyond what we ever rendered reports BOTH numbers instead of claiming N were struck. Both normalizers drop rewound messages from `turnMap()` (the minimap must not point at a ghost turn), and the codex side ALSO threads `noticeKind` through `_create` — it was silently dropped there since 2.369.20, the exact 2.227.4 gap the claude normalizer documents. **Gates:** test-codex-history (three turns + `thread_rolled_back` num_turns=1 ⇒ two rendered, WITH the negative control that renders three without the record; live/rebuilt agreement; double rollback; over-deep rollback), test-stdout-registry (tombstone leg + the negative control for a tombstone naming a message we never rendered), test-turn-truth-ui (the two treatments measured in chrome).

  **Compaction stage (§2.11) — `system/status` is the lane, `compact_progress` is shape parity.** THE WIRE (round 4, a REAL AUTO compaction captured verbatim in data/session-buffers/sess-5-1788332329337.buf lines 57-62; pre_tokens 997587 → post_tokens 11159, duration_ms **174751**): `system/status {status:"compacting"}` → `system/hook_started SessionStart:compact` → `hook_response` → `system/status {status:null, compact_result:"success"}` → `system/compact_boundary {compact_metadata:{trigger:"auto",…}}`, and **zero** `compact_progress` records. The CLI's host callback `onCompactEvent` CONSUMES `compact_progress` (`case"compact_progress":P.main.applyCompactProgress(x.event);return`, offset 201341255 — a TUI spinner store) and forwards only its `sdk_status` twin, which `HRt` (198800990) maps to this record (`{type:"system",subtype:"status",status,compact_result?,compact_error?}`, 190037796); the whole channel carries just two values (15 `sdk_status` emitters, all `"compacting"` or `null`) plus a `"requesting"` the forwarder filters out itself (`function wJt(e){return e!=="requesting"&&k5()}`, 198800730). So the consumer drives §2.11 from `system/status`: `{status:'compacting'}` sets `session._streamingKind='compacting'` + the label and broadcasts a `compact-progress {event:'compact_start'}` frame; a `hook_started` while that kind is set names the hook phase (gated on the kind — hook_started is in 13 of 24 production buffers and is none of this branch's business in a normal turn); `{status:null}` with `compact_result`/`compact_error` (or while compacting) clears the kind and reports the OUTCOME. **This is also the ONLY lane that sees an AUTO compaction** — the one nobody typed `/compact` for, which ws-handler's send-site label structurally cannot see and which is the only compaction a long session ever hits; the Stop two-step guard (2.365.0) now arms from it. NEGATIVE CASE, in code and in tests: the SAME subtype carries the CLI's permission-mode echo (`_r(p,P)` = `{status:null, permissionMode}`, 199038328) — an outcome field (or the absence of `permissionMode`) is what makes the record ours, never `status:null` alone. `'status'` is in HANDLED_SYSTEM_SUBTYPES, card-less like `api_retry`. The client keeps ONE frame shape for both lanes and KEEPS the terminal stage: `chat-renderers.compactHintText()` is the one sentence under "Compact now", the hardcoded *"…takes 1–2 minutes — do not press Stop…"* apology is its FALLBACK, and a finished compaction reports success/failure instead of silently reverting to an apology for something that already ended. **But a KEPT terminal stage belongs to the compaction it describes, not to the view forever (round 5).** Nothing ever cleared `_compactStage`, so the FIRST compaction of a view — including the AUTO one this lane newly covers, which no user action precedes — permanently replaced the guidance every later "Prompt is too long" card exists to give (the button's sentence read "Compaction finished." instead of the rewind-and-retry instruction; reproduced at 375×667 against the real bundle by replaying the production AUTO frames with no card on screen). `compactInFlight()` is the ONE predicate: `setCompactStage` still rewrites every hint ALREADY on screen (a card that WATCHED the compaction keeps its outcome — round 4 intact), while `appendContextFullCard` renders `compactFallbackHint()` unless a compaction is actually running. **AND A CLAIM ABOUT *RIGHT NOW* NEEDS ITS OWN CLOSING (round 6).** Round 5 closed the TERMINAL half; the NON-terminal half stayed open. `session._streamingKind === 'compacting'` is a claim about this instant, the client mirrors it as the held `_compactStage`, and `compactInFlight()` gates the WHOLE guidance card — but only the `status:null` outcome record retired that claim OUT LOUD. The other two turn-lifecycle exits (`result`/`compact_boundary`, and the harness's own idle `session_state_changed`) cleared `_streamingKind` SILENTLY. A compaction that ends with no outcome record is a real wire shape, not a theoretical one — a PreCompact hook that BLOCKS it makes the CLI emit a bare `sdk_status status:null` with no metadata (which this branch correctly refuses to read as an outcome), and ws-handler's `/compact` send site sets the kind BEFORE the CLI has said anything at all — so the client kept "Compacting: running <hook> hooks…" forever and every later "Prompt is too long" card lost the rewind-and-retry sentence it exists to give (reproduced on the real engine: frames `["hooks_start"]`, `_streamingKind` already null, no `compact_end`). Fix = ONE named `retireCompaction(sess, sid)` — acts only when the kind is 'compacting', clears it and broadcasts `compact_end` — called at BOTH exits; on the normal path the outcome record has already cleared the kind, so there is never a second frame. `result: null` on purpose: round 5's law holds at the new exit too, so the card says "Compaction ended.", never "finished". The suite pins both exits' frame sequences, two negative controls (a SECOND turn end broadcasts nothing; an ordinary turn produces no frame at either exit) and a WIRING PIN on the call sites — a THIRD exit's silence is visible only through that. The browser leg no longer hand-writes the frames: it runs the REAL consumer in node, takes what it actually broadcasts, and replays THAT into the real client. **And "ended" is not "succeeded":** a `compact_end` with no outcome is a real wire shape — a PreCompact hook that BLOCKS the compaction makes the CLI emit a bare `sdk_status status:null` with no metadata (`if(ye.blockedBy)…onCompactEvent({type:"sdk_status",status:null})`), and the dormant `compact_progress` lane hardcodes `result:null` on every frame — so only the CLI's own `"success"` is reported as finished and an empty outcome says "Compaction ended." (new zh/ja keys). **The declared lane `{type:'compact_progress', event: hooks_start{hook_type} | compact_start{hint_text?} | compact_end}` is kept as a reader for shape parity only** — no VibeSpace-spawned CLI has ever emitted one. Its round-2 lesson still stands and is still pinned, because inside the CLI the emitters and the schema disagree: the zod union (offset 179096059) says `hook_type`/`hint_text` while **every producer builds camelCase** (183979983 / 183980713 / 185190125 / 185193937 / 185195701 / 185198872 / 185209427 / 192261073), the CLI's own consumer reads `t.hookType` / `t.hintText` (189086200), and `grep -aob 'hint_text:'` finds exactly ONE hit — the schema literal. So that reader takes **both spellings at ONE point** (`ev.hook_type ?? ev.hookType`, `ev.hint_text ?? ev.hintText`), declared-first, and publishes the same normalized broadcast. **Invariants: (1) a record shape is what the EMITTER writes, not what the schema declares; (2) a record that EXISTS is not a record that ARRIVES — dump the emit sites AND look at our own stdout, because a suite that synthesizes the record can never tell "we parse it right" from "it never came".** test-stdout-registry pins the `system/status` sequence against the production capture (start / hook stage / outcome / error) with four negative controls (compact_boundary adds no second frame; a permission-mode echo inside and outside a compaction changes nothing; hook_started in a normal turn is invisible), keeps the camelCase/snake_case parity legs for the dormant lane, re-dumps the binary when a CLI is installed (SKIP with the reason otherwise), and runs the wire probe — whose leg ⓕ′ measures that the probe left NOTHING behind (scripts/probe-claude-stdout.mjs), **applying the SWEEP'S OWN RULE rather than one of its own (round 6)**: the sweep spares leftovers younger than 10 minutes because a CONCURRENTLY RUNNING probe owns them (two worktrees pushing minutes apart really do overlap), so an assert of ABSOLUTE ABSENCE turned the mandatory pre-push gate red on exactly the case the sweep exists to spare — and blamed the sweep. The probe now REPORTS its rule (`cleaned.staleMs`, `cleaned.spared:[{name,ageMs}]`) and both readers (the project-dir scan and `discoverClaudeSessions`) filter by it, measured in BOTH directions on the SAME directory (fresh ⇒ both readers pass and it IS visible; backdated past the threshold ⇒ both name it). Leg ⓕ″ then drives the probe's contracts DETERMINISTICALLY against a FAKE claude in an isolated HOME+TMPDIR (zero vendor cost, and the fake leaves the real CLI's footprint — transcript + per-session env dir + a ledger of both — so "left nothing behind" is never vacuous): stale swept / concurrent spared / own footprint gone, and the RAW CAPTURE's two symlink attacks. Round 5 had moved that capture from the 0700 mkdtemp dir to a PREDICTABLE name directly in /tmp, and `fs.writeFileSync` FOLLOWS symlinks — /tmp's sticky bit stops another local user deleting our file, not CREATING that name first, so a planted `/tmp/vs-wire-probe.last.jsonl -> victim` had the victim truncated and rewritten with CLI stdout under the developer's uid on every non-docs push (reproduced). It now lives at `<tmp>/vs-wire-probe-raw-<uid>/last.jsonl`: a dir this uid owns, created 0700 and re-checked with `lstat` (the DIR name can be planted too), opened `O_NOFOLLOW|O_CREAT` — **no O_TRUNC** (round 7: this line used to say there was one, and the code deliberately omits it; truncating inside the open would empty somebody else's plain file BEFORE the fstat that learns whose it is, which is the hole the block exists to close) — then fstat-verified (regular file, our uid) and only then `ftruncate`d, and anything unexpected SKIPS the capture WITH A REASON instead of writing where it was not asked to; the sweep skips that dir and retires round 5's old filename.

  **ROUND 7 — the same three questions, one layer down.** (a) **A GUARD IS PINNED ON THE WRITE THAT BREAKS IT, NOT ON THE CALL SITE.** Round 6's `retires === 2` detects a DELETED call and is blind to a FOURTH exit clearing the kind itself (reproduced: a `system/vs_fake_silent_exit` branch writing `session._streamingKind = null` left all 168 asserts green, pin included). `endCompaction(sess, sid, {result, error, announce})` is now the ONE WRITER of the cleared kind — `retireCompaction` is its guarded wrapper, the outcome record calls it with the CLI's verdict, the dormant `compact_progress` lane calls it with `announce:false` because it publishes its own frame — and test-stdout-registry pins a CENSUS of `_streamingKind = null` writes in that file at exactly ONE (comment lines excluded), so a fifth exit is a new write and goes red by construction. A second, BEHAVIOURAL leg reads the consumer's OWN vocabulary (every literal compared against `msg.type`/`msg.subtype`) and drives each shape through the real consumer with the kind armed: the law is "either the claim survives, or a `compact_end` went out in the same record". (b) **SESSION DEATH IS THE THIRD EXIT.** The wrapper dying is the exit no record can announce: the server retires through the consumer's own bound `session._retireCompaction` (session-schema row) BEFORE the `exited` broadcast, and the client retires locally in the `exited` branch via `ChatView._retireCompactionStage()` — a server that CRASHED sends no frame at all, and 'exited' is the one thing the view always learns. Both are guarded (`compactInFlight()` / kind === 'compacting'), so a session that never compacted is never made to claim one ended, and both send `result:null` — ENDED, never "finished".
