(c) **THE SWEEP EXPORTS ITS CLOCK, NOT JUST ITS THRESHOLD.** Round 6's readers re-derived staleness at ASSERTION time, so a leftover the sweep spared at 9m58s was flagged at 10m01s — the mandatory gate going red on exactly the case the sweep exists to spare, again. `cleaned` now carries `sweptAt`, `spared` is built by the SAME pass that decided (name + the age measured THEN), and both readers use that clock AND exclude the spared names — a decision, not a measurement. (d) **THE FAKE CLI NAMES THE INTERPRETER RUNNING THE SUITE** (`#!${process.execPath}`, its directory on the probe's PATH): `#!/usr/bin/env node` against a PATH of `<fakebin>:/usr/bin:/bin` is unrunnable on an nvm-only box / the project's own node:22-slim image / an actions/setup-node runner, and leg ⓕ″ then died on `path.dirname(undefined)`, taking ~65 later asserts with it. The leg is now a labelled block: a probe that could not run gets ONE loud red naming its `skip` reason and bails, never a crash. test-turn-truth-ui adds the round-5 legs: the production AUTO frames replayed with NO card on screen, then a card appended and asserted actionable again, with three negative controls (a card built while a compaction is IN FLIGHT still opens on the live stage; the card that watched it keeps its outcome; only `"success"` says finished).

  **Per-element marks must be RE-DERIVED, never remembered by an element (`chat-view._applyElementMarks`, round 2).** Both of the client-side marks this batch added — the retraction strike/hide (`_applyRewound`) and the executing-tool dot (`_onToolsInProgress`) — were written straight to the DOM at their origin, and **FOUR code paths build an element for a message**: `_onCreateMessage` (also the paging path, via `_renderDetached`), the status-transition re-render inside `_onEditMessage`, `_rerenderVisible` (the compact-mode toggle), and — round 3 — `_renderGapMsg` (the huge-session seek renderer in chat-view-seek.js). The two replacement paths carried over `dataset.msgId/ts/line` and the run open/closed marks and nothing else, so a mark died on the first re-render. **The claude tombstone case ALWAYS gets one:** the message it retracts is a *streaming partial orphan*, and `MessageManager._finalizeStreaming` emits `{op:'edit', id, fields:{status:'complete'}}` for exactly that message at the next `result` — so the retracted answer came back on screen one record later, while an attach/rebuild (which reads `msg.rewound` off the normalizer) still hid it: the precise live-vs-rebuilt divergence the create path's comment claims cannot happen. The in-flight dot had the same shape with a worse tail — for a long-running tool the next `set_in_progress_tool_use_ids` delta may never come, so scrolling the card out and back silently un-marked it forever. The fix is NOT a third copy of the mark code at each site (that is the same miss with more copies): ONE `_applyElementMarks(el, msg)` called at every place an element is **BUILT FOR A MESSAGE**, which asks the VIEW STATE what the element should be wearing (`msg.rewound` → `_markRewoundEl`; `this._inFlightTools` → `.chat-tool-inflight`, toggled so it is idempotent). A new per-element mark is added there and is correct in all four paths. **Round 3 corrected the RULE, not the mechanism.** It was first written as "every place an element enters `this._elements`" — and gap-slab elements are deliberately kept OUT of that map (they sit outside the virtual window's accounting), so the hook skipped `_renderGapMsg` entirely and a retraction reached through a seek slab rendered as ordinary live history. Reachability is not theoretical and it is the §2.10 failure itself: past 34MB (`JSONL_HEAD_BYTES + JSONL_TAIL_BYTES`) the seek path is the ONLY way to read the earlier history, `transcript-service.gapSlab` normalizes each slab through the same message manager, and codex carries `thread_rolled_back` in the ROLLOUT — so `msg.rewound` genuinely arrives there. Measured at 375×667 before the fix: rollback `{display:'block', opacity:1, tags:0}`, superseded `display:'block'`, executing card `::after` width `auto`. **A count written in prose cannot fail**, which is why the enumeration is now a test: the suite's leg ⓪ (no chrome needed) counts every `.renderAssistantMsg(` call under `src/lib/` and demands a `_applyElementMarks` call within 2000 chars of each, with a synthetic marked/unmarked pair as its own negative control. **Gate:** test-turn-truth-ui leg ⑤ measures COMPUTED STYLE (display / opacity / tag count / `::after` width) at 375×667 across the three rebuild paths and leg ⑥ measures the same five elements built through `_renderGapMsg`, each with two negative controls — a never-retracted message and a pending-but-not-executing tool card, which must stay unmarked (re-deriving must not mark everything). Neutering the round-2 fix turns exactly its three asserts red; neutering the round-3 call turns leg ⓪ plus leg ⑥'s three asserts red (`display block / [block,1,0] / auto`) while both controls stay green.


### mounts.js

  mounts.js            — MountManager (rclone mounts, MULTI-SOURCE: typed records s3/drive/onedrive/gmail/webdav/sftp/vibespace/rclone via _rcloneFor; detached + boot adoption; one-click rclone install (data/bin, pinned 1.65.2); guided Google Drive OAuth (2.226.2: all four OAuth entry points render the auth URL as a copyable row — consent completable from ANY browser, paste-back relay finishes; popup-block reported honestly); rclone.conf import; per-mount S3 share minting mc→STS; NATIVE cephfs type (2.109.1, all-flash deployment storage — kernel `sudo mount -t ceph`, per-user quota, env-provisioned My storage VIBESPACE_CEPHFS_* replaces S3, no CSI; **2.237.2: _mountCephfs ensures a no-op /sbin/modprobe shim before EVERY attempt — mount.ceph hardcodes that path and treats exit-127 as fatal, so a kmod-less image broke every connect even with the ceph module loaded host-side (a container can never modprobe the shared kernel; when kmod existed the call always failed harmlessly). Never remove the shim 'because modprobe exists in the current image'**); flat-connections model — no special my-storage slot, env auto-migrates to a normal mount. **SUBMOUNT model (2.108.0→2.108.2, user-refined twice): ANY top-level storage record can hold submounts — children reference the parent via `parentId` + their own path fields (`remote:path`) and resolve connection through `_connOf()` at use time (addChild allows any non-child parent; no nesting). `kind:'credential'` now means CREDENTIAL-ONLY (root unmountable — bucket-scoped token): UI shows a key ICON in place of the status dot (no text badge, user directive) and NO Connect action; mount() auto-heals kind on a later successful root probe — refreshing the credential's token/keys heals every child (update() bounces mounted children). A credential itself mounts fine when its token can list the remote root (GDrive, account-wide keys); mount() AUTO-DETECTS bucket-scoped tokens (pathless s3-family + root-list probe AccessDenied → convert to credential + guidance) because the fuse mount otherwise "succeeds" and EIOs on every IO (the FishR2 trap — real bucket was `example-prod-data`; S3 names are lowercase). AccessDenied WITH a path fails fast pre-mount with a check-the-bucket-name error (v2Auth probe extension; v2Auth stays undefined so a fixed path re-probes). addChild/convert/remove-guarded; export re-links children by parent NAME. Drive re-auth: startDriveAuthForMount(id) runs the guided OAuth with the mount's own client creds, applyDriveToken() writes back + reconnects children — the invalid_grant recovery path; **applyDriveToken accepts drive AND onedrive AND cloud holders (2.268.8 — it rejected non-Google types, leaving the edit-dialog re-auth button dead for them), and the client re-auth surfaces word themselves per provider (`_oauthProviderNames` in sidebar-mounts.js), never hardcoded "Google Drive". ONEDRIVE REQUIRES RESOLVED drive_id+drive_type IN CONFIG (2.268.8, real report: every fresh native add failed with rclone's cryptic "upgrading from older versions" error — imported rclone.confs worked only because the conf carried both): `_resolveOneDriveDrive` (Graph /me/drive, region-aware GRAPH_BASE) runs as a mount-time backstop when driveId is missing (error recorded into _errors — "token expired" wording surfaces the row re-auth button) + after each re-auth write-back; explicit driveId never overridden. Test: scripts/test-onedrive-resolve.mjs.** Non-env mounts expose ALL connection params in the edit dialog PREFILLED with their real current values, SECRETS INCLUDED (2.108.8, user directive: no more blank "=default" fields — `_showEditMountDialog` fetches the decrypted config via `GET /api/mounts/:id/config`/`config()` and fills every input, keys/tokens/passwords/rclone params too; single-user instance, inputs stay type=text); save is a DIFF — a field is sent only when it changed from the fetched original (unchanged secrets aren't needlessly re-encrypted; server ignores an emptied secret so clearing one is a no-op there), an emptied rclone per-param row removes it. Only my-storage locks connection fields (edit shows name+mountpoint only; `config()` returns `{envLocked:true}` with no secrets). **HUNG-MOUNT DEFENSE (2.108.3, real outage)**: a fuse mount to an unreachable backend (home-LAN SMB host from a datacenter pod) hangs every IO — node's libuv THREADPOOL fills with stuck fs ops and the whole server stops answering while the event loop stays idle (ep_poll; /login 130s → readiness dead → pod out of Service). mount() runs a post-mount child-process `ls` probe (6s guard; error-exit=responsive, only a STUCK child trips) and startHealthWatchdog() sweeps every mounted record (60s + 15s-after-boot, covers boot-ADOPTED mounts that skip mount()) — hang ⇒ lazy unmount + `_killMountDaemon` (exact /proc cmdline argv match — a wedged rclone survives fusermount -uz) + desired=unmounted persisted + row error. Never probe mountpoints with node fs — child processes only. 2.108.4: pathBlocked() circuit breaker — mount() blocks the root for the WHOLE connect window and hang-detection blocks 90s; a files.js router middleware fails every op under a blocked root fast with 503 (an open explorer window otherwise stuffs the pool during the probe window — real follow-up incident); UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=32 at the top of server.js (before any pool-touching require). **2.109.0 STRUCTURAL FIX: src/safe-fs.js worker_threads pool** — every LOCAL user-path fs op in routes/files.js runs in a worker with its OWN libuv pool + per-op deadline + kill/respawn (path resolution/traversal stay in main; worker executes the resolved absolute path only; ?host= untouched; app.locals.safeFs wired in server.js, inline fallback if the pool fails). Revoked/expired share SURFACING: _probeMountpoint is 3-state (ok/error/hung) + _probeBackendAccess (uncached rclone lsf re-auth, gated to _revocable = imported/vibespace/expiring) catches a 401/403 that a cache-served mountpoint ls hides → row shows "connected but every file errors" (clears on re-grant). 2.108.5 SELF-MOUNT GUARD: a vibespace-bridge record whose bearer token exists in OUR OWN MountTokens store points back at this instance — fuse→HTTP→self deadlocks the threadpool (real incident); refused at add/import/mount via mounts.selfTokenCheck = mountTokens.has(raw) (no lastUsedAt bump)**. **DIRECT CEPHFS SUBTREE SHARING (2.111.7): sharing a folder from a cephfs My-storage mount calls an in-cluster minter (env VIBESPACE_CEPHMINT_URL/_TOKEN) to mint a PATH-SCOPED cephx key (`ceph fs authorize <fs> client.vibespace-share-<rand> <path> <r|rw>`) and emits a `vibespace-cephmount:v1:<b64>` link; import → a normal `cephfs` mount that KERNEL-mounts the subtree directly (full flash bandwidth, no WebDAV relay). Key scoped to exactly the subpath (mds allow r path=…), recorded in `_state.shares` (kind:cephmount), revoke deletes it cluster-side. Env-gated — absent the minter, sharing falls back to the WebDAV bridge; cross-cluster/external always uses the bridge. mintCephShare/parseCephMountLink/revoke path in mounts.js; canCephShare in list(); `_showCephShareDialog` UI. Helm: storage.existingClaim (mount a pre-made PVC — the home-SC migration lever) + cephMint.{url,token}.** **2.110.0 HARDENING (user directive 最稳定+性能最好+自动恢复): every rclone mount runs `--vfs-cache-mode full` with a PERSISTENT per-mount cache dir (data/vfs-cache/<id>, root overridable VIBESPACE_VFS_CACHE_DIR; setting mounts.vfsCacheMaxSizeGB default 10G) — dirty writes survive a daemon crash and resume uploading on remount (verified live: SIGKILL 0.5s post-write → auto-remount → object lands); bounded IO (--timeout 60s --contimeout 15s + retries) so flaky backends error instead of hanging; new flags gated via _rcloneHasFlag (old system rclone falls back to writes mode). AUTO-RECONNECT supervision: desired now ONLY reflects user intent — internal teardowns (hang defense) keep desired=mounted and _healthSweep's dead-mount branch remounts with backoff 1→2→5→10min (_maybeAutoRemount; _noteReconnectBackoff), auth-class errors (denied/revoked/expired/AccessDenied…) excluded from retry so the actionable message survives; unmount(id,{internal:true}) is the internal teardown that doesn't rewrite intent; mount() is wrapped by a _connecting guard (one connect in flight per record). rcloneBin() NFS trap: executing the 57MB binary from a network FS demand-pages it per exec (~22s measured) — _fastBin copies it once to ~/.cache/vibespace keyed by size+mtime (22s→0.03s), detection via /proc/mounts longest-prefix fstype**) **OAuth-death detection (2.368.6, real OneDrive incident): a dead refresh token hides behind a HEALTHY-looking mount — fuse dir cache keeps listings working while every download fails 'unauthenticated: Unauthenticated' (EIO on every file open, zero UI signal). _oauthBacked mounts (drive/onedrive/cloud/rclone-OAuth) now get the fresh-process backend probe on a slow clock (10min; every sweep while an auth error shows or the mountpoint fails), the denied-regex knows the OAuth phrasings (unauthenticated/invalid_grant/InvalidAuthenticationToken), the health message names the fix (re-authorize), and the client button regex matches it. Gated by scripts/test-mount-oauth-probe.mjs (15). Do not narrow _revocable back to 'my own Drive can(not) expire' — it can. SECOND HALF (2.368.7): an EIO-wedged daemon SURVIVES fusermount -uz, so the re-auth bounce stacked a fresh daemon on the dead-token one and changed nothing (4 leaked daemons found); unmount() now polls _daemonAlive and kills survivors BEFORE resolving (bounce callers mount() right after — a later kill would murder the fresh daemon), and mount() kills any stale daemon before spawning. Never resolve an unmount while the daemon lives. TRUE ROOT CAUSE (2.368.8, corrects 2.368.6's dead-token diagnosis): the token was ALIVE — refresh/list/upload all worked, only the /content DOWNLOAD endpoint 401d (Microsoft's migrated consumer OneDrive vs rclone 1.65.2; A/B with identical config: 1.69.3/1.75.0 download fine). Pin bumped to v1.69.3 (stays in the Cloudflare-STS-safe 1.63–1.69 range) + restore() self-heals data/bin/rclone to the pin at boot (nothing else ever re-runs the installer — a pin bump alone reaches no existing install). The sweep also runs _probeBackendRead (1-byte cat of the first root file) because this failure mode PASSES lsf — auth-class failures must be located per-ENDPOINT (refresh/list/upload/download), never collapsed into 'token dead'.** **STRANDED WRITES (2.369.15, real OneDrive "is not empty, use --allow-non-empty" incident): a mount point that is NOT mounted is a bare local directory, and anything written there (the task store's generated TASK.md recreated a whole tree while OneDrive was down) both disappears once the storage reconnects and blocks that reconnect. Never `--allow-non-empty` (it hides the files). `_ensureMountpointDir(mp, {quarantine:true})` on BOTH connect paths (rclone + cephfs; never on the mount-point edit path) moves leftovers aside to a sibling `<mp>.stranded-<ts>/` — never delete, never merge — recreates the mount point empty and broadcasts a level-2 server-notice naming the path and items. `shadowedBy(p)` = the registered storage whose mount point contains p but is not mounted (gmail/credential records excluded; `isMounted(m, live)` accepts a pre-read /proc/mounts): files.js refuses every op under it with 503, task-groups skips its TASK.md and retries on every `mounts-updated` (the wiring tees the broadcast into `onMountsUpdated` + one boot resync after mounts exist — the constructor's boot regen runs before them). New server-side writers into user paths consult `shadowedBy` first. Gated by scripts/test-mount-stranded.mjs (26).**

### harnesses/ (the harness registry — S1/S4/S6, 2.369.18–22)

  **`store.lastTurnModel` / `store.lastTurnEffort` (B-6b6d)** — "what did this CONVERSATION last run at", for the resume ladder (src/resume-continuity.js). The hook's PRESENCE is the declaration that this harness can answer, so there is no second boolean to drift: **codex** declares both (its rollout writes a `turn_context` per turn), **opencode** declares the model (`serve.facts().sessionModel`, round 2), **claude** declares NEITHER (round 2 — its transcript cannot express the model's context variant and records no effort at all; see src/session-store.js), **shell** and the generic ACP harness neither. ws-create asks the descriptor and never branches on a backend id — this is where the B-21e4 codex-only env post-fill went. A hook returning `''` means "this conversation has nothing recorded" ⇒ the instance default, LOGGED; a missing hook means "this harness cannot answer" ⇒ send nothing.

  src/plugin-manifest.js — PURE manifest validator + the consent vocabulary (Ph2 2.369.24, Ph4 2.369.30): validateManifest(raw, {hostVersion, folderName, homeDir, forbiddenRoots}) normalizes tiers (none|iframe|module; "trusted" = alias), clientEntry, contributes windows/agentTools/routes/settings(boolean|string|number|select)/themes, capabilities.server {fs.read/write (abs or ~, no .., never covering a forbidden root), childProcess (spawn⇒childProcess warned), net (declared only)}; needsConsent(m) = module tier, any declared server capability, OR any contributed agentTool (2.369.43 — a generated shim is a program on every session PATH, outside the plugin sandbox); capabilitySummary(m) = [{id,text,params}] the SAME items server (409 payload) and client (dialog via t(text, params)) render; capabilitiesHash(m) = key-order-independent fingerprint of the consent surface (tier/server/capabilities/agent tools/routes) — array order is significant by design. **2.369.43 hardening: cleanText() strips control chars + U+2028/U+2029 from EVERY free-text field (description/label/window title/tool description/setting+theme labels) — free text ends up in generated code, so it is sanitized ONCE here; normalizeFsPath COLLAPSES the path (own segment walk — the file has ZERO requires by design) before the forbidden-root prefix test, because node's permission model collapses `//`, `/./` and `/.` (`--allow-fs-read=/.` = the whole filesystem); the fs-write summary says "write AND read" (permissionArgs adds a read grant per write path).** **2.369.44: normalizeFsPath also MODELS node's `*`** — node reads a declared path only up to the FIRST `*` and grants every path whose STRING starts with what came before it (probed against node v24: `--allow-fs-read=/t/a/*` and `/t/a/*/ignored` both read `/t/a/b/c/f.txt`; `/t/a/z*` reads `/t/a/zz.txt` but not `/t/a/b/…`; `/*` reads /etc/hostname). Treating a pattern as a plain path let `["/*"]` and `["<parent-of-a-forbidden-root>/*"]` pass validation and grant the install/data dir. Now: at most ONE `*`, only as the LAST character (a mid-pattern `*` is refused LOUDLY — it grants everything before it and silently ignores the rest); the containment test runs on the literal prefix (`r.startsWith(prefix)` — a wildcard over a parent of a forbidden root is forbidden, and `…/dat*` catching `…/data` too); `/` and `/*` are refused with or without roots; `/dir/*` is never collapsed into `/dir*` (different grants — the latter also covers `/dirty`), and the accepted pattern is stored VERBATIM so the string the owner consented to is the string node gets. **B-7638: the collapse rule and the wildcard rule MEET at `~/.*`** — node only collapses a LONE `.` segment, so `<home>/.*` is the prefix `<home>/.` (dotfiles), but collapsing the dot HERE emitted `<home>*`: a string prefix covering the whole home directory AND every sibling path that starts with those characters (`/home/u*` reaches `/home/u2/…`). A home-dir glob may not widen to every user, and the widening was invisible — the manifest said one thing and node was handed another — so a `.` segment immediately before `*` is REFUSED with the prefix it would have produced (write the directory `…/` or a literal name prefix). `~/.config/*`, `~/./*` (a lone `.` node itself collapses) and `~/.ssh` are unaffected. Invariant: no other module mutates or reinterprets a manifest — and any rewrite this file performs on a declared path must be one node performs too, or it must refuse.
  src/server/plugin-install.js — plugin INSTALL SOURCES (Ph4): path / git (execFile `clone --depth 1`, https+ssh forms only, 60s, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0) / zip = .vsp (unzip -Z1 listing audit refuses traversal BEFORE extraction) / github-release (public API, the *.vsp asset) → ONE staging pipeline under data/plugins-staging (same fs ⇒ rename), auditTree refuses symlinks + caps files/bytes, manifest validated in staging against the loader's forbiddenRoots, then moved into data/plugins/<id>; a previous copy MOVES to data/plugins-trash/<id>-<ts>/{plugin,state} (trash(), never rmSync; why.json records the reason). auditTree is ASYNC (fs/promises, depth-capped) and returns a `contentHash` — sha256 over relpath\0size\0filehash in sorted order — which the loader uses to tell a byte-identical reinstall from a different package under the same id; stageFromPath copies with fsp.cp (the sync walk + sync ≤200MB copy against an OWNER-SUPPLIED path blocked the event loop, 2.369.43). Invariants: never delete user data; never a shell string; the id comes from the manifest (update refuses a different id); the uploaded temp file is removed on EVERY exit path (the source whitelist once threw above the try/finally).
  src/server/plugin-loader.js — PLUGIN LOADER (Ph2 + Ph4): discovery validates with forbiddenRoots + reads theme files (readThemeFile: css keys must be --custom-properties, values short and without {};), CONSENT DRIFT check (enabled && needsConsent && !isTrusted ⇒ disabled + notice at discovery), registry data/plugin-registry.json {enabled, trust:{id:{trusted,trustedAt,capabilitiesHash}}, installs:{id:{source,value,installedAt,updatedAt}}}; setEnabled(id, on, {trusted}) throws 409 {consentRequired, capabilities, changed} when consent is needed and absent; startChild forks server.js under `node --permission` (permissionArgs: plugin dir read + data dir rw + declared fs paths, write implies read, --allow-child-process only when declared; NODE_OPTIONS stripped; the child 'error' event is handled — ERR_IPC_CHANNEL_CLOSED from a send racing an exit would crash the host), classifyExit turns an ERR_ACCESS_DENIED stderr into the panel's one-line error; routes: /plugins/<id>/* serves declared theme JSON (any tier) → the module entry SAME-ORIGIN as javascript only while isTrusted (else 403 consentRequired) → iframe ui/ under the sandbox CSP; POST /api/plugins/install (JSON or multipart field `file`), DELETE /api/plugins/manifests/:id (uninstall → trash), POST …/:id/update, GET …/:id/capabilities; shims bake instanceUrl.url() as INSTANCE_URL fallback (syncShims re-runs on instance-url onChange), shimNames() feeds hosts.js agentTools(); telemetry events plugin-install/-update/-uninstall/-trusted/-crash/-consent-drift. **2.369.43: shim generation puts manifest text through jsLiteral() ONLY (const DESCRIPTION / const ARGS_SCHEMA; a CR/U+2028/U+2029 in a description used to end the `//` comment and execute — the shim runs outside the sandbox with the session token and ships to every host); install() resets enabled+trust unless the package is byte-identical AND from the same recorded source (update() keeps consent and records the new hash) — **2.369.44: that reset is gated on the REGISTRY RECORD, never on `r.replaced`**: enabled/trust live in data/plugin-registry.json keyed by id and outlive the directory, so with a hand-deleted (or half-installed) plugin folder there was nothing to "replace" and the next, DIFFERENT package under that id landed pre-enabled and pre-trusted with its client module served same-origin and no dialog at all; proxied /x/* replies always carry nosniff + `sandbox; default-src 'none'` and an untrusted plugin's document content-type is downgraded to text/plain; the intentional-stop mark is a Symbol on the CHILD (rec._stopping mis-filed a fresh child's crash while the old one was still exiting).** Node's permission model does NOT restrict network — documented in the dialog and docs/plugins.md.
  src/lib/plugin-client.js — PLUGIN CLIENT (Ph2 + Ph4): apply() is serialized and idempotent — for every enabled plugin registers iframe window types, registerPluginSettings(contributes.settings), fetches + registers theme JSON (ThemeManager.registerPluginTheme('plugin-<id>-<themeId>', '<label> (plugin)')), and for client:'module' `import()`s /plugins/<id>/<clientEntry>?v=<version> ONLY when the manifest row says trusted (the server serves it only then too) and calls activate(api); disable/uninstall ⇒ deactivate() + AbortController + unregister settings/themes; a throwing module ⇒ toast + `errors` map (the panel shows it), never a boot failure. HOST API (documented at the file head + docs/plugins.md): id/version/manifest/signal/app, registerWindowType/openWindow (plugin:<id>:<wid>), showToast/createModalShell/t, fetch scoped to /api/plugins/<id>/x/*, settings.get/set/path/onChange (per-path settings.js listeners), storage (vsp_<id>_), on('theme-changed' | 'plugins-manifests-updated' | 'ws'). Keep the API small; every addition is documented in both places.
  src/lib/plugins-ui.js — …Ph4 (2.369.30): Install plugin… dialog (path/git/github-release JSON, .vsp multipart), per-plugin ⋯ menu (Show capabilities… / Update / Uninstall with showConfirmDialog), Enable → 409 consentRequired ⇒ _pluginConsentDialog (capability items via t(item.text, item.params), 'Enable (trusted)') ⇒ enable with trusted:true; tier/trust badges, notices, client-module errors — every manifest string escHtml-ed. **HARNESS BACKGROUND SERVICES (2026-09-07):** the ⚙ → Plugins card for `opencode-serve` (state line running/starting/parked/runaway/turned-off, an env-forced notice that DISABLES the controls instead of offering a button that can only fail, Start/Stop + one "Run this service whenever VibeSpace runs" switch, no config box) (the env-forced card is the ONLY control surface for an ops-forced instance, which is safe because the keeper now guarantees nothing is RUNNING under `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0` — see src/opencode-serve.js) AND the FIRST-USE offer used from the session lifecycle: `maybeOfferHarnessService(backend,{needsService,retry})` → `_harnessServiceState` (a fresh `/api/plugins/<id>/status`, falling back to the broadcast-cached BACKEND_META.service so a network blip never claims the service is off) → `_harnessServiceDialog` (createModalShell, NEVER a native confirm: what it does, that it is loopback-only and stops when disabled, "Not now" / "Enable & start") → `enableHarnessService` (POST enabled + start, then poll ≤25s and run the caller's `retry` — the pending action continues, and a service that never answers TOASTS what state it is in instead of spinning forever). We record the "asked" flag on EITHER answer (an accepted-then-disabled service is exactly when the sidebar's hidden-history row must appear), and ONE offer is in flight at a time (a layout restore can open five history windows in a tick; joiners still get their own retry).
  src/lib/settings-schema.js — …registerPluginSettings/unregisterPluginSettings/pluginSettingPath/listPluginSettings (Ph4): the ONLY mutation path into SETTINGS_SCHEMA/SETTINGS_CATEGORIES from outside; plugin.<id>.<key> under category 'Plugin: <label>'.
  src/lib/themes.js — …registerPluginTheme/unregisterPluginTheme/getPluginThemes (Ph4): plugin-* namespace apart from custom-* (the server-sync sweep never touches them); applyPendingTheme keeps a pending plugin-* theme instead of falling back to dark before the plugin client registers it.
  HARNESS STORE (S3, 2.369.31) — every chat descriptor's `store` = { discover({activeSessions,webuiPids,devSnap}) → session entries (claude: session-store.discoverClaudeSessions = the lock-first sweep moved VERBATIM out of routes/sessions; codex: codex-session-store.listCodexThreadsAsync = rollout walk + /proc liveness in the transcript worker; ACP: none), locate(id,cwd) → transcript path|null (codex: .jsonl or .jsonl.zst twin; ACP: null — the wrapper journal is the history), createReader/Reader (SessionMessages-shaped), forkChain(id), writerSweep(rid,shq,opts) (writer-sweep.js legs per backend), remoteFind(id) → { root, findExpr, cacheRel, maxBytes } for hosts.fetchTranscript, transcriptDirs, conversationIdField }. routes/sessions.js's sweep = `for (const h of listHarnesses()) h.store.discover(...)` + merge (first harness to name a key wins, claude before codex = the old order); transcript-service.localTranscriptPath/fetch go through the descriptor; hosts.fetchTranscript(host, backend, id) is THE remote fetch (fetchSessionJsonl/fetchCodexJsonl = shims). Invariant: no `backend === 'codex'` ternary in a store consumer — unknown ids throw in get().
  src/codex-session-store.js — …S3: `collectCodexThreadMetas()` (pass 1, pure facts: the ~/.codex/sessions walk with a PER-DIRECTORY mtime cache — unchanged mtime older than 2s ⇒ cached listing, no readdir — + extractCodexThreadMeta's per-file cache) and `assembleCodexThreads(facts, {activeSessions, openThreadIds})` (pass 2: live/external/stopped, forkedFrom sources hidden); `listCodexThreadsAsync` runs pass 1 + the /proc scan as transcript-worker ops (`codexThreadMetas`, `codexOpenThreads`; inline fallback when the pool is down) so the 5s poll never walks the tree on the loop; `listCodexThreads` (sync twin) is unchanged for user-action consumers (capture, migration map, spawn baseline). Liveness = discovery-facts.listOpenCodexRolloutPaths (ONE /proc-or-lsof implementation shared with the daemon snapshot). dirCacheStats() for tests. **FORK ORDINAL (B-21e4, codex 0.153 paginated forks):** `resolveCodexForkAncestry(threadId, wrapperChain)` → `[{id, untilOrdinal|null}]` oldest→newest is what `CodexSessionMessages._ensureParsed` prepends: the wrapper's `forked_from` chain merges WHOLE as before (superseded incarnations; turn-scoped fingerprint dedup unchanged) ∪ codex's OWN `forked_from_id` parents whose boundary is KNOWN (a 'Referenced' fork's rollout holds none of the parent's records — only `history_base.end_ordinal_exclusive`/`forked_from_ordinal_exclusive`, PARENT-numbered), cut with `cutRecordsAtOrdinal` (records without a top-level `ordinal` are kept; the walk is depth-8, cycle-safe, and an id in both sources takes the boundary). A sub-agent's `subagent_history_start_ordinal` is CHILD-numbered and its rollout already copies the inherited context ⇒ never an ancestor. Native fork parents are NOT hidden by `assembleCodexThreads` (they are their own conversations; only the wrapper chain hides); entries carry `forkedFromId/forkedFromOrdinal/historyMode` (additive). Bounded caveat: parseCodexSessionJsonl is tail-only (32MB) — a Referenced fork off a bigger parent reads its tail, which the boundary rejects (bounded, never wrong records). Gate: scripts/test-codex-0153.mjs ①. **RECORD PROVENANCE (round 3, 2026-09-06):** `_ensureParsed` tags every file's records with THAT file's thread id before merging — `tagRecordThread(records, id)` sets a NON-enumerable `__threadId` (never in JSON.stringify ⇒ fingerprints, wire payloads and caches unchanged; `sortRecords` re-attaches it on its spread copy; `recordThreadOf(record)` reads it) on the ancestry cuts and the thread's own file alike, covering the remote-jsonl cache and the thread/read cache because both are served through parseCodexSessionJsonl. The codex normalizer keys each response's ledger meta (`cx:<file>:<cumulative>`) by that tag, exactly as the usage walker keys the FILE — a reader-wide id had keyed every parent-half record of a merged fork read under the child. Buffer (live) records stay UNTAGGED on purpose: they follow the normalizer's in-stream default (the wrapper's wrapper_meta.threadId = the file the wrapper writes), which a re-point mid-life moves. Liveness = discovery-facts.listOpenCodexRolloutPaths (ONE /proc-or-lsof implementation shared with the daemon snapshot). dirCacheStats() for tests. **FORK ORDINAL (B-21e4, codex 0.153 paginated forks):** `resolveCodexForkAncestry(threadId, wrapperChain)` → `[{id, untilOrdinal|null}]` oldest→newest is what `CodexSessionMessages._ensureParsed` prepends: the wrapper's `forked_from` chain merges WHOLE as before (superseded incarnations; turn-scoped fingerprint dedup unchanged) ∪ codex's OWN `forked_from_id` parents whose boundary is KNOWN (a 'Referenced' fork's rollout holds none of the parent's records — only `history_base.end_ordinal_exclusive`/`forked_from_ordinal_exclusive`, PARENT-numbered), cut with `cutRecordsAtOrdinal` (records without a top-level `ordinal` are kept; the walk is depth-8, cycle-safe, and an id in both sources takes the boundary). A sub-agent's `subagent_history_start_ordinal` is CHILD-numbered and its rollout already copies the inherited context ⇒ never an ancestor. Native fork parents are NOT hidden by `assembleCodexThreads` (they are their own conversations; only the wrapper chain hides); entries carry `forkedFromId/forkedFromOrdinal/historyMode` (additive). Bounded caveat: parseCodexSessionJsonl is tail-only (32MB) — a Referenced fork off a bigger parent reads its tail, which the boundary rejects (bounded, never wrong records). Gate: scripts/test-codex-0153.mjs ①. **TURN_CONTEXT TWINS + THE EFFORT PAIR (2.369.62):** the wrapper synthesizes a `turn_context` the moment `turn/started` arrives (live visibility) and codex writes its own when the turn really begins — same turn id, same fingerprint, and OURS is always the EARLIER of the two, so first-wins silently suppressed codex's authoritative copy for the whole life of a conversation (a turn codex recorded as 'ultra' stayed 'xhigh' forever; the owner's own rollout has both, 07:06:44.566Z vs 07:06:46.484Z). A repeat fingerprint on a turn_context is a REFRESH of one turn, never a second turn: `mergeCodexRecords` keeps the FIRST copy's position and folds the better copy's VALUES onto it — codex's own always wins (`isWrapperTurnContext` = `payload.wrapper === true` or the legacy `modelPinned !== undefined` marker), a later wrapper copy overrides only an earlier wrapper copy (that is the wrapper's own late correction), and codex's copy deletes `effort_next` (nothing is pending in a rebuilt history). The fold never mutates a shared parsed payload — `kept` is sortRecords' own shallow copy and gets a NEW payload object. `chatStatus()` gained `effortNext` alongside `effort`, filled from the wrapper sidecar, `turn_context`, `wrapper_meta` and `thread_settings_applied` in that ranking. **SETTINGS TWINS fold the same way (r2 review):** our `thread_settings_applied` and codex's differ in key set, so the fingerprint can never see them as twins — `settingsSignature()` keys on the VALUES both spellings carry (model / approval_policy / reasoning_effort / personality) and the fold is scoped to ADJACENCY among settings records within one turn, because `high → ultra → high` is a real sequence and folding the third into the first would leave a rebuilt status reporting `ultra`; codex's unmarked copy replaces our `wrapper: true` one in place, and `lastSettings` only ever points at a record that really made it into `merged`. Gate: scripts/test-codex-effort-meta.mjs ⑤⑥⑥b. **THE ID-KEYED USER TWIN (2026-09-07, mergeCodexRecords):** one user message reaches a rebuild TWICE — the wrapper's buffer copy (written as the text passed through it, carrying `webui_msg_id`) and codex's rollout copy (written when the app-server COMMITS it into a turn, carrying its own `msg_…` id and nothing of ours). `recordFingerprint` keys the first by ID and the second by CONTENT, so they never dedupped: measured on the owner's real session, all three typed messages rendered TWICE after a reload, and the queued path can even put the two copies in DIFFERENT turns (ours at send time, codex's in the turn that drained it), where a turn-scoped content key would not have collided either. Keying user records on content is wrong on BOTH sides (round 2, 2026-09-07 — two different sends of the same text are two messages): the round-1 content key for an inherited bubble deleted the second of two same-text steers on reload, and the pre-existing content key for CODEX's own records deleted **27 of the 95** user messages in the owner's own rollout and **133 across the local corpus** of 89 rollouts (every collision a different `id` AND a different `create_time` — distinct submissions, zero genuine duplicates). So EVERY user record is keyed by the id its producer minted: ours by `userRecordIdentity` (webui_msg_id / client_msg_id / **webui_queue_id** — the cid a previous wrapper minted, one namespace because it names the same submission), codex's by `codexRecordIdentity` (its `msg_…`, own namespace). Pre-0.15x records carry no id (144/572 in the corpus, all in files ≤2026-05, none mixed) and fall back to a CONTENT key — but ours and codex's stay in SEPARATE namespaces there too (`user#ours:` / `user#codex:`), so `seen` can only ever collapse one producer's own record; that is what makes "a record dropped as a duplicate has already had its twin effect applied" true. The ours↔codex pair is retired by the **CLAIM** (`userTwinKeys`, turn-independent): every copy of OURS claims its content key and the next codex-side copy consumes it and is dropped — n copies in, n bubbles out, in order, with OUR copy surviving so the queue chip's `webuiMsgId` join and the live message id are preserved. **FORWARD ONLY, never a pre-pass:** letting a late claim swallow an EARLIER codex record would DELETE an old message from history the moment its text was typed again after the buffer had rotated away (a duplicate is a nuisance, a deletion is data loss). The producers of ours that write AFTER the commit — the app-server's drained `item/completed` twin and the IDLE peer path — cannot be retired by a forward claim, so they say so with `webui_after_commit` (`twin.late`) and YIELD to an unconsumed codex copy of the same content **in the same turn** (dropping themselves, never deleting anything); an early copy never yields, so a same-text neighbour cannot swallow it. One more asymmetry, deliberate: a record short-circuited by `seen` retires the claim it stands for ONLY when its identity was inferred from content (a pre-0.15x record) — there a "duplicate" is a guess, and an unretired claim would later delete an unrelated message. `userRecordIdentity` / `codexRecordIdentity` / `userContentKey` are the ONE spelling of each question, shared by the fingerprint and the twin rule. **ROUND 3 — ONE SPELLING, AND A CLAIM ONLY WHEN THERE WILL BE A TWIN.** (a) `userContentKey` now normalises each content block before hashing: an `input_image` is compared on `{type, image_url}` alone, because codex stamps its own `detail` on the block it persists (34 of the 40 image blocks in the local corpus, `input_text` always exactly `{type,text}`) — the record-level `TWIN_VOLATILE_FIELDS` rule applied inside the array. The other half of that pair is upstream: THREE producers write our copy (the server preview `CodexAdapter._buildUserPreview`, the wrapper's chat-input record, the inherited-queue bubble) and two of them hand-rolled `[...attachments, text]`, reversing codex's own order (0 of 5489 corpus user records begin with an image) — they all spell it `userInputToContent(encodeUserInput(…))` now, and the PREVIEW is the one that matters most because it carries the same `webui_msg_id` and is written first, so it wins the fingerprint and its key is the one that claims. (b) `userTwinKeys` returns `claims`, TRUE for every copy of ours (round 4 removed the write-time exemption): a submission answered by the wrapper itself (its slash commands) never becomes a user message, so a claim under it can never be consumed and would later DELETE an unrelated codex-only record of the same text (round-2 finding ④'s second back door) — but a MARKER could not say so, because the copy that claims is the server's PREVIEW (written first, and only the wrapper knows its own command list; the r3 marker was inert in production and its test scaffold simply had no preview). `webui_no_commit` survives on WEBUI_USER_MARKERS as a compat strip only, so an r3-era wrapper's buffer still keys like the same message (dtach outlives an update). Every case now speaks out of line: `mergeCodexRecords` keeps `oursByIdentity` (submission id → the content key that claimed under it) and a `webui_user_retracted` event (`retractionIdOf`) withdraws exactly one claim by IDENTITY — never by content, because the text can be a megabyte data URL and a second copy of this file's key algorithm inside the wrapper would drift. Clamped at zero, idempotent under `seen`, a no-op for an unknown id; the RECORD is never dropped, only its claim. (c) The queued PEER copy now carries its app-server cid as `webui_queue_id`, so it has an identity to be retracted by — and two peer messages with the same text in one turn stop colliding on the content key. S9: opencode.js's store gains discover/createReader/forkSession/forkChain over src/opencode-serve.js (next entry) — locate stays null (no file); ws-create mints the serve fork BEFORE the spawn (data.resumeId = the forked id, refused loudly on failure); capsOf('opencode').fork is a runtime verdict (setVerifiedCap from the serve OpenAPI), merged into the client's BACKEND_META from /api/home harnesses[].caps and the 'harness-caps-updated' broadcast.

  data/bin/acp-wrapper.js — the generic ACP v1 CLIENT under dtach (S8): argv `<buf> <sidecar> <cmd> [args…]`; JSON-RPC 2.0 over the child's stdio; `initialize` advertises protocolVersion 1 and NO fs/terminal capabilities (fs/*, terminal/*, unknown agent requests get a JSON-RPC -32601 error — never a hang, never a crash); session/load when the agent adverts loadSession, session/resume when sessionCapabilities.resume, else a LOUD 'no-load' notice + new session; prompts queue locally while one runs (ACP has no queue verb); stdin verbs chat-input/interrupt(session/cancel — but ONLY for a turn already DISPATCHED — + every pending permission answered cancelled + the local queue ALWAYS dropped — the count in the 'queue-cleared' notice is what the USER queued (an internal bookkeeping nudge is not a message anyone can "send again"; the log line keeps the true total), a dropped peer message re-emitted as peer_result ok:false so the ladder re-stashes it, and a dropped NUDGE entry resets `nudgeTurnActive` because per-turn state must die with the turn it belongs to (only that nudge's own endPrompt clears the latch, which a queue entry that never runs never reaches))/permission-response(explicit optionId, else the option whose kind matches approved/alwaysAllow; a rejection with NO reject-kind option FAILS CLOSED to {outcome:'cancelled'} + a 'deny-cancelled' notice — never an allow option)/set-model/set-effort/set-mode|set-permission-mode (set_config_option by category, session/set_mode fallback; unsupported/rejected = loud notices)/peer-message ('Message from <name>:' prefix + peer_result honesty)/_frame_file/_stdin_ack. RECORD SHAPE (one JSON line per event, stdout + buffer file): {ts,type:'acp',kind} with kinds session/config/user/prompt_start/prompt_end/update(raw SessionUpdate, replay:true during load)/permission_request/permission_resolved/client_request/notification/notice/peer_result — documented in the file head; the buffer IS the history (no transcript API in ACP). Sidecar meta = caps {frameFile:true, peerMessage:false}, streaming, sessionId, model/agentMode/configOptions/modes/availableCommands/todos/usage/pendingRequests/acp{agentInfo,capabilities}. Program-use law: never a vendor call, never a secret. TURN INVARIANTS (review batch after 2.369.31, kb-bugfix-invariants "ACP WRAPPER: STOP DIDN'T STOP…"): the turn is claimed synchronously but `fetchContextPrefix()` is a real HTTP round trip, so `runPrompt` re-checks its OWN handle's `cancelled` after the await and a cancelled prompt ends as prompt_end 'cancelled' WITHOUT dispatching (a lone prompt_end is legal — prompt_start is a no-op in the normalizer and the consumer only toggles streaming); the boot-failure notice fires on ANY exit before the session is ready, exit code 0 included (finalizeExit exits synchronously, so boot()'s own catch never runs from the child-exit path — the gate on code!==0 left a blank window). Gate: scripts/test-acp-harness.mjs (107) — cancel-during-await, queued+interrupt, Stop-drops-the-queued-nudge, Stop-inside-the-stop-check-round-trip, deny-with-no-reject-option, exit-0-before-initialize, each with a positive control. **THE BOOKKEEPING NUDGE IS OURS, NOT THE USER'S (B-7638, three residuals):** ① a Stop landing INSIDE the `/api/agent/stop-check` round trip must not be followed by a nudge turn — the answer lands after the interrupt (activePrompt is already null), so the old code walked into runPrompt and BILLED a turn the user had just stopped, with a reminder appearing out of nowhere as the only trace; the interrupt bumps `stopEpoch` and aborts the in-flight request, and an answer whose epoch is gone is discarded with a logged reason (the latch is never set, so the NEXT completed turn nudges normally). ② when it queues behind a running turn it queues with `silentQueue` — a `queued_input` for an internal reminder painted a "Queued" chip on a bubble that does not exist (peer messages already ride silentQueue); it still appears in `queue_changed` as kind 'system', so the strip stays honest about what is waiting. ③ the 'queue-cleared' notice counts USER entries only — a dropped PEER message is not the user's to re-send either (it goes back to the delivery ladder and arrives on its own), so nudges AND peers are subtracted; the log line keeps the true total (`dropped=N (nudges=M, peers=P)`). **THE INPUT QUEUE (2026-09-06):** ACP v1 has no queue verb, so `promptQueue` IS the queue — and therefore the wrapper is its PUBLISHER: `queue_changed {items:[{id,msgId,preview,ts,kind,from}], turn_id}` on every push/drain/drop/remove (plus an empty baseline right after the session is up), `queued_input {msg_id, turn_id}` where the old "Queued — runs after the current turn" NOTICE used to be (the state is now a chip on the bubble; codex parity, one client path), and the `queue-op` stdin verb for `remove` only. A steer that somehow reaches this wrapper answers `queue_op_result {ok:false, reason:'not-steerable'}` — `backend-caps.inputModes.steer=false` stops it at the ws layer and `AcpAdapter.formatQueueOp` refuses it with a reason, so this arm exists purely so nothing is ever dropped in silence. `remove` obeys the same two rules Stop already obeyed: a removed PEER entry goes back to the delivery ladder (`peer_result ok:false`, the consumer re-stashes), and a removed NUDGE entry clears `nudgeTurnActive` (per-turn state dies with the turn it belongs to). Removing an id that already drained answers `reason:'gone'` — never a fake success. **Stop reports its drop as removals**: each dropped entry gets its own `queue_op_result {op:'remove', ok:true, msg_id, reason:'stopped'}` BEFORE the emptied republish — the normalizer clears the chip of anything that leaves the queue with no result (= "it ran"), so a bare empty `queue_changed` told the user their message had RUN when Stop had thrown it away (round-1 review). Order is the fix; the result stamps `Removed` first and the republish then leaves those bubbles alone. **REORDER + EDIT (2026-09-07):** `promptQueue` is a local array, so the verb table adds `reorder` and `edit` as splices — driven by the SAME relative frame the codex wrapper translates (`afterId:null` = the front; an anchor that has left puts the entry BACK where it was and answers `reason:'anchor-gone'`), with the same exclusion rule for edit (the first text block takes the new words, every other content block stays in place) and the same two refusals: a PEER entry is not yours to rewrite and a NUDGE entry is VibeSpace's own bookkeeping (`reason:'not-editable'`), an empty edit is `reason:'empty-text'`. `run-now`/`run-all` are declared FALSE for a structural reason, not an unfinished one: this queue only HAS entries while a prompt is running (an idle wrapper dispatches immediately), so "run it now" could only ever answer busy — a frame that arrives anyway says exactly that (`reason:'not-startable'`). Entries carry their full `text` (user entries only) so the client's edit control opens the message rather than the preview, and `ACP_QUEUE_VERBS` is advertised in the sidecar AND on every `queue_changed`. Pins: test-acp-harness (reorder/edit/anchor-gone/empty-edit/not-startable + the advert parity with the caps row; negative control: an anchor-ignoring reorder fails the leg).
  src/acp-message-manager.js — AcpMessageManager (S8 normalizer, same duck contract as MessageManager/CodexMessageManager) + AcpSessionMessages (store reader over the buffer: raw/tail/slice/wrapperMeta/chatStatus/taskState/isStreaming). Mapping: tool_call kind read|search→Read/Search (fold read), edit|delete|move→write fold, execute→Bash, think→thinking, fetch|other→mcp, switch_mode visible; tool_call_update edits the card (diff content → Edit-style old/new input); agent_message_chunk/agent_thought_chunk stream per messageId (a kind switch closes the other stream so order is kept); user_message_chunk = load replay (the live echo of the wrapper's own `user` record is deduped by text); plan → todos meta; available_commands_update → slash commands on the init card **AND the same `meta`/`slash-commands` op the claude normalizer emits (one client path; an `edit` on a COMPLETE system card is never re-rendered, so patching the card alone never reaches the composer). That op is emitted UNCONDITIONALLY — it does not wait for the init card, because the agent may send the command list BEFORE the `session` record (the mock's session/new result resolves an async continuation while the notification is journaled synchronously off the same stdout chunk, so `[update, session]` is an ordinary interleaving; measured 1 failure / 16 runs of test-acp-harness against a leg that asserted `ops[0].op === 'create'`). The leg now looks the init card UP by shape and a deterministic property leg feeds BOTH orders, with the index form kept as the negative control**; usage_update → context %; permission_request → the harness-neutral card {options:[{optionId,name,kind}], kind:'approval'} on the tool card, resolved by permission_resolved OR the ws-handler's echoed permission-response frame; prompt_end cancelled → Interrupted, error → error card, refusal/max_* → notice. The init card is a LIVE artefact (history rebuild makes none; attach status carries the facts). Ids are content-derived (stable across rebuilds). **UNKNOWN UPDATE KINDS ARE NAMED, NOT DROPPED (2.369.58):** `_processUpdate`'s `default:` used to `return;` — the invisible-record class twice convicted elsewhere (claude's `cli-unknown-system-subtype`, codex's `codex-unknown-record`). It now calls `_noteUnknownUpdate(kind)`: telemetry `acp-unknown-update:<kind>` ONCE per process per kind (`AcpMessageManager._seenUnknownUpdates`) plus one console line, name only — an update body can carry user content. The installed opencode's zod SessionNotification union has ELEVEN variants and the switch now covers all of them; the eleventh, `session_info_update {title, updatedAt}`, is handled as a KNOWN, deliberately card-less kind (session METADATA, not conversation content — VibeSpace's session name is the user's, so an agent-driven rename is a product decision, not a normalizer one; the title is kept on `_status.agentSessionTitle`). Gate: scripts/test-harness-honesty.mjs.
  src/opencode-serve.js — OpenCode serve-mode STORE FACTS (S9 of design-harness-plugins §2.4, B-03f2; SHARED — facts about a machine). WHY: the ACP harness has no transcript files (OpenCode keeps sessions in ~/.local/share/opencode/opencode.db), so stopped OpenCode conversations never reached the sidebar. `opencode serve` (verified 1.18.29, OpenAPI at /doc) exposes that store on 127.0.0.1. THREE PIECES: (1) OpencodeServeClient — fetch over a base URL, 1.5s default timeout, every failure an OpencodeServeError {status, code: timeout|network|http|NotFoundError…}; listAllSessions = /project × `/session?scope=project&directory=<worktree>` deduped (VERIFIED 1.18.29 on the real store: a bare `directory=` is an EXACT-directory filter — `directory=/` lists NOTHING, which the first live run caught as "0 sessions"; `scope=project` lists the whole project; a serve's bare /session is scoped to ITS cwd's project, so the iteration is the safe shape); v1 `/session/:id/message?limit=` returns the NEWEST N, so the first user message comes from v2 `/api/session/:id/message?limit=3&order=asc`; fork = POST /session/:id/fork?directory= (body {messageID?}) → the new Session (title "<parent> (fork #n)", no parentID — parentID means a SUB-AGENT child). Basic auth only when OPENCODE_SERVER_PASSWORD is in the spawn env. (2) createServeLocator — ONE instance per VibeSpace: reuse data/opencode-serve.json {port,pid,startedAt,command,cwd} when /global/health answers AND its own project is safe (a previous server's child that outlived a SIGKILL restart; never killed by stop() — only our own child is), else spawn `<cmd> serve --port <free> --hostname 127.0.0.1 --log-level WARN` detached + stdio ignored under agentEnv() **from its own empty data/opencode-serve/cwd** (2.369.50 — see WHERE IT RUNS below), wait ≤20s for health (1.2s locally), write the record atomically, probe /doc ONCE → onCaps({fork}) (the CLI version comes from /global/health — /doc's info.version is the API doc's own "1.0.0") — the ONLY writer of capsOf('opencode').fork (setVerifiedCap in backend-caps, PURE in-place row mutation) which cli-env then BROADCASTS as 'harness-caps-updated' (open clients learned fork:false from /api/home BEFORE the lazy start — the notify law). Keeper: exit → respawn after 1s·2^n (≤30s), crash counter resets after 60s of uptime, PARKED after 5 crashes (loud in user actions, silent in the poll), stop() on process exit (stopOnExit) — a SIGKILLed server leaves the serve running for the next boot to reuse. Started LAZILY by the first discovery, never at boot, only when cli-env resolved the CLI AND autostart is on: `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0` disables it explicitly, and the test-harness belt `VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS=1` disables it implicitly (every worktree smoke sets it and SIGKILLs its server — a spawned serve outlives that kill and would pile up per `npm run ci`; `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=1` wins over the belt); with autostart off a RECORDED live instance is still reused, otherwise discover is [] and user actions name the switch. **WHERE IT RUNS AND WHAT IT MAY TOUCH (2.369.50, the 2.369.42 runaway — full incident in kb-bugfix-invariants):** OpenCode boots an INSTANCE per DIRECTORY and each instance recursively indexes + inotify-watches that tree (`fff-*` fast-file-finder + `notify-rs` threads). Measured with /proc against a real 1.18.29 serve: the v1 routes (`/session`, `/session/:id`, `/session/:id/message`, `/project`) boot NOTHING; **every** `/api/session/{id}/…` v2 route boots an instance for that session's `location.directory` (+19 indexer threads, +180 MB, a full-tree watch). So: (a) the serve runs from `data/opencode-serve/cwd`, an empty directory made into a throwaway git repo by `ensureServeCwd` — OpenCode's project resolution walks UP for a git worktree, so a plain directory inside the checkout resolves the WHOLE VibeSpace checkout and a FAKE `.git` does not stop the walk, while with no repo above it the project is the `'/'` catch-all (a README next to it says why; `git init` failing only degrades the isolation, loudly). Accepted side effect: while our serve runs it pins the store's mutable `'global'` project row worktree to that empty dir — the field is last-writer-wins and every `opencode` invocation rewrites it, sessions carry their own `directory`, and our listing is worktree-independent. (b) NAMING uses v1 only, byte-capped. (c) `listAllSessions` takes the DIRECTORY-LESS listing first and only issues `scope=project&directory=` for worktrees passing `bootstrappableWorktree` (never `'/'`, `$HOME`, `os.tmpdir()`, nor the vcs-less `'global'` row — a real project row carries `vcs`, the API's own signal, so no fs call is needed); skipped worktrees surface in `facts.state().skippedWorktrees`. (d) `forkSession` — the one call handing a directory to a mutating endpoint — calls `POST /instance/dispose?directory=` afterwards (best effort; the forked session stays readable). **RUNAWAY GUARD:** the child's own `/proc` (`readProcUsage`) is sampled every 60s; >150% CPU sustained ≥5 min, or RSS >2 GB on a single sample, STOPS it, parks the locator `parkedKind:'runaway'` with the numbers in `lastError`, `console.error`s what to turn off, fires telemetry `opencode-serve-runaway`, pushes `harness-store-updated` and refuses to respawn for an hour (then exactly one retry). An unreadable /proc never parks anything; the locator never signals `process.pid`. **BOOT SELF-HEAL:** a recorded serve whose `GET /project/current` worktree is `'/'` or `$HOME` (a 2.369.42 leftover — its record also has no `cwd`) is SIGTERMed and replaced from the isolated cwd; a record written by this code (matching `cwd`) skips the probe, and a FAILED probe never churns a healthy instance. **AUTOSTART** is `agents.opencodeServeAutostart` (default ON, live-read through a function): `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0/1` wins, `VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS=1` forces off, else the setting.  (3) createFacts — the store members: discover({activeSessions}) = 10s list cache → 10s negative cache (returns the LAST GOOD list, [] before any) → refresh bounded by the 1.5s budget (client({budgetMs}) returns null while the serve boots; a HUNG serve costs ≤1.5s, pinned <2s) — NEVER throws, NEVER stalls the 5s /api/sessions poll; entries {backend:'opencode', backendSessionId, sessionId, sessionKey, cwd=directory, startedAt=time.updated, status live|stopped (live = an activeSessions opencode session holds the id; 'external' is undetectable — another process driving the same sqlite leaves no signal), name, agentKind primary|subagent (parentID), parentThreadId, webuiId/Name/Mode, opencode:{slug,agent,model,projectID}}; names = discovery-facts nameFromText over the FIRST user message read from the **v1** `/session/:id/message` list (the WHOLE conversation oldest-first — v1 `limit` is the NEWEST N so it cannot page from the front), capped at NAME_MAX_BYTES=1MiB by the client's streaming `maxBytes` (over the cap → code `too-large` → the title fallback; six unbounded whole-conversation fetches into this process is an OOM). THE v2 asc endpoint IS SMALLER AND MUST NEVER BE USED HERE — it boots an instance (2.369.42). ≤6 lookups per tick (progressive, no burst), cached per id, retried every 60s while a session has no user message, fallback = OpenCode's title unless it is the "New session - <date>" placeholder; readConversation(id) (8s, LOUD: not installed / parked / unreachable / 404 all name the cause) and forkSession(id,{cwd}) (refused with code unsupported when the OpenAPI lacked the fork path); **sessionModel(id) (B-6b6d round 2)** = the model this conversation is on, as `provider/model`, for the resume-continuity ladder — the opencode descriptor's `store.lastTurnModel`, so an OpenCode resume keeps its own model instead of taking `opencode.defaultModel` (a NEW-session default). It shares `discover`'s cache through the one `listNow(budgetMs)` helper, so it is v1-only, bounded and adds NO route the sidebar poll does not already use; `''` = we could not read it (the service is opt-in and default OFF, or parked/unreachable/gone) and the ladder then takes the instance default and LOGS which rung it used. RECORD SYNTHESIS messagesToAcpRecords(messages, session) → 'acp-events' so the unchanged AcpMessageManager renders a stopped conversation read-only: session{how:'serve', model '<providerID>/<modelID>', mode=agent} · user text parts (non-synthetic first, else synthetic; `ignored` dropped) + "[file: name]" lines → {kind:'user', msgId} · assistant text → agent_message_chunk (messageId = part id, one card per part) · reasoning → agent_thought_chunk · tool → tool_call (kind from the tool id: read→read, glob|grep|list|ls→search, edit|write|patch|multiedit|apply_patch→edit, bash|shell→execute, webfetch|websearch|codesearch→fetch, todowrite|todoread|plan→think, else other; rawInput {tool,…input}, locations from filePath|path|file) + tool_call_update (completed|failed, output/error text, edit oldString/newString as a diff block; running/pending = in_progress with no update) · subtask → completed tool_call "Task: <description>" · compaction/retry → notice · a modelID/mode change between assistant messages → config · assistant end → prompt_end (MessageAbortedError→cancelled, other error→error, completed→end_turn; an open assistant message gets none) · step-start/step-finish/snapshot/patch/agent skipped. OpencodeServeSessionMessages extends AcpSessionMessages: live (the activeSessions membership fact from createSessionMessages, or a non-empty buffer) = the wrapper journal as before — a just-spawned live session with an empty journal is NOT read from the serve (the wrapper's session/load replay would double); stopped = records on prepare() (transcript-service awaits it at its three reader sites; a caller that does not gets an empty reader, never a wrong one). The installed singleton (install() by cli-env; facts() = NULL_FACTS when unwired: discover [] silently, user actions say "not configured"). NOT wired (seen in the 1.18.29 OpenAPI): POST /session/{sessionID}/revert, GET /question + POST /question/{requestID}/reply, /pty*, SSE /event + /global/event (could replace the 10s list poll), /session/{sessionID}/todo. Gate: scripts/test-opencode-serve.mjs (131 — mock serve in scripts/dev/mock-opencode-serve.mjs, which MODELS the instance boot: any v2 per-session route adds `state.instances`, so 'a whole discovery tick bootstraps ZERO instances' is a real assert, plus a spawn-cwd spy, the '/'-worktree boot self-heal, the runaway guard driven by a fake /proc reader + fake clock, dispose-after-fork and the byte cap); manual: `node scripts/dev/opencode-serve-live.mjs --measure [seconds]` runs the REAL loop against the REAL store and prints CPU/RSS/threads/indexer-threads/inotify-watches (healthy = single-digit CPU%, flat RSS, 0 indexer threads, 0 watches).
  src/acp-message-manager.js — AcpMessageManager (S8 normalizer, same duck contract as MessageManager/CodexMessageManager) + AcpSessionMessages (store reader over the buffer: raw/tail/slice/wrapperMeta/chatStatus/taskState/isStreaming). Mapping: tool_call kind read|search→Read/Search (fold read), edit|delete|move→write fold, execute→Bash, think→thinking, fetch|other→mcp, switch_mode visible; tool_call_update edits the card (diff content → Edit-style old/new input); agent_message_chunk/agent_thought_chunk stream per messageId (a kind switch closes the other stream so order is kept); user_message_chunk = load replay (the live echo of the wrapper's own `user` record is deduped by text); plan → todos meta; available_commands_update → slash commands on the init card; usage_update → context %; permission_request → the harness-neutral card {options:[{optionId,name,kind}], kind:'approval'} on the tool card, resolved by permission_resolved OR the ws-handler's echoed permission-response frame; prompt_end cancelled → Interrupted, error → error card, refusal/max_* → notice. The init card is a LIVE artefact (history rebuild makes none; attach status carries the facts). Ids are content-derived (stable across rebuilds).

Started LAZILY by the first discovery, never at boot, only when cli-env resolved the CLI AND autostart is on — and **autostart is DEFAULT OFF since 2026-09-07** (owner decision): the switch is the built-in `opencode-serve` PLUGIN (src/plugins.js), so nothing starts a third-party background daemon on a fresh instance. With the PLUGIN off a RECORDED live instance is still reused (adopting costs nothing and the panel keeps a Stop button for it), otherwise discover is [] and user actions name the plugin — **but the OPS kill switch is authoritative over ADOPTION too (2026-09-07 follow-up)**: the reuse rung runs BEFORE the autostart gate, so `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0` used to stop us STARTING a serve while happily adopting the one that outlived the last restart — a third-party daemon indexing under an instance whose panel disables every control BECAUSE it is forced off. Under the switch a recorded serve is SIGTERMed + its record cleared (the same replace path an unsafe worktree uses), and `install()` runs that ladder ONCE at boot (setImmediate, unref) so an instance nobody polls does not inherit one either. Also: the isolated cwd is only materialised when it can be USED (a record to compare, or a spawn we are allowed to make) — a service nobody turned on creates nothing, not even the throwaway git repo. Also exported here: `start()` (an explicit user start CLEARS a previous `stop()` and any crash/runaway park — a user asking for it IS the deliberate retry) and `stop({killRecorded})` (the process-exit path leaves an ADOPTED serve for the next boot; the plugin's disable passes killRecorded so "off" means the process is gone). **WHERE IT RUNS AND WHAT IT MAY TOUCH (2.369.50, the 2.369.42 runaway — full incident in kb-bugfix-invariants):** OpenCode boots an INSTANCE per DIRECTORY and each instance recursively indexes + inotify-watches that tree (`fff-*` fast-file-finder + `notify-rs` threads). (b) NAMING uses v1 only, byte-capped. (c) `listAllSessions` takes the DIRECTORY-LESS listing first and only issues `scope=project&directory=` for worktrees passing `bootstrappableWorktree` (never `'/'`, `$HOME`, `os.tmpdir()`, nor the vcs-less `'global'` row — a real project row carries `vcs`, the API's own signal, so no fs call is needed); skipped worktrees surface in `facts.state().skippedWorktrees`. (d) `forkSession` — the one call handing a directory to a mutating endpoint — calls `POST /instance/dispose?directory=` afterwards (best effort; the forked session stays readable). **RUNAWAY GUARD:** the child's own `/proc` (`readProcUsage`) is sampled every 60s; >150% CPU sustained ≥5 min, or RSS >2 GB on a single sample, STOPS it, parks the locator `parkedKind:'runaway'` with the numbers in `lastError`, `console.error`s what to turn off, fires telemetry `opencode-serve-runaway`, pushes `harness-store-updated` and refuses to respawn for an hour (then exactly one retry). An unreadable /proc never parks anything; the locator never signals `process.pid`. **BOOT SELF-HEAL:** a recorded serve whose `GET /project/current` worktree is `'/'` or `$HOME` (a 2.369.42 leftover — its record also has no `cwd`) is SIGTERMed and replaced from the isolated cwd; a record written by this code (matching `cwd`) skips the probe, and a FAILED probe never churns a healthy instance. **AUTOSTART (2026-09-07 — the setting `agents.opencodeServeAutostart` and the `VIBESPACE_SKIP_AGENT_HOOKS` smoke belt are BOTH GONE):** the decision is ONE pure function here, `decideAutostart({env, pluginWantsUp})` — the ops override `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0/1` wins (`serveEnvOverride`, the single parse of that var, also read by the plugin panel so it can show "forced by the environment"), else the `opencode-serve` plugin record (`enabled && desiredUp`); DEFAULT OFF. cli-env passes it as a FUNCTION over `plugins.wantsServiceUp(SERVICE_PLUGIN_ID)` so enabling takes effect without a restart, and the descriptor names its control plugin as `store.servicePlugin` so `/api/home` can carry `plugins.serviceState()` on the harness row (the client's first-use prompt and the sidebar's hidden-history row gate on THAT, never on a backend id). With default OFF a fresh smoke instance starts nothing, which is why the belt could be deleted. **A DELIBERATELY-OFF SERVICE IS NOT A BROKEN STORE (2026-09-07 follow-up):** `/api/home harnesses[].storeReason` is now the SAME predicate as the live `harness-store-updated` push — `storeFailureReason()` in src/server/cli-env.js, exported for the gate — and it fires ONLY on a PARK (crash loop / runaway). The old half also fired on "not ready and the keeper's autostart flag is off", which was the ops kill switch alone while autostart defaulted ON; with the plugin default OFF it became the normal shipped state, and app.js turns every reason into a RED ERROR TOAST ⇒ one per page load, on a fresh instance, after "Not now", and on instances that never had OpenCode. Off-ness travels on `row.service` instead and is shown passively (sidebar row + ⚙ card); the sidebar row also renders for the ops-forced case now, with no Enable button, since nothing the user clicks there could work.  (3) createFacts — the store members: discover({activeSessions}) = 10s list cache → 10s negative cache (returns the LAST GOOD list, [] before any) → refresh bounded by the 1.5s budget (client({budgetMs}) returns null while the serve boots; a HUNG serve costs ≤1.5s, pinned <2s) — NEVER throws, NEVER stalls the 5s /api/sessions poll; entries {backend:'opencode', backendSessionId, sessionId, sessionKey, cwd=directory, startedAt=time.updated, status live|external|stopped (see THE S9 REMAINDER below — 'external' became DETECTABLE, with positive evidence only), name, agentKind primary|subagent (parentID), parentThreadId, webuiId/Name/Mode, opencode:{slug,agent,model,projectID}}; names = discovery-facts nameFromText over the FIRST user message read from the **v1** `/session/:id/message` list (the WHOLE conversation oldest-first — v1 `limit` is the NEWEST N so it cannot page from the front), capped at NAME_MAX_BYTES=1MiB by the client's streaming `maxBytes` (over the cap → code `too-large` → the title fallback; six unbounded whole-conversation fetches into this process is an OOM). THE v2 asc endpoint IS SMALLER AND MUST NEVER BE USED HERE — it boots an instance (2.369.42). ≤6 lookups per tick (progressive, no burst), cached per id, retried every 60s while a session has no user message, fallback = OpenCode's title unless it is the "New session - <date>" placeholder; readConversation(id) (8s, LOUD: not installed / parked / unreachable / 404 all name the cause) and forkSession(id,{cwd}) (refused with code unsupported when the OpenAPI lacked the fork path). RECORD SYNTHESIS messagesToAcpRecords(messages, session) → 'acp-events' so the unchanged AcpMessageManager renders a stopped conversation read-only: session{how:'serve', model '<providerID>/<modelID>', mode=agent} · user text parts (non-synthetic first, else synthetic; `ignored` dropped) + "[file: name]" lines → {kind:'user', msgId} · assistant text → agent_message_chunk (messageId = part id, one card per part) · reasoning → agent_thought_chunk · tool → tool_call (kind from the tool id: read→read, glob|grep|list|ls→search, edit|write|patch|multiedit|apply_patch→edit, bash|shell→execute, webfetch|websearch|codesearch→fetch, todowrite|todoread|plan→think, else other; rawInput {tool,…input}, locations from filePath|path|file) + tool_call_update (completed|failed, output/error text, edit oldString/newString as a diff block; running/pending = in_progress with no update) · subtask → completed tool_call "Task: <description>" · compaction/retry → notice · a modelID/mode change between assistant messages → config · assistant end → prompt_end (MessageAbortedError→cancelled, other error→error, completed→end_turn; an open assistant message gets none) · step-start/step-finish/snapshot/patch/agent skipped. OpencodeServeSessionMessages extends AcpSessionMessages: live (the activeSessions membership fact from createSessionMessages, or a non-empty buffer) = the wrapper journal as before — a just-spawned live session with an empty journal is NOT read from the serve (the wrapper's session/load replay would double); stopped = records on prepare() (transcript-service awaits it at its three reader sites; a caller that does not gets an empty reader, never a wrong one). The installed singleton (install() by cli-env; facts() = NULL_FACTS when unwired: discover [] silently, user actions say "not configured"). **THE S9 REMAINDER (B-eac2, 2026-09-07) — every one of those "NOT wired" endpoints, measured on a real 1.18.29 serve before it was wired:** **ROUND 4 (the third adversarial pass, all three reproduced first):** ①**THE PTY FAMILY HANDS THE SERVE NO `directory`** — it was the one NEW path that took a directory from the USER, and `?directory=X` is exactly what BOOTS the instance for X while NOTHING in the pty family releases it (only `POST /instance/dispose` does, which is why `forkSession` calls it). Same-origin A/B, fresh serve per arm, target = a 200-dir/4046-file repo, /proc `Threads` + `inotify wd` over `/proc/<serve>/fdinfo/*`: WITH the query base[16,0] → open [44,204] → close [41,204] → open/close ×2,×3 still [41,204] (never freed, so N terminals on a node_modules-bearing repo reproduce the 30 021-watch shape of 2.369.42 and the only backstop is the runaway guard, whose remedy is to SIGTERM the whole serve and park it for an hour); WITHOUT it [16,0] → [45,4] → [42,4] and `readlink /proc/<shell>/cwd` is the SAME target in BOTH arms — the query buys the SHELL nothing, the `cwd` BODY field is what places it. So ptyList/ptyCreate/ptyGet/ptyResize/ptyRemove/ptyTicket/ptyConnectUrl all drop it and land on the DEFAULT instance (our own empty throwaway repo, the 4 watches). **CHANGE THEM TOGETHER OR NOT AT ALL**: the pty REGISTRY is per instance — measured, `PUT /pty/{id}?directory=X` on a pty created without the query answers 404 PtyNotFoundError and vice versa — so a half-migrated family is a terminal that opens and can never be resized, closed or reaped; the mock now models both facts (instance booted by the QUERY only, per-instance registry incl. the ws upgrade) and the gate carries the real-serve /proc delta with the old shape as the control. ②**`reapPtys()`, because a serve pty OUTLIVES us.** `session._opencodePtyId` was written in ws-create and read NOWHERE, and `ptyList()` was never called: a serve pty is deliberately not dtach-restorable (`socketPath` is null), so a SIGKILL/OOM restart — or ANY restart while the serve was ADOPTED from data/opencode-serve.json, where `state.child` is null and our exit hook has nothing to kill — left the shell running with nothing able to reach or kill it (verified: a pty survives our socket closing and is re-connectable; it only 404s once its own shell exits). The sweep runs on the READY EDGE of each serve PROCESS (`${pid}:${port}:${startedAt}`, idempotent — a routine notify must not re-sweep and race a terminal being opened), asks the DIRECTORY-LESS `GET /pty`, and KEEPS `live.ptys` (every pty THIS process opened — registered before the ticket call, so the open→session-register window is not a kill window) UNION `heldPtyIds()` (server.js → cli-env → facts; the field's first consumer). A failed listing does NOT mark the serve swept, so it retries on the next ready edge. **ROUND 5 (the fourth adversarial pass, both reproduced first):** ①**THE WARM `que_…` MAP OUTLIVED THE SERVE THAT MINTED IT.** `/question` is answered out of ONE serve process's own memory (real 1.18.29: a fresh process answers `[]` whatever the last one held), and `readConversation` joins an OPEN ask to the warm map, re-reading the authoritative list ONLY when that map is EMPTY — so a keeper respawn / new port / replaced adopted instance left every open ask joined to a `que_…` nobody holds: an answerable card whose Submit can only 404, the exact dead Submit `stale` exists to prevent. The map now carries the serve PROCESS it was filled under (`live.questionsFor` = the reaper's `${pid}:${port}:${startedAt}` key): a map stamped for a DIFFERENT process is not known, it is UNKNOWN — `pendingQuestionsFor` answers `[]`, so the read re-lists and the card renders `stale`; `answerQuestion`/`rejectQuestion` take their cold-map re-read; `state().pendingQuestions` stops claiming asks belonging to a dead process. AND every lane `connected` DROPS the map before re-reading it (`refreshQuestions`, beside the busy-map `statusMap()` the reconnect already paid for) — drop FIRST, so a failed read leaves unknown (⇒ stale, honest) instead of known-from-a-serve-that-is-gone. ②**THE LANE ONLY RUNS WHILE THE SERVICE DOES.** `install()` armed it unconditionally, and `lane.start()` arms an `fs.watch` on the USER's real store (resolved from the env THIS server runs under) plus an SSE reconnect loop with its own timers — so on the shipped default (plugin OFF, 2.369.59: nothing of ours runs or watches) a fresh instance held an inotify watch on `~/.local/share/opencode` and an unrelated `opencode` process's writes woke a server whose OpenCode feature was off (measured on a real boot: 1 inotify wd in `/proc/<pid>/fdinfo` while `sse.lastError` said "the background service is off"). The lane now follows ONE predicate — the SAME decision the keeper spawns on (`opts.autostart` = decideAutostart) OR a serve that is actually READY (the ADOPTED case the decision does not cover) — re-evaluated on every locator state change, so enabling the plugin brings it up with no restart and disabling tears down socket, timer and watch. Gated by a REAL boot with the plugin off (0 watch handles, no record, not even the throwaway `data/opencode-serve/cwd`) whose positive control is the same instance with the plugin turned on through the real route. **ROUND 7 (the sixth adversarial pass, both reproduced first) — THE CHECK BELONGS AT THE ACQUISITION POINT, and round 6's rule applies one layer UP.** Round 6 made the live lane's `stop()` terminal for an arm already in flight; but nothing the user clicks starts that lane — `install()` starts it from what the KEEPER publishes (`laneWanted = st.ready || wantsAutostart()`), and `locate()` tests `state.stopping` only at ENTRY. Every rung below that entry reaches `adopt()` — the ONE place `state.client` is ever assigned — after an await `stop()` cannot cancel: the reuse `/global/health` probe (a BUSY recorded serve that outlived a VibeSpace restart answers in hundreds of ms; 1.18.29 takes ~1.2s cold), `unsafeReuseReason`'s `GET /project/current`, `ensureServeCwd`'s `git init`, and the ≤20s boot wait — which is precisely the "starting…" window a user gives up in. So a Disable landing in one of them re-published `ready:true` for a service that was OFF, RE-ARMED the runaway-guard interval `stop()` had just cleared, and through `laneWanted` built a BRAND-NEW live lane whose own `laneStopped` is false by construction: the user's real OpenCode store watched again, and FIRING (`onExternal` ⇒ `opencode-updated` ⇒ a fleet-wide /api/sessions sweep) for a feature they had just turned off — with `state()` reporting `stopped:true` and `ready:true` at once, and the client pointing at a process `stop({killRecorded})` had already SIGTERMed.
