Two more windows in the same shape: a stop inside `git init` still reached the spawn (a brand-new third-party daemon started AFTER "off" — refused only by luck, because `_ocStop` writes the plugin record before calling `stop()` and `autostartOn()` is re-read; a stop that is authoritative must not depend on a SECOND fact), and the boot loop's old `return null` walked away from a child it had spawned, leaving a live `opencode serve` plus a record the NEXT boot would ADOPT. Fixes: `adopt()` refuses when `state.stopping` (the acquisition point — one guard covers every rung, present and future); `locate()` re-checks after the isolated-cwd await, after the reuse health probe and after `freePort()`; and the boot loop bails through `abandon()`, which SIGTERMs the child and clears the record only when it names that port (`why` is set only when WE decided, so a child that crashed on its own keeps `onChildExit`'s honest `lastError`). Gated by scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs's ROUND 7 legs — three windows × {no disable = the positive control that a normal boot still adopts and watches, disable = zero published client / zero lane / zero fs.watch / zero `onExternal`}, each with a NEGATIVE CONTROL built from this very file with exactly these checks removed (it must publish `ready:true` after the stop, arm a lane, leak an inotify wd and fire; its 6 replacements are asserted to match their expected counts so a stale control fails loudly). **ROUND 8 (the seventh adversarial pass, reproduced first) — A LEVEL IS NOT A CANCELLATION.** Round 7's guards all asked `state.stopping`, which `stop()` raises and the very next `start()` lowers, so they were not terminal across a **Disable→Enable pair**: `start()` cleared the flag while `ensure()` still held the in-flight `locate()`, so the Enable JOINED the cancelled ladder instead of beginning one, and that ladder then published what it had gathered BEFORE the stop. Measured through the same real wiring (`_ocStop` then `_ocStart`, Disable at T and Enable at T+50 ms inside the 700 ms reuse probe): TWO adoptions — the Enable's own ladder spawned and adopted a serve, then the stale ladder adopted `source:'reused'` on the exact port `stop({killRecorded})` had SIGTERMed and whose record it had deleted, overwriting the live one and leaving the spawned child untracked. Fix = per-attempt cancellation, the shape round 6 used for the lane: `state.stopEpoch` is bumped by `stop()` AND by every new `locate()`, which captures `const epoch = ++state.stopEpoch` and passes it to `adopt(port, pid, source, epoch)` and every post-await check (`cancelled(epoch) = state.stopping || epoch !== state.stopEpoch`) — a cancelled attempt can never become valid again and only the NEWEST attempt may take ownership; `stop()` also detaches the in-flight attempt (`ensuring = null`) so the Enable starts its OWN ladder (without it `start()` resolves the cancelled run's null — a click reported as "did nothing"), and `ensure()`'s settle handler only clears the slot it still owns. `abandon()` writes `why`/`lastError` only while `state.stopping` holds (a run cancelled by a newer ladder must not narrate over it), SIGTERMs the child in its CLOSURE, and keeps its `state.child === child` null CONDITIONAL — a no-op on every path today (`stop()` nulls that slot first; after an Enable it belongs to a newer ladder), pinned by a leg whose mock child records kills by phase. Gated by scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs's ROUND 8 legs with 2 single-mechanism controls (drop the epoch ⇒ two adoptions, the last a SIGTERMed process; drop the detach ⇒ the Enable's `start()` resolves null) plus one that removes `abandon()`'s SIGTERM.
  • **ROLL BACK / RESTORE.** Client methods `revert(id,{messageID,partID})` / `unrevert(id)` + facts `revertTo` / `unrevert`. **v1 ONLY, and that is a measurement, not a preference:** with /proc against a fresh serve, `POST /session/:id/revert` restored the working tree (hello.txt really disappeared) while the process stayed at 16 threads / 0 `fff` indexer threads, whereas ONE `POST /api/session/:id/revert/clear` took it to 37 threads / 19 indexer threads / +165 MB — the v2 stage/clear/commit trio is the instance-booting family, so it is deliberately not wired and `unrevert` IS the v1 "clear". There is NO v1 "commit"; the next prompt commits, which is what the UI says. The staged state rides the Session's own `revert` field: `messagesToAcpRecords` emits a `notice` (noticeKind `'revert'`, text from `revertNoticeText`) immediately BEFORE the message it rolled back to, so a rolled-back conversation SAYS so wherever it renders, and the discover entry carries `opencode.revert` so the chat's message-metadata popup can offer "Restore" only while one is actually staged.
  • **THE ASK CARD.** `GET /question` + `POST /question/:id/{reply,reject}` (both boot no instance). A `question` tool part becomes the harness-neutral `permission_request` with `kind:'user_input'` — the SAME card claude's AskUserQuestion uses — carrying `via:'opencode-serve'` so the ws layer answers it on the serve's route instead of a session's stdin (a stopped conversation opened read-only has no process here at all). Verified shapes on a real turn: the pending request is `{id:'que_…', sessionID, questions:[{question, header, options:[{label,description}], multiple?, custom?}], tool:{messageID, callID}}`, an answered part carries `state.metadata.answers = [["Blue"]]` (one array of labels per question, in order), and `POST /question/:id/reply {answers:[["Blue"]]}` completed the turn. `askAnswersToPositional` converts the card's question-text map back to that positional form and splits a comma-joined multi-select ONLY when every piece is a known option label (a free-text answer containing ", " survives intact). RELOAD SURVIVAL: the transcript only knows the tool CALL id, the live route wants the `que_…` id, so `readConversation` joins them (re-reading `GET /question` once when the live map is cold — a just-restarted server); an ask with no live request behind it is marked `stale` and the card renders it WITHOUT a Submit that could only fail. **REACHABILITY, measured:** `opencode acp` (the mode our live sessions run in) does not expose the `question` tool at all — the model itself listed its tools and there was none — and question events are per-PROCESS, so this lane fires for turns the SERVE drives, not for our own ACP sessions. That is recorded here rather than hidden behind a card nobody can reach. **`answerQuestion` re-reads `GET /question` when the warm map does not know the request**: the card's answers are keyed by question TEXT, so converting them to OpenCode's positional form needs the question list, and a server that restarted between rendering the card and the user pressing Submit had an empty map — it refused its own user's answer with "no answers". Positional answers need no lookup and skip it.
  • **THE SERVE TERMINAL** — see src/server/opencode-pty-bridge.js below.
  • **THE LIVE LANE** — see src/opencode-events.js below. The 10s list-cache timer is GONE while the lane is healthy: `discover` refreshes only when an event marked the cache dirty (with a 1s serial-burst floor, because five browser tabs each poll /api/sessions), and falls back to the timer STRUCTURALLY when the lane is down — a broken lane must not freeze the sidebar forever. **The dirty signal NOTIFIES** (`onChange`, wired in src/server/cli-env.js): the sidebar caches this list too, so a change nobody asked about — a TUI writing the same sqlite — is pushed as `opencode-updated {kind:'store'}` instead of waiting for that browser's own 5s poll. Coalesced at 2s and with streamed message parts excluded, because each push costs EVERY open client one /api/sessions discovery sweep and our own opencode turns write that sqlite too. **A (re)connect also PRIMES the busy map** from `/session/status`: `session.status` frames are its only other source, so a serve that was already running a turn when the stream came up read `stopped` for the length of that turn — rung 1 of honest liveness blind exactly when it matters.
  • **'external' LIVENESS, with positive evidence only** (`deriveStatus`): `live` = one of OUR sessions holds the id; `external` = either this serve's own `/session/status` says busy/retry (in-process truth, no directory needed, boots nothing) OR the row's `time.updated` MOVED while we were not the ones moving it, within `EXTERNAL_WINDOW_MS` (90s) — the cross-process signal, since another opencode's events never reach our bus but the sqlite row it writes does reach our list, and the store-watch lane makes us re-read within ~0.4s. Anything else is `stopped`; the label DECAYS on its own, and `state().liveLaneHealthy` says when we cannot see at all, so the honest answer is a fact in the panel rather than a lie on every row. Verified against two real serves on one store: an external write flipped the row to external, our own write stayed live, and stale evidence decayed back to stopped. **"WE" INCLUDES THE USER'S OWN CLICK (round 3, a reproduced defect): "we were not the ones moving it" originally meant only *a live session of ours holds this id*, so every mutation through our OWN serve routes on a STOPPED conversation — roll back, restore, answer/reject an ask — moved `time.updated` and was then attributed to a stranger for 90s** (reproduced 3/3 through the real product routes: the card dimmed to opacity .7, its title read "Running in unsupported terminal (PID ?)", the Fork… row vanished from its menu, and a sidebar filtered to exclude 'external' lost the conversation entirely — all from the user's own click). `noteOwnWrite(id, session)` writes the mutation down and `ownWriteVerdict` consumes it: **by VALUE where we have one** — MEASURED on 1.18.29, `revert`/`unrevert` return the row's FINAL `time.updated` and nothing bumps it afterwards (0ms delta at +250ms…+8s), so the listing that reports exactly what we wrote is ours no matter how late it arrives, a listing carrying an OLDER value (one that was already in flight while we wrote) keeps the entry, and the first value PAST ours is somebody else's and says so immediately — and by a short `OWN_WRITE_WINDOW_MS` (10s) clock only for the actions that return no Session (an answered ask; the reply/reject paths therefore re-read `/question` even for positional answers, because the question is the only thing that names the conversation they are about to move). Fork writes nothing down and that is measured too: forking does not move the SOURCE row, and the fork's own row is a first sighting. The gate carries both directions — our own roll-back stays stopped on a REAL store, while a SECOND real `opencode serve` writing that same store still reads external.
NOT wired, with the reason: the v2 `/api/session/:id/revert/{stage,clear,commit}` trio (measured instance boot, above) and `POST /sync/history` — the DURABLE cross-process event log, which DOES carry another process's writes (proven: a session created by a second serve appeared in the first serve's history), but whose first read has no "from now on" cursor and would download the whole log; the list re-read the store-watch triggers is bounded and answers the same question. Gates: scripts/test-opencode-serve.mjs (135) + scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs (the remainder: roll-back/restore incl. "the v2 family is never requested", the ask card end to end incl. the stale case and the free-text-with-comma control, the pty bridge over a real websocket, the live lane replacing the poll with a negative control for the timer fallback, liveness with three negative controls, wiring pins, and a REAL-BINARY section that SKIPS WITH EVIDENCE when `opencode` is absent) + scripts/test-opencode-remote.mjs (the three rungs cannot drift). test-opencode-serve details: (mock serve in scripts/dev/mock-opencode-serve.mjs, which MODELS the instance boot: any v2 per-session route adds `state.instances`, so 'a whole discovery tick bootstraps ZERO instances' is a real assert, plus a spawn-cwd spy, the '/'-worktree boot self-heal, the runaway guard driven by a fake /proc reader + fake clock, dispose-after-fork and the byte cap — mock serve in scripts/dev/mock-opencode-serve.mjs, which MODELS the instance boot: any v2 per-session route adds `state.instances`, so 'a whole discovery tick bootstraps ZERO instances' is a real assert, plus a spawn-cwd spy, the '/'-worktree boot self-heal, the runaway guard driven by a fake /proc reader + fake clock, dispose-after-fork and the byte cap); manual: `node scripts/dev/opencode-serve-live.mjs --measure [seconds]` runs the REAL loop against the REAL store and prints CPU/RSS/threads/indexer-threads/inotify-watches (healthy = single-digit CPU%, flat RSS, 0 indexer threads, 0 watches).
  src/opencode-events.js — THE LIVE LANE that replaced OpenCode's 10s list poll (S9 remainder piece (d), B-eac2; SHARED — node builtins only, the daemon bundles it). TWO SOURCES, because one of them is provably blind and saying so is the point. ① `GET /global/event`: measured with /proc, it boots NO OpenCode instance and needs no `directory`, so it is the ONE subscription per serve; frames are `{directory, project, payload:{id,type,properties}}` and a `server.heartbeat` lands about every 10s (undocumented, observed on the wire) — which is why an open socket with NO frame for 45s is treated as DEAD and aborted, not as idle. `createEventStream` reconnects with 1s·2^n backoff (≤30s) and re-asks the LOCATOR each time, so a serve that respawned on a new port is followed. ② `fs.watch` over the store dirs (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/opencode`, `~/.local/share/opencode`, and the serve's own `GET /path` home when it differs — resolved lazily, because before the serve exists we do not know whose HOME it runs under): **THE MEASURED BLIND SPOT** — a SECOND opencode process on the SAME sqlite (a TUI, another serve) produces NO frame on `/global/event` at all (reproduced: serve A subscribed, serve B created a session, A's stream carried only `server.connected` + heartbeats for 30s while A's `GET /session` listed it immediately). The store is shared, the event bus is per process, so SSE alone cannot replace the poll; the watch never READS the db, only its mtime events, is debounced 400ms, and an unwatchable dir degrades to "no external signal" LOUDLY once and is retried on the next reconnect. `classifyEvent` is PURE: one testable table from event type → {kind, sessionId, dirty:{sessions|conversation|questions}, status, question, …}; an UNKNOWN type is `kind:'other'` with NO dirty flags, so an OpenCode upgrade can never make us drop a lane. `createLiveLane` is the pair behind one `state()` — `{sse:{connected,…}, watch:{watching,failed,active,…}}` — and "healthy" means BOTH, which is exactly the condition under which the timer refresh is allowed to be gone. **The lane FOLLOWS the serve, on an EDGE**: while nothing is reachable the stream is in backoff (up to 30s), so `install()` wraps the locator's own `onState` and calls `lane.kick()` when a port becomes READY or CHANGES — enabling the plugin, or a keeper respawn on a new port, goes live at once. It must be the edge and not every `notify()`: the runaway guard samples /proc once a minute, and kicking on each of those would re-open the SSE socket for nothing (both directions are pinned in scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs). **AND THE SLEEP IS PART OF THE SOCKET (round 3, a reproduced defect): `kick()` originally reset `attempts` and aborted the fetch controller — but during a backoff there IS no fetch to abort, and nothing cleared the `setTimeout` the loop was sitting in, so the kick only shortened the NEXT wait.** Since the service ships OFF, "nothing to connect to" is the normal state before first use, so this was the first-use path, not a corner: measured 25.1s from "the serve is ready" to "the lane is healthy" end to end (and 11.0s to the next connect attempt in the unit shape), during which `laneHealthy()` is false and the 10s timer this feature replaced is still the only source of freshness. The loop's sleep now keeps its resolver (`wake`), and `kick()`/`stop()` both wake it before aborting — 51ms after the fix, with the regression driving the REAL stream to a ≥3s pending backoff so a sub-second reconnect cannot be an accident of a short timer. **ROUND 4 — the lane could LIE about itself, two ways, both closed.** `active` is what `laneHealthy()` switches the list-refresh fallback OFF on, and it was true for ANY directory that merely existed; meanwhile `armWatch()` ran at `start()` — before any serve — and latched (`watchTried` was only reset when the watch attached NOWHERE), so the documented "resolved LAZILY once the serve answers GET /path" never ran on any machine where `$HOME/.local/share/opencode` already exists, which is every machine that has ever run opencode. Reproduced with the real wiring: `state().liveLane.watch` = the SERVER process's store with `liveLaneHealthy` true, and a session created by a SECOND opencode process on the serve's REAL store never appeared (8s of 100ms-spaced `discover()`, ZERO `GET /session` issued because the lane claimed health); the same probe with the serve's actual store saw it in 493ms. In the default deployment the two resolve to the same dir (agentEnv keeps HOME/XDG_DATA_HOME), so this was a latent gap plus a FALSE HEALTH SIGNAL — and a false `active` is worse than none, because it is the flag that turns the fallback off. Fixes: `createStoreWatch` refuses a directory with no `opencode.db` (recorded in `failed`, `active` stays false ⇒ the timer fallback stays on and the next reconnect re-arms — the serve creates the db at boot); and `armWatch(why)` RE-RESOLVES on every `connected` against the serve's own `GET /path` home, re-arming only when the dir set actually DIFFERS (`watchedDirs` + a `rearms` counter in `state()`), so a reconnect storm cannot churn the watchers. **ROUND 5 — the lane is only STARTED while the service is on.** Nothing changed in this module: `start()` still arms the watch and the stream, and that is exactly why `install()` (src/opencode-serve.js) may not call it while the OpenCode background service is OFF — a lane that merely has nothing to connect to still holds an fs.watch on the user's store. The owner of "may this run at all" is the locator's autostart decision; this module owns only "how well is it running". **ROUND 6 — and `stop()` is TERMINAL for an arm already IN FLIGHT.** Round 5 gated whether the lane may START; both of its starts are async before they own anything, so a disable landing in the gap still leaked. `armWatch()` awaits `resolveDirs()` (the serve's `GET /path` on a 2s budget — spent in full exactly when the serve is parked or absent, the state a user turning the service off is in), and the continuation used to assign `watch = createStoreWatch(...)` onto a lane nobody holds: an fs.watch on the user's real store unreachable by any later `stop()` or `uninstall()`, and a LIVE one — a write to `opencode.db-wal` still fired `onExternal` ⇒ `markDirty` ⇒ an `opencode-updated` broadcast ⇒ every client re-running /api/sessions for a feature that was OFF (measured: 1 inotify wd at a 0 ms disable and at a 1200 ms disable, and with the keeper PARKED). `connectOnce()` had the same shape around `locator.client({budgetMs:8000})`: a stop there aborted a controller that did not exist yet and the continuation opened an SSE subscription the serve then held open forever (heartbeats keep it under the 45s idle abort), with `state().sse` reporting `stopped:true` AND `connected:true` at once. So: `laneStopped` is set FIRST in `stop()`; `armWatch()` re-checks it on entry AND hands the built watch to the single attach point `attachWatch()`, which closes it rather than store it; `resolveDirs()` skips its second round trip when the lane died during the first; `connectOnce()` re-checks `state.stopped` after the locator and after the fetch settles. The lane is SINGLE-USE by construction (`stopLive()` drops it, `armLive()` builds a fresh one per enable), which is what makes a one-way door safe — `kick()` is a no-op once stopped. Gated by scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs's ROUND 6 legs, each run twice: once against this file and once against a copy with exactly these guards removed (the negative control MUST leak, and its replacements are asserted to match once each so a stale control fails loudly).
  src/opencode-remote.js — THE OP TABLE (S9 remainder piece (e), B-eac2; PURE — imports nothing). `OPENCODE_OPS` maps an op name to {member, required[], build(params), result(v)} and `runOpencodeOp(facts, op, params)` executes it against a facts object. That is the WHOLE machine-side implementation, and all three rungs use it: the local rung (hostId falsy, in-process), the `opencode-serve` agentd op (the daemon bundles src/opencode-serve.js and calls this same function against ITS facts), and — mirrored, because a checkout-less ssh host cannot require it — data/bin/vibespace-opencode-op. `checkOpParams` refuses an unknown op WITH the known list and a missing param WITH the op and key names, so no rung can fail as an unexplained 500. Gate: scripts/test-opencode-remote.mjs parses the shipped script's own OPS object and fails on any op the table has and the script lacks (or vice versa), compares result KEYS rung by rung against a mock serve, and pins the three-touch rule on the device side. **The daemon holds its facts in a MODULE-LEVEL `ocFacts`, never on `this`**: inside a Mux control handler `this` is the MUX — the CONNECTION — so a per-`this` cache is rebuilt on every reconnect and each `install()` arms another live lane (an SSE subscription + fs.watch handles) while the old one keeps running; a dial-out device reconnects on every link blip, which makes that leak silent and unbounded. The suites assert the declaration sits ABOVE `serveConnection` and that `this._ocFacts` appears nowhere. **Rung choice** (src/hosts.js `opencodeOp`): a DIAL device has no ssh fallback and rethrows the device error (2.271.0 T3-6 — the ssh path would misdiagnose it); an SSH host falls through on ANY device failure, like the usage-scan and transcript rungs, because the daemon may be old, down or mid-upgrade and the shipped script answers the same question. That fallback is FREE: the daemon's locator and the script use the SAME record `~/.vibespace/opencode-serve.json`, so the ssh rung REUSES the daemon's serve instead of starting a second one (pinned).
  data/bin/vibespace-opencode-op — REMOTE-side op runner for checkout-less ssh hosts (STATIC tracked, shipped to ~/.vibespace/bin; the documented single-file exception, same class as vibespace-usage-scan). Reads `{op,params}` on stdin, prints ONE `{ok,result}|{ok:false,error}` line. It carries the full 2.369.50 resource discipline onto someone else's machine: the serve is started from its OWN empty `~/.vibespace/opencode-serve-cwd` git repo (never $HOME — OpenCode's upward worktree walk would adopt it), ONLY v1 routes are used, the listing is directory-less and never bootstraps a project row that is not ours, every response read into memory is byte-capped, the record is written atomically, and a healthy recorded serve is reused instead of spawned per call. `read` ships the RAW v1 messages — the 'acp-events' synthesis stays in ONE place (the hub's `messagesToAcpRecords`, applied by the access layer), proven record-for-record identical to the local rung. The pty ops REFUSE with the reason (the serve's pty websocket is loopback-only on that host). Its rows are always `status:'stopped'` and that is deliberate, not a lag: liveness rung 2 is a LEDGER across listings (and, since round 3, across our own writes), and a one-shot ssh invocation has no memory between calls — so this rung claims nothing rather than guessing. The paired-device rung keeps the ledger because the daemon's facts live per PROCESS.
  src/server/opencode-access.js — the ONE way to reach the OpenCode serve on ANY machine (ORCH; wired once from mounts-plugins-wiring so server.js stays inside its size ratchet, and published as a module singleton `access()` that ws-handler / ws-create reach without a server.js cross). `call(hostId, op, params)` picks the transport and nothing downstream ever asks "is this remote?" again. Two normalisations live here because they must be identical on every rung: `readConversation` synthesises records from the ssh rung's raw messages with the SHARED function, and `publicView` strips the serve ws url + auth header from anything a ROUTE returns (they are how the SERVER connects; only ws-create's bridge sees them, through `openPtyBridge`). `LOCAL_ONLY_OPS` refuses the pty family for a remote machine BEFORE the transport is used, naming why.
  src/server/opencode-pty-bridge.js — THE SERVE TERMINAL (S9 remainder piece (c), ORCH). `opencode serve` owns real ptys and streams them over a websocket; this connects to it SERVER-SIDE and presents `{pid,onData,onExit,write,resize,kill}` — the same duck the daemon pipe-session shim uses — so ws-create can hand it to `setupSessionPty` and the xterm window, input, resize, the buffer file and every window action work with ZERO terminal-side changes. THE SERVE PORT NEVER REACHES THE BROWSER. Verified on the wire against 1.18.29: `GET /pty/{id}/connect` upgrades with NO ticket on an unsecured loopback serve (and `POST /pty/{id}/connect-token` answers PtyForbiddenError there, so the bridge mints one when it can and connects without one when it cannot); TEXT frames are terminal output; BINARY frames are `\0`-prefixed control json (`\0{"cursor":73}`) and rendering them would paint json into the user's shell; frames written in reach the pty. Resize is NOT on the socket — `PUT /pty/{id} {size:{rows,cols}}`. The pty belongs to the SERVE, so a dropped socket RECONNECTS (5 tries, backoff) instead of ending the session, and input typed meanwhile is queued (bounded at 200 — a dead socket must not grow memory). **But "the serve does not have this pty" is a VERDICT, not a hiccup (round 3, measured on the wire): after the shell exits, `GET /pty/<id>` answers PtyNotFoundError and the upgrade is a plain HTTP 404 — which the bounded reconnect burned all five rungs against (800+1600+2400+3200+4000 ≈ 12s of a dead-but-open terminal, plus five bogus `socket error: Unexpected server response: 404` warnings) before the window admitted the shell was gone.** `ptyGone(message)` is PURE (404/410 terminal; 502/503, ECONNREFUSED, ECONNRESET, "socket hang up" transient — a truth table in the gate) and the `error` handler sets a `gone` flag that short-circuits `close` into ONE honest log line + `onExit`; the mock serve now answers 404 to an upgrade for a pty it no longer has, exactly like the real one, so the end-to-end leg can assert ONE upgrade attempt and an exit under a second. Known limits, deliberately: it is LOCAL-only (a remote serve's ws is not reachable from the hub — a device would need the port-forward data plane), and a server restart leaves the pty alive on the serve without re-adopting it. **ROUND 4:** the bridge's `pty-close`/`pty-resize` calls still carry `cwd` on the wire (the op table's shape) but the CLIENT no longer turns it into a `directory` query — see src/opencode-serve.js: opening a terminal used to boot and permanently watch the user's whole worktree. The "a server restart leaves the pty alive on the serve without re-adopting it" limit above is now BOUNDED rather than open: it is still not re-adopted (no re-bridge), but `facts.reapPtys()` removes it on the next serve ready edge, so a stranded shell no longer runs forever.
  src/routes/opencode.js — the OpenCode action routes (roll back / restore / asks / status / todos). Every route takes `host` and passes it straight to the access layer; every MUTATING route broadcasts `opencode-updated` (persistent state → other browsers see it without a refresh); every failure answers `{error}` with the machine-side reason (fetchJson never throws, so a 200-with-nothing would be a silent failure of a user action). Full shapes: docs/kb-api.md.
  src/harnesses/index.js — ONE descriptor per agent harness (claude.js, codex.js, shell.js, opencode.js via acp.js) = identity, `caps` (the backend-caps row), `Adapter` + `adapterConfig(cfg)`, `Normalizer`, `wrapper` file, `store` (locateTranscript/transcriptDirs/conversationIdField…), `quota` (S4 QuotaSignalSource: normalize/signalFromStream/probe/classifyAuthFailure — claude-quota.js/codex-quota.js/null-quota.js; the pool engine's probe dispatcher routes on `quota.probe`), `inject` (S6: kind hooks|wrapper|acp, hookFile, hookEvents, sessionStartHonoured — agent-tool-generators builds HOOK_FILES/HOOK_EVENTS_FOR from it; agent-routes' seen-gates consult sessionStartHonoured), `creds` (S2, 2.369.26-27: subsDirName/authFile/spawnEnvVar/loginLabel/defaultIdField/keychainSensitive/parseAuth(dir) + files/bumpFile/hostFactsKey/longLivedToken/supportsApiKeys/remoteSymlinks/ensureTargets/probe/seedDir(dir) [codex: sharedHome()] — accounts.js resolves dirs/auth/labels/default-account/export files/import seeding/delete/verdict host-facts/pool-swap bump/spawn env var/remoteCreds ship shape through `_credsOf/_acctDir/_readAuthFor/_remoteCreds`; readSubCreds/readCodexSubAuth/_seedConfigDir/_seedCodexDir/_codexSharedHome are shims over it; createPool validates capsOf().pool + registry. Deliberately still dispatched: resolveForSpawn (claude oat/api-key ladder vs codex CODEX_HOME) and the claude-only hostSubs held-check), `settingsPrefix`. API: `HARNESSES` (built-ins), `get/has/list/ids` (unknown id THROWS — never a claude fallback), `register/unregister` (plugin tier-5; built-ins immutable), `assertQuotaContract`. adapters/index.js and normalizers.js are built from it. Conformance: scripts/test-harness-contract.mjs runs the same assertions over every registered harness (descriptor keys, adapter base methods + buildSessionArgs, normalizer duck contract incl. injectPeerCard/convertHistoryAsync, wrapper file + caps advert, store, settings keys, client BACKEND_META row, inject shape, wiring pins). S7 (2.369.23) client META caps + settingsPrefix parity pinned here (2.369.26 — the pins missed their 2.369.23 commit: a patch batch aborted before the suite). S3 (store, 2.369.31) and S5 (stdout pipeline per descriptor — see the HARNESS STDOUT entry below) are landed. S5 decision: the descriptor names its live-stdout consumer ONLY through `caps.streamProtocol` (the backend-caps row it already carries); there is deliberately no `stdout:{protocol}` field — a second copy is a twin waiting to drift, and the contract suite pins that none exists. **store.warmTranscript (B-21e4 item 5):** the OPTIONAL pre-read hook `(id, cwd, {remote}) → Promise` every history consumer awaits through the descriptor before constructing a reader — ws-handler attach + view-only and transcript-service.view (the claude-only `warmSessionJsonlAsync` ternaries are gone): claude = the worker parse-cache warm (unchanged), codex = the thread/read fallback for a missing rollout, a harness without it skips.

### gmail-sync.js

  gmail-sync.js        — GmailSync (2.134.0, backlog B-64db): Gmail-as-a-folder — a 'gmail' mount = local dir of .eml files synced READ-ONLY from the Gmail API (GYB-style; NOT FUSE). Own loopback OAuth for gmail.readonly (rclone authorize is drive-only; same guided UX incl. remote paste-back); seed = STREAMING + CHECKPOINTED (2.136.1-4): each page downloaded then a CROSS-RESTART cursor persisted — a DATE (`state.seedOldestMs` → `before:<sec>`), NOT the messages.list pageToken (pageToken is only stable within one run; new mail between runs shifts/expires it and silently skips OLD mail the incremental never back-fills — real user catch). historyId anchored at seed START (covers mail arriving during a long seed). Total for the N/total bar comes cheaply from users.getProfile `messagesTotal` (whole mailbox) or labels.get `messagesTotal` (label filter), persisted as `state.seedTotal` (free-text query → indeterminate). Progress broadcasts throttle (indeterminate 2.5s — a fast cadence rebuilt the card and restarted the bar animation, stuttering it 'in the first third'; the bar is a symmetric pulse so a mid-anim rebuild is invisible). Incremental via history.list (404 = expired → reseed; dir tree IS the dedup index — msg id in filename tail, _seenIds recurses one date/label dir level), messages.get?format=raw → base64url → .eml (atomic tmp+rename, RFC2047-decoded subject slug in name). Client id/secret from VIBESPACE_GDRIVE_CLIENTS presets (they carry gmail scope) or custom — NO built-in fallback. mounts.js integration: isMounted=worker alive, health sweep skips fuse probing (dead worker → auto-restart), unmount stops worker but keeps files (it's the archive), mode forced ro. Viewer: src/lib/eml.js (dependency-free browser-safe MIME parser, 41-case test) + file-viewer _renderEml (header card, text/html toggle, html in sandbox="" iframe, attachment blob downloads). **2.151.0 incremental-freeze fix (real 3h hang):** history.list lists ADDED ids that can be deleted by fetch time (spam auto-purge; guaranteed with includeSpamTrash) — the per-message 404 failed the whole pass and the cursor only advances after a COMPLETE pass ⇒ every 120s retry re-listed + re-404'd the same dead id forever. _writeGone404 skips gone messages (both paths); all API/token fetches bounded (AbortSignal.timeout 60s/30s) so a wedged socket errors into the backoff. Tests: scripts/test-gmail-sync.mjs (mock API e2e incl the dead-id regression), scripts/test-eml.mjs

### transcript-worker.js

  transcript-worker.js — heavy JSONL transcript work OFF the main thread (2.235.0, the userL degradation follow-up): a persistent SafeFs-hosted worker pool (SafeFs gained opts.workerPath/inlineRun generalization + in-flight ref()) running the EXISTING sync implementations by requiring the same modules — worker-side mtime+size caches, zero duplication. Async variants jsonlGapInfoAsync/readJsonlLineRangeAsync/scanJsonlUserTurnsAsync/readJsonlBoundedParsedAsync serve the gap/turnmap/slab endpoints; session-store.warmSessionJsonlAsync pre-warms the parse cache before the ws attach rebuild + /api/session-messages (sync machinery then cache-hits). Worker down ⇒ sync inline fallback. RULE: new heavy transcript scans go through this worker, never inline on the loop (measured: 1ms max main-thread block vs 78ms inline on a 39MB fixture, 0.6-1s on real files). Test: scripts/test-transcript-worker.mjs. Related: sysinfo.js samples event-loop lag (500ms cadence, 3-min median; ≥300ms amber / ≥800ms red → sysinfo-alert kind:'evloop' toast "sessions are NOT dead" + history field `e`)

### agentd/

  agentd/               — CS refactor (B-5052) machine agent — ALL milestones' protocol+device side COMPLETE and acceptance-tested (2.140.0–2.146.0; docs/design-remote-cs.md + M0 addendum + milestone record). THREE flags, DEFAULT ON since 2.158.0 (CS graduation — local=device#0; automatic legacy fallback preserved). **ONE MACHINE MODEL since 2.160.0 (B-f3e8, resolves the user's 2026-07-15 insight that row inconsistencies = two underlying concepts): a machine is a host record, transport ∈ {ssh(absent), dial}** — the pairing credential lives ON the dial record (`dialTokenHash`, redacted from list(), migrated ONCE from dial-tokens.json via hosts.migrateDialTokenFile → `.migrated`; LOSSLESS is load-bearing: in-field daemons hold the raw tokens); roster/test/unpair = /api/hosts + POST /api/hosts/:id/test (hosts.test dial branch = mux runCmd probe) + DELETE /api/hosts/:id (server unpairDialDevice: mounts+token-file+live-stream teardown); /api/agentd/devices is GONE; `hosts-updated` broadcasts on pair/unpair/dial-in/out; hosts.dirComplete rides the device link (dial machines get real remote autocomplete); sidebar has ONE _buildHostRow for all transports + a 'This machine' local row (local=device#0 made visible). The flags: `agentd.sessions` (M1: local sessions run in the daemon — dtach attach inside agentd, survives server restarts), `agentd.remoteSessions` (M2: remote chat = persistent pipe session in the remote daemon via the vibespace-agentd-attach bridge, keeper-run-identical contract so chat-wrapper is untouched; auto install/refresh at spawn), `agentd.dataPlane` (M3 switchovers: RemoteFs fs ops / discovery raw-facts synthesized into the ssh-script line format→UNCHANGED parser / transcript INCREMENTAL slab sync (append-only delta via read-range — no more whole-file remote-jsonl pulls) / usage harvest via run-stream; per-path ssh fallback; hosts.device(id) = per-host DeviceManager registry). mux.js = length-prefixed binary mux (chan0 JSON control + credit-flow-controlled byte channels; data() SPLITS at the window — all-or-nothing starved big writes; zero deps, shared by daemon+server = invariant #3). **CONTROL-OVERTAKE INVARIANT (2.187.0, real incident): chan0 control is credit-EXEMPT, so a completion marker (fs-done/stream-exit) OVERTAKES data still queued behind the 256KB window — any "resolve on the done marker" consumer truncates big transfers to exactly INITIAL_WINDOW bytes.** fsReadRange/runStream are COUNT-GATED (resolve when the counted bytes landed; daemon reports `sent`, run-stream exits on 'close' not 'exit', both pace under window pressure via onWritable); the incident: a 45MB remote transcript cached as a 256KB prefix stamped complete → permanently ancient chat history (fetchSessionJsonl now also refuses to stamp meta for bytes not received + caps on FETCHED delta not total size; **2.188.1: cache-valid additionally requires the cached FILE to hold meta.size bytes — a pre-fix stump over a STOPPED transcript passed size/mtime forever since the self-heal only fired on remote-file change**). Any NEW byte-channel consumer must gate on a byte count, never on a control-channel done. Test: scripts/test-agentd-bigread.mjs. agentd.js = daemon (flock singleton, setsid, 0700 unix socket, multi-server, vsht_ hello auth, self-upgrade→versioned dir→re-exec (2.185.2: the re-exec PRESERVES the original argv via reExecArgv — dropping `--dial/--dial-token` re-exec'd a dial device into LISTEN mode and wedged the link behind the singleton, the userW-class 'device goes offline after updates' root cause); pipe-sessions = keeper semantics in-daemon (setsid child + buffer/fifo + offset reattach + sentinel + drain-only-never-respawn); **pipe-child identity is exec-PROOF startTime, never cmdline (2.184.1, real userL outage: chat children spawn as `sh -lc '… exec env … claude'` so cmdline loses argv0 — every daemon upgrade re-exec then misjudged the live claude as a recycled pid, synthesized a crash sentinel (session died, claude orphaned) AND made kill-pipe-session a no-op; adopted children additionally get a liveness watcher because a non-child can't be wait()ed — no watcher meant no exit sentinel ever; test-agentd-adopt.mjs guards both)**; fs-op incl read-range byte-chan slab; discovery snapshot w/ tailIds/headCwd/userLines enrichment + fs.watch dirty push; run-cmd (argv-only bounded); run-stream (unbounded stdout on byte chan); tcp-connect (loopback only = VNC bridge shape); **serve-folder (2.150.0; WEBDAV since 2.151.0, device-folder-mount): a minimal in-daemon read-only WebDAV subset (OPTIONS/PROPFIND Depth 0-1/HEAD/GET+Range; zero deps, mirrors src/webdav.js) bound to 127.0.0.1 that rclone `webdav`-mounts via tcp-connect (src/device-mount.js) — the plain-`http` backend was the 2.150.0 stall: it requests a fixed 128MB range per read then waits ~6s on the keep-alive connection after the clamped 206; webdav requests sane ranges and reads in ~7ms (verified). GOTCHAS: clamp ranged end to size-1 (rclone asks 128M ranges → clamp or truncated EIO); the SERVER-side bridge net.createServer MUST be `{allowHalfOpen:true}` + attach the data listener BEFORE `await tcpForward` (curl/rclone half-close a GET's write side + write-then-read, both lose bytes otherwise); NEVER do sync fs/exec on a device mountpoint from the process hosting the bridge — the blocked event loop can't pump the tunnel, the FUSE read never completes, SELF-DEADLOCK (the acceptance test hung itself this way for a whole debug cycle; a dir `ls` still 'works' off rclone's dir-cache, which hides it; test IO now runs in async children w/ watchdog — production safe by construction: bridge in the server, reads from other processes/safe-fs workers). Still not a MountManager UI type (next step). **SSH-BRIDGE STDIN EPIPE (2.241.1, userN's exit-code-1 crash): the client.js ssh-transport wrapper writes to child.stdin — pipe errors arrive ASYNC as 'error' events on the stdin socket itself (the wrapper's on('error') only covered the child process; the write try/catch only stops sync throws), so a dying ssh child during the heartbeat PING was an UNCAUGHT write EPIPE that killed the whole server 28s after an update restart. stdin/stdout error events are swallowed; 'close' drives mux teardown+reconnect. Any new pipe-wrapped transport must attach error listeners on the PIPE STREAMS, not just the process.** dial-in device consumption = DeviceManager `stream` transport + server deviceForDial(id) wrapping a dialed-in ws stream — **deviceForDial NEVER reuses a stop()ed DeviceManager (2.169.0, real userW outage: hours of phantom-offline against a healthy dialed-in Mac): stop() is terminal (_connectLoop throws 'stopped' forever), so a stopped dm is evicted like a stale stream and a failed connect() never stays cached; any new dm.stop() caller must not leave the object in agentdDialDevices**. Dial chat TERMINATE kills the device-side claude via the daemon's `kill-pipe-session` (client killPipeSession) + rm's the session token over the device link — the ssh-only teardown threw for dial and silently orphaned claude (double-JSONL-writer class). Pairing commands render `/api/device-dial`; `/api/agentd-dial` is a PERMANENT alias (in-field daemons hold it in dial.json); env `VIBESPACE_DEVICE_ROOT` preferred, `VIBESPACE_AGENTD_ROOT` honored forever. **RE-PAIR / IDENTITY ROTATION (2.170.0, the userW-incident class): the daemon re-reads dial.json on EVERY dial attempt and the host-token file on EVERY hello — a re-pair heals a RUNNING daemon within one retry; never cache either at startup (the cached identity dialed a dead pairing forever while the singleton blocked its replacement + launchd respawn-spammed). The installer replaces a running same-root daemon (cmdline-verified kill); macOS singleton verifies the lock pid via `ps` (no /proc — recycled pids read as "already running" forever). Multi-instance = one root/daemon per instance (`~/.vibespace/device@<dialhost>`), re-pair replaces within a root.** **REVERSE forward tcp-listen/tcp-accept/tcp-unlisten (2.148.0, the NAT-traversal tunnel)** — daemon binds 127.0.0.1:<port> ON THE DEVICE, accepts push back over the mux as new byte channels (chan ids from 0x40000000 — never collides with server-allocated 2..N; the tcp-accept control MUST precede channel data), server pipes them into its own loopback port; on link death the listener is DISOWNED not closed (port stays bound → reconnecting server re-owns same-port via connect-loop re-registration → remote mounts heal in place); both tcp paths backpressured (credit-after-drain + mux.onWritable resume); --stdio ssh bridge mode; --dial ws dial-out (Transport B; ws-min.js = hand-rolled RFC6455 client — the `head` bytes-with-the-101 upgrade trap is handled, dropping them ate first frames on fast reconnects)). client.js = DeviceManager (transports local/ssh, openSession/openPipeSession/fs*/runCmd/runStream/tcpForward/reverseForward/discovery). Server: /api/agentd-dial upgrade branch (dial-token gated, verifies hosts.dialTokenHash since 2.160.0) + dial-pair mint (writes the hash onto the dial host record; /api/agentd/devices + /api/device-mounts RETIRED in 2.160.0 — roster/test/unpair = /api/hosts, mounts = /api/machine-mounts, see machine-mounts.js). Pairing dialog has per-OS commands (mac/linux bash; WINDOWS EXPERIMENTAL via /agentd-install.ps1, PS 5.1-compatible + win32 named-pipe SOCK in the daemon; the ps1 now also persists state\dial.json + registers a logon SCHEDULED TASK, so a Windows device survives a reboot). **EVERY URL in those commands must come from `httpBase` (= `r.relayUrl || location.origin`), NEVER location.origin directly (2.246.0 bug): a relay-paired device correctly dialed the relay but was handed origin-based installer/bundle URLs its network cannot reach — it failed before the daemon ever started.** MULTI-INSTANCE pairing: dial-out installs root at ~/.vibespace/agentd@<dialhost> (per-instance daemon/tokens/bundle; installer exports VIBESPACE_AGENTD_ROOT — forget that and the daemon reads the default root's token). INSTALLER GOTCHA (2.152.1, real Mac report): macOS has NO setsid(1) — the installer's unconditional `setsid node …` started NOTHING and printed ✓ anyway (stderr redirected into agentd.out); now setsid-or-nohup + kill-0 verification before claiming success. **NODE-FREE PAIRING (2.246.0): the installers (bash AND ps1) no longer REQUIRE node — resolution order is `--node` override → OUR private `$ROOT/node/bin/node` → PATH → newest nvm → common absolute paths → PROVISION (pinned official tarball from nodejs.org/dist, verified against that release's SHASUMS256.txt, smoke-run, then atomically `mv`d into `$ROOT/node`; `--node-only` = resolve/print/exit, the support probe). Private-BEFORE-PATH is deliberate (a launchd plist pointing at an nvm path dies at the user's next nvm upgrade); nvm-before-common-paths is the `curl|bash`-is-non-login lesson (nvm.sh never sourced ⇒ the #1 false 'no node'). Everything lands INSIDE the root, so `rm -rf $ROOT` uninstalls the runtime too — and because agentd's `spawnEnv()` prepends `path.dirname(process.execPath)`, the `#!/usr/bin/env node` agent tools resolve on a machine that never had node (2.244.4 chicken-and-egg, solved structurally). CONSEQUENCE: a provisioned device is PINNED to that node forever (no OS security updates) until the pin is bumped — documented in docs/device-agent.md § Node runtime. RULE: every later node/npm invocation in the installers uses the resolved absolute `$NODE_BIN`/`$NODE_DIR/npm`, NEVER bare `node` (launchd/systemd get no PATH of ours). set -e footguns in that block: `[ -e x ] && mv` and bare `x=$(cmd|pipe)` under pipefail both exit the script — keep new predicates inside `if`/`|| true`. musl + 32-bit Windows refuse honestly with fix instructions (official builds are glibc/64-bit only). Instance-side fallback mirror `GET /vibespace-node/:version/:file` (server.js, auth-exempt, fixed upstream + strict filename allowlist + disk cache in data/node-cache/) for devices that reach this instance but not nodejs.org. Tests: scripts/test-node-bootstrap.mjs (19 asserts, local HTTP fixture + a bwrap node-free sandbox; covers tamper/unlisted-checksum refusal, re-use, nvm discovery, override).** E2E: scripts/dbg-pair-smoke.mjs (17 asserts, REAL daemon dial + mount chain + UI rows). Build: npm run build:agentd → data/bin/vibespace-agentd.js + vibespace-agentd-attach.js (both gitignored artifacts). Tests (all green, 3 environments incl the devbox real-ssh + fleet test pod): test-mux/-agentd/-agentd-session/-agentd-remote/-agentd-robustness (bidi/jitter/latency/concurrency)/-agentd-wired/-agentd-dial/-agentd-m3m4 (18 scenarios)/-agentd-switchover (real-host legacy-vs-device cross-checks). u-vstest soaks with ALL flags on. **2.369.12 push阶梯(inc-mtl78uhs)**: mountpoint的~/$HOME按机器home展开+去尾斜杠(liveness探针比裸路径); rclone daemon子进程--log-file写入dialog tail的同一日志(真实原因不再被--daemon吞掉); macOS阶梯=rclone nfsmount(≥1.66, 免macFUSE)→FUSE mount(有macFUSE时)→mount_webdav, 每级失败都保留展示; 设备rclone pin v1.69.3, 自有旧安装force重装, 系统rclone不动; method 'rclone-nfs'与umount家族同处理.

### machine-mounts.js

  machine-mounts.js    — MachineMounts (B-f3e8 2.160.0 — HostMounts 2.147.0 + DeviceMounts 2.153.0 collapsed into ONE manager; records carry dir:'push'|'pull', keyed by hostId, store data/machine-mounts.json, both legacy stores constructor-migrated → .migrated; routes /api/machine-mounts* (POST :hostId {dir,…}), broadcast machine-mounts-updated). PUSH = mount THIS instance's folder ON the machine, OS-aware (Linux rclone/FUSE; macOS rclone/macFUSE else native mount_webdav — macFUSE probed ON the Mac since 2.160.0, the old check read the SERVER's /dev/fuse; Windows rclone/WinFsp else net use; rclone auto-installed remote-side w/ unzip→busybox→python3 fallbacks). Transport: _davBase prefers the agentd REVERSE-FORWARD TUNNEL (remote mounts http://127.0.0.1:<port>/dav over the device link — NAT-proof; dial machines tunnel-ONLY, ssh falls back to agentd.publicUrl); tunnelPort per record. PULL = the machine's folder mounted INTO this workspace over hosts.device(hostId) (serve-folder→tcp-forward→rclone webdav ro — works for ssh AND dial identically); live chain in-memory, restore()/onMachineLinked heal recorded pulls + re-own push tunnel ports (a re-dial means the daemon RE-EXECED → stale handles torn down first, real Mac report 2.158.1) + 90s child-process-ls health sweep (never node fs on a fuse mountpoint). unmount = best-effort remote teardown but ALWAYS token-revoke + record-drop (dead machine can't hold a credential); host removal → onMachineUnpaired drops all its mounts. GOTCHAS (real e2e catches): mint() returns {raw, rec} not {token,id}; NEVER execFileSync in anything sharing the process with /dav (event-loop block = FUSE IO hang); detached remote rclone needs FULL fd redirect (</dev/null >/dev/null 2>>log) or the ssh ControlMaster never frees; setsid is LINUX-ONLY — conditionally prefixed (macOS died at command-not-found with the error swallowed into the remote log, now tail'd back into the dialog); **TRAILING SLASH on a pull's shared path 403'd EVERY file (2.168.0, real userW Mac→VibeSpace report): the daemon serve-folder confines with `root + path.sep`, so a root ending in `/` (e.g. `/Users/me/Downloads/`) double-slashes the prefix (`…Downloads//`) — the root listing passes but every subpath 403s → rclone "couldn't list files: 403 Forbidden", mount never comes up. PUSH was immune (mountPush runs path.resolve). Fix strips the trailing slash in THREE places: mountPull (intake), _up (heals an existing record without a device-daemon update), serveFolder (daemon defense) — dbg-pair-smoke now pulls a trailing-slash path so its file-read assertion guards it.** Tests: test-machine-migrate.mjs (migration guard) + test-host-mounts(-tunnel).mjs (push e2e) + dbg-pair-smoke.mjs (pull e2e over a real dialed daemon)

### exit-proxy.js

  exit-proxy.js        — ExitProxyManager (task #164, 2.186.0): ON-DEMAND egress — an agent borrows a paired machine's network for a SINGLE command (a region / internal-VPN / fixed source IP), NOT the whole session. Two tiers via the `vibespace-exit` CLI (data/bin, static tracked, in AGENT_TOOLS): `use <machine>`/`url` = the device daemon serves a zero-dep in-daemon SOCKS5 (serve-socks/unserve-socks ops, CONNECT-only, DNS-on-exit) on its loopback, the server reaches it via the existing tcpForward and binds local `socks5h://127.0.0.1:<port>` (tool stays local, egress = the machine; proxy-aware TCP only); `run <machine> -- cmd` = execute natively on the machine via runCmd (universal fallback: ICMP/UDP/proxy-unaware/its DNS). Opt-in per machine (hosts.allowExit, default off — SSRF into its LAN; Remote-tab ↗ toggle). Routes: /api/exits + /api/hosts/:id/allow-exit (cookie UI) + /api/agent/exit[/use|/run] (vsst_). The loopback-only tunnel boundary holds (tcp-connect stays never-a-general-proxy; the SOCKS server is the one sanctioned egress, inside the machine owner's network). Tests: test-agentd-socks.mjs + test-exit-proxy.mjs

### port-forward.js

  port-forward.js      — PortForwardManager (B-0b60 tunnel path, 2.165.0): vscode-style port forwarding over the agentd data plane — NO frps/public exposure. detect(hostId) = `ss -tlnH || lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN` over dm.runCmd (lsof matched FIRST — its `(LISTEN)` suffix; an ss IP:port pattern also matches an lsof line; 2.177.0: ss runs with -p for process names, the /proc/net fallback resolves socket inodes→proc via a bounded /proc/*/fd scan, and _classify flags known NON-web daemons `hidden` (both ports UIs fold them behind '+N system listeners', the watch never toasts them; DBs deliberately stay visible); 2.180.0 (B-16d9): scans carry the pid and _classifyOrphan flags LOCAL listeners whose /proc/<pid>/cwd readlink ends ' (deleted)' (zombie dev server from a removed worktree — userL's 2×6GB next-servers) — watch announces each pid once incl. the baseline sweep, ports UIs show an 'orphan' tag + Kill (POST /api/ports/kill-orphan re-verifies deleted-cwd at kill time, never kills a healthy process)); 2.185.0: PROTO chips + override — detect({probe:true}, the UI route only) tags visible ports http/https/tcp via probeHostPort (local direct, remote through a fresh dm.tcpForward duplex; 5-min cache, cache-first + ≤12 uncached probes/scan, 3.5s budget); forwards carry proto/protoDetected/protoOverride (effective = override||detected), `POST /api/port-forward/:id/proto` sets the override and transparently RE-publishes a published forward in the new mode (publish passes the override as frpPublish's proto hint; restore passes override||publicProto so restarts keep the URL shape); forward(hostId, remotePort) binds a local 127.0.0.1:0 server piping each conn into dm.tcpForward(remotePort) (same primitive VNC/device-mounts use; loopback-only on the device — **the device is resolved PER CONNECTION via hosts.device(), never captured at _start (2.169.0): a dial re-dial stop()s the old DeviceManager, so a captured one bricked the forward forever while onMachineLinked skipped the "already live" record**), returns {localPort, url}; 2.186.3: a forward may target `targetHost:port` — another machine on the device's LAN (jump host); daemon tcp-connect gained an optional host (loopback default), the proto probe + record key follow the target, UI manual box accepts ip:port. persists data/port-forwards.json {id:`pf-<host>-<port>`, hostId, remotePort, label}, restore()/onMachineLinked re-establish (fresh local port each time; **re-publish passes preferSub/preferPort so shared public URLs survive restarts**), onMachineUnpaired drops a machine's forwards (**and frpUnpublishes its public URLs first — orphaned relay proxies had no remaining UI handle**; ssh host removal also drops forwards via the DELETE /api/hosts hook). Routes: GET /api/hosts/:id/ports, POST /api/hosts/:id/port-forward, GET /api/port-forwards, DELETE /api/port-forward/:id; broadcast `port-forwards-updated`. UI = sidebar-mounts `_showPortsDialog` (🔌 on a machine row; detected list + manual box + active forwards). **ARCHITECTURE GOTCHA: the forward binds the SERVER's loopback — the user's browser can't reach it directly**, so "Open" routes through the embedded browser's PROXY (`openBrowser(url, {proxy:true})` → node-unblocker on the server → server loopback → tunnel). frps PUBLIC exposure = a separate future path needing the reverse-proxy server infra (B-0b60 frps half). 2.352.0-2.353.0 scan-detail ladder (owner report: docker-host rows had zero detail — `ss -p` only names sockets YOUR user owns): `_enrichDocker(ports, run)` is ONE implementation fed the local execFile runner or the remote dm.runCmd runner — rung 1 = docker's own port table (`docker ps --format`, no root needed) → `docker:<container>`; rung 2 (2.353.0) = `/proc/net/tcp{,6}` uid column + `/etc/passwd` → `user:<name>` for whatever is STILL anonymous (root/other-user listeners; the owner is the best truth available when the process is invisible) — a docker-rung failure must NOT skip the uid rung. 2.353.0 WATCH INVARIANT (owner report: our reverse-tunnel port re-toasted as "new" per session lifecycle): `_watchSweep`'s per-host seen-set ACCUMULATES across sweeps (bounded 4000, clear = one silent re-baseline) — never reset it to the current snapshot; novelty is computed against the set BEFORE recording (seen === prev by reference). Regression-pinned (churn scenario: vanish → reappear stays silent). 2.358.0: publish(id,{sub}) = user-chosen FIXED frp subdomain (persisted publicSub); setPathMount/pathMountTarget = main-domain /svc/<name>/ mounts (unique names, live-port resolution per request — full essay in kb-api.md under POST /api/port-forward/:id/path; proxy = src/server/path-mounts.js, suite scripts/test-path-mounts.mjs in the gate). Tests: test-port-forward.mjs (unit, detect parsing + watch churn + real piping) + dbg-dial-session-e2e.mjs (5, real dialed daemon)

### plugins.js

  plugins.js           — PluginManager (2.140.0, B-2d44): generic host-capability plugins — install + PERSISTENT state (~/.vibespace/plugins/<id>/, home=PVC in fleet) + boot replay (enabled+desiredUp → restart with server) + guided setup + status. First plugin TAILSCALE: dual mode (kernel via /dev/net/tun+NET_ADMIN(sudo) / userspace --tun=userspace-networking + SOCKS5 localhost:1055), system tailscaled detected-never-managed (own --socket/--statedir coexist), login = capture `tailscale up`'s auth URL (Drive-OAuth pattern), node key in statedir ⇒ pod rebuild reconnects sans re-login. Routes /api/plugins* (server.js); UI src/lib/plugins-ui.js (⚙ → Plugins…); helm optional tun device (tun.enabled → NET_ADMIN + device-plugin resource). Second plugin FRP (2.166.0, B-0b60 public-URL half): runs frpc on the instance → a shared frps RELAY, so a forwarded machine port can be PUBLISHED to the public internet as a shareable link. Relay addr/port/token INJECTED via env `VIBESPACE_FRPS_ADDR/_PORT/_TOKEN` (fleet infra, NEVER in the repo — absent ⇒ `configured:false`, plugin no-ops). frpc runs with a base config + a local admin API (127.0.0.1:7400) + `includes` proxies dir; `frpPublish(name, localPort)` writes a per-port proxy toml, `frpc reload`s, polls the admin `/api/status` for `running` (retries a new public port in `VIBESPACE_FRP_PORT_MIN..MAX` on collision — the relay is fleet-shared), returns `http://<relay>:<port>/`. PortForwardManager.publish/unpublish wire it to a forward (persisted, re-published on restore). The relay itself (frps) is set up per the PRIVATE deploy dir runbook (the PRIVATE deploy repo, frp/), NOT this repo. **SUBDOMAIN mode + double-NAT pairing (2.183.0, B-5c1e): with `VIBESPACE_FRPS_SUBDOMAIN_HOST` set (or the plugin's subDomainHost config), frpPublish emits `type:https`+`https2http` proxies → `https://<sub>.<host>/` (persisted preferSub survives restarts). The Pair-a-device dialog's `viaRelay` option self-publishes the instance's own PORT and uses the relay subdomain as the dial base (bundle fetch + device-dial WS both route through it) — so a NAT'd instance can pair a NAT'd device (relay bridges both). Persisted selfDialSub keeps the URL stable across re-pairs. dial-pair route: `viaRelay` branch in server.js; plugin: setSelfDialSub/selfDialSub in status. PROTOCOL-AWARE publish (2.184.0): frpPublish `_probeProto`s the backend — HTTP→routed `type=http` subdomain (relay terminates TLS server-side w/ a wildcard cert via a Caddy front → browser-trusted, ZERO cert on instances), HTTPS→`https://ip:port` passthrough (backend's own cert), raw-TCP→`tcp://ip:port` (a DB/VNC has no Host/SNI). Never blindly https2http. `proto` rides the publish result + `publicProto` on the forward record. Relay side (private runbook): Caddy :443 wildcard-cert termination → frps vhostHTTP :8080; acme.sh Cloudflare DNS-01 auto-renew.** Test: scripts/test-frp-plugin.mjs (live, against the real relay) **THIRD plugin OPENCODE-SERVE (2026-09-07, owner decision — the ONE switch for the OpenCode background service, DEFAULT OFF):** the keeper, resource guard and every serve FACT stay in the SHARED module src/opencode-serve.js; this manager is only the CONTROL SURFACE and owns exactly three bits of user intent in data/plugins.json — `enabled`, `desiredUp`, `promptedAt`. `defs()` gains the row; `status('opencode-serve')` = `_ocStatus()` (installed/configured = the `opencode` CLI resolved by cli-env, running/port/pid/source/version/cpuPct/rssMb/parkedKind straight off `facts().state()`, `enabled` overridden by the env force, `prompted`, and `reason` = the shared `reasonUnavailable()` so the panel and every user action quote the SAME sentence); `start()` = `_ocStart()` (sets enabled+desiredUp, refuses loudly when the CLI is missing or `VIBESPACE_OPENCODE_SERVE=0` forces it off, then `locator.start()` — which CLEARS a previous stop/park, an explicit user start being the deliberate retry — and DROPS the facts caches so the sidebar does not keep showing the 10s negative-cached "nothing"); `stop()`/`setEnabled(id,false)` = `_ocStop()` (desiredUp false + `locator.stop({killRecorded:true})` — "off" must mean the process is GONE, including a serve merely ADOPTED from data/opencode-serve.json; the process-exit path deliberately leaves an adopted one alone so the next boot reuses it); `setConfig` REFUSES (there is nothing to configure — it binds a free loopback port); `install()` cannot install anything (VibeSpace runs the user's own CLI) so it says how to get OpenCode instead of offering a dead button; boot replay = enabled+desiredUp ⇒ start with the server. NEW GENERIC MEMBERS used by the harness layer: `wantsServiceUp(id)` (the plugin half of `decideAutostart`), `serviceState(id)`/`services()` (the compact {enabled,desiredUp,prompted,installed,running,starting,parked,envForced,reason} row cli-env puts on the /api/home harness row when a descriptor declares `store.servicePlugin`), and `setPrompted(id)` behind POST /api/plugins/:id/prompted (the first-use dialog is asked ONCE **per instance** — the flag is store state that BROADCASTS, never per-browser localStorage). Every `plugins-updated` broadcast now carries `services` beside `plugins`. Test: scripts/test-opencode-plugin.mjs.

### accounts.js

  accounts.js          — AccountManager (per-session billing-identity switching, backend-scoped via a `backend` field on each account — legacy records = 'claude'. THREE account types: (1) Claude API key — AES-256-GCM in data/accounts.json + .accounts-key, injected as ANTHROPIC_API_KEY; (2) Claude SUBSCRIPTION (2.56.0) — a dir data/subs/<id>/ holding ONLY that account's .credentials.json, read by the CLI via CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR (relocates the SECRET store ONLY; projects/sessions/settings stay in ~/.claude so transcripts+discovery stay shared — verified vs claude 2.1.205: Wde()=env??sn(). Empty dir→loggedIn:false, dir-with-creds→that login, ~/.claude untouched. **CONSEQUENCE (2.244.2, forensically verified on 2.1.222): claude's /status Organization/Email reads the NON-relocated ~/.claude.json — a relocated/host-held spawn DISPLAYS the machine login's identity while the TOKEN (= billing) is the dir account's (proof: the spawn refreshes the dir's token, machine store untouched). Never treat /status identity as billing proof for relocated logins; the host-held Test toast explains this to users**); (3) Codex/ChatGPT SUBSCRIPTION (2.59.0) — a dir data/codex-subs/cxs-<id>/ = an isolated CODEX_HOME whose sessions/+config.toml SYMLINK the shared ~/.codex (threads/settings shared, unified discovery — VERIFIED codex 0.142.5: symlinks survive a run, rollout lands in the shared dir), auth.json real per-account; createCodexSubscription/readCodexSubAuth(parses auth_mode + id_token JWT→email/plan)/finalizeCodexSubscription; login = `codex login --device-auth`. resolveForSpawn(requested, backend) returns {kind, localEnv:{VAR:val}, secret?, remoteCreds?} — claude-api→ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, claude-sub→CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR, codex-sub→CODEX_HOME; all PROCESS-ENV only, never argv. Separate defaults per backend (defaultAccountId / defaultCodexAccountId). REMOTE (P3, 2.59.0): subscriptions ship their creds dir to a host via `remoteCreds` — ws-handler tars the whitelisted files (.credentials.json/.claude.json | auth.json), streams over ssh-stdin into 0700 ~/.vibespace/<subs|codex-subs>/<id>, symlinks shared subdirs, prefixes the env var; 2.59.1: NO rm -rf (concurrent same-account sessions), tar --keep-newer-files newest-wins (host-side refresh rotates the live token — don't clobber it) + poison-heal probe (remote creds file missing its validity marker is rm'd pre-extract). list() returns tail-8 for keys, email/plan/loggedIn for subs (read-only). importFromCli() captures a Console primaryApiKey. **remove() POOL HYGIENE (2.335.0, owner report: deleting the account a pool sat on broke the whole pool): deleting a claude subscription strips it from every pool's explicit member list, re-points the default symlink to a live member (or unlinks it — absent = honestly signed-out, a dangling link is a phantom login), and re-points/drops per-session links that billed to it (`_healPoolsAfterRemoval`); gated by test-pool-signed-out (17).** **Re-login identity guard (2.333.0, owner directive): `reloginResolve(id)` finalizes an on-this-machine re-login by COMPARING the fresh login's identity to the record — same ⇒ in-place; matches ANOTHER record ⇒ creds move there ('moved'); unknown ⇒ auto-SPLIT to a new entry named by the email while the original stays signed-out with history intact (a friend logging the wrong account can never rebrand/pollute a record); route POST /api/accounts/:id/relogin-finalize, watcher in manage-agents toasts all outcomes; matrix scripts/test-account-relogin.mjs (13).** Lets the user's OWN subscription logins coexist per backend, switchable per session — the CLI's own /login only keeps one active at a time, so VibeSpace remembers each separately (like signing in/out without losing the others). **§ban-safety (2.59.1, after a real Max ban+refund — see [[reference_multi_subscription]]):** NO background /api/oauth/usage polling with sub tokens (that off-CLI fixed-cadence automated call on idle accounts is the #1 bot-flag: ToS §3.7 + 2026-02-20 OAuth-clarification) — usage is now captured PASSIVELY by data/bin/vibespace-usage (STATIC file), a statusLine hook injected via --settings into LOCAL TERMINAL sessions only that harvests the CLI's OWN rate_limits into data/usage-cache/<key>.json (zero API calls; chat/stream-json has no statusline → last-known; server ingestPassiveUsage reads the dir, no network). Subscription creds shipping to REMOTE is OFF by default (setting accounts.shipSubscriptionToRemote; ws-handler remoteAccountEnv gate throws with guidance) — a sub token from a datacenter IP is an impossible-travel/abuse signal; default = "log in on the host"; API keys always ship (sanctioned programmatic path)) **list() POOLED-FIRST (2.369.18, P1 codex pool UI): the pooled branch is evaluated BEFORE the per-backend branches for every backend — the codex branch used to win, so a codex pool listed as a bare ChatGPT login with no `pooled/current/currentName/memberOptions/auto/hot` (no client surface could operate it AND the engine's `type==='pooled' && a.auto` tick enumeration never saw it). The pool row reads identity THROUGH the symlink with the member's own auth reader (readCodexSubAuth / readSubCreds) and carries `hotSupported` = capsOf(backend).hotSwitch==='verified' (src/backend-caps.js) so the UI hides the hot toggle where the engine ignores it (codex). Pinned in test-codex-pool.**

  **LOGIN-SESSION EXPIRY (2026-09-07, src/login-expiry.js):** `loginStateOf(id, now)` answers "when does this account's LOGIN end" through the harness descriptor's OPTIONAL `creds.loginState` — claude declares it, every other harness simply does not and gets 'unknown' (an absent reader is honest; a fabricated 'ok' is not). Every subscription row in `list()` carries `loginState`; a POOL's row carries `poolLoginState` = its members' WORST plus `worstId`/`worstName` (a pool has no login of its own). `_poolLoginCandidates` asks TWO questions, not one (round-2 verifier, reproduced against this instance's own store): an EXPLICIT `members: [...]` list is what the user NAMED, so a signed-out member of it IS the pool's finding and stays in the summary; an IMPLICIT pool (`members: null` — the default, and the shape of the only pool here) has its membership DEFINED as `poolMembers()`, so an account that is signed out was never a member of it. Round 1 used the over-broad set for both, and on a pool whose three routing members were all logged in it read `logged-out` forever, drew a permanent red chip offering to re-login an account dead 700 h that the pool never routes to, and — because worst-of ranks 'logged-out' above 'expiring' — MASKED the CURRENT target's login dying in 16 h. The current link target is always in the set even if its own login has since died: the pool is pointed AT it, so it is the pool's problem however it got there. `resolveForSpawn`'s pool self-heal now ORDERS the alive list by `loginRank` (an 'expired' member still parses as loggedIn, because the tokens are only blanked at the CLI's next failed refresh, so without this the heal happily re-points onto a login that fails the first turn) — an ordering, never a filter: every member being expired must still start the session and then say so. **SAME-SPELLING TRAP:** the `/relogin` + `/subscription/:id/finalize` routes also return `loginState`, a STRING ('error') about how the login TERMINAL run went. Different objects, different types; never join them (a comment at that route says so too).

### accounts.js — LONG-LIVED TOKENS continuation

  (LONG-LIVED TOKENS, B-211a 2.253.0: a claude subscription can hold a `claude setup-token` oat (sk-ant-oat01-, 1yr, NO refresh token, inference-only — mechanism verified vs the 2.1.225 binary: CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN has TOP precedence in the credential getter, adapted as refreshToken:null/expiresAt:null, client never touches the token endpoint). AES-GCM at rest (a.oatEnc); setOat/clearOat/getOat + routes POST|DELETE /api/accounts/:id/oat; resolveForSpawn returns secret:{var:'CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN'} so REMOTE spawns reuse the API-key 0600-file+$(cat) channel (ssh AND dial — the sub ship-gate/dial-no-ship never fire for oat accounts) while loggedIn keeps the local DIR spawn (hot-swap/pool intact); oat-ONLY accounts spawn locally via env token. evaluateOnHost ranks held > linked > 'oat' > ship. EXPIRED oat: create refuses with re-mint guidance (a long-lived 401 has NO self-heal — the string in the binary says re-mint, no fallback to stored creds), logged-in+expired just drops the secret; boot serverNotice ≤21d. AMBIENT STRIP: CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN(+_FILE_DESCRIPTOR) joined AGENT_ENV_DROP + the local spawn env deletes — top-precedence means an inherited copy silently re-bills EVERY spawn; deliberate oat spawns re-add via localEnv AFTER the strip. Quota ⟳ can't ride an oat (no user:profile scope); passive statusline capture unaffected. UI: manage-agents _oatDialog (mint terminal + paste-back; the BROWSER login decides the account — the dialog warns in bold), row tags `· long-lived token` amber<30d red-expired, `· via long-lived token` on host views; New Session + billing switcher offer oat accounts (subBlock's oat short-circuit covers the LOCAL/no-verdict path — locally there ARE no verdicts, the legacy fallback used to grey oat-only accounts as never-signed-in). REVIEW-HARDENED INVARIANTS (17-finding adversarial pass, do not regress): _billingHow has an 'oat' rung BEFORE the remoteCreds 'ship' rung (a logged-in+oat account carries both — 'ship' misreported the exact action oat avoids); the ws-handler explicit-pick probe runs for oatOnly too (oatOnly doesn't throw, so the not-logged-in rescue never arms — without the probe a host-HELD account with an oat silently bypassed held precedence AND the identity-mismatch refusal; held wins, the probe grafts remoteCreds onto the oatOnly shape); hasOat in evaluateOnHost counts only VALID tokens + expired ⇒ reason 'oat-expired' everywhere (a usable expired verdict made the switcher kill-then-fail and let a DEFAULT account silently flip billing to the host login at expiry); export/import/merge all carry oatEnc (oat-only accounts otherwise migrate as dead records); the dial deviceAgentSetup catch throws only when `secret && !_hostSubReady` (held billing rides dialAcctAssign and cannot fail — an oat on the same account must not make degradable tool errors fatal); the expiry sweep is boot+6h-interval; helper terminals must NEVER open while a createModalShell overlay is up (z≈99998 vs window z≈9000 — the mint dialog closes FIRST; general rule for any dialog that opens a terminal); a locally-logged-in account with an expired remote-only oat is NOT blocked locally (subBlock: expired blocks only where the oat is the actual channel). ACCEPTED residuals: setOat cannot distinguish a pasted short-lived OAuth ACCESS token (same sk-ant-oat01 prefix — the dialog copy directs to the setup-token output); dial devices' 0600 .key sweep is ssh-only (same limit as account delete). Matrix: scripts/test-account-verdicts.mjs 45 asserts (incl. the 2.269.0 local-only-mac rung). **INTENDED-USAGE (official docs code.claude.com/docs/en/github-actions, 2.253.1): setup-token/CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN is Anthropic's DOCUMENTED path to run your subscription on remote/CI machines (generate locally → use as a secret on the runner) — cross-machine use is BY DESIGN. But the docs steer to an API key "for a secret shared across repositories … an OAuth token is tied to the subscription of the person who ran claude setup-token", so broad multi-context sharing is OUTSIDE the intended lane — the UI copy says "tied to your subscription, safer than shipping the login, use an API key for broadly-shared credentials", NOT "zero-risk anywhere". §ban-safety reality: oat removes refresh-rotation + shipping the interactive login, NOT the "subscription inference from a datacenter IP" signal (identical to shipping creds) — but it IS the sanctioned mechanism so the ToS posture is better. LIVE E2E scripts/dbg-oat-pool-live.mjs (throwaway server + REAL subscriptions + REAL inference turns, 21 asserts): real claude runs THROUGH the pool symlink, a target switch is picked up by a fresh spawn, a normal + a pooled session on one account resolve to the SAME real dir (shared refresh lock, verified via /proc), an oat-only account completes a real turn via the env token, ambient CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN is stripped from a normal spawn (/proc-verified). SAFETY: copies of subs with >1.5h token life ONLY, so a short test can never trigger a refresh that would rotate production's single-use token; production instance never restarted, its accounts.json/creds only READ.)

### account-pool-auto.js

  account-pool-auto.js — Pooled pseudo-account (B-6217, 2.251.0-2.252.0): account type 'pooled' — ONE switchable billing identity over the logged-in Claude subscriptions. The pool's creds dir data/subs/<poolId> IS a DIRECTORY SYMLINK to the current target's dir (the shape the whole design rests on — see the hot-swap SOLVED note in the billing section); switching = accounts.setPoolTarget (symlink-to-temp + rename + utimes on the target's creds so the CLI's mtime-gated cache invalidates). remove() UNLINKS only (rmSync on a symlinked dir THROWS, probed — never reaches the target). Members default = ALL logged-in claude subscriptions (explicit list narrowable; narrowing away the current target re-points). LOCAL-ONLY (evaluateOnHost verdict pool-local-only; macOS keychains key on the env STRING so the sharing breaks there). Attribution: passive statusline key + ledger by-time attribution both resolve pool→real target (recordUsageAttribution + the spawn env). **USAGE ACCOUNTING (2.256.0, user #4): the pool id is ALSO baked as a separate `pool` tag on each event** while `acct` stays the real target — so per-account $ and the global sum stay exactly correct with NO double-count, AND the Usage window gains a "By pool" breakdown (classic view) + a `pool` dashboard dimension showing the total that flowed THROUGH each pool (a legitimately-overlapping SEPARATE lens, never added into totals). recordUsageAttribution captures pool separately + dedups by `acct|pool`; recordAttribution writes it to attribution.ndjson; `_poolAt(sid,ts,attrib)` (by-time walk mirroring `_acctAt`) bakes `pool` onto Claude + Codex events at scan; aggregate() adds a sparse `pool` dim (SKIPS the null bucket so non-pooled events don't collect). Historical pre-2.256.0 events carry no pool tag (fine — they just don't appear in the pool lens). v2: `auto` = at claude turn end (maybePoolAutoSwitch in server.js), current target <5% remaining (MIN across 5h/7d/scoped-weekly; account-pool-auto.js pure decisions — reset-passed bucket = full, unknown member = 50, unknown CURRENT = never switch, 60s throttle) → re-point, re-record ledger attribution, and (cold) ask exactly ONE connected client to restart affected conversations via `pool-auto-switched` (headless = de-facto hot until a client appears). **v3 EDF RANKING (2.259.0, user-designed): quota is PERISHABLE — the target choice is earliest-WEEKLY-deadline-first, not most-remaining** (KEY FACT, user-confirmed + cache-verified ±1s: scoped weeklies like Fable are COMPONENTS of the same 7d window — same resetsAt — so each account has ONE weekly deadline and per-account EDF is optimal; the coupled-bucket objection collapses). 5h is EXCLUDED from ranking (burst limiter refilling ~33×/week — else every account's soonest reset is its 5h and the sort is noise) but stays in the <5% usability GATE. Same deadline ±60s → more remaining first (equal expiry ⇒ order can't change utilization; fewer switches); known-deadline members outrank unknown; fresh-after-reset (deadline unknowable from stale cache) ranks last. **HOT pools get a PROACTIVE tier** (`proactive: !!a.hot`): switch toward a strictly-sooner deadline (≥1h margin, never onto unknown data) BEFORE exhaustion — hot re-points are free; cold pools stay exhaustion-only (each switch restarts conversations). Test: scripts/test-pool-auto.mjs (29). **v4 ESTIMATED VIEWS (2.263.0, B-fcff): maybePoolAutoSwitch's readCache overlays dead-reckoned utilizations (usage-estimator estimateFor→overlayCache) so decisions see NOW, not the last stale reading; hot pools pass exhaustPct 10 (est<10% 提前切 before a limit interrupts a long task), cold keep 5; candidate gate stays 5.** `hot` = re-point only, no restart — the CLI re-reads creds on its next request (2.1.222-verified). ZERO API calls anywhere in the auto path (§ban-safety — decisions read data/usage-cache/*.json only). KNOWN SEAM: hot re-point leaves a terminal session's VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_KEY on the old target until restart (ledger correct either way). UI: Manage Agents + Add account… → pooled; row `→ target · email`; ⋯ Switch target / Members… / Auto / Hot. **Members dialog (2.257.0, user request): checkbox per claude subscription + an 'All subscriptions' option = the store's DYNAMIC default (members:null — includes accounts added LATER, the user's 全选 semantics), explicit selection = fixed narrowed set; an empty explicit selection is REFUSED in the dialog (updatePool maps [] → null = All, a silent surprise otherwise).** **v6 PER-KIND THRESHOLDS (2.268.2, user-designed: ABSOLUTE headroom decides — 1% of 7d ≈ \$17 vs 1% of 5h ≈ \$2-5): THRESH = 5h{hot 10, hard 5} / weekly{hot 5, hard 3} (7d + scoped share 'weekly'); exhaustion = ANY bucket under its kind's threshold, candidate gate = every bucket ≥ its hard floor, settle bar = every bucket ≥ kind hot + MIN_GAIN. decidePoolSwitch takes `hot` (exhaustPct param retired); a weekly-88% member is a healthy EDF target (~\$200 headroom) — the immediate effect that pulled Personal back as a target.
