Test suite 38 rewritten per-kind.** **v5 OSCILLATION GUARD (2.266.1, real incident: Personal<10% ⇄ Fish every tick — soft-exhaustion one direction, EDF-soonest the other, each hot re-point cold-starting BOTH sides' prompt caches): every VOLUNTARY move (soft-exhaustion at the raised hot threshold OR proactive EDF) must land on a member clearing the SETTLE BAR (exhaustPct+MIN_GAIN, i.e. the target won't itself trip exhaustion) — applied at candidate SELECTION (a below-bar EDF-first member is skipped for a later qualifying one); hard-dead (<5%) still takes scraps (best.eff > cur+MIN_GAIN). Engine belt in server.js: `_poolSwitchAt` — 180s dwell after any actual switch before the next voluntary move (hard-death exempt).** **v7 LIVENESS (2.312.0, real incident): an optimality ranking must never be the only escape hatch.** The hard-dead branch took `ranked[0]` — the EDF pick — but a member whose weekly window just ROLLED has no known deadline and sorts LAST by design, so a fully-fresh account was invisible and a pool at 0% with a 100%-on-every-bucket member present returned null for hot AND cold (reproduced from the reporter's own usage-cache shape). Now: `bestSettle || max MEASURED remaining` — the headroom scan excludes unknown members because their fabricated `UNKNOWN_REMAINING_PCT` beats every real reading and turns escape into a jump onto ignorance (caught by the existing anti-flap assert = built-in negative control). Paired no-silent-failure: `decidePoolSwitch({explain:true})` returns `{to:null, reason:'stuck'|'no-members'|'no-settleable'|'healthy'|…}` (the historical null contract is unchanged without the flag) and the engine notices on EVERY blocked reason — 2.312.0 surfaced only `stuck`, and the candidate gate dropping every member returns through `no-members`, i.e. the same incident down a different branch (2.313.0). TWO rules for any new notice here: the key must carry an hour bucket (`_sentNotices` is a per-BOOT permanent Set, so an unbucketed key reports a recurrence never again), and it must NAME THE BUCKET — buckets carry a reporting `label` (`5h`/`7d`/scoped name) because "every member is out of quota" is wrong under the nested model and points at the opposite user action from the truth ("Fable 0% spent, 7d still 39%" means run a different model, not wait a week). **PER-SESSION LINKS (plan C, 2.315.0 — B-a612 SHIPPED, user-decided): a local pooled session gets its OWN directory symlink `data/pool-links/<poolId>/<sessKey>` → the chosen member; the default `data/subs/<poolId>` link stays the state for display + identity-less sessions. Model identity = newest(last-assistant served model, set-model pick) else spawn model — persisted (spawnModel/pickedModel in session meta, restored in BOTH restore blocks). Placement + per-session auto-switch decide on the engine's SAME estimator-overlaid readCache PROJECTED to the session's family (src/model-family.js — the family vocabulary's shared home; unknown family ⇒ NO projection, never relax on ignorance); the switch re-points ONLY that session's link (per-session dwell keys; cold restarts only the moved session; pool-level `affected` skips own-link sessions). Attribution + resolveUsageKey + the billing badge resolve pool→poolCurrentFor(pool, webuiId) = the SESSION's target. Lifecycle: create at spawn / drop at kill / sweepSessionPoolLinks at boot; pool remove clears the links dir (symlinks only). Sealed orders carry `linkPaths`; the daemon matches a banner to the LONGEST-matching link and re-points only it (old daemons ignore the field = reduced coverage, never a wrong re-point). Invariant pinned in test-account-pool (31): N links → M accounts still leave ONE real dir per account = one refresh lock.** **PLACEMENT NEEDS A FAMILY, NOT A COMMAND (B-6b6d round 3):** `poolChooserForModel` short-circuits on `familyOfModel(null)` BEFORE `decidePoolSwitch`, so a spawn that declares no model is placed on the pool's current target with no evaluation at all — and since B-6b6d a claude resume deliberately commands nothing. ws-create therefore passes `data.model || data._placementModelHint` (the instance default, resolved beside the ladder) as a PLACEMENT-ONLY hint: families are coarse (`fable`/`opus`/`sonnet` — no point release, no `[1m]` variant), so the reasons the claude model READER was deleted do not apply to a family projection, and the hint never reaches `data.model`, the spawn spec, the argv or `_spawnModel`. Unset setting ⇒ `null` ⇒ the pre-plan-C default target. Pinned in test-codex-effort-meta leg ⑬ against the REAL engine + a REAL pool. **MANUAL HOT-SWITCH SWEEPS LIVE LINKS (2.355.0, userW's inc-msz495u6 "热切换死了"): plan C silently demoted the manual target change to new-sessions-only — poolCurrentFor prefers the session's own link, and setPoolTarget only moved the DEFAULT symlink (verified live: default → new member, 16 live links → old member). setPoolTarget(id, subId, {sweepSessionLinks}) now repoints every live per-session link when the USER route passes it (gated on pool.hot; logged '[pool] manual target → X: repointed N live session link(s)'); the ENGINE's calls never pass it — its per-session moves ARE the model-family projections a blanket sweep would clobber. Both semantics pinned (test-account-pool 33).** **MODEL BLINDNESS (the pre-2.315.0 state — kept for the exhaustion-test half that remains):  every model-scoped weekly is flattened to kind 'weekly' and `accountRemaining` is min-across-all, so one spent cap for a model NOBODY IS USING declares the account unusable and gates it out as a candidate; and one pool = ONE creds dir (`resolveForSpawn` returns `subDir(poolId)`), so concurrent conversations on different models always bill the same account. Adversarially verified: buckets are NESTED (5h + 7d + the family's cap when the account exposes one — `seven_day_opus:null` is real), and the server can NOT reliably know a live session's model (`_servedModel` is written only in the chat branch, never persisted, stale between set-model and the next assistant record) — so any model-aware gating must fall back to today's behaviour on ANY unknown. Design + real-data evidence: SharedContext/vibespace-pool-quota-model.md.** Tests: test-account-pool.mjs (16) + test-pool-auto.mjs (51, incl. the oscillation pair, the liveness set + its negative control, and the model-scoped semantics) + test-creds-symlink-swap.mjs (8) **codex池(2.368.20, P2冷切v1): createPool({backend}), 池symlink在data/codex-subs/<poolId>, 决策核心原样复用(codex读数同桶形), 热切结构性禁用等P3 symlink-swap验证; 信号=wrapper转发的rate_limits_updated+typed codex_error_info→recordCodexQuotaSignal(读数落当前成员cache, 耗尽先池切后arm auto-resume); sealed orders/plan-C保持claude-only。test-codex-pool(17)。** **2.368.21: codex撞限逃生梯=reset credit(codex.limitResetCredit默认off, wrapper动词codex-reset-credit/codex-read-limits经live app-server RPC)→池切→auto-resume; 引擎门控走src/backend-caps.js能力注册表(hot只在hotSwitch=verified——codex实验判定impossible: CODEX_HOME启动canonicalize+auth内容换垃圾turn照常完成即token驻内存)。test-codex-pool(28)。** **codex池UI(2.369.18, P1): accounts.list()池分支对所有backend先于codex分支求值(此前codex池行无pool字段, 客户端无法操作+引擎auto tick枚举不到); manage-agents两个名册共用ONE池菜单块`_poolMenuItems`(Switch target/Members…/Auto-switch/Hot——hot项按服务器`hotSupported`能力判定, codex显示禁用行说明结构性冷切)+`_createPoolDialog`(api()而非fetchJson: createPool 400曾被吞成'已创建')+`ROSTER_ICONS`/`rosterSort`共享(codex名册此前只按名排序); Members对话框按池的backend筛成员; 三处错标修正: server.js sessionAuth codex分支对池返回source:'pooled'+poolTarget(状态栏芯片/标题栏徽章/会话属性此前把codex池当普通ChatGPT账号), usage-meter codex芯片排除池(池不是配额持有者), session-card ⚙账号列表池行'name → target'(曾是'— API …undefined'), session-props计费行pooled/codex-subscription/codex-cli不再'Unknown'。**

**LOGIN LIFETIME IS A SECOND WAY TO BE UNUSABLE (2026-09-07, src/login-expiry.js).** Quota was the only thing that could make a member unusable, so a member whose OAuth LOGIN had expired kept being picked (its cache still read healthy) and every turn on it died. `decidePoolSwitch`/`rankPoolMembers` take an OPTIONAL `readLogin(id)`: **omit it and every rule is inert — the decision is byte-identical to the pre-login one** (a harness that cannot read a login deadline must not have its members quietly demoted; the suite pins that with an all-unknown control). Three rules: ① a member whose login is 'expired'/'logged-out' is never a candidate, and when that empties the list the reason is its own named outcome `all-logins-expired` with `loginBlocked: [{id,name,state,msLeft}]` — NOT `no-members`, because the fix is a 30-second re-login, not waiting a week for a window. **ROUND 2: that claim may only be made when the login gate is the ONLY thing that emptied the list.** The login gate runs BEFORE the quota gate, so round 1 let one login-blocked member outrank any number of quota-dead ones: with two members out of 5h quota and one 20 min from its login deadline, the engine announced "every other member's login session has expired or is about to", suppressed `alt`/`what`/`rest`, and sent the user to re-login accounts whose logins were fine. Now `quotaBlockedN` counts the quota drops, `why` is `'all-logins-expired'` only when it is zero, `loginBlocked` is carried on the quota reasons too, and the notice appends "Also needing a re-login: …" — and each member states its OWN fact through the pure `loginBlockedText` (expired / signed out / expires in 20 min), because "has expired" is a lie about a member that is merely `expiring`. **ROUND 3: the CURRENT member's own dead login is a THIRD wall, and it was being smuggled through `deadBuckets`.** `loginBlocked` skips the current member BY CONSTRUCTION, and `all-logins-expired` is derived from that list, so the round-2 login sentence could only ever describe the OTHERS. Round 2 prepended `loginBucketLabel(current)` into `deadBuckets` instead — which the engine renders as `spent: <list>` followed by the quota remedy — so a pool whose current member was signed out printed `Pool "P": no member can serve it — spent: login signed out. Conversations on it will hit a limit until a window resets, you add a member, or you move them off the pool.` once per hour, forever: a quota remedy for the one wall that never heals on a timer, and the single account the user had to act on was the only one the notice could not name. Now `bucketDetail` emits `fromLogin` (the pure `loginWallPhrase`, a CLAUSE — "login session expired" / "login is signed out" — not the list-item label) as its own field and `deadBuckets`/`liveBuckets` stay a pure QUOTA sentence; the current member's quota facts ride as a side note (`(its quota is also spent: 5h 0%)`) and are dropped entirely when its quota is fine, because "out of quota" about a 90% member is simply false ② a member inside NEAR_MS (30 min) is never a **VOLUNTARY** switch target (a conversation moved onto a login about to die just buys a second outage) — it still serves its OWN conversation, and `rankPoolMembers` drops it too because sealed orders are executed by the DEVICE hours later. **ROUND 2: it is NOT a filter on the ESCAPE path.** Round 1 made it one, so a hard-dead current member whose only quota-healthy candidate had 20 min of login left refused to move at all — strictly worse than the shipped behaviour (measured both ways: shipped moved to a 100%-quota member, round 1 returned `all-logins-expired`). NEAR candidates now collect in their own `nearRanked` list (they still pass the quota gate) and a hard-dead current member falls back to it when `ranked` is empty; the decision keeps the `reason` that says why we LEFT ('login-expired'/'exhausted' — a new reason string would have lost the 180s dwell-belt exemption) and adds `toLoginNear` for what we landed ON, so the notice can say the reprieve is short. Soft-exhausted and proactive tiers never see the scraps ③ `edfCompare` gains `loginPenalty` as its LAST tiebreak, so an 'expiring' member ranks below an EQUAL 'ok' one and nothing the quota rules separate is ever reordered. The CURRENT member's dead login is treated as hard exhaustion (escape now, take scraps, `reason: 'login-expired'`) and OVERRIDES the no-data hold — a file saying "cannot authenticate" is a fact, not ignorance; the MIN_GAIN anti-flap floor is skipped there because it compares remaining QUOTA, which is meaningless (and `cur.remaining` is null, so `null + 3` would have blocked every escape). Engine side: ONE memoized `poolReadLogin()` per tick feeds every decidePoolSwitch/rankPoolMembers call; `quotaVerdictFor` cannot answer `usable` through a dead login and contributes NO blockedUntil for it (unlike a quota wall it does not heal on a timer — only the user does); the auth-failure notice says "login session expired (refresh token expired at T) — re-login needed" when the file says so, keeping today's wording otherwise. **The hourly pool-blocked NOTICE is the PURE `poolBlockedNotice(d, {poolName, currentName})`, not an inline composition in the engine** (round 3): it was inline, so every test ever written asserted the decision object and none the sentence — which is exactly where the login-as-a-spent-bucket defect lived. Three walls, each claiming only what it knows: `fromLogin` (the current member — leads, and its `fix` is "Re-login <name> in Manage Agents"), `all-logins-expired` (the others — `why` lists them), and the quota arrays (the shipped sentence, byte-identical when no login is dead); a sibling that also needs a re-login is still named under the `Also needing a re-login:` clause when the CURRENT login is the headline, and the "best other member is at N%" clause is suppressed under any login wall because waiting is not the fix there. Gate: scripts/test-login-expiry.mjs §3 + §3d (the notice STRING, with the pure-quota and all-logins-expired sentences as byte-exact negative controls).

**AUTH-FAILURE EVICTION (2.335.0, owner report: 封号/欠费/过期 never triggered a switch — quota was the engine's only signal and a dead-auth account often still SHOWS rich quota):** pure `classifyAuthFailure({status,message,attempt})` (403 immediate; 401 needs attempt≥2 — a lone first 401 is the mid-refresh-race shape; ban/credit/oauth-expired messages immediate; 5xx/529 never). Engine side (usage-pool-engine): `notePoolAuthFailure(session, sid, info)` fed from session-stdout's api_retry + error-result records — marks the member (10min TTL, cleared early when the creds file is REWRITTEN i.e. re-login), re-points the session's link + the pool default off it via the same rankPoolMembers EDF, notices the user, cold pools get the restart ask; quota eval + chooser exclude marked members (`healthyPoolMembers`, falling back to the full list when EVERY member is marked). Runs regardless of `auto` — routing around a dead account is the 2.330.2 heal's live-session sibling, not quota optimization. **2.335.1 review hardening: evict throttle per (member,session) not per member (else sibling conversations stay pinned 60s each); default-move collects ALL default-billing sessions into the cold-pool restart ask; auth-mark clears on TOKEN-MATERIAL change never mtime (repointPoolSymlink utimes-bumps creds on every re-point); message-only classification needs API-layer context (`API_CTX_RE`) so agent tool output can't evict; removing a pool's LAST explicit member keeps the stale list (null = ALL subs — silent widening re-pointed billing to excluded accounts). ALSO: the restore smoke now overlays the WORKING TREE (pre-commit runs used to test HEAD — how 2.335.0's lost engine binding passed a green smoke).**

**WALL = GROUND TRUTH + OBSERVED-ORG AWARE EVALUATION (B-2c9b, owner-approved plan A+B 2026-09-05; usage-pool-engine).** Three same-day production occurrences: a walled turn's `onWalledTurn` read the POOL scope and logged "usable via <linked member>" off the linked member's healthy cache, while `usage-limit-banner-marked` had landed on a DIFFERENT account — the org the CLI was actually running on (193 `usage-reading-reattributed` events had moved the session's readings there); the pool only moved 1.5-3 min later when an edf/exhaustion reading arrived, and in-flight subagents died with "session limit" in the gap. **Invariant: a wall is ground truth for the account it lands on; readings only confirm.** ① **Plan A — demotion on BLOCKED entry.** Every wall signal carries the org-verified cache key its mark landed on (`noteWallSignal(session, {resetsAtMs, bucket, scopedName, key})` — claude rejected event, banner boolean (bucket = the name parseLimitBanner gave the cache mark; still NO time from text), all three codex sites) and a per-account ring of walled-turn timestamps (`_wallRing`, one entry per turn per key — a turn's banner + rejected event are ONE wall; 120s window, `wallCount` sums the identity group). `demoteWalledAccount(session, sigs)` resolves the wall's member (the last signal's key, else `orgVerifiedKey(session, usageCacheKeyFor(session), 'wall')`; pool members or the linked member only — non-pooled sessions return `not-pooled`, their own cache mark already is the truth) and marks the affected bucket exhausted NOW through `captureRateLimitEvent(…, source:'wall')` — the SAME write path readings use (utilization 1, status limited, resetsAt = the signal's future reset else the cached future reset else the bounded 5h/24h guess; fetchedAt now, so anchors / the estimator (re-anchors on the newer rawCache) / verdicts see it in this tick). **Misattribution guard (the 2.368.34 class — the link is never the org a running CLI is on):** demote only when the account saw ≥2 walled turns inside 120s (any sessions) OR it IS the session's OTel-observed org (≤10min); a single wall on an account the session is not verifiably on HOLDS (journal `[wall] <id>: single wall on <name> (session not observed there) — holding the demotion`, event `wall-demote-held`) — the near-fire/pre-fire probe or the next wall inside 120s settles it. Journal `[wall] demoted <name> <bucket> until <iso> (n walls / observed-org)`, event `wall-demote`. **②bis RE-BASED 2026-09-07 (owner decision ut-1c6c15a2db ①/④, after the fire loop): plan B's rule is REFUTED for BLOCKING decisions and kept for VALUES.** The premise was "a hot-switched CLI keeps its old token, so the observation names what it is burning". The owner's post-mortem corrected it: `organization.id` on an `api_request` is the identity the CLI cached in its config dir at SPAWN — the credential file itself IS re-read (that is exactly what the re-point's mtime bump exists for). Under plan B every rejection was therefore filed against the spawn-time org while the LINKED member — whose credentials the process actually reads — stayed "healthy" in the cache forever; the verdict answered "usable via <link>" on every cycle, the per-session pass "switched" to the member the link was already on (740 `(re-point, same target)` lines in the incident journal), and `fireNow()` continued a session that was rejected again half a second later. Now: **a REJECTION is a fact about the credential SLOT** — `wallSlotFor(session)` = the per-session link (or the pool default link for a session without one), validated as a real member of THIS pool (`validateBillingSlot`, reasons `no-slot`/`slot-not-a-member`/`slot-unreadable`; ~~`poolMembers()` already filters by loggedIn, so a separate credentials leg would be an unsatisfiable guard — deleted functionality wearing a check's clothes~~ — **REFUTED 2026-09-07**: `parseAuth` reports `loggedIn:true` for a login whose access AND refresh tokens have BOTH expired, so that leg is satisfiable and it is exactly the gap that let dead-account readings look authorised; `validateBillingSlot` now carries it as `slot-wiped`/`slot-expired`/… and the suite proves the state leg fires. The suite still pins that every named reason is reachable), and its `slotOk` verdict rides ON THE SIGNAL because it is only true AT THE MOMENT THE REJECTION ARRIVES (by turn end the pool has usually re-pointed the link off the account that just refused us — measured in the repro harness). The function split is the fix: `sessionBillingMember` (link, slot-validated) answers every BLOCKING question — the demotion target, the verdict order + `viaId`, the per-session switch's `currentId`, both probe targets, `_wallScope`, and the loop breaker's fire identity; ~~`sessionReadingMember` (observed over link, the old body verbatim) answers only `resolveUsageKey`~~ — **REFUTED AND DELETED 2026-09-07 (readings-by-slot, the entry further down this file)**: a utilization NUMBER does describe whatever token produced it, but the observation never NAMED that token — it names the identity the CLI cached at SPAWN, so this half filed every hot-switched session's values under the account it started on. The one resolver is now `readingSlotFor` (the validated credential slot, turn-pinned, `wallSlotFor`'s twin) and `resolveUsageKey` calls `sessionBillingMember` like every blocking question; the observation survives only as `corroborateReading` (a label). The observation is corroboration: it still logs the divergence once per session per 10min, still appears in the walled-turn line as `[billing <link>, OTel observed <org>]`, and still authorises a demotion on the observed member (that rung stands). **The verification ladder gains a top rung**: a wall on the session's OWN validated slot is ground truth by itself (we know what the symlink points at — strictly better evidence than a telemetry attribute); anything else still needs ≥2 walls in 120s or the observed-org match, and the journal names which rung authorised it (`(1 walls / credential slot)` / `(1 walls / observed-org)` / `(2 walls / 2-walls)`). **Plus a per-SESSION memory**: `noteSessionWall(sid, member)` records "this member rejected THIS conversation" (10min, `SESSION_WALL_MS`, written even when the demotion is HELD — "it rejected me" is true either way), unioned with the breaker's own `recentFireFailures`; `quotaVerdictFor` cannot answer `usable` through such a member and `decidePoolSwitch({exclude})` cannot pick one (a NAMED pure input, reported as `all-rejected` — never a silently empty candidate list; `pessimism` was the wrong tool: "it just said no to this conversation" is not a percentage, and docking it would let a big enough gap re-select it next tick). Known bound: a member whose demotion was HELD and whose cache reads healthy is quarantined for 10min, so the arm may sit on the rejection's own `resetsAt` rather than the shorter quarantine — the wall probe ladder (0→30m→1h→2h) re-verdicts and takes over. **③ ROUND 3 — the slot is resolved ONCE PER REJECTION, not once per RECORD.** ONE rejection arrives as several records (`rate_limit_event` :323, the assistant banner :328, a banner inside a `task_notification` :563 — three producers in claude-stream-json.js, and `noteWallSignal` already counts a banner + its rejected event as ONE wall), and EVERY producer calls `maybePoolAutoSwitch` the moment it marks the cache — so the first record re-points the link and asking `wallSlotFor` again for the second one keyed it to the member the pool had just moved TO, demoting that HEALTHY member with `credential slot` authority and `noteSessionWall`-ing it (reproduced both orders + the two-events shape; harm = `usable:false, blockedUntil:+2h` against a fresh reading showing it 95% healthy, where the fix near-arms and continues in ~60s). Rejection records now read **`rejectionSlotFor(session)`** = `wallSlotFor` PINNED to the first keyed signal of `_turnWallSigs` (key AND `slotOk`); `wallSlotFor` remains the fresh reading for `wallKeyFor`/`fireIdentityFor` ("where would a CONTINUE land right now" is a different question) and for the pin's no-signal fallback. The pin is the TURN because that is what defines it — a re-point does not reach a RUNNING CLI (2.361.0) — and `noteTurnEnd` clears the signals, so it is exactly one turn wide. **The near-arm's `nearArmVeto` is an ASSERTION, not the protection** (it re-reads `sessionWalledMembers` and `console.warn`s `INVARIANT VIOLATED`; the protection is noteSessionWall + the verdict's walled override, which the suite executes functionally). Gate: test-auto-resume §11 (rewritten: the refuted rule is recorded, not deleted) + scripts/test-auto-resume-loop.mjs (158; §4d two-records-one-rejection + §4e near-arm, both mutation-tested).

② **Plan B — observed-org aware evaluation** (BLOCKING half superseded by ②bis above; the VALUES half stands)**.** `observedMemberFor(session, poolId)` = the OTel truth stream's latest org for the session (`OBSERVED_ORG_RECENT_MS` = 10min) resolved to a pool member; `sessionCurrentMember` = observed else link ({id, linkedId, observedId, divergent}), logging the divergence once per session per 10min (`[pool] session <id> observed on <X> while linked to <Y>`, event `pool-observed-divergence`). It drives: `resolveUsageKey` for pooled sessions (the live odometer feeds, probe-session matching and every derived cache key now attribute to the observed member — pooled sessions with a fresh observation therefore resolve straight to it and `usage-reading-reattributed` counts drop for them, by design); `quotaVerdictFor(scope, {model, session})` (the current member is judged FIRST so `via` names the account the session is on; the verdict carries `on/linked/divergent` + a reason note); the per-session auto-switch pass (decides FROM the observed member — a session walled on it moves even when its link reads healthy; when the chosen target is the link itself the re-point is journal-only `(re-point, same target)` + the creds-mtime bump the CLI re-reads on, no user notice); both probe targets (wall ladder + pre-fire gate). The hot-switch symlink still targets the CHOSEN member — this changes what the engine BELIEVES the session is burning, never the material. ③ **Order on BLOCKED entry** (`noteTurnEnd` → `onWalledTurn`): demote → session-aware verdict → arm → pool evaluation in `finally` (AFTER the arm, so a hot switch's `fireNow()` finds the session armed and continues it in the same tick; the normal-turn branch keeps its own `maybePoolAutoSwitch`). Gate: test-auto-resume §11 (functional — a REAL AccountManager pool with real per-session links + the real engine + the real auto-resume module + a fake OTel source, fixtures shaped from the journal: one wall holds, two walls inside 120s demote with the signal's reset and switch+continue in ONE noteTurnEnd, observed-org divergence names the observed member and moves the link, stale observations fall back, named non-demotions, the ring counts turns not records, order-of-operations pins) + test-rate-limit-capture ⑧ (the `source` option).

### harnesses/ (the harness registry — S4 QuotaSignalSource)

  harnesses/index.js — THE harness registry (docs/design-harness-plugins.md §2.2 "src/harnesses/index.js 是唯一注册表"; S4 landed it with the QUOTA contract first, later slices add theirs beside it). `get(id)` is LOUD on an unknown or empty id — core never falls through to claude (the gemini-as-claude class); `has/list/ids`; `register(descriptor, {replace})` for contributed harnesses (built-ins can never be replaced/unregistered; a duplicate contributed id needs the explicit flag — silent shadowing is how twins are born); `assertQuotaContract` validates every registration (normalize/signalFromStream/classifyAuthFailure functions + `probe ∈ QUOTA_PROBE_RUNGS` = 'cli-usage'|'rpc-rate-limits'|null); `NULL_QUOTA` (null-quota.js, frozen) is the shell descriptor's source AND the engine's degrade target for an unknown id. Descriptors: claude.js / codex.js / shell.js = `{id, label, quota}`. SHARED tier (test-architecture): pure hooks over SHARED parsers, never reaching up into ORCH — the daemon may bundle them; the S5 stream CONSUMERS themselves stayed ORCH (src/server/stdout/, below) because they need the engine/hosts/ledger, so a descriptor only names the protocol.
  harnesses/claude-quota.js — the claude QuotaSignalSource. `normalize(raw, nowMs)` dispatches by SHAPE over the three claude reading channels: a string = the `claude -p /usage` panel text (parseCliUsageText, MOVED here verbatim from usage-routes — the MONTHS/zonedEpoch/_parseCliResetTime helpers with it), an object with `rate_limits` = the get_usage control payload (ClaudeCodeAdapter.parseGetUsageResponse, unchanged), an object with five_hour/seven_day/limits[] = the GET /api/oauth/usage REST JSON (parseOAuthUsage, MOVED verbatim = usage-routes' former `_parseUsage`; the route + the device-op consumer bind the same function). `signalFromStream(record)`: `rate_limit_event` → `{source:'rate_limit_event', kind: rejected⇒'exhausted' | utilization⇒'reading' | else 'meta', bucket, scopedName, resetsAtMs, ev}` (the parse is rate-limit-capture's parseRateLimitEvent — reached through the registry, no twin); an assistant text matching the LIMIT BANNER regex → `{source:'limit-banner', kind:'exhausted', bucket, scopedName, resetsAtMs:0}` (a BOOLEAN wall signal — design-wall-machine §1: text is never a data source; parseLimitBanner only names the bucket for the passive cache mark). `probe = capsOf('claude').quotaProbe = 'cli-usage'`. `classifyAuthFailure` = account-pool-auto's Anthropic-wording classifier, the SAME function object (pinned).
  harnesses/codex-quota.js — the codex QuotaSignalSource, PURE. `normalize` = the former usage-routes normalizeCodexRateLimit MOVED verbatim (2.368.18 P0 semantics: windows classify by LENGTH ≤480min⇒fiveHour else sevenDay, never by primary/secondary position; every exhaustion marker survives: rateLimitReachedType/spendControlReached/credits; the tripped window reads dead) — usage-routes re-exports it under the old name and the engine reads it through the registry. Owns the typed exhaustion vocabulary `EXHAUSTION_RE` (usage_limit_reached|quota_exceeded|usage_not_included|workspace_owner_usage_limit_reached|workspace_member_usage_limit_reached|workspace_member_credits_depleted) and `trippedWindow(snap)` (primary→burst window when present else weekly; secondary→weekly else burst — the engine's 2.368.20 rule). `signalFromStream(record)` accepts the server frame `{type:'event_msg', payload}` OR the bare payload: `rate_limits_updated` → kind 'exhausted' (with `tripped` + resetsAtMs) | 'reading' | 'probe-failed' (the on-demand `{error}` reply — never silent), snapshot carries the on-demand resetCredits count; `task_failed` → 'exhausted' (errorInfo, resetsAtSec/Ms, snapshot when one rode along) | 'auth-failure' (the typed `unauthorized` enum only — a plain error text is not a quota signal) | null; `reset_credit_result` → 'recovered' | 'reset-credit-failed'. `probe = 'rpc-rate-limits'`. `classifyAuthFailure` in OpenAI/codex wording (typed unauthorized; "401 Unauthorized"/invalid_grant/token expired|revoked/not logged in/org|account deactivated|suspended; 403 immediate; 401 needs attempt≥2 — the same refresh-race rule as claude; 5xx/429 never). Codex auth-failure EVICTION is still v1 surfaced-only (`codex-auth-failure` event): the engine's member mark keys on the claude creds path (credsTokenSig) — the S2 accounts strategy object owns that arm.
  **THE CAPS-ROUTED QUOTA PROBE (S4, usage-pool-engine `probeQuotaForKey(target, {session, timeoutMs=20000})`)** — ONE dispatcher for every "refresh this identity's quota now" need: the wall machine's BLOCKED-entry verification probe, the wall probe ladder (0→30m→1h→2h), and the auto-resume pre-fire gate (`beforeAutoResumeFire`). `quotaBackendFor(key, session)` names the identity's harness (a named account's `backend` — legacy records = claude, same rule as accounts._acctBackend/capsOf; `__global_codex__` = codex; identity-less keys `__global__`/`host-*` take the asking session's own backend; else claude) and `quotaSourceFor(backend).probe` names the rung: **'cli-usage'** = `getQuotaProbe()` = usage-routes refreshViaCliPanel (`claude -p /usage`, the owner-approved auto-cli channel — server.js wiring unchanged, still the ONE spawn site); **'rpc-rate-limits'** = pick a LIVE local codex chat session on the identity (the asking session first — it sits idle-walled on exactly that identity with its app-server up; else any session whose `codexQuotaKeyFor` = account→pool current member→`__global_codex__` lands in the target's identity group), write the wrapper verb `{type:'codex-read-limits'}` (account/rateLimits/read on the session's own app-server — the official client makes the fetch, §ban-safety class unchanged) and await the next `rate_limits_updated` through `session._codexLimitsWaiters` (registered in session-schema; `recordCodexQuotaSignal` settles the waiters AFTER the cache write so the verdict taken next already reads the fresh file; a failed on-demand read settles `ok:false` with the wrapper's error; a bounded timeout drops the waiter); **null** (shell / unknown harness → NULL source) = nothing runs, the verdict stands. Before S4 every probe spawned `claude -p /usage` whatever the backend — a codex identity burned a pointless claude process and never got a fresh reading (design-harness-plugins.md §1 P2). Result `{ok, rung, backend, reason}` — every miss is named. Unknown harness id ⇒ one console.warn + `quota-harness-unknown` event + the NULL source, never a claude parse. Gate: scripts/test-quota-source.mjs (functional: real engine.create() with fake sessions — codex identity rides the live session + never spawns claude; claude identity keeps cli-usage; shell/unknown run nothing; timeout + failed read honest; beforeAutoResumeFire routed; + registry contract, every harness's normalize/signalFromStream/classifyAuthFailure on real shapes, wiring pins, and a vendor-whitelist sub-run).

### rate-limit-capture.js

  rate-limit-capture.js — PASSIVE quota capture from the CLI's own `rate_limit_event` stream-json records (B-e5c9, 2.289.0; discovered by 2.1.226 binary forensics — a first-class stdout record "emitted when rate limit info changes" VibeSpace dropped as unknown forever). Rides real API responses ⇒ ZERO extra calls (§ban-safety: purely passive; the banned auto-get_usage and a billable `-p` probe are both worse AND `-p` must carry a prompt + returns no 5h/7d data). Closes the chat/stream-json gap (no statusline ⇒ last-known quota only). `parseRateLimitEvent(msg)` (both key casings, 2.227.6; utilization integer-%→0..1; all 16 rate_limit_info fields) + `captureRateLimitEvent({cacheDir,key,identityIds,ev})` — ONE impl for local AND remote (remote chat stdout relays through the same server parse at server.js:1616; `usageCacheKeyFor` routes a host-login remote session to `host-<id>` not `__global__`, also fixing remote banner attribution). `status:'rejected'`⇒bucket dead+pool re-eval (banner parity); `utilization`⇒usage-cache write that becomes a dead-reckoning ANCHOR next sweep (estimator self-calibrates from live chat). Carries the 2.267.0 identity-group anti-poison discipline + ONE addition: **`fetchedAt` bumps ONLY for a real READING** (utilization or rejected) — a resetsAt/overage-only event updates fields in place WITHOUT promoting a stale file to 'freshest' (false-anchor poison). Server: recordRateLimitEvent in the claude stdout dispatch. Test: scripts/test-rate-limit-capture.mjs (20). **B-2c9b: `captureRateLimitEvent({…, source})` labels the reading write (default 'rate-limit-event'; the wall machine's ground-truth demotion passes 'wall') — ONE write path for readings AND walls, never a twin writer (suite ⑧).** **2026-09-07: `{…, corroborated}` rides the same write** — whether the OTel observation agreed with the credential SLOT this reading was filed on. A LABEL for the panel and for forensics; the KEY comes from `readingSlotFor` and nothing else (see the readings-by-slot section).

### usage-anchors.js

  usage-anchors.js     — Quota dead-reckoning DATA FOUNDATION (2.261.0, user-designed 惯性导航 step 1): every ground-truth usage reading (statusline/⟳/get_usage/limit-banner — any usage-cache write) becomes an ANCHOR paired with the local ledger cost since the previous anchor (split by model family so Fable-bucket rates stay derivable) in data/usage-anchors/anchors-<identity>.ndjson; 60s local sweep (sweepUsageAnchors in server.js), zero API calls. **PRIMARY KEY = identity (orgUuid > lowercased email > account id), NOT the minted sub-<hex> id** — a sub's history survives remove+re-add (re-add mints a fresh id; same-login re-add WITHOUT remove already merges). Raw pairs are kept forever (never just an EWMA) so prediction models re-derive offline. **2.263.0: the sweep GROUPS BY IDENTITY** (an org-merged login — machine login + named sub — double-anchored one identity from two cache files, each record's costSince missing the sibling's spend; real data bug from the ProblemFactory analysis): one identity = one anchor stream, freshest cache wins as source, cost sums across ALL the identity's account ids via `costBetweenMulti` (union incl. ids from the anchor file — removed/re-added subs). Anchors also carry `calib` (predicted-vs-actual per bucket, from usage-estimator.predictCalib) + `accountIds`. Empirical constraint (recorded in SharedContext): a real account's LOCAL ledger saw only ~1/7 of its consumption (multi-device) — fixed token→% rates are impossible, per-identity learning is mandatory; conversely a POD-EXCLUSIVE account (ProblemFactory) measured rate≈1.0 and the odometer method (cost(window)/u) recovered its quotas to ±3% (5h≈$500, 7d≈$1730, Fable≈$875 — Max 20x). Test: scripts/test-usage-anchors.mjs. **2.340.0 calibration batch (owner audit: bursts under-estimated 20-45%): pairs require SAME-SOURCE endpoints; ≥5pt jumps need cost corroboration (≥20% of prior-implied) or taint; org-merged identities union '__global__' cost under a 14d stream-recency gate (deliberate 2.263 reversal, both semantics tested); estimator extrapolates the ~20s un-scanned ledger tail (lagS knob); seven_day_<model> rate_limit_events write scopedWeekly readings — the scoped buckets' only passive channel.** 2.369.36: costBetweenMulti is interval-memoized (key ids|from|to|events-at-or-before-to, 20k entries) — anchor pairs are immutable history; only a backfill INTO the interval recomputes it. **2.369.43 (review batch) closed the key's two holes: the PRICE TABLE is part of the key** (`usageHistory.pricingToken()`, a sha1 of {tiers,accounts} recomputed only when `_pricing` is replaced) — a tier edit or a per-account discount changes every historical cost while the event count is untouched, so the memo served pre-edit dollars into the learned rates forever (repro: 10x tier edit and a 50% discount both froze at the old total); a ledger that cannot version its prices is NOT memoized. **Eviction picks a RANDOM victim, not FIFO**: learnRates re-walks one identity's pairs in the same order each recompute = a cyclic reference string, where FIFO/LRU cliff (measured: 20000 keys 100% hits, 21000 keys 0.0% — the inc-mtox23xw per-pair walk returns) while random decays as cap/working-set (53% at 1.3x cap). The working set is real and grows: production carries 5124 qualifying pairs over 28 days across 8 identities (~180/day, biggest identity 1126) and anchors are never pruned. The memo hangs off the UsageHistory instance (WeakMap), never the module. **2026-09-07: anchors inherit whatever the caches were keyed by** — the readings-by-slot defect poisoned 248 records across 3 identity streams on this instance before it was found, and the repair migration archives them and voids the pairs they anchored (`costSince:null`, which extractPairs already skips). An anchor names an ACCOUNT, never a session, so it can only ever be discarded, not re-attributed — which is why the fix had to move upstream to the producers.

### usage-estimator.js

  usage-estimator.js   — Quota dead-reckoning ESTIMATOR (B-fcff v2, 2.263.0): est_u = anchor_u + learned_rate × ledger_cost_since_anchor, per identity per bucket. Rates = WLS through origin over ALL anchor pairs (Σ Δu / Σ cost — integer-% quantization noise averages out; NEVER use a single pair, ±3×) blended with the measured Max-20x priors (CLAUDE_MAX_PRIOR_FULL_USD 5h $500 / 7d $1730 / scoped:fable $875; pseudo-pair weight PRIOR_WEIGHT_DU=0.15 so real data dominates by ~15% observed movement; Pro accounts start off and self-correct — banner is the hard backstop). Pair guards (2.265.0 calib-driven additions FIRST: **at-cap readings u≥0.995 censor the pair** (banner marks + exhausted ⟳ carry no linear information — the 9%→100%-over-$50 pair taught full≈$139 vs true $500) and **pair costs RECOMPUTE from the live ledger at learn time** (frozen costSince lags fast burns; 10-min rate-cache TTL; zero-live-total falls back to the snapshot)); reset moved >120s inside a pair = void; u-decrease >0.02 without reset change = anomaly skip; small negatives clamp to 0 with cost KEPT. Window-roll estimation: 7d/scoped re-base from 0 at the passed reset boundary (+7d steps — fixed cadence); **5h ABSTAINS after its reset (window opens at first-request-after-idle, start unknowable)**. UsageEstimator class: mtime-cached anchor reads, rate memo invalidated per new anchor, 30s estimate memo (taskbar polls /api/usage at 8s), rates.json snapshot for offline inspection; **usageHistory is passed as a LAZY () => ref — the estimator is constructed ~2900 lines above usageHistory's declaration (TDZ boot crash, smoke-caught)**. **MEMO STALENESS (review major, do not regress): the 30s estimate memo records baseFetchedAt and BUSTS the moment rawCache.fetchedAt is newer — a frozen estimate otherwise falsely trips the est<reading roll display AND masks a limit-banner mark (utilization 1) from the pool's immediate switch.** More review-hardened guards: resetsAt-less buckets (statusline defensive `resetsAt:0`) dead-reckon only within ONE window length then abstain (a 3-day anchor + $600 cost rendered a 120% 5h arc, verifier-repro'd); `status:'unknown'` fabricated buckets never anchor (maybeRecord nulls them — a u:0 fabrication + next real reading forged ~6× rate inflation); legacy pre-2.263 interleaved records (org-merge under-counted cost) excluded from learning via the accountIds grouped-record signature (mixed-id files learn from marked pairs only); scoped pairs with identical asOf carry no information (skipped); MIN_GAIN_PCT=3 anti-flap on the pool exhaustion tier. **2.268.0/2.268.1 5h TOKEN-CLASS REGRESSION (B-536b; 3-class final): the 5h bucket ≈ INPUT-CLASS token count with ~ZERO weight on cache READS — real-data 3-var LS across 3 identities in tight agreement: cr-full $2660-2782, fresh $179-192, cw $190-261 (cw's premium over fresh = exactly our 1.25-2× cache-write pricing; the interim 2-class 'cw burns half' was an artifact of cr hiding in both groups). learnRates fits du = rateCw×cw$ + rateCr×cr$ + rateFresh×fresh$ per identity (pure-class priors $500/$3000/$200, PRIOR_WEIGHT_DU each; per-class sanity bounds + positive coefficients withhold absurd fits → blended fallback), costBetweenMulti emits byClass{cw,cr,other} (historical pairs get splits RETROACTIVELY via the learn-time recompute), the live ring carries cwUsd+crUsd, estimateBuckets predicts per component when the source has the split. 7d/scoped stay blended (long windows average the mix; calib error already 0-3pts). Incremental caching makes conversation windows cr-DOMINATED — that's why the 2-class version still overestimated (60-vs-51; 3-class re-predicts +2.5 vs actual +3).** **2.267.3 EST-2× (live-odometer double count, arithmetic-confirmed: 2×$34.73/$256.5 = the exact 27% the user saw vs ⟳ 9%): STDOUT records carry NO requestId — ring entries key on msg.id while ledger events key on requestId, so `known.has(rid)` NEVER excluded a scanned entry and every streamed request counted twice while a conversation was active. Ledger events now bake `mid` (message.id), _loadEvents keeps a `mids` set, _liveDelta excludes against BOTH id spaces; a re-noted rid upgrades to max-usd (stdout emits the same msg.id up to 3× with growing usage). JOIN-FIELD RULE: any dedup between the stream and the ledger must join on an id BOTH transports carry.** **2.267.0 CALIBRATION PASS (user report 高估; the reset logic was sound — the poison was upstream): ①BOUNCE-BACK TAINT — a u-decrease anomaly pair voids itself AND the next pair off that anchor (a stale sibling cache file promoted to freshest anchored a week-old reading; the recovery pair du+32%/$1.68 taught a ~30%-hot fable rate = the user-visible overestimate; real-data re-derive: fable implied-full $556→$724). ②markLimitBanner writes the dead bucket into EVERY identity-group cache file, basing the promoted write on the group's FRESHEST file and never bumping a sibling's fetchedAt (the flap's source: one-file marks promoted week-stale sibling buckets). ③predictCalib honesty guards (at-cap/one-sided-resetsAt/window-length span skipped — p100/a0 noise rows drowned the calib signal).** Consumers: maybePoolAutoSwitch overlays estimates onto readCache (hot pools pass exhaustPct 10 = est<10% 提前切; cold keep 5; candidate gate stays 5; **readCache reads through the IDENTITY GROUP's freshest cache file** — org-merged accounts keep TWO files and ⟳ truth lands in whichever was targeted, exhaustion-#2 root #2) + **EVENT-DRIVEN evaluation (2.266.0, user-designed after exhaustion #2: Fable 51%→100% in ~4min between polling ticks): every streamed usage record (main + sidechain) feeds `usageEstimator.noteLive` (in-memory ring, rid-deduped against the ledger's own rid set — never double-counts once the scan absorbs; noteLive busts the estimate memo) and `kickPoolEval` (5s throttle; per-pool gate 10s; anti-flap = MIN_GAIN margin, never cadence); workflow agents are file-only (no stdout, IN-PROCESS API calls — no process to wrap) so the launch ack "Run ID: wf_…" arms the workflow tailer (src/workflow-usage-tailer.js since 2.270.0): run-dir fs.watch + 5s belt poll tails agent-*.jsonl growth (per-file byte offsets, complete-lines-only) into noteLive within ~1-6s — the file-level equivalent of wrapping them; teardown 30min idle/session death; rid-dedup makes re-reads harmless. **THE ACK-BEATS-MKDIR RACE (2.270.0, user caught est 30-vs-65 live): the ack lands ~17ms BEFORE the harness creates the run dir — the original one-shot existsSync precondition lost EVERY run since 2.266.0 (zero armed events in a month of telemetry, proven against healthy sibling event names), so workflow bursts only reached the estimator via the 180s ledger scan. The tailer now RETRIES until the dir appears (2s cadence, 3min give-up); RULE: anything waiting on a path the harness creates asynchronously must retry, never probe once. Observability so it can't die silently again: `wf-usage-noted` metric per drain + the armed event fires on FIRST REAL DRAIN (an arm-time event proves nothing). Test: scripts/test-workflow-usage-tailer.mjs (9, incl. the race).**** + a 30s TIMER re-evaluates every auto pool (banner wordings: `reached|hit`, model-name ⇒ scoped even without 'weekly', task-notification blobs scanned) + /api/usage `estimates` field. UI (user-specified 虚线/浅色): popup bars = light diagonal hatch reading→est with dashed right edge + `est N%` stat; taskbar pies + Agents mini-donuts = light arc for the est delta + dashed outer ring; **ROLL DISPLAY RULE: est < reading ⇒ window reset ⇒ dark collapses to 0, whole arc light (est ≥ reading within one window, so est<reading is the clean discriminator — utils.estDisplayPair, shared client-wide)**. Zero API calls anywhere. Tests: scripts/test-usage-estimator.mjs (35) + dbg-est-shot.mjs (screenshot smoke incl. rolled case + pool row) **准确度度量=delta相对误差(2.368.13, owner纠正): 绝对|pred−act|随刷新频率变小——每分钟刷新任何模型都完美; calib行带u0/predDu/actDu/rel(|actΔ|<2pt时rel:null), 引擎指标usage-est-rel-err-pct只在窗口真动过时发; 诚实口径下14天同源≥2pt配对: 5h捕捉~85%/Fable周~78%/7d仅~61%真实移动——系统性低估待查(外部消费份额漂移/学习滞后)。test-usage-estimator(83)自此进CI gate(此前在gate外=silent-stale类)。**

### login-state.js / slot-transitions.js / reading-repair.js (READINGS BY SLOT, 2026-09-07)
  **THE ROOT CAUSE (owner: "为啥不是从根源上解决假数据而是加 guardrail").** Every VALUE reading — statusline ingest, `rate_limit_event` capture, limit-banner marks, the auto-cli panel result, the anchors they feed, and the OTel "corrections" — was keyed by `orgVerifiedKey()` = the OTel-observed org. That org is the identity the CLI cached in its config dir at SPAWN, while the credentials a RUNNING CLI reads can be re-pointed later (2.1.257 `rpe()` re-reads `.credentials.json` on an mtime change — exactly what the pool's hot switch does). So after any hot switch a session's readings were filed under the account it was SPAWNED on, forever. Two faces: the VISIBLE one (this instance's Personal Max, credentials emptied 2026-09-03T05:55Z, still receiving limit-banners on 09-07 — measured, see below) and the SILENT one (between two logged-in members a Fish-billed session spawned under B-Stack files Fish's numbers under B-Stack; nothing looks wrong at all). 2.369.66 had already fixed the BLOCKING half the same way (`rejectionSlotFor`/`wallSlotFor`/`sessionBillingMember`); this is the VALUE half. A "logged-out members reject readings" guardrail was explicitly REJECTED as the fix — logged-out members are the symptom.
  **THE ONE RESOLVER.** `readingSlotFor(session, at)` (usage-pool-engine) = the validated credential slot, PINNED FOR THE TURN and cleared by `noteTurnEnd` — the exact twin of `rejectionSlotFor`, deliberately sharing `wallSlotFor`'s body, because a reading and a rejection are facts about the SAME thing. The pin exists for the same reason: every producer calls `maybePoolAutoSwitch` the moment it writes, so one turn's ~20 readings would otherwise be split across the members our own re-points moved to while they were arriving. A re-point reaches the CLI on its NEXT request, so the turn boundary is when a switch becomes true. `resolveUsageKey` now resolves `sessionBillingMember` too, and **`sessionReadingMember` and `orgVerifiedKey` are DELETED** — keeping two functions for one question was the bug's home, and the one that was wrong owned every value on the instance. The observation survives as `corroborateReading()`: it logs the divergence once per session per 10min, telemeters `usage-reading-observation-diverged`, and stamps `corroborated:true|false` on the cache write as a LABEL. `resolveUsageKey`'s fallback also learned the backend (`__global_codex__` vs `__global__`) — one resolver serving both CLIs has to know they are different identities.
  **THE STATUSLINE half.** `data/bin/vibespace-usage` was keyed by `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_KEY`, fixed at SPAWN, with a comment claiming "a hot re-point is a known attribution seam handled by the ledger's time-based records" — wrong on both halves (the CLI does re-read, and the statusline cache is not the ledger). ws-create now also ships `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_LINK` = the credential SYMLINK the CLI reads (`spawnAccount.localEnv[CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR]`, i.e. the per-session plan-C link or the pool default) and the tool `readlink`s it per write — the slot resolved by the process that produced the reading. The REMOTE branch deliberately has no twin: a host gets its credentials tarred at spawn and the engine's re-points skip host sessions, so there is no later switch to be wrong about.
  **src/login-state.js (SHARED, fs-only).** **INTEGRATION NOTE (2026-09-07, after the login-expiry branch landed on the same tree):** `src/login-expiry.js` is NOT this module's twin — it answers the LOGIN SESSION's absolute DEADLINE from an already-parsed credential object (ok/expiring/expired/logged-out/unknown, `refreshTokenExpiresAt`), while this one answers whether the FILE can produce a reading right now and WHEN it stopped. The pool consumes both (either one saying "unusable" is enough); the panels and the repair migration ask `accountLoginState`. One exported `loginState` for both questions stays an OPEN follow-up — the two suites (test-login-expiry / test-readings-attribution) pin different state vocabularies, so collapsing them is a design change, not a merge. `loginState(credsPath) → {state: live|expired|wiped|missing|unreadable, usable, since, expiresAt, refreshExpiresAt}`. `parseAuth` answers a BOOLEAN and collapses four states into it; two of them matter. (i) WIPED is the production shape, verified byte-for-byte: the file stays, `accessToken`/`refreshToken` are `""` and `expiresAt` is 0 — and its mtime is the LAST-KNOWN-GOOD MARKER, the only durable evidence on the instance for "believe nothing this identity produced after T" (rounded to a whole ms: mtimeMs is fractional on ext4/NFS). (ii) 2.369.66 called a credentials leg "an unsatisfiable guard" — REFUTED; it is not — `parseAuth` reports `loggedIn:true` for a login whose access AND refresh tokens have BOTH expired (it only nulls the accessToken field), so `poolMembers()` keeps it and a slot pointing at it validated. `validateBillingSlot` now has an explicit state leg (`slot-wiped`/`slot-expired`/…), and **`healthyPoolMembers` excludes such a member from switch CANDIDATES with NO fallback** — `ensureSessionPoolLink` would happily point a conversation at credentials that cannot authorize a request, and moving a session there is not routing around a dead account, it IS the dead account; an all-unusable pool returns EMPTY so `decidePoolSwitch`'s `no-members` speaks (the auth-FAILURE mark keeps its 2.335.0 escape hatch, because that one is a live inference). The state is memoized on the credential file's mtime+size, never on a clock: a time box would keep answering "signed out" for N seconds after a re-login, and this answer gates switch targets and the panel's warning. **INTEGRATION NOTE:** a parallel branch introduces `src/login-expiry.js` with its own `loginState` — there must be exactly ONE; on integration `login-expiry.js` re-exports from here (or absorbs it and updates the three importers: usage-pool-engine, usage-routes, reading-repair).
  **src/slot-transitions.js (SHARED).** A symlink has no history, so every LATE question ("which member was this conversation on THEN") had no answer and the code substituted the spawn-time org. Now every re-point appends `{sessionId, poolId, from, to, at, why}` to `data/slot-transitions.jsonl`; `sessionId:null` = the pool DEFAULT moved (it decides for sessions with no link of their own). **accounts.js is the SINGLE WRITER** — `ensureSessionPoolLink` and `setPoolTarget` (plus its sweep and the remove-account heal) are the only functions that move a link, so spawn / engine / manual route / signed-out self-heal are covered by construction and no caller can leave a hole. `slotAt(sid, ts)` answers by TIME (own link, else the default, else null = UNKNOWN, never agreement); **`sid` may be an ARRAY of webui keys** (r3 — one conversation is carried by many `sess-*` over its life) and a default-only hit for a NAMED session carries `ownLinkUnknown:true`, because the default is not evidence about a session that may have had a link of its own. The DEDUP key is the LINK (`sessionId || '__default__:'+poolId`), never the sessionId alone — every pool used to share one `__default__` bucket. Bounded + archive-never-destroy: past the cap the oldest half is APPENDED to `data/archive/slot-transitions-<YYYYMMDD>.jsonl` (an earlier `Date.now()` filename let two trims in one millisecond overwrite each other — an archive that loses what it exists to preserve).
  **src/reading-repair.js + the migration `2026-09-reattribute-readings-by-slot`.** Evidence in strength order: ① the transition ledger (exact, but empty for the past by construction), ② each member's last-known-good marker (decisive and inference-free), ③ an OPTIONAL operator-provided frozen journal (`data/pool-journal.log` / `data/archive/pool-journal*.log`) whose `[pool] per-session switch …` / `[pool] auto-switch …` lines backfill into ①; a line we cannot place in TIME is COUNTED, never guessed. It repairs four stores: a foreign `usage-cache` snapshot is archived-with-reason and REBUILT from the newest anchor predating the wipe (identity fields carried over; no surviving anchor ⇒ identity-only remnant with no bucket and no `fetchedAt`, so nothing claims to be a reading); foreign ANCHORS are archived and the record following a removed one is re-chained with `costSince:null` (extractPairs already voids a record with no costSince — the cost is real, the Δu is not); `rates.json` is archived and dropped so the estimator re-learns; `attribution.ndjson` entries on a dead member are RE-ATTRIBUTED when the ledger can place them — through `_sessionKeyMap(dataDir)`, the session-meta join from the CLAUDE conversation id this store is keyed by to the WEBUI keys the ledger is keyed by (r3), and only from a `scope:'session'` row — and archived otherwise (which makes the by-time walk fall back to that session's previous, un-refuted entry), and the existing `.attrib-rebake-v1` marker is cleared so UsageHistory's own proven re-bake recomputes every baked event from the cleaned walk. Idempotent, restart-safe (tmp+rename), never destroys.
  **MEASURED on a COPY of this instance's stores (dry run, originals untouched):** 3 wiped members ⇒ 3 foreign cache snapshots (all restorable), **248 foreign anchors across 3 identity streams** (145/102/1), 44 foreign attribution entries; a second run finds 0. Personal Max's archived snapshot was written 2026-09-07T06:30:53Z, **4d20h after** its login was wiped at 2026-09-03T05:55:23Z; a second member wiped at 12:39 the same day received a reading at 19:24 — the defect was still producing damage while this was written.
  **PANEL HONESTY (src/lib/usage-source.js, PURE + DOM-free).** "Updated 3min ago" is the sentence the incident could not survive. Each panel now names the SOURCE of its latest reading (own /usage panel / own session / own session (limit hit) / session on another machine / **session transcript** / marked spent — an UNKNOWN source is printed verbatim, never absorbed into "own session"; but a producer WE SHIP must be named, so the two codex writers stamp `codex-rate-limits`/`codex-rollout` at the write — r3), adds the corroboration verdict when one exists, and for a member that cannot produce readings at all shows its last REAL reading with "signed out since <stamp>" — plus an explicit WARNING when the newest cached reading POSTDATES the death, because that reading is provably not its own. `/api/usage` carries `logins[acctId] = {state, usable, since}` from the same shared predicate.
  **ROUND 2 (adversarial verifier — six defects, every one reproduced against the real engine / the real merge / the real migration before it was fixed).** ① **MAJOR: a REMOTE CODEX reading was routed into the host's CLAUDE bucket.** `usageCacheKeyFor`'s first rule ("a remote session with no account → `host-<id>`") is a CLAUDE fact — usage-routes seeds `_hostUsage` from those files, the remote statusline harvest writes the host's `__global__` into them, the on-demand ⟳ overwrites them with an OAuth panel and the Agents machine rows render them. Codex remote chat is supported and `recordCodexQuotaSignal` has no host gate, so the moment readings and rejections shared ONE resolver a remote codex session's `rate_limits_updated` snapshot began OVERWRITING the host's claude numbers (rendered as the host's own quota, "via unknown" because the codex snapshot carries no `source`) AND vanishing from codex's own panel, whose disk seed only matches `cxs-*`/`__global_codex__` — while `noteWallSignal` on the same session still keyed `codexQuotaKeyFor` = `__global_codex__`, so a reading and its wall twin landed on different identities. The rule is now a TRANSFORM OF THE ANSWER rather than a backend test — whatever machine identity `resolveUsageKey` names, only `__global__` (the claude machine login) has a per-host form, and an account-billed session never resolves to it, so "no account" is implied and every other machine identity passes through untouched (a `backend === 'claude'` spelling would have silently moved remote OpenCode/ACP sessions OFF the host bucket they already used — pinned). ② **The daemon's sealed-orders reflex wrote no ledger row.** `agentd._execute` re-points a pool link while this server is DOWN (bypassing accounts.js by construction — that machine has no AccountManager), so `slotAt()` answered with the last ORCHESTRATOR row: a confident WRONG answer, not the "unknown" the ledger promises, in the exact window where late attribution has nothing else to go on. The device already reports every field the ledger needs; `accounts.noteDeviceRepoint()` (idempotent under a replayed log, `sessionId` derived from WHICH link moved, an unresolvable `from` left null) keeps "accounts.js is the single writer" physical instead of a grep that hides a hole. ③ **A conversation whose LAST attribution entry was archived kept the refuted account in the ledger forever.** UsageHistory's re-bake only recomputes `if (e.sid && attrib[e.sid])` — a correct rule from when the baked value came from the WALK, and false the moment this repair empties a sid (such sids exist by construction: the old OTel corrective record fired whenever the observation disagreed with `attribAt`, which answers `acct:null` for a sid with no entries, so a conversation's FIRST and only entry could be a corrective one). The repair now NAMES the emptied conversations (`data/usage-history/.attrib-emptied.json`, kept — it stays true) and the re-bake falls through to the session-meta account for them. ④ **`deathMarkers` equated "the local credential dir is wiped" with "this account cannot produce readings"**, but a wiped dir + a valid LONG-LIVED TOKEN (B-211a) is a supported spawnable configuration (`oatOnly`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`), and such a session still carries `_accountId = sub-X`, so its readings are its own. Measured on a fixture: the panel rewound from 42%/61% to a 6-day-old 10%/20%, the fresh anchor dropped, the instance-wide `rates.json` deleted and the attribution row archived. `src/login-state.js` now answers TWO questions — `loginState` (the FILE) and `accountLoginState` (the ACCOUNT: file OR token, dying at the LATER instant, and an oat even DATES an account whose dir never existed) — and the callers choose deliberately: the panels and the repair ask the account question, `validateBillingSlot` deliberately keeps the FILE one, because a token in accounts.json cannot be delivered by re-pointing a symlink and making it account-aware would turn a wiped member into a valid switch TARGET. `AccountManager.OAT_TTL_MS` now READS the constant from login-state (one definition, suite-pinned). ⑤ **`corroborated` survived the preserve-merge**: it is a verdict about ONE write, but `writeUsageCacheForKey` / `refreshViaCliPanel` / the remote-statusline merge inherited the previous producer's, rendering "via own /usage panel · not corroborated" about a reading the account made about itself (a session-less producer has nothing to corroborate WITH). All three now apply captureRateLimitEvent's own rule — `undefined ⇒ delete`, a supplied verdict is honoured (an unconditional delete would be accept-and-ignore) — and a drift guard over EXECUTABLE lines requires every usage-cache merge to DECIDE the label for its own write. ⑥ this essay's own stale sentences, marked REFUTED in place above. Invariants: **a key rule that is true for one backend must SAY so** (a shared resolver inherits every unstated assumption of the code it replaced); **"X is the single writer" is only an invariant while every writer routes through X** — a grep that passes because the second writer lives on another machine is the hole, not the proof; **a repair that changes what a store knows must tell every derived store what it changed** (the re-bake's "leave it" rule was correct for the world before the repair ran); **the credential FILE is not the ACCOUNT** — ask the question the consumer actually has, and write down which consumers must NOT inherit the wider answer; **provenance belongs to the write that made it** (identity is preserved by a merge, a verdict never is). **Gate: scripts/test-readings-attribution.mjs (91 → 139, §11a-f + eleven mutation-tested negative controls)** — every producer × {before switch, after hot switch, after logout} against the REAL engine + a REAL pool with real symlinks (driven through the producers, never by injecting a key), the pre-fix negative control (orgVerifiedKey's rule applied to the same state files the reading on the spawn-time org), the turn pin, the ledger, login-state incl. the satisfiable gap, the migration on a fixture shaped like this instance's stores (+ idempotence + "a dead login we cannot DATE contributes no marker"), the session-less producer's pin, and a headless-chrome 375×667 leg of the real panel (source line, stale-since warning, XSS escaping, geometry, and a live-member negative control). Also added to the release gate: `test-usage-anchors` and `test-auto-resume-loop`, both of which were shipping OUTSIDE it.
  **ROUND 3 (adversarial verifier — five defects, every one reproduced on the real modules / the real script before it was fixed).** ① **MAJOR: the ledger and the attribution log speak DIFFERENT NAMESPACES, and the repair joined them anyway.** The transition ledger is keyed by the WEBUI session key (`sess-<seq>-<ms>` — what `ensureSessionPoolLink` is called with, what a plan-C link's basename spells, what the engine's `[pool] per-session switch <pool>/<sid>` line prints), while `attribution.ndjson` is keyed by the CLAUDE CONVERSATION id (a UUID — what `recordUsageAttribution` receives). `repairAttribution` looked the ledger up with the conversation id, so `slotAt`'s `r.sessionId === sessionId` test could NEVER be true for a session-scoped row: every plan-C conversation fell silently into the pool-DEFAULT branch, and that answer was WRITTEN INTO THE LIVE STORE — the conversation's spend moved to a member its own credential link was never on. Reproduced on the real modules with the shapes on disk today (`ls data/pool-links/<pool>/` → `sess-1-1788764792907…`; `head data/usage-history/attribution.ndjson` → `{"sid":"c136a0b4-…"}`): ledger rows `{sessionId:null,to:'sub-bstack'}` + `{sessionId:'sess-7-…',to:'sub-fish'}`, entry `{sid:'2f1a9c40-uuid-conv',acct:'sub-dead'}` ⇒ the repair rewrote it to `sub-bstack` with reason "scope default". The r2 suite could not see it because its fixture used ONE id for both stores (the `winId` vs `id` fixture class) — that fixture is now production-shaped. Fixed by making the stores JOINABLE rather than assuming they are: `_sessionKeyMap(dataDir)` builds `claudeSessionId → [{key, at}]` from `data/session-meta/cw-<seq>-<ms>.json` (the key is a pure function of the meta FILENAME — 2.304.0 derived the socket name from the session id, so nothing depends on a field being persisted), `slotAt` accepts an ARRAY of keys because one conversation is carried by MANY webui sessions over its life (every resume/fork mints a new one) and the latest matching row wins = the session that was live at that instant, and a key minted AFTER the entry is not a candidate for it. Translating at the READ side (rather than recording both ids on the row) is deliberate: a row is written the instant a LINK moves and at spawn the conversation id does not exist yet, while the daemon's sealed-orders reflex and the journal backfill have only a link path / a printed webui id — session-meta holds the mapping for all of them, uniformly and after the fact. **And the repair no longer substitutes the default for a conversation it cannot place**: `slotAt` returns `ownLinkUnknown:true` when a session was NAMED but only the default answered (asking about the default ITSELF — `sessionId` null — carries no flag, because that is exactly the question it answers), and only a `scope:'session'` row may rewrite a stored fact; unjoinable and default-only entries are ARCHIVED with a reason naming which evidence was missing, which is the honest outcome the ledger's "unknown, never agreement" contract already promised. ② **MEDIUM: the r2 emptied-sid fix re-baked a POOL id into the ACCOUNT dimension.** `_maybeRebakeAttribution` now recomputes when `emptied.has(e.sid)`, which falls through `_acctAt` to the session-meta account — and session-meta's `accountId` is the SPAWN identity, which for a pooled session is the POOL (`resolveForSpawn` returns `{id: a.id}` for a pooled account, ws-create writes `_accountId = spawnAccount.id`). Reproduced: shard event `{sid:'S',acct:'sub-dead'}` + empty attribution + `.attrib-emptied.json=["S"]` + session-meta `{accountId:'pool-1'}` ⇒ `{"acct":"pool-1","atype":"pooled","aname":"My Pool"}`, i.e. a pseudo-account that holds no credentials surfacing as a spender — which server.js forbids in so many words at the OTHER site that reaches this decision ("an unresolvable pool target falls to GLOBAL, never to the pool id itself"). `_nonPoolAcct` now applies that same invariant here (and to the ordinary scan path, which had it too). Which MEMBER it was is deliberately NOT guessed: this fallback only runs for a conversation with no attribution entry at all, which is exactly the case ① could not resolve from the ledger either — global is the honest answer, and the `pool` tag (kept, and now also derived from meta on the scan path) keeps the per-pool total correct. TWO legs on purpose: the injected `resolveAccount` maps `backend === 'codex'` to a single type BEFORE `a.type` is read, so it reports a CODEX pool as `'codex-subscription'` and the type test alone misses every one of them — the minted id shape (`'pool-' + randomBytes(6).hex`, one call site) is the primary test and the type is the belt; both are pinned with a leg that fails without it. ③ **MINOR: the provenance line labelled EVERY codex reading "via unknown / No producer recorded this reading".** `normalizeCodexRateLimit` is a PURE payload mapper and cannot know which channel carried it, and none of the three codex snapshot writers stamped a `source` — confirmed against production (`data/usage-cache/__global_codex__.json` → `source: undefined`, while every claude file in the same directory carries one). The verbatim-unknown rule exists for a producer we have not MET; using it on one we SHIP is the honesty feature lying. Stamped at the write, where the channel is known — and as a PARAMETER, because the engine's `writeSnap` helper serves TWO channels: the live `rate_limits_updated` push says `codex-rate-limits` (the codex twin of claude's `rate-limit-event`, so "own session"), the `task_failed` REFUSAL says `limit-banner` (the existing vocabulary already names it "own session (limit hit)", and that branch's `sig.snapshot ||` fallback SYNTHESIZES a spent bucket, which is not a reading at all — calling either one `codex-rate-limits` would claim a producer that did not produce it), the wrapper-sidecar reader says `codex-rate-limits` (the same push, read back off disk) and the rollout-tail reader says `codex-rollout` ("session transcript" — a different freshness story: as fresh as that session's last turn, not as of now); zh+ja entries for both new keys, and the pure normalizer still stamps nothing, pinned. ④ **MINOR: the statusline honoured a re-point one request EARLY.** `accountKey()` resolves the credential link at WRITE time, but the payload's `rate_limits` is the CLI's LAST API RESPONSE, made with the credentials the link pointed at BEFORE the re-point — the exact property `readingSlotFor` reasons about server-side ("a re-point reaches the running CLI on its NEXT request, never the reading already in flight"). The statusline had no pin and no content dedup (`writeUsage` throttles on file mtime and always stamps `fetchedAt: Date.now()`), so the first post-switch render filed the old member's numbers under the new one, stamped fresh — and `sweepUsageAnchors` treats any usage-cache write as ground truth, re-creating the B-b3cd odometer-flap class on the write path. Reproduced by driving the real script with a re-pointed link: 42%/61% produced by `sub-old` landed in `sub-new.json`. It now has the same one-request lag, using the only evidence it has — the NUMBERS: a `.slot-<session_id>` sidecar beside the cache (no `.json`, so every usage-cache scanner keeps ignoring it) records `{key, fp}` for every payload OBSERVED (not only every one written — the 8s throttle skips most renders and a payload seen under the old target is still evidence), and while the link has moved but the fingerprint has not, the reading is not re-filed. The first DIFFERING payload is the first one the new credentials produced. Only pooled sessions pay anything (no `VIBESPACE_ACCOUNT_LINK` ⇒ no state file at all: a session with no link cannot switch under itself). ⑤ **LOW: every pool shared one `__default__` dedup bucket.** `record()`'s key was the sessionId alone, and a member can belong to several pools (this instance's has `members:null` = every subscription), so two pools re-pointing their defaults to the same member inside DEDUP_MS dropped the second row — and `slotAt`, which filters by `poolId` when reading, then answered that pool with its previous, now-WRONG default: a confident answer out of a ledger whose whole contract is "unknown, never agreement". The key is now the LINK (`sessionId || '__default__:' + poolId`); a session key is globally unique and a conversation belongs to one pool, so session rows need nothing more. **Invariants:** **two stores are joinable only if something joins them** — a lookup that crosses id namespaces is a silent no-match, and the FIXTURE that uses one id for both is what makes it invisible (shapes come from disk, always); **a fallback that cannot be proven right must not overwrite a stored fact** — say `ownLinkUnknown` and archive, because "the default answered" and "this session had no link of its own" are two different facts that look identical from here; **an invariant stated at one site is owed at every site that reaches the same decision** (the pool-is-not-a-spender rule lived only in server.js's comment); **a predicate whose primary leg is an id SHAPE and whose belt is an injected type needs a test for each leg** — the injected resolver flattens a whole backend's pools and would have made the type leg silently unfalsifiable; **a producer we ship must have a NAME** — "unknown, reported verbatim" is for producers we have not met, and stamping belongs at the write, never inferred from the shape of the payload; **when the same rule exists on the server and in a shipped single file, the single file needs the rule too, in whatever evidence it actually has** (the server pins the slot for the turn; the statusline has no turn, so it pins on the numbers). **Gate: scripts/test-readings-attribution.mjs (139 → 179, §12a-e)** — production-shaped join fixtures (ledger `sess-*`, attribution UUIDs, session-meta joining them) incl. resume/fork one-to-many, the real UsageHistory re-bake for the pool cases, the real `recordCodexQuotaSignal`, the REAL statusline script driven across a re-pointed symlink, and a negative control per finding (the pre-fix lookup, the pre-fix dedup key, the pre-fix snapshot with no `source`, and the statusline with its state file deleted). The headless-chrome leg now also renders the CODEX section and asserts it names its producer, with the pre-fix snapshot as the control.

### usage-walker.js

  usage-walker.js      — THE ledger walk, ONE module (2.297.0 twin-killer): UsageHistory.scan() consumes it IN-PROCESS (cursors injected, onEvent enrichment orchestrator-side), the daemon runs it as the usage-scan op child, and data/bin/vibespace-usage-scan mirrors it for checkout-less ssh hosts (parity-pinned). **usage-events PUSH (2.299.0, R4 complete): the daemon watches transcript dirs → incremental walk → chan-0 batches → server acks after durable ingest → ONLY THEN the device commits its cursor (two-phase, roles inverted; unacked batches re-emit, rid dedup absorbs). Remote ledger = seconds-fresh; covers external sessions + workflow file-only usage the stdout relay never sees; 60s-floor dirty-kick harvest stays as the fallback rung. E2E scripts/test-usage-events-push.mjs (7) incl. the unacked-batch-does-not-advance-cursor proof.** Event fields: {rid, mid, ts, sid, be, model, cwd, i, cw5, cw1, cr, o, tier} + codex-only `effort` (v3, 2026-09-06). Codex block v3: `mid` = the preceding `token_usage_record.response_id` (0.153; cursor-parked `pendMid`/`pendTotal`, total-checked, consumed by the next token_count), `effort` from turn_context; the `cx:<sid>:<cumulative>` rid is the ONE key the codex normalizer must derive identically (its meta.requestId): sid = the rollout FILE's uuid (never a session_meta inside it — a sub-agent rollout also carries the PARENT's at line 1), and an identical re-emitted token_count is dropped on `rid === cur.lastRid` — the normalizer mirrors both (round 3: per-RECORD file provenance `__threadId` tagged by the merged reader, the reader's id only a default for provenance-less records, wrapper_meta re-points it; `_lastRidKeys` per thread = this cursor per file). test-usage-walk-parity + test-codex-history assert the equality on the same rollout, and test-codex-history's corpus smoke asserts minted-key-set equality against this walker over ≤30 real local rollouts (skips with a reason on CI).

### conversation-index.js

  conversation-index.js — Conversation-location index (2.297.0, R3 tail): data/conversation-index.json records which machine owns each conversation from BOTH discovery listings and transcript fetches — resume host-inference locates conversations the raw remote-jsonl cache never saw; two live claims = honestly ambiguous (null), a week-fresher claim is decisive, removed-host claims ignored. hosts.conversationOwner(sid); ws-handler inference consults it FIRST and forces ambiguity on disagreement with the cache scan. Test: scripts/test-conversation-index.mjs (7).

## src/server/wrapper-files.js (2.339.2 resolver + 2.364.1 capability reader)

Two files belong to a local chat session and they have DIFFERENT writers: `data/session-meta/<sock>.json` is the SERVER's record (webuiSessionId, claudeSessionId, account, task…; written by writeSessionMeta) and `data/session-buffers/<id>.json` is the wrapper's SIDECAR (pid, startedAt, mode, streaming, tasks, todos, goal, `caps` — written by data/bin/chat-wrapper.js itself). `resolveWrapperFiles(BUFFERS_DIR, id, sockPath)` (2.339.2) finds the sidecar even for counter-collision sessions whose wrapper argv carries a different id (reads the dtach master's argv from /proc). `wrapperCaps(BUFFERS_DIR, id, sockPath)` (2.364.1) is the ONE reader of the wrapper's advertised capabilities: `{frameFile, peerMessage, inputQueue, caps, reason:'ok'|'no-caps'|'no-sidecar', startedAt, pid}`, stateless (`inputQueue` since 2026-09-06: the wrapper publishes `queue_changed` and serves the `queue-op` verb — the ws `queue-op` case and the client's `queueSupported` both hang off it, because backend-caps only says what a KIND of agent can do). The 2.361.1 gate read caps from the session-meta file instead — which never has them — so every wrapper tested old and the frame-file bypass was dead for two releases (kb-bugfix-invariants: CAPABILITY GATE READ THE WRONG FILE). Rules: new wrapper capabilities are added to the `caps` object in chat-wrapper.js AND read through wrapperCaps only; callers cache only positive verdicts; test-chat-frame-guard §2 spawns the real wrapper and asserts wrapperCaps sees its sidecar (parity). **Both wrappers speak the same contract (design-harness-plugins §1 P1)**: data/bin/codex-chat-wrapper.js writes `caps: { peerMessage, frameFile }` into the same sidecar shape and resolves `_frame_file` pointers on its stdin path (read → unlink always → must be ONE JSON object, nested pointers refused → dispatched through `handleInput` exactly as a stdin line; every failure logs AND emits a `task_failed` event, as does an unparseable raw stdin line — the old `if (!msg) continue;` was a silent message loss; and it acks every received stdin line with `_stdin_ack` on stdout like chat-wrapper, so ws-handler's 5s broken-pty re-send never replays a pointer whose file is already consumed).
