The chat-input bypass in ws-handler is therefore TRANSPORT + CAPABILITY only (`>64KB && !host && socketPath` → wrapperCaps decides pointer / raw ≤1MB / evidenced refusal) — it must never regain a backend-id branch; resolveWrapperFiles' /proc fallback already matches `codex-chat-wrapper.js` (substring of the argv marker). test-chat-frame-guard §2b drives the REAL codex wrapper against a stub app-server (pointer → intact turn/start input; shredded/missing/nested files + unparseable lines → loud) and §4 pins the gate shape. **`queueVerbs` (2026-09-07):** the reader also returns WHICH queue verbs the running wrapper serves — `caps.queueVerbs` when it names them, and `LEGACY_QUEUE_VERBS` (`remove/steer/steer-all`) when it adverts `inputQueue` with no list, i.e. a 2.369.55-or-older build. Both halves are load-bearing in the same skew rule: an old process must never be ASKED for a verb it would silently drop, and must never LOSE the verbs it does serve. The ws `queue-op` case gates each op on this list (falling back, for a remote wrapper whose sidecar is on the other machine, to the normalizer's in-band `queueVerbsPublished()`), and the attach payload ships it to the client so a rendered control is always one this process will honour.

## src/server/auto-resume.js (2.368.0 — continue after a usage limit resets) 2.369.32: the continue prompt carries originKind 'auto-resume' (server.js sendToSession tags the user record: claude `originKind`, codex `payload.webui_origin`) → both normalizers set msg.originKind='auto-resume' → chat-renderers shows a labelled VibeSpace card, never an anonymous user bubble. The four "who said continue" lanes: auto-resume continue (server, user-role, labelled) / Stop-hook block reason (the CLI renders it as a user turn: "Stop hook feedback: …") / UserPromptSubmit additionalContext (<system-reminder>, VibeSpace's prompt-context) / "The user hasn't heard from you in a while" (Claude Code's own idle nudge — not VibeSpace).

The CLI ships this feature but only in the interactive REPL: `/rate-limit-options` is **not** in a stream-json session's `slash_commands` (verified against a real init record) and the timer is a TUI `useInterval`, so a VibeSpace chat session hits the limit and stops dead. This module is ours, with the same shape and one deliberate difference: **the wait is persisted and survives a restart** — the CLI's own says "Automatic continue cancelled · Claude Code relaunched during the wait", and a multi-hour wait that a deploy cancels silently is worse than no feature.

**Order of preference (do not invert):** exhaustion first tries `maybePoolAutoSwitch` (another account = seconds, not hours); only then does `armIfEnabled` run. The module disarms itself on any proof of recovery — a fresh non-rejected `rate_limit_event`, the user's own prompt (ws chat-input), a dead session — because firing on a recovered session is a billed turn for nothing. **Gate is tri-state**: `session._autoResume` (spawn value or the live ws toggle) wins over the `claude.autoResumeOnLimit` default, so a per-session OFF survives the default being ON; default is OFF because firing spends quota unattended. Fires at `resetsAt + 15s`, never early, never twice, never while `_isStreaming`, refuses waits >26h out or with no reset time, retries on delivery failure, and announces both arming and firing in the conversation. The continue wording is the CLI's own, verbatim. Gated by scripts/test-auto-resume.mjs (48). **2.368.27 nearest-reset arming (c1206711 incident 2026-08-26)**: the rejection may carry a FAR bucket's resetsAt (seven-day ~100h, org overage off) while a nearer reset that will actually free the account (five-hour, rolling-window recovery) sits in the usage cache — the old flow refused the far wait journal-only and left NOTHING watching. Now `pickArmReset` (PURE, exported) picks the nearest in-range future reset from event + cache buckets (fiveHour/sevenDay/opus/scoped); the engine's `armBestReset` is the ONLY way exhaustion arms (all three sites: claude dead-branch + both codex — wiring pinned, 2.355.0 lesson); a still-dead account simply re-runs the ladder on its next rejection (self-limiting at bucket cadence). A too-far refusal now NOTIFIES the session (1/h floor) — a refusal the user can't see is the incident. Suite now 71. **2.368.28 (the owner's correction — the real c1206711 story)**: the pool had hot-moved the session onto a member whose 7d then died; the 5h-exhausted sibling (7d fine) was the way back, and the pool even switched back on its own — but a hot re-point never moves an idle blocked session. ① armBestReset merges POOL SIBLING members' bucket resets (poolReadCache × poolMembers — the freeing reset lives in the sibling's cache); ② HOT switches (per-session + pool-level) `fireNow()` ARMED sessions — continue delivered the moment a healthy account lands, armed-only, cold keeps client restarts; ③ the timed fire runs `beforeFire` (maybePoolAutoSwitch) before spending. Suite 79. **2.368.32 (owner caught the premature fire — "7d重置没和5h对齐"): the wait target is the MAX over the identity's DEAD buckets** (a session unblocks only when ALL dead buckets reset; a healthy bucket's nearer reset is no candidate), min across identities (self + pool siblings) unchanged; dead = utilization≥0.999 / status limited|rejected / usedPercent≥99.5 with a future resetsAt. The limit-banner path now (re-)arms too — it used to mark the cache dead and leave nothing waiting. Suite 81. **2.368.33 false-arm suppression (owner: '没被打断任何对话'): a dead event races the hot switch that just fixed it — armBestReset skips when the CURRENT pool member has no dead bucket (shared isDeadBucket; unknown never guessed), and ANY main-thread assistant record disarms (`noteSessionProduced`, engine→session-stdout) — the readings-based disarm misses accounts that emit no events. Suite 86.** **2.368.34 — that pre-check ATE A REAL WALL (9h dark) and is REMOVED: a live CLI holds its OLD token, the org-verified banner marks the OLD member dead while the link points at a healthy one — the link is never the org a running CLI is on. Replacements that cannot eat a wall: arm unconditionally; announcement DELAYED 90s (disarm inside the window ⇒ silent); noteWorked age-gates the produced-work disarm to >30s post-arm (the banner's own trailing records land within seconds). parseBannerResetMs: the banner's "resets 12:40pm (TZ)" is timezone-parsed into the precise arm target (was a now+5h guess). Suite 91.**

**2.369.0 — THE WALL MACHINE (owner-designed, docs/design-wall-machine.md; supersedes every arm/disarm mechanism above where they conflict).** auto-resume.js is now the EXECUTOR only (persisted armed waits, tick fire, delayed announcement, veto-capable beforeFire); detection + recovery live in the engine's turn-granular machine: wall signals (rejected events / banner as a BOOLEAN — text is never a data source; parseBannerResetMs deleted) accumulate on the current turn via `noteWallSignal`, the `result` record (codex: task_complete/task_failed) classifies via `noteTurnEnd` — walled (signals with ≤1 assistant record after the last one) → BLOCKED entry: pool eval, then `quotaVerdictFor` (the account system's ONE usability answer — account-pool-auto.quotaVerdict pure: 5h<10%/weekly<5% THRESH hot tier, blockedUntil=max over dead buckets, pool=any-usable/min-over-members, model-projected + estimator-overlaid) → usable ⇒ arm now+45s (announce delay 90s keeps a quick success silent), blocked ⇒ arm blockedUntil, unknown ⇒ PROBE auto-cli /usage 0→30m→1h→2h then loud give-up. **2.369.6: blocked-with-target ALSO fires one throttled verification probe (10min/scope, _wallVerifyAt) — a confidently-lying cache armed a 2.6-day wait on an alive account (inc-mtdsoj5f); confident ≠ correct, every BLOCKED entry verifies.** A normally-completed turn unconditionally disarms (sufficient proof — kills noteWorked/30s-age; the pool-rescued mid-turn case classifies NORMAL by construction). Pre-fire: probe + re-verdict, false VETOES the spend and re-arms. Blocking identity = orgVerifiedKey (OTel-observed org over the link — the .33/.34 root cause). Session fields _turnWallSigs/_turnWorkAfterSig/_wallProbeTimer/_arFiring registered. Gate: test-auto-resume §8-10 (80). **B-2c9b (2026-09-05): BLOCKED entry is now demote → session-aware verdict → arm → pool eval (`finally`)** — the walled branch of `noteTurnEnd` no longer runs `maybePoolAutoSwitch` BEFORE `onWalledTurn`, so the wall's account is demoted (ground truth) before the verdict reads the cache and the hot switch's `fireNow()` finds the session ARMED and continues it in the same tick; `quotaVerdictFor` takes `{model, session}` and judges the member the session is OBSERVED on first. Full essay under account-pool-auto.js ("WALL = GROUND TRUTH"); gate test-auto-resume §11.

**THE LOOP BREAKER (2026-09-07, owner decision ut-1c6c15a2db ①④ — the 130-fire incident; full essay in kb-bugfix-invariants).** A fire the session answers with ANOTHER limit rejection is a FAILED fire, and until this release nothing remembered it: the walled-turn path re-armed "switched to a usable account", the hot pool switch called `fireNow()`, the CLI rejected again half a second later, and the cycle repeated 130× on one conversation (32× on a second) between 23:32 and 04:03 — ~150 junk cards in one transcript, every cycle indistinguishable from the first to every component. Now: ① ONE fire path (`attemptFire`) serves the timed tick AND `fireNow`, so the immediate path can no longer skip `beforeFire` (it used to — the incident's 130 continues all came down it, each bypassing the gate that would have re-verdicted the target; the in-flight flag `_arFiring` is raised BEFORE the gate runs, because the real gate calls `maybePoolAutoSwitch` → `fireNow` and everything before its first `await` is synchronous re-entry — measured 1992 levels deep with the flag one line late); ② a per-session breaker record `{n, windowStart, fails:[{key,at}], last, lastFireAt, notified, noticeAt, refuse}` PERSISTED under `fires` in data/auto-resume.json (a deploy must not hand the loop a fresh budget — the same reasoning as the armed wait itself); ③ rules, every refusal NAMED: `fire-pending` (never two continues in flight, 10min staleness), `same-identity` (an identity that just rejected us is quarantined 10min), `backoff` (immediate fires 0/60s/5min), `hourly-cap` (3 immediate fires per session per hour, after which only the TIMED reset path — the one anchored to a real reset — may fire); ④ the identity a fire is keyed on comes from the ENGINE (`fireIdentity` dep → `fireIdentityFor` = `wallKeyFor`), deliberately the same fact the wall machine demotes, or the breaker would quarantine an account nothing ever rejects; ⑤ `noteFireOutcome(id, ok)` — the engine's walled turn reports `false` FIRST in `onWalledTurn` (before anything re-arms or re-switches; that ordering IS the off-switch), any proof of work reports `true` through `noteRecovered`, which now clears the breaker BEFORE its armed-record early-return (after a fire there is no armed record left, so anything gated behind it would never see the proof) — **and ONLY for a caller whose evidence is WORK (round 4): `noteRecovered(id, why, {worked = true})` runs `noteFireOutcome(id,true)` only when `worked`, because round 1 handed the entire record (quarantine + hourly counter + both notice budgets + the persisted copy) to its loudest caller, the PASSIVE non-rejected `rate_limit_event` this instance sees ~20× per rejection, so neither budget was ever enforced in production; the DISARM stays unconditional for every caller. Exactly two callers claim work — a completed turn and the user's own prompt — the other four (claude reading, codex reading, codex reset credit, `disabled`) pass `{worked:false}`; the table lives in kb-bugfix-invariants and is DERIVED + enforced by test-auto-resume-loop §5, so an unclassified new caller fails the suite instead of re-opening the loop**; ⑥ the in-chat "账号池已切换到 X，已自动继续" line goes out at most ONCE per distinct target per session per hour, a repeat is journal-only, and when the breaker trips the session gets ONE honest line naming the account and either the reset time or "no member can serve — add a member / move this session" (immediate path only: a refused TIMED fire self-heals inside 10min, so saying so would be noise). `recentFireFailures(id)` is read back by the engine so a restart-surviving failure still steers the next target choice.

**Round 2 (same day, adversarial verifier — four reproduced defects; full essay in kb-bugfix-invariants).** ⑦ **What the conversation is told is now a PURE function of the refusal reason** (`refusalNoticeFor`, exported): round 1 computed the named check and then told the SAME exhaustion story for all four reasons, so a `fire-pending`/`backoff` refusal named an account that had rejected nothing, claimed the pool was empty, and said retrying had stopped while the session was still armed and continuing seconds later — and it spent the once-per-window budget, silencing the genuine line. Now `same-identity` may say "it refused us too"; the "nowhere else to go" clause requires the POOL's own verdict (`noteNoPoolTarget`, called from the `all-rejected`/`no-members`/`stuck` branch of the per-session pass — the breaker cannot know it); `hourly-cap` states its count and pause; `backoff`/`fire-pending` are JOURNAL-ONLY and spend nothing (a pacer on an intact promise). Budgets are per notice CLASS (`r.notices`), so the cap line can never eat the exhaustion line's. ⑧ **The identity is re-resolved INSIDE `deliver()`, after the gate**, and re-admitted through `canFire` (abort, never spend, if the gate moved us onto an identity we already burned): `beforeAutoResumeFire` runs `maybePoolAutoSwitch`, which re-points the link, so round 1 keyed `noteFired`/`announce`/the journal to the account we had just LEFT — quarantining the wrong one and leaving the real rejector fireable. ⑨ **The continue card names what actually unblocked us** (`continueNoticeFor`, exported): `kind === 'now'`, a near-arm reason, or the identity having MOVED during the gate ⇒ "账号池已切换到 X"; only otherwise "用量上限已重置". This matters because the near-arm is delivered by the TIMED path — the real producers re-point the link BEFORE the session is armed, so `fireNow` is not reached at all (measured: injected signal ⇒ 1 immediate continue, real `recordRateLimitEvent`/`markLimitBanner` ⇒ 0; recovery is 45 s arm + 15 s grace on a 30 s tick ≈ 60–90 s). The switch-class card keeps the once-per-target-per-window dedup on BOTH paths; the reset-class card is never deduped. `_poolAutoLast`/`_poolSwitchAt` are exported from the engine so a suite can wind the wall-clock gates back instead of sleeping. Gate: scripts/test-auto-resume-loop.mjs (122 — the matrix negative control: each pre-fix behaviour re-created through a public seam, both off ⇒ ≥10 unbounded fires, either on ⇒ it stops; plus per-fix mutation controls and the real-producer ordering legs).

**Output style (same release, same suite).** The CLI's Concise/Explanatory/Learning/Proactive are a SETTINGS key with no flag, and stream-json never gets `/output-style` — so it is spawn-only: `buildSessionArgs({outputStyle})` merges it into the ONE `--settings` flag (alongside `ultracode`/`switchModelsOnFlag`; never a second flag), ws-create defaults it from `claude.outputStyle`, the status-bar chip persists it as per-session config, and every surface says "applies on the next resume" instead of pretending otherwise.

## src/server/instance-url.js (2.367.0 — this instance's own public address)

**One resolver.** "The URL a machine outside this box uses to reach this VibeSpace" is needed by reverse mounts (machine-mounts), remote agent installs (mounts-plugins-wiring), the agentd auto-graduation gate and published-page share links; all four read `agentd.publicUrl` inline before this. `url()` is the single reader and resolves **explicit frp mapping → `agentd.publicUrl` → `VIBESPACE_PUBLIC_URL` → null** (null = callers fall back to the asking browser's origin, 2.366.1). The mapping is a LAYER: publishing never writes the setting, so unmapping lands exactly back on it — the owner's requirement ("不要覆盖原来的设置, incase取消了映射直接回归原来的设置").

**One publisher.** `plugins.frpPublish('vibespace-instance', PORT, …)` predates this module but ran as an invisible side effect of installing a remote agent over the relay — two callers writing one relay proxy name, with nothing recording the URL. This module owns that proxy; the install path calls `ensurePublished()`. **Explicit vs auto is load-bearing**: a user pressing Map sets `desired` and outranks the setting; an install-driven publish is `auto` — recorded and shown in the Ports panel, but never repointing share links (a side effect must not move everyone's URLs). `publish()` is REFUSED while auth is off (an open instance on the internet is RCE as the owner). `restore()` re-asserts a desired mapping at boot without clearing the persisted URL (frpc is its own process and usually outlives a restart); a failed re-assert keeps the intent and surfaces the error. UI: `_renderInstanceUrlRow` in sidebar-rail.js (Ports panel), which also states what is kept underneath. Gated by scripts/test-instance-url.mjs (39).

## src/server/design-kit.js + data/bin/vibespace-page (2.366.0 — design canvases from chat, published HERE) **2.369.14 zstd阶梯**: CLI≥2.1.257把skill/helper/payload打成bun内zstd帧(SKILL-<hash>.md.zst / seed-canvas.mjs-<hash>.txt.zst / payload), 模板字面量锚点消失; extractFromZstd扫全部zstd magic候选按头部分类(frontmatter name: design / '// Design-canvas seeding helper' / doctype+appifact-doc), 220MB仅176ms, 旧CLI仍走模板字面量路径, Node<22.15无zlib zstd时明说.

**Problem.** Claude Code's bundled `/design` skill (design canvas, early preview) is only reachable from the TUI: the init `slash_commands` list carries ONE `design` (plus `design-consent`/`-revoke`/`-sync`); the terminal picker pairs the skill with the consent command, stream-json has no picker and resolves to consent, and the Skill tool refuses `design` ("built-in CLI command, not a skill"). The skill = `seed-canvas.mjs` (helper) + `payload.template.html` (~2 MiB editor) + ~55 KB instructions; the CLI extracts the first two to `/tmp/claude-<uid>/bundled-skills/<ver>/<hash>/design/` when the slash command runs, and injects the instructions as a user message (they live nowhere on disk).

**design-kit.js** (`create({dataDir, claudeCmd, log})`): `ensure()` builds `data/design-kit/<cliVersion>/` once per installed CLI version — helper + payload from the CLI-extracted dir when present (the CLI's own bytes; parity-tested) else extracted from the binary (`payload.template.html.asset` name → the one occurrence followed by `<!doctype html`, up to the first NUL; the helper and the skill text are backtick template literals with `\uXXXX`/`\xHH` escapes → `unescapeTemplate`; all scans stream in 8 MB chunks — pods are small), `SKILL.orig.md` (verbatim) and `SKILL.md` = `adaptSkill()`: step 4 (Artifact publish) and "Updating an existing canvas" (artifact read-back) replaced by the VibeSpace publish step (`vibespace-page publish`), banner after the frontmatter; anchors missing ⇒ `{error}` and the kit is NOT ready — never a half-adapted document. Every build is VALIDATED by seeding a sample artboard with the kit's own helper and running its `--check` (`ok:`). `status()` feeds `GET /api/design-kit/status` (cookie; `?refresh=1` rebuilds) — shown in the chat status bar's design popover, so "not ready" always carries its reason (no CLI binary / layout changed in a new CLI / check failed). Nothing of Anthropic's is vendored in the repo: extraction is per-user, per-version, at runtime; only the adaptation text is ours. Warmed 15 s after boot, off the critical path.

**The flow.** Chat status bar design chip (`chat-status-bar.js` `_renderDesignPopover`; SVG `UI_ICONS.design`; view-only windows have no chip) → brief + "public link" → `chat-view._sendDesignRequest` sends a VISIBLE user message (`[VibeSpace design request] …` — no hidden injection; the transcript shows what the agent was asked): `vibespace-page kit` → read `<dir>/SKILL.md` → `vibespace-page publish <file> --title "…" [--public]` → reply with the link. `vibespace-page` (STATIC, in AGENT_TOOLS): `publish` uploads the file body to `POST /api/agent/pages/publish` (vsst_ or jbt_; raw ≤25 MB; `?title&path&host&public`), `kit` asks `GET /api/agent/design-kit` and, on the hub machine, prints the server's kit dir, else mirrors `seed-canvas.mjs`/`payload.template.html`/`SKILL.md` from `GET /api/agent/design-kit/file/:name` into `~/.vibespace/design-kit/<ver>/` (remote hosts need no CLI extraction), `list` shows this session's pages. Publishing attributes the page to the session; `onPublished` → `page-published` broadcast → the chip count/list update live. Hosted canvases are view + PNG/PDF export (CSP sandbox, no online Save) — SKILL.md tells the agent to say so at handover. Invariants: the kit is per CLI version and validated before use; adaptation is all-or-nothing; the request is visible, never injected; remote hosts mirror the hub's kit (one kit, one adaptation); the CLI never receives tokens on argv.

## src/server/published-pages.js (2.364.0 — Published pages, "接管artifact"; 2.366.0 content publish)

Instance-hosted shareable HTML. Owner incident: the /design canvas skill publishes to claude.ai artifacts, which could not be shared outward; the canvas is a self-contained local HTML file, so the instance hosts it itself. Store data/published-pages.json + snapshots data/published-pages/<id>.html (publish COPIES; re-publish of the same srcPath UPSERTS the same id — stable share URL across design iterations). Serving: GET /p/:id, middleware-exempt in auth.js with the gate INSIDE the module (private ⇒ requestAuthed cookie; per-page `public` flag = anyone with the link) — the /svc doctrine verbatim. **CSP `sandbox allow-scripts …` on every /p response is LOAD-BEARING**: pages are arbitrary user HTML with scripts served from the APP ORIGIN; the sandbox directive gives them an opaque origin (no cookies, no same-origin /api) — removing it turns the feature into stored XSS against the owner. Never add `allow-same-origin`. UI = the explorer right-click dialog (create+manage in one; Open uses a browser window). Gate: scripts/test-published-pages.mjs (60, real HTTP + CSP assertions + wiring pins).

## src/msg-acl.js + src/server/conversation-deliver.js + data/bin/vibespace-msg (2.362.0 — Communication Channels v1)

**What.** Agent-to-agent messaging with Task-Group-scoped reach, built as the first channel of the owner's Communication Channels vision (external sources — Gmail/Lark/Slack — later feed the same ladder with their own `source` tags; every stash envelope already carries one).

**ACL (msg-acl.js, PURE).** Same group = mutual see+message (the default collaboration boundary). Outside: group `externalVisibility` (`none` default / `visible` / `messageable`, task-groups field + task-detail select) and a per-session `_msgReachability` override (schema-registered, meta-persisted, `POST /api/sessions/:id/msg-reachability`, widening ONLY). Levels rank none<visible<messageable; effective = max of grants; multi-group targets = union; ungrouped sessions are closed singletons. HONESTY: this is a coordination/attention boundary, NOT security — same-OS-user agents can always read transcripts and raw sockets; the native ListAgents/SendMessage tools still see the whole machine. Uniform not-found errors (no existence oracle).

**2.366.1 (published-pages.js) — the hosting layer.** `/p/<id>` is a SHELL (our own HTML on the real origin, no user content) that frames `/p/<id>/raw`, where the published HTML runs under the sandbox CSP (opaque origin; **never** `allow-same-origin`). Both routes share one `gate()`; the shell escapes the agent-chosen name. `/raw` injects a COMPAT PRELUDE (`COMPAT_PRELUDE`/`injectShim`, cached per snapshot): `crypto.randomUUID` polyfilled from `getRandomValues` — it is SECURE-CONTEXT-ONLY and VibeSpace is normally plain http on a hostname, which hung every canvas artboard forever (kb-bugfix-invariants: HOSTED PAGE NEEDED A SECURE CONTEXT) — plus in-memory `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` for the opaque origin; each only when genuinely missing. Share URLs: `urlFor(id, req)` = `agentd.publicUrl` → the ASKING request's Host → relative `/p/<id>`; nothing is remembered (the server cannot know about proxies/tunnels), agents get the path, and chat-renderers linkifies it against `location.origin`. **2.369.7 geo/storage bridge (B-74de, owner-directed via 生活方式助手's verified spec)**: the shell ships a per-request-nonce'd postMessage bridge (`script-src 'nonce-…'` added to the SHELL CSP only) forwarding a geolocation watch stream + `vp_<pageId>_`-namespaced localStorage into the sandboxed iframe. Protocol FROZEN (consumer pages ship it): `vibeBridge:'ready'{geo,store}` on iframe load / `vibeGeo:'start'|'stop'` → `'pos'{lat,lng,accuracy,heading,speed,ts}|'err'` / `vibeStore:'set'|'del'{k,v}`. Source verified (`e.source===iframe.contentWindow`), pages cannot read each other's keys, NO user content enters the shell (interpolations = ID_RE id + own nonce), and the red line stands verbatim — iframe never gains allow-same-origin, raw CSP untouched. Accepted trade-off: any published page may surface a geolocation PROMPT on this origin (browser still asks the user; per-page gating is the escalation path if that ever bites). Pins: test-published-pages (bridge presence, source-check+namespace, per-request nonce). **2.369.11 offline + blobs**: /p/sw.js service worker (location-capped scope /p/ — structurally cannot touch the app; network-first + 4s cache fallback, NOT cache-first — an online logged-out browser must never read a private page from cache) + vibeBlob protocol (IndexedDB vibespace-pages/blobs, `<pageId>:` keys, on-demand get — 10-30MB tile caches; vibeStore/localStorage stays for small values). Shell shows a 6s 'offline ready' badge when a cached copy exists.

**2.366.0 additions (published-pages.js).** `publishContent({html, name, srcKey, makePublic, sessionId, conversationId})` = the upload path (agent CLI / remote hosts; body in the request); upsert identity is `srcKey` (`local:<abs>` for file publishes, `<host>:<abs>` for uploads — legacy records get `local:`+srcPath on load); records carry `sessionId`/`conversationId` (`list({sessionId})`, `GET /api/pages?sessionId=`); `onPublished(page, {replaced})` is the ONE notify point (server.js broadcasts `page-published` to the session's clients) for dialog AND agent publishes; `noteBrowserOrigin(req)` (every management call) teaches the server the browser's origin (`x-forwarded-*` first) so `url` is absolute when `agentd.publicUrl` is unset — relative `/p/<id>` stays the honest fallback and the client joins it with `location.origin`.

**Delivery (conversation-deliver.js).** ONE ladder for jobs notifications AND agent messages (jobs-wiring's inline twin deleted — the 2.361.5 lesson: delivery topology must have one home): ① channel socket (experimental) ② local CLI inbox (peer-messaging findPeer/postToPeer — peer-messaging is now a SHARED-tier module, the daemon bundles it) ③ the OWNING machine's daemon via the `peer-post` agentd op (capability-gated three-touch; conversation-index.ownerHost names the machine — raw conversation uuids are the ONLY address, agents never learn topology) ④ durable stash `data/msg-stash.json` (cap 30/conversation, debounced atomic write) drained at the conversation's next context injection — machine-agnostic because remote sessions' hooks call back to the hub anyway. **2.368.26 rung ①.5 — registry lane `rpc-queue`** (between ② and ③, local only): when `capsOf(backend).peerDelivery === 'rpc-queue'` (codex; NEVER a backend-id branch — src/backend-caps.js is the registry) AND the wrapper's OWN sidecar adverts `caps.peerMessage` (2.361.1 law: gate on what the process wrote; negative verdicts never cached — stateless read per attempt), deliver writes a `peer-message` stdin frame to the live wrapper. The wrapper owns the app-server RPC connection: idle ⇒ `turn/start` (billed turn + reply, claude-inbox parity), busy ⇒ `thread/queue/add` (runs right after the current turn — upstream-test-pinned, codex 0.149 source). The frame carries `fromName`+`cardText` (P1, design-harness-plugins §1); the wrapper records the peer user message itself WITH a `webui_peer:{name,body}` marker (item notifications never carry `userMessage` ⇒ no double render; the codex normalizer renders that record as the LABELLED peer card live and on rebuild — marker-blind twin dedup, see codex-message-manager.js) so this rung does NOT emitPeerCard (an in-memory card on top would double-render live, and a stdin write is not yet a delivery); it reports `peer_message_result` both ways (ok:false echoes text+fromName) and ok:false makes session-stdout re-stash the echoed text with its label — a promised message is never lost. Old wrapper / claude session / no sidecar = honest miss falling to later rungs. Gate: scripts/test-peer-delivery.mjs (19 — functional real create() + sidecar + negative controls + the 2.355.0-class WIRING pins, incl. the recordCodexQuotaSignal dead-wiring fix this work uncovered). **2026-09-07 — THE FRAME CARRIES A TYPED ORIGIN, and it is the ladder's ONLY new job.** `deliverToConversation(cid, text, {kind})` resolves `kind` to `'notification'` (VibeSpace itself speaking: jobs.js `_deliverTo` stamps every owner/subscriber notification, and system notices join it) or `'peer'` (a person's message via `POST /api/agent/msg/send` — and the DEFAULT for absent/unknown, so an older caller can never silently change lane), puts it on the rung-1.5 stdin frame and echoes it in the result. It does NOT pick the lane: only the WRAPPER knows whether a turn is running right now, so the decision lives there (codex: busy notification ⇒ `turn/steer`, busy peer ⇒ `thread/queue/add`, idle ⇒ `turn/start`). The reason is an owner incident: a codex session had accumulated **20** "[VibeSpace Background Work] … done" items as 20 separate queued submissions = 20 billed turns waiting behind the one it was running ("系统通知默认应该是steering的" → "按照TUI实现吧"). WHICH lane a harness takes at all is DERIVED from its own capability row — `backend-caps notificationDelivery({peerDelivery, inputModes})` → `steer` (rpc-queue + a `steer` verb) / `queue` (rpc-queue without it) / `cli-inbox` (claude: its CLI queues a mid-turn delivery itself; that lane is the CLI's) / `stash` — the same derivation law that makes `inputModes.steer` a VIEW of `queueVerbs`, so "notifications steer" is never declared in a second place and never a backend-id branch. The client mirrors `peerDelivery` next to `inputModes` in BACKEND_META and derives through the SAME pure function (`notificationDeliveryFor`), which is what Session Properties → Background Work states in words. **jobs.js is UNCHANGED apart from the tag**: the 30s per-conversation flood floor and the `pendingNotifs` stash stay, and they are what makes a burst ONE frame — only the first distinct event is posted, the rest are stashed, and the stash is drained by the injection routes (agent-routes SessionStart + prompt-context) as ONE rendered block (`job-model renderNotifStash`) riding the conversation's next turn; it is never handed back to this ladder entry by entry, which is exactly how a batch would become N queued messages again. Gate: scripts/test-peer-delivery.mjs (44 — the derived lane table incl. a hypothetical rpc-queue-without-steer row, the client-mirror drift check, frame tagging + unknown/absent normalisation, and the wiring pins for jobs/msg-send/session-props/codex-events) + test-jobs-engine (the floor→stash→ONE-block drain leg) + test-codex-p2-wrapper ②f (the wrapper-side lanes against a real stub app-server).

**Routes/tool.** `/api/agent/msg/peers` + `/api/agent/msg/send` (vsst_; integration master switch gates; 30s per-pair floor + 10min dup dedupe + 16KB cap — a delivered message opens a BILLED turn on an idle receiver, the intro/manual/CLI all teach this). CLI `vibespace-msg list/send`; manual `docs/agent/msg-manual.md` (`vibespace-docs msg`). Incoming messages render via the existing peer-message card (origin.name → 2.361.6).

**Delivery-site card rendering (2.363.0).** Server-posted injections reach the CLI as an UNREGISTERED poster → the CLI records origin {kind:'peer',from:'unknown'} with no name/msg_id/body, so neither the JSONL rebuild nor result-mining can attribute or even display them. The ladder therefore renders the receiving chat window's peer card ITSELF on every ok lane (`emitPeerCard` dep from server.js → session normalizer `injectPeerCard`; opts.fromName/opts.cardText on deliverToConversation carry the label + raw body). Stash drains (agent-msg ×2 + job-notifications ×2 sites in agent-routes) emit the same card at injection time. injectPeerCard is deliberately containment-free — the delivery site posts once per fire, and same-body repeats are legitimate (the 2.362.2 review lesson).

**Invariants.** Delivery topology stays in ONE module — any new notification-shaped feature consumes conversation-deliver, never scans registries itself; ACL changes stay in the PURE module with matrix tests (scripts/test-agent-msg.mjs, 41); the per-session override only WIDENS; stash envelopes always carry `source`; message VISIBILITY is owned by the party that has the information — when the transport strips attribution, the POSTER renders the card.

## src/otel-truth.js + src/server/otel-ingest.js (2.361.0, B-345b 终案 — the per-request billing-TRUTH stack)

**Why it exists.** Pool hot-switches do NOT take effect inside a RUNNING claude process — repointPoolSymlink's utimes trick invalidates the CLI's mtime-gated credential cache only for NEW processes / token expiry; a live CLI kept billing the OLD org ≥25 minutes after being switched away (forensic case: 79928a2b, 6 auto-switches in 35min, old org climbed 35%→95% and rate-limited FROM that session while attribution said three other orgs; 558 mid-session switches / $7.9k post-switch spend in 12 days). Link-intent attribution therefore mis-books storm spend in both directions, poisoning per-org odometers AND rate learning (the ≥10pt burst calibration residual of -30~-49% — six alternative mechanisms tested and REFUTED: walker gaps ($0 diff vs raw transcripts), reading lag (storm-level residual doesn't cancel), training censoring (73 big pairs in-set), output share, family mix, cw5/cw1 mix, per-request overhead, unresolved-host exclusion).

**The channel.** Transcript records, statusline payloads and rate_limit_event all carry quota VALUES but never identity (verified field inventory, CLI 2.1.235). The CLI's built-in OpenTelemetry export is the ONLY per-request source that NAMES the org: every `claude_code.api_request` log record carries organization.id + user.account_uuid/email + request_id (the ledger's rid) + model + all four token classes + cost_usd; prompt content is redacted upstream. Base endpoint + `/v1/logs` path semantics verified empirically.

**Data flow.** ws-create's r6Env IIFE injects OTEL_* env into LOCAL claude spawns (gate `!session.host && backend==='claude' && otelEnv()`; a user-set OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT in the session spec wins; process-env channel — the PERSISTED token (data/usage-history/otel-token, 0600; per-boot would silently 403 sessions surviving a restart) rides OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_HEADERS, never argv). The CLI POSTs to `/otel/v1/logs` on THIS instance (cookie middleware exempts `/otel/`; the module's loopback-remoteAddress + token gate is the ONLY door — /svc precedent). otel-ingest then: ① `truthLookup(rid)` → consumed by `UsageHistory.scan` at BAKE time (`setTruthLookup`; undefined = fall back to the attribution walk, null = machine global login); ② corrective `recordAttribution({sid, trueAcct, pool-preserved, ts})` when observed ≠ current walk (dedup per sid on last truth acct; org flips re-write; agreeing truth just arms the marker); ③ append-only stash `data/usage-history/otel-truth.ndjson` (acctKnown honesty for unknown orgs, boot-replayed into the rid map — bake overrides survive restarts, 30d/12MB trim).

**2026-09-07 — THE OVERRIDE IS DEMOTED TO A CORROBORATING SIGNAL (owner decision ut-1c6c15a2db ①).** The channel's premise held that `organization.id` names the org that AUTHORIZED each request. The owner's post-mortem corrected it: it is the identity the CLI cached in its config dir at SPAWN, so for a hot-switched session it names the org the process STARTED on while the credential file it re-reads is the link's. **Round 1 (2.369.66) changed only the CONSUMER:** `usage-pool-engine` stopped letting `observedOrgFor` decide anything about BLOCKING; quota VALUES kept the observed-org routing (`orgVerifiedKey`). **Round 2 (the same day, this module) finishes it — the observation now attributes NOTHING:** ① `truthLookup` is RENAMED `observedOrgForRid` and DELIBERATELY UNWIRED (`usageHistory.setTruthLookup(...)` is gone from server.js; the seam survives in usage-history for a future channel that really does name the AUTHORIZING identity, and a source pin fails any re-wire) — it had overridden the by-time attribution walk at bake time, booking a hot-switched session's spend to the account it started on for the rest of its life; ② the corrective `recordAttribution` writes are REMOVED — a disagreement is now COUNTED (`arrivals.disagreed`, `/api/otel-stats`), logged once per (sid, transition) and stamped on the stash row as `{attributed, agreed}`, so the refutation stays analyzable offline forever; ③ the receiver, the stash, the boot replay and `observedOrgFor` are untouched — the observation still corroborates a wall on the observed member and still speaks in the journal. The bake is the WALK again, and the walk's re-points are now recorded in `data/slot-transitions.jsonl`. **Open follow-up:** ③ projection-driven re-reads (the ledger BACKFILL is done — see the readings-by-slot repair migration). Treat any future "the observation is the truth" claim about BILLING as needing new evidence — this one was measured wrong twice.

**Invariants (do not regress).** Zero vendor calls — the CLI pushes to us (§ban-safety compatible by construction; test-vendor-whitelist unchanged). Unknown orgs are COUNTED and logged once, never silently dropped (api_retry lesson) — an org resolves via usage-cache orgUuid (identityGroups 'org:<uuid>') else roster email; teach a missing org by clicking ⟳ once. envFor() returns null when `usage.otelTruth` is off (kill switch, default ON). OTEL_METRICS_EXPORTER stays 'none' (logs carry the events; metrics/traces routes 200 tolerantly). Gated by scripts/test-otel-truth.mjs (28: sanitized REAL-payload fixtures, real-HTTP ingest, REAL bake-path scan, wiring pins) + a chat-E2E assert that proves env→receiver→parser on every push's live haiku turn. Known residue: events scanned BEFORE their truth arrived keep the link-intent acct (scan races OTel by ≤15s throttle vs 5s flush — rare; a truth-aware shard repair mirroring the existing rebake precedent is the follow-up if soak metrics show material counts — watch otel-truth-correction / otel-truth-req in Diagnostics).

### usage-history.js

  usage-history.js     — UsageHistory (2.61.0, codex 2.63.0): PERMANENT per-request token LEDGER for the Usage window. Mines Claude's OWN JSONL transcripts (terminal AND chat — mode-independent; **2.265.0: ALSO `<sid>/subagents/**` agent + workflow transcripts — workflow agents' usage exists ONLY there (~$205/run measured invisible), events attribute to the PARENT sid, journal files excluded, rid-dedup absorbs parent-sidechain overlap; the top-level-only scan under-reported every workflow run AND poisoned dead-reckoning rates**) AND Codex rollouts (~/.codex/sessions/**/rollout-*-<threadId>.jsonl: `event_msg`/`token_count` → `info.last_token_usage` = one request {input⊇cached, output⊇reasoning}; info:null = rate-limit heartbeat, skip; NO requestId → synthetic rid = `cx:<sid>:<cumulative total_tokens>` (strictly monotonic per thread — replays dedup); model/cwd from the preceding `turn_context` record PERSISTED IN THE CURSOR since incremental scans start mid-file; cw5/cw1 always 0 — rollouts don't report cache-write counts) into append-only monthly shards data/usage-history/events-YYYY-MM.ndjson; incremental per-file {offset,lastRid[,model,cwd]} byte cursor (offset is BYTES not string length — CJK bug, do not regress), reads CHUNKED via _scanFileLines (32MB slabs, complete-lines-only — a 1.9GB rollout exceeds Node's max string; first 2.3GB pass ≈8s, then incremental). **PERF (2.70.0):** /api/usage-stats used to re-read+re-parse EVERY shard per request — seconds of CPU at 218k events even on fast storage, and observed at 18s while a concurrent full-disk scan saturated IO (the workspace is a FUSE NFS mount; its IDLE baseline is fast — the '40ms/read' measured that day was contention, per the user's correction). Now: in-memory event cache (`_loadEvents` — shards are append-only, so after one full load each call reads only appended bytes, last-newline-aligned BYTE offsets; rid-dedup at load), scan() throttled 15s (force flag for the 3-min timer path), `_metaMap` TTL-cached 60s (66 tiny files = 2.7s over NFS), `_tier` memoized per model, `warm()` at boot (+8s). 18s→0.3s hot. DEDUP BY requestId TWICE (scan-time contiguous + read-time Set in _events — one request = 2-3 identical/sometimes-non-adjacent records). Account attribution BAKED per event (acct+atype: subscription/api/codex-subscription/global) PER-REQUEST-BY-TIME via attribution.ndjson (**2.84.0 ATTRIBUTION FIX, real data bug**: requests predating a session's FIRST attribution entry are GLOBAL (10-min grace for spawn skew) — the old `list[0].acct` fallback billed a week of pre-registration history to a newly added subscription during the initial 2.61.0 backfill (19.7k of Fish Max's 20.9k events were pre-registration); one-time `_maybeRebakeAttribution` (.attrib-rebake-v1 marker) rewrote 20,304 baked events) (appended from writeSessionMeta→recordUsageAttribution; sid = claudeSessionId||backendSessionId so codex threads attribute too) so sub $ and API $ NEVER mix. aggregate({from,to,backend,accounts}) → totals + series + groups(day/model/account/billing/project/mode/hour/weekday/session) (seq dims day/hour/weekday sort by KEY, not cost — cost-sorting scrambled the dashboard's hour axis, 2.180.2; the dashboard also self-sorts + gap-fills the closed hour/weekday scales + names weekdays); accounts = Set of bucket keys (account ids / '__global__' claude-global / '__global_codex__' codex-global — the two CLIs' machine logins are SEPARATE buckets since 2.64.0, billing keys 'cli-global-login'/'codex-cli-login') for the window's account filter. Pricing v2 pricing.json {tiers,accounts:{id:{discount}|{tiers}}} — account-aware _cost; _tier is DATA-DRIVEN (longest tiers-key substring match — new model = new pricing.json key, no code); official rates verified 2026-07-09 (Fable $10/$50, Opus 4.5-4.8 $5/$25, GPT-5.6 Sol $5/$30 Terra $2.5/$15 Luna $1/$6, GPT-5.5 $5/$30; missing default tiers back-fill into an existing pricing.json without clobbering edits). See [[project_usage_ledger]] 2.369.36 (inc-mtox23xw): `_events(from,to)` walks a lazily sorted view with binary search (O(log n + k)); `_evCountUpTo(t)` is the memo version for interval costs; `_loadEvents` re-checks shards at most once/s (scan()'s own appends reset the throttle). The estimator called `_events` once per anchor PAIR — with the permanent ledger that was a full scan + readdir/stat per pair and blocked the loop 10-59s (caught by Debugger.pause; the CPU profiler mislabelled it idle). **Codex join fields (2026-09-06):** scan() and ingestRemoteEvents pass the walker's `mid` (codex = the 0.153 `resp_…` response id) and new `effort` through unchanged (undefined for claude ⇒ not serialized); `eventForRid` matches the `cx:` key exactly / `h:<host>:cx:…` by suffix, `eventForMid` matches `ev.mid` — the rid-info route's two lookups resolve codex events without any new index; the `mids` cache set now also holds `resp_…` ids (harmless to the live-odometer exclusion join, which keys codex live entries on the same cx rid). Pricing v2 pricing.json {tiers,accounts:{id:{discount}|{tiers}}} — account-aware _cost; `pricingToken()` (2.369.43) is the table's memo VERSION for anything that caches computed costs (the anchors' interval memo is the one consumer) — recomputed only when setPricing REPLACES `_pricing`; _tier is DATA-DRIVEN (longest tiers-key substring match — new model = new pricing.json key, no code); official rates verified 2026-07-09 (Fable $10/$50, Opus 4.5-4.8 $5/$25, GPT-5.6 Sol $5/$30 Terra $2.5/$15 Luna $1/$6, GPT-5.5 $5/$30; missing default tiers back-fill into an existing pricing.json without clobbering edits). See [[project_usage_ledger]] 2.369.36 (inc-mtox23xw): `_events(from,to)` walks a lazily sorted view with binary search (O(log n + k)); `_evCountUpTo(t)` is the memo version for interval costs; `_loadEvents` re-checks shards at most once/s (scan()'s own appends reset the throttle). The estimator called `_events` once per anchor PAIR — with the permanent ledger that was a full scan + readdir/stat per pair and blocked the loop 10-59s (caught by Debugger.pause; the CPU profiler mislabelled it idle).

  **A POOL IS NEVER A SPENDER IN THE ACCOUNT DIMENSION (2026-09-07 r3, reproduced).** `_acctAt`'s last resort is session-meta's `accountId`, which is the SPAWN identity — and for a pooled session `resolveForSpawn` returns the POOL's own id, so the fallback baked `acct:'pool-…', atype:'pooled'` onto ledger events: a pseudo-account that holds no credentials, double-counted against its own members, in the per-account totals. server.js states the invariant verbatim at the OTHER site that reaches this decision ("an unresolvable pool target falls to GLOBAL, never to the pool id itself"); `_nonPoolAcct` applies it here, on BOTH the ordinary scan and the r2 emptied-sid re-bake. Which MEMBER it was is deliberately not guessed — this fallback only runs for a conversation with no attribution entry at all, exactly the case the repair could not place from the transition ledger either, so global is the honest answer and the `pool` tag (now also derived from meta on the scan path) keeps the per-pool total correct. `_poolIdOf` has TWO legs on purpose: the injected `resolveAccount` maps `backend === 'codex'` to one type BEFORE `a.type` is read, so it reports a CODEX pool as `'codex-subscription'` and the type test alone misses every one — the minted id shape (`'pool-' + randomBytes(6).hex`, one call site in accounts.createPool) is primary, the type is the belt, and each leg has a test that fails without it.

### login-expiry.js / server/login-expiry-watch.js (2026-09-07 — the LOGIN SESSION's own deadline)

  src/login-expiry.js — PURE (imports nothing; the daemon and the browser bundle can both carry it). THE FACT, measured on this instance: a Claude subscription's `data/subs/<id>/.credentials.json` carries `claudeAiOauth.refreshTokenExpiresAt` = the ABSOLUTE end of the LOGIN SESSION, and it does NOT move when the access token refreshes (a member refreshed 30 min earlier still showed an expiry 17 h out while siblings showed 5 and 16 days). Past it the CLI's refresh gets `invalid_grant`, prints "Failed to authenticate: OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed", and BLANKS accessToken/refreshToken/expiresAt while KEEPING refreshTokenExpiresAt + scopes — that wiped shape is what "logged out" looks like on disk (three members were in it when this shipped). `loginState(creds, now)` → `{state: 'ok'|'expiring'|'expired'|'logged-out'|'unknown', refreshExpiresAt, accessExpiresAt, msLeft}`: EXPIRING_MS = 24 h (the chip + the first warning), NEAR_MS = 30 min (a SWITCH TARGET must outlive the switch). **'unknown' means NO CLAIM and every consumer must treat it as "do not block"** — missing/garbage fields are unknown, NEVER 'ok' (claiming health we cannot read is how the old behaviour surprised people); an oauth record with no tokens AND no residue is also unknown, because `{}` is what a Console /login wipe writes and it is not evidence of an expiry. Predicates: `loginUsable` (dead = expired|logged-out), `loginSwitchTarget` (dead OR inside NEAR), `loginRank` (0 ok/unknown · 1 expiring · 2 near · 3 dead — the LAST tiebreak, never a reordering of what quota separates), `loginBlockReason`/`loginBucketLabel` (the SPEAK rule's named bucket), `loginBlockedPhrase`/`loginBlockedText` (round 2 — how a notice SAYS a blocked member: each one states its own fact, `A (login expired), B (signed out), C (login expires in 20 min)`, because round 1 wrote one sentence over a list mixing all three and reported a member with 20 min left as expired), `loginWallPhrase` (round 3 — how the CURRENT member's own dead login is said inside a sentence that has already named the account: a CLAUSE, `Fish Max's login session expired` / `Fish Max's login is signed out`, where `loginBucketLabel` is a LIST-ITEM label. It exists because round 2 had no output for that fact at all and put the label into `deadBuckets`, where the notice renders it as a spent quota bucket), `reviewWarnings(info, entry, now, opts)` = the once-per-member-per-threshold transition (a DIFFERENT refreshTokenExpiresAt is a re-login ⇒ the old ledger is dropped; a SKIPPED rung fires the most urgent one and marks the rest done — a stale "expires in 24 h" filed when 40 min are left is worse than silence). **ROUND 2 — the terminal rung had no staleness gate.** The ledger loads empty on a fresh install, so the FIRST sweep after the upgrade filed an `urgent` "For you" item for every login that had ever died: three on the instance this was measured on (dead 15 h, 116 h and 700 h, none of them a routing member), i.e. the feature opened on a red blinking badge about accounts abandoned weeks ago. `opts.watchingSince` (the ledger's own birth, persisted as `since`) makes a death that happened BEFORE we were watching AND is older than `STALE_GRACE_MS` (= EXPIRING_MS, one day) a PRE-EXISTING CONDITION: recorded as done, `suppressed: 'pre-existing'`, never spoken — its surface is the permanent red chip in Manage Agents, not an event inbox. BOTH clauses are load-bearing: a death inside the grace is still news on a fresh install, and a death AFTER `watchingSince` is one WE missed (server was off), which is the whole promise. The FUTURE rungs (24 h / 1 h) are untouched — a coming death is always news. A logged-out record with no readable deadline cannot be dated, so it is never suppressed (firing is the fail-safe direction). Omit `watchingSince` and the gate is inert (pinned with a control). **RECOVERY (2026-09-07): `reloggedIn(info, prevExp)` — did a REAL new login session start?** Three clauses, each load-bearing: the login must be ALIVE again ('ok'/'expiring'), because a wiped file KEEPS its deadline and that deadline can be in the FUTURE, so "the number went up" alone reads a signed-out account as recovered; there must be a readable deadline now (a live token with no deadline is 'unknown' = NO CLAIM, and no claim never overrides a warning filed from a fact); and the deadline must be DIFFERENT from the recorded one — a session's deadline is fixed for its whole life, so a change can only come from a new session, while a REWRITE that keeps it (an access-token refresh, a creds file copied between machines, an import) is the SAME session and nothing may be retracted. Deliberately NOT "strictly newer": an org with a short SSO policy hands back a deadline that can be EARLIER than the one we warned about (warned with 20 h left, the new session is 12 h long), and a `>` test leaves that now-false item open forever — the same defect one scenario over. Retraction cannot lose a deserved warning either way, because the ladder re-arms on the new deadline in the SAME sweep. `prevExp == null` with a live deadline now IS a re-login (the recorded row was the un-datable wiped shape). `SHORT_SESSION_MS` (36 h) + `shortSession(spanMs)` back the session-policy hint: a MEASURED login session under 36 h is not how Claude subscriptions normally behave (the deadlines here were 5, 9, 12 and 16 DAYS out) and looks like an org SSO policy — never true for a missing measurement.
  src/server/login-expiry-watch.js — the PASSIVE sweep (create(deps) factory; server.js starts it in ONE wiring line). Boot+5 min (the 1 h rung needs a cadence well under an hour — the oat sweep's 6 h would sail past it), reads the account roster + each subscription's credential file through `accounts.loginStateOf`, and files at most THREE "For you" inbox items per login: 24 h (normal) / 1 h (high) / expired (urgent), each naming the ACCOUNT and the EXPIRY TIME. **`itemTextFor` branches on the login's STATE, never on the RUNG (round 3).** `warnStageFor` maps BOTH 'expired' and 'logged-out' onto the terminal 'expired' rung — the rung answers "how urgent", the state answers "what happened". A WIPED credential file is produced by any unrecoverable refresh (a revoked or rotated session, an explicit logout in an isolated dir), not only by the deadline passing, and the CLI KEEPS `refreshTokenExpiresAt` when it blanks the tokens, so that deadline can still be in the FUTURE: round 2 filed an `urgent` item reading `Claude login for "Fish Max" expired 2026-09-27 20:01 UTC` — a past-tense claim about a date 20 days out — while `loginExpiryChipHtml`, which DOES branch on the state, said "login signed out — re-login" on the same row. Two surfaces, one record, opposite claims. (Every wiped dir on this instance happens to have a past deadline, which is why the fixtures never hit it — they inherited the local sample, not the shape.) The wiped shape now reads "is signed out — the CLI cleared its tokens", with `(its login session ended <t>)` appended only when the deadline really has passed; the `detail` carries the deadline beside `State: <state>`, in the tense the number earns ("ends" vs "ended"). Ledger `data/login-expiry.json` (atomic tmp+rename), carrying `since` = when this ledger started watching ⇒ a restart replays nothing, a re-login goes silent, and the upgrade day does not open on a screen of urgent items about logins that died before the feature existed (round 2; the grace is spent exactly once because `since` is persisted). A REFUSED inbox write (the 20-open-per-key cap) rolls the rung back so the next sweep retries — marking it sent would be the no-silent-failures violation this feature exists to fix. Items land in the `accounts` inbox bucket (like jobs' `jobs` bucket); user-todos-panel jumps them to Manage Agents. **§ban-safety: file reads only — no vendor call, no probe, no token ever touched.** **RECOVERY — a filed item is a CLAIM, and the thing that filed it must RETRACT it (2026-09-07, measured here).** Dropping the ledger row on a re-login only silences FUTURE filings: the owner re-logged two members (fresh tokens, a deadline 29 days out) and the "For you" panel still listed `Claude login for "Fish Max" is signed out — the CLI cleared its tokens…` as OPEN, forever, until someone ticked it off by hand — while the chip on the same row had already gone green. The member row now carries `items: [{id, text}]` (the ids of what THIS watch filed for THIS deadline, persisted — a server that restarts between the warning and the re-login must still be able to retract, or the bug is back with an extra step), and a `reloggedIn` transition resolves each of them through `userTodos.setStatus(id,'done','system')` — the store's OWN path, so it saves AND broadcasts and every open client updates live — then journals ONE line, `X: re-logged in — N warning(s) cleared`. Three things it must never do, each with a negative control: **reopen or re-resolve an item the USER already handled** (any item that is not `open` is dropped from the list untouched), **touch an item filed by anything else** (it only ever acts on ids from its own ledger, and re-checks `sessionKey` + text before writing — an agent's vibespace-ask item in another session is invisible to it), and **treat a credentials rewrite with the SAME deadline as a re-login**. `resolvedBy: 'system'` is neither 'user' nor 'agent'; the panel's resolved tail labels it `automatically`, because an item that vanishes on its own must say it vanished on its own. **THE IMMEDIATE SWEEP:** the poll is 5 min, which is a long time to keep staring at the item about the login you JUST fixed, so every login FINALIZE route (`/api/accounts/:id/relogin-finalize`, `/api/accounts/subscription/:id/finalize` — placed ABOVE the auto-merge branch's early return, so both exits sweep — and the codex device-auth finalize) sweeps when the login actually succeeded. server.js keeps the handle and passes it as `getLoginExpiryWatch`, a lazy getter like every other late singleton. **ROUND 2 — the gate has to be the answer the method actually gives, and a POLLED route may not carry an unbounded side effect.** (i) Round 1 gated the re-login route on `r.loggedIn`, a property `accounts.reloginResolve` returns on NONE of its four outcomes — it answers `{outcome:'pending'}`, `{outcome:'same'|'split', account}` or `{outcome:'moved', movedTo}`, so the captured login rides in a NESTED `account` and 'moved' has no account at all. The sweep was therefore DEAD on the exact route the inbox item and the login chip send the user to, and the suite was green only because its fixture invented that shape (the §5b fixtures are now CAPTURED from a real `reloginResolve` call against a temp store, so the method's shape cannot drift away from them). The gate is `!!r.account?.loggedIn || r.outcome === 'moved'`: 'moved' relocated a live login into ANOTHER record, whose ledger row is the one to retract; 'pending' stays silent. (ii) manage-agents polls `/relogin-finalize` every 3 s for up to 5 minutes and 'same' repeats, so a side effect hung on the answer runs ~100× (measured: 100 polls → 100 sweeps). The route sweeps only on the TRANSITION — `sweepOnLoginTransition` compares the answer's CREDENTIAL FINGERPRINT (outcome + record + identity + access-token expiry, all read off the answer the route already has, so it is NOT a second credential reader) with the last one it swept for. Deliberately not a boolean 'already swept this account' latch: re-logging a STILL-logged-in account answers 'same' from poll #1, minutes before the browser flow finishes, so a latch would skip exactly the sweep that matters. (iii) The Add-flow finalize sweeps AGAIN, right after a successful `accounts.mergeSubscription`. The sweep above `fin` runs while the fresh credentials are still in the THROWAWAY record's dir; the survivor — the record carrying the open warning — still has its dead file, so that sweep reads a stale deadline and retracts nothing. `mergeSubscription` is the moment the survivor's file changes. The pre-merge sweep stays because it is the only sweep on every non-merge exit, and because the sweep is idempotent (pinned). **ONE READER:** the sweep asks `accounts.loginStateOf`, which is exactly what the roster row's `loginState` — the chip — is built from, so the chip and the inbox cannot disagree about the same file and there is no second credential reader to drift; the failure is logged, never thrown (a login must not fail because a follow-up sweep did). **THE SESSION-POLICY HINT** on the 24 h / 1 h rungs: when this member's last login session was MEASURED under 36 h, the item adds `(its last login session lasted only ~24 h — this org's session policy may be short)`. The measurement is the load-bearing part. The naive predicate — deadline minus the credential file's mtime — reports a healthy 30-day login as a 20-hour one, because every access-token refresh rewrites that file while `refreshTokenExpiresAt` stays put; at the 24 h rung on an actively-used account it is < 36 h essentially always. So `measureLoginSpan` only trusts the mtime when the login was WITNESSED: an OBSERVATION LOG (`seen`, one row per subscription written every sweep, deliberately separate from the sparse warning ledger — a healthy member has no warning row, and that is precisely the member whose next login we need to have witnessed) confirmed this member ≤3 sweeps ago, the deadline has CHANGED since, and the file was written after that confirmation. That pins the login write to a window one sweep wide. Nothing witnessed ⇒ nothing said; and a recorded span shorter than what is still LEFT of the current deadline is stale, so `itemTextFor` drops it too. It says "may be": one session of one member is evidence about a policy, not a reading of it. `src/harnesses/claude.js` is the only place that touches the file, and it reports `writtenAt` = the mtime alongside the pure verdict. Gate: scripts/test-login-expiry.mjs (255, incl. a fake-clock ladder, restart survival, the re-login retraction end to end against the REAL UserTodoManager with every negative control, the routes factory driven with a stub express app so the lazy getter is proven to resolve at request time, §5b's fixtures captured from the real `reloginResolve`, the 100-poll transition measurement, and §5c which drives the REAL AccountManager + UserTodoManager + watch through the REAL routes and asserts the inbox EFFECT — a call count cannot tell you the sweep found anything, which is how round 1's merge leg stayed green — plus a REAL 375×667 headless-chrome measurement of the chip).

### user-todos.js

  user-todos.js        — UserTodoManager (2.65.0, the GLOBAL user-facing TODO list / "For you" inbox): discrete items an AGENT filed that need the USER (decision/input/review) — the inverse of the agent's own TodoWrite. One item = one owning session (sessionKey like session-status, webui:→backend rekey in the agent route); data/user-todos.json (session-status persistence pattern: debounced 500ms + content-compared + exit flush); add() idempotent BY TEXT across ALL statuses (open → refresh; resolved/dismissed → REOPEN the SAME id — review finding: fresh-id minting let an add→resolve loop or a dismissed-text re-file toast-spam every client despite the open cap); caps: 20 open/session (add throws with guidance), 1000 total (oldest RESOLVED pruned, open never); resolveByAgent by id or unique text substring (own session only); snapshot() = {open: urgency-then-newest sorted, resolved: last 15}; 'user-todos-updated' broadcast on every change. Client = src/lib/user-todos-panel.js. KNOWN LIMIT: webui:→backend rekey happens on the agent's NEXT call only (same as session-status) — in practice unreachable since vibespace-ask runs mid-turn when the backend id already exists. Client review fixes (do not regress): jump falls back to attachSession for a live session with NO open window (goToWindow is a no-op then); resync fetch on ws reconnect (items filed while offline stayed invisible); setStatus checks r.success (fetchJson never throws); knownIds includes the resolved tail (reopen must not self-toast). Panel detail+origin (2.69.1): item detail renders behind a collapsed `.ut-detail-exp` expander (open AND resolved rows — inline 2000-char details swamped the list); resolved rows carry the source session name (`.ut-sess`) and row-click jumps like open rows; clicks inside the expander are guarded from the row's jump-and-close

### task-groups.js

  task-groups.js       — TaskGroupManager (岗位/Task-Group store; renamed from tasks.js in 2.39.0 — data/task-groups.json authoritative, migrated once from legacy data/tasks.json; ⊃ legacy groups, CRUD + bind/unbind + progress, tasks-updated broadcast, export/import; groupsForSession = LIVE many-to-many belonging, renderContext/renderMultiContext injection, contextDirSignature, per-group injectContext toggle; **2.369.15: `_syncTaskMd` consults the `isPathShadowed(contextDir)` dep (server.js lazy getter → mounts.shadowedBy) and SKIPS a context folder whose storage is disconnected — writing would recreate the tree on the bare mount point (the OneDrive stranded-writes incident); `syncAllContextMd()` retries on mounts-updated;** design: docs/design-task-system.md + design-task-refactor.md. NOTE: internal ids/wire names still say task/plan/progress — see the 2.39.0 refactor note)

### vnc.js

  vnc.js               — VncManager (2.104.0, in-container desktop): LAZY lifecycle — POST /api/vnc/start spawns Xtigervnc (-localhost -SecurityTypes None, safe: the cookie-authed /api/vnc WS bridge is the ONLY route in) + XFCE session, both DETACHED (app restart keeps the desktop; boot ADOPTS an already-listening port — also the KasmVNC/bring-your-own path and the Xvfb+x11vnc test path). Env: VIBESPACE_VNC_PORT/DISPLAY/GEOMETRY. Client = src/lib/desktop-window.js ('desktop' window type, ⚙ menu entry gated on /api/vnc/status.available probed once at startup; noVNC is a SEPARATE ESM bundle public/novnc.js dynamic-imported on first use — top-level await can't live in the IIFE main bundle) **VNC BLACKLIST TRAP (2.111.1, real report 'Too many security failures'):** Xtigervnc launches with **`-UseBlacklist 0`** — MANDATORY, not optional. TigerVNC blacklists a source host after N unauthenticated connect-then-drop attempts (default 5, timeout doubles per strike); ALL desktop traffic is 127.0.0.1 (the bridge) AND `portListening()` connects+immediately destroys the socket on every status poll → TigerVNC counts each probe as a failure and locks localhost out. The cookie-authed bridge is the only route in, so the blacklist protects nothing. An already-tripped blacklist is in-memory: kill the stale Xtigervnc (or redeploy) to clear it.

### ssh-key-format.js

  ssh-key-format.js    — Private-key FORMAT classifier, PURE + shared server/browser (CJS pulled into the bundle like task-color-seq.js). **WHY a decoder and not a regex (2.246.0): an `openssh-key-v1` key's PEM armor is BYTE-IDENTICAL with or without a passphrase — the word ENCRYPTED appears NOWHERE** (only classic PEM's `Proc-Type: 4,ENCRYPTED` and PKCS#8's `BEGIN ENCRYPTED PRIVATE KEY` announce themselves). The old first-3-lines /ENCRYPTED/ check therefore missed effectively every key produced since OpenSSH 7.8: they imported "fine" and failed at connect with a bare `Permission denied (publickey)` that never mentions a passphrase at ANY -v level. Encryption state lives in the DECODED body (`magic | ciphername | kdfname`; none/none = plain). classifyPrivateKey → {format: empty|openssh-v1|pkcs8|pem|ppk|ssh2|unknown, encrypted, cipher?, usable, malformed?}; TOTAL (runs on every keystroke — bounded to the first 256 base64 chars, length words capped at 64, hostile input returns a verdict and NEVER throws). ssh.com armor is FOUR dashes with spaces (`---- BEGIN SSH2 …----`), not the PEM five

### ssh-key.js

  ssh-key.js           — Server-side private-key UNLOCK at import (2.246.0). prepareImportedKey(text, passphrase) → {body, wasEncrypted, format, fingerprint, type}, KeyError codes key-encrypted/key-bad-passphrase/key-ppk/key-unsupported/key-not-a-key/key-too-large/key-no-ssh-keygen/key-unlock-unavailable/key-unlock-failed (the CLIENT renders localized text off the code). **PASSPHRASE TRANSPORT IS ENV, NEVER ARGV**: ssh-keygen reads a passphrase only from a tty or SSH_ASKPASS (never stdin), so a throwaway 0700 helper (`printf %s "$VIBESPACE_KEY_PASS"`) in a 0700 mkdtemp dir is exec'd with the secret in the CHILD ENV (/proc/<pid>/environ is 0400) + SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=force; argv is only `-p -N '' -f <tmpfile>` (/proc/<pid>/cmdline is world-readable — the 2.126.0 rule) and HOME points at the temp dir so ssh-keygen can never touch the server's ~/.ssh. Temp dir removed on EVERY path incl. throws. **NON-NEGOTIABLE ERROR ORDER: an askpass helper that can't be exec'd (noexec tmpdir) makes ssh-keygen exit 255 saying "incorrect passphrase supplied" for a CORRECT passphrase — the only discriminator is the `ssh_askpass:` marker in stderr, which MUST be checked BEFORE the passphrase branch** or a hardened container lies to users forever. Fingerprint is computed in JS from the `-y` blob (byte-identical to `ssh-keygen -l`, verified — no extra spawn). node crypto is NOT an option: it cannot parse openssh-key-v1 at all, so a crypto-based impl passes RSA tests and fails every real ed25519 key. **The STORED key is UNLOCKED — this removes the passphrase, it does not preserve it** (every ssh here is BatchMode=yes and can never prompt); disclosed in docs/device-agent.md. Tests: scripts/test-ssh-key.mjs (60) + scripts/test-ssh-key-dialog.mjs (24, real headless browser)

### ctx-sync.js

  ctx-sync.js          — **THE ctx-folder sync, ONE implementation for every machine (2.277.0).** The old split — ssh=bidirectional rsync NO caps, dial=hashed sync with a SILENT ≤400-files/≤2MB cap — meant a 3MB context file reached every ssh host and never reached a dial device, invisibly. Hashed newer-wins sync (sha-equal ⇒ untouched, echo can't ping-pong; traversal-guarded pulls) over the device link on EVERY transport; ssh degrades to its legacy rsync pair only when the link is down; caps 1000 files/16MB and **every capped skip is REPORTED** (deduped serverNotice — a bounded sync may refuse a file, never lose one silently). Local sessions read the contextDir directly (TRANSPORT-ONLY, not a divergence). Test: scripts/test-ctx-sync.mjs (13).

### cli-identity.js

  cli-identity.js      — **THE agent-CLI process identity — ONE rule, two spellings (B-3185 r3).** "Is pid N the `claude`/`codex` CLI?" is asked by three consumers that used to answer it three ways: the pre-resume WRITER SWEEP (shell — decides who receives a SIGTERM), the ssh discovery **CO leg** (shell, the same text embedded verbatim — decides which codex threads read RUNNING) and **src/discovery-facts.js in JavaScript** (the local listing, the device snapshot's lock scan and its CO lines). B-3185 r1/r2 replaced the substring rule with an EXECUTABLE test in the two shell copies and RECORDED the JS one as a deliberate twin, on the grounds that it only labels a card RUNNING. r3 killed it: the blast radius genuinely differs, but a rule with two spellings has two behaviours the moment either is touched, and the loose spelling was doing real damage of its own — measured on the dev box, `cmdline.includes('claude')` answered YES for **106 of 4277 processes** against **16 real claude CLIs** (dtach masters, chat-wrappers, the generated `code` editor helper, a zsh shell-snapshot), and `isCodexCommandLine` for **30 against 4**, so any lock file whose pid had been RECYCLED by one of them produced exactly the phantom "running" session the PID-reuse guard exists to prevent. Exports `isCliProcess(pid, name)` (JS) + `cliIdentityShellFns()` (the POSIX `vs_argv`/`vs_is_cli` text, re-exported by writer-sweep.js so existing call sites are unchanged — the suite asserts it is the SAME function object, not a copy). **THE RULE, three rungs, one per shape the CLIs ship in:** ① argv[0]'s basename is the CLI name (`~/.local/bin/claude`; the codex vendor binary); ② argv[0] is an interpreter (node/nodejs/node.exe/bun/deno) and its FIRST NON-FLAG operand is the entry point (`node <prefix>/bin/claude`, `node …/@anthropic-ai/claude-code/cli.js`, `node …/@openai/codex/bin/codex.js`; up to 6 leading flags skipped); ③ `/proc/<pid>/exe` basename IS the CLI, or the exe is the native install's version FILE (`…/<name>/versions/<ver>`) **and** the process still PRESENTS as the CLI (argv[0] basename starts with the name, or argv[0] basename EQUALS the exe basename = nothing renamed) — the r2 narrowing that keeps a re-exec'd bundled helper (argv[0] `ugrep`, same image) out. A later ARGUMENT never identifies a process; that is exactly what the retired rule got wrong. **` (deleted)` IS STRIPPED FROM /proc/<pid>/exe (r3, defect 2).** The kernel appends it once the image is replaced on disk — which is what `claude` auto-update and `npm i -g @openai/codex` do to LIVE sessions; two of the four running agent CLIs on this box carry the suffix at any given moment. Without the strip BOTH executable rungs miss (basename `claude (deleted)`; the "nothing renamed" disjunct compares `2.1.258` against `2.1.258 (deleted)`), so the ONE process the sweep exists to stop — a mid-update CLI still appending to the transcript — survived it, and a double-writer is *most* likely right after an update lands. **argv IS A LIST OF NUL-SEPARATED WORDS AND A WORD MAY CONTAIN A NEWLINE (r4).** The JS side reads that list; the shell side could not, so `vs_argv` turned NULs into newlines and took the Nth LINE — which is the FIRST LINE of the Nth word. The two spellings therefore answered differently about the same live process whenever a word carried a newline, in BOTH directions and both harmful: argv[0] `/usr/bin/claude<LF>/usr/bin/tail` is basename `tail` to JS and `claude` to the old shell (the sweep SIGTERMs a reader — B-3185's own harm through a different door), and `<dir>/dir<LF>name/claude` is the reverse (a real writer survives the sweep, which is the double-writer the sweep exists to prevent). The shell now parks real newlines on `\001` for the line select and restores them after (`tr '\n' '\001' | tr '\0' '\n' | sed -n Np | tr '\001' '\n'`), so "the Nth line" IS "the Nth NUL-record" in either spelling; the residue is a literal `\001` inside an argv word coming back as a newline — a byte no path or flag carries, and since the swap cannot move a `/` the basename splits at the same place in both spellings, so it can only make a word look LESS like the CLI. The `ps` fallback flattens its blob first (`tr '\n' ' '`) because the JS twin's `/\s+/` split always treated a newline as a space while awk splits per line. **THAT LAST SENTENCE WAS A DESCRIPTION UNTIL r5.** The `ps` branch is the NO-/proc rung (macOS/BSD ssh hosts); on a Linux box every fixture takes the /proc rung, so the half of the r4 fix that lives there shipped with no assertion and no control — (r) again. It is now DRIVEN, with no edit to the text under test: the `/proc/$1/` root is re-rooted so BOTH reads miss (three literals, asserted — leaving the exe read live let rung 3 decide and the `ps` rung was never reached), a stand-in `ps` sits first on PATH, and the JS twin runs in a child whose two /proc reads THROW so the shipped `procArgv`/`isCliProcess` really take their own fallback. The stand-in is asserted to be necessary rather than assumed: measured, procps 4.x `ps -o args=` renders an embedded newline as a SPACE, so this box's own `ps` cannot produce the multi-line blob the flattening exists for. Both spellings must return the identical word for argv[0..2] and the identical verdict on three live fixtures (`ps` cannot recover word boundaries — fixture `…/claude<LF>/usr/bin/tail` is `claude` to BOTH here while the /proc rung calls it `tail`; that is a property of `ps`, not a divergence); negative control = the verbatim pre-r4 line without the flattening, which makes awk print field i of EVERY line and answers the OPPOSITE of the JS twin on both newline fixtures while staying indistinguishable on a plain one. **AND THE VALUE STILL HAD TO SURVIVE `$(…)`, WHICH STRIPS EVERY TRAILING NEWLINE (r4, second half — found by measuring the r4 fix instead of describing it).** A word or path that ENDS in one read `…/claude` in the shell and `…/claude<LF>` in JS, i.e. the shell said YES where THE identity says NO, on the paths that kill. All three captures were affected and each needed its own live fixture: argv[0] `…/claude<LF>` (rung 1), argv[0] `…/claude<0x01>` (the park byte comes back out AS a newline, so a TRAILING one was eaten too — that is the one case where the residue sentence above would have been false), and `/proc/<pid>/exe` `…/claude<LF>` under a launcher argv[0] (rung 3, unreachable from any argv fixture). One helper, `vs_cap <cmd> <args…>`, captures all three: a sentinel byte appended INSIDE the subshell protects the tail, then exactly ONE terminator — the one `sed`/`awk`/`readlink` printed itself — comes off (`vs_c_v` is the out-parameter; POSIX has no nameref). Gate: two live fixtures for the mid-word half (one per direction) with the pre-r4 `vs_argv` reader as the negative control (it answers the OPPOSITE of the JS twin on both), three for the trailing half with the three pre-`vs_cap` `$(…)` captures as the negative control (it answers YES to all three, and is proven not-dead on a normal claude / a `tail`), all five in the cross-login-shell verdict matrix (`${v%"$nl"}` is the same quoted-pattern construct the `(deleted)` strip got wrong under zsh) and in the JS⇄shell parity matrix. Reverting `vs_cap` in the product turns five asserts red. **THE SHELL TEXT RUNS UNDER THE REMOTE LOGIN SHELL, NOT `sh` (r3 round 2).** The device rung runs it as `sh -c`, but BOTH ssh rungs — the sweep's fallback (`sweepWriters`) and the discovery CO leg (`hosts.js _ssh`) — hand the script to `ssh host -- <script>`, which the REMOTE USER'S LOGIN SHELL interprets, so "POSIX sh" is the floor and not the target. This bit the `(deleted)` strip on arrival: in zsh `(…)` is a glob GROUP, so the unquoted `${vs_c_e% (deleted)}` matched " deleted" and stripped NOTHING — the auto-update fix was dead on every zsh-login host (a very common default) and invisible to a suite whose every identity assertion ran `sh -c`. The pattern is QUOTED (`${vs_c_e%' (deleted)'}`), which POSIX makes literal in every shell (verified dash/bash/busybox/zsh/ksh). **Quote any pattern whose literal characters are special somewhere, and test embedded shell text under every shell that can interpret it.** Gated functionally: test-writer-sweep drives the SHIPPED text through every login shell present on the box against the live `(deleted)` fixtures and demands identical verdicts, with the UNQUOTED spelling as the negative control — it parses and is indistinguishable under `sh`, and diverges only under zsh. **Dependency-free on purpose** (node builtins only): the agentd bundle carries discovery-facts, so it now carries this too — the arch suite's SHARED tier holds the line. Parity is FUNCTIONAL, not hoped for: scripts/test-writer-sweep.mjs §6 drives both spellings over ~18 live fixture pids × both CLI names in the same instant (with a not-vacuous check that the matrix contains both verdicts, and the retired JS rule as the negative control that a divergence really does turn it red), and scripts/test-discovery-interpret.mjs §8 does the same on the discovery side (real `bin/claude`/`bin/codex` holders, a `tail -f` reader, a wrapper that merely NAMES the codex binary in its arguments while holding an inherited rollout fd). **AND THE `lsof` RUNGS ARE THE SAME RULE TOO (r3).** Every consumer has a no-/proc body for macOS/BSD, and being unreachable from Linux is how BOTH of them kept the retired identity after the /proc bodies were fixed: discovery-facts' rung asked `/codex/.test(cmd)` (lsof's COMMAND field = comm, a SUBSTRING) and the hosts.js CO leg's `else` asked `awk … c ~ /codex/`. Worse, r1/r2 emitted the shared shell functions INSIDE that leg's `then` block, so `vs_is_cli` did not even EXIST in the branch that needed it — they now sit above the `if`. Both rungs emit `-Fpn` (pid + name) and credit the pid through the shared predicate.
