Gate: test-writer-sweep §9 RUNS the CO leg's else-branch by substituting ONE literal (the `[ -d /proc/self ]` probe), with the verbatim pre-r3 awk as the negative control (a `codex-keeper`-only rollout reported RUNNING), and §12 pins `-Fpn`/no-`c ~ /codex/` plus the definitions-before-the-`if` ordering. **A branch no test can reach is a branch that drifts; make it reachable by substituting one literal rather than by trusting it.** **AND IT NOW ALSO OWNS THE EXISTENCE PROBE — `pidAliveShellFn()`, `vs_alive <pid>` (B-3185 r6).** "Does this pid exist?" is the question that comes BEFORE identity, it runs on the same three transports under the same floor (`sh -c` on the device rung, the REMOTE USER'S LOGIN SHELL on both ssh rungs — so busybox `ash` is real), and it had TWO spellings: r5 fixed `hosts.js killPidShell` and left `src/server/sysinfo-wiring.js signalProc` its own `ps -p` on a written reason, which described only ONE of that script's two `ps -p` calls. The other — the post-signal aliveness check on the SUCCESS branch — MANUFACTURED a false `{ok:true, gone:true}` under busybox for a process that ignored the SIGTERM (measured). The ladder is now emitted from here and embedded by both: `kill -0` (POSIX builtin everywhere; SUCCESS is proof, FAILURE is ambiguous between EPERM and ESRCH so it is handed DOWN, never believed) → `[ -d /proc/N ]` → `ps -p N` (the no-/proc rung). No JS twin on purpose: the local branches call `process.kill(pid,0)` and read `e.code`, which is the errno distinction the shell cannot make and the whole reason the ladder exists. **A twin kept alive by a comment is a twin — the comment is only as good as its author's count of the call sites**, so the guard is a STANDING SWEEP (test-writer-sweep §17) rather than a better sentence: no `ps -p` may be an EXISTENCE test on any kill/signal path, each surviving one is allowlisted WITH its reason, and a dead entry fails too. **r7: that sweep was itself gated on a hand-written seven-file list** — the same instrument the bug had already defeated three times, enforcing the rule only where its author had looked (measured: 47 real signal-sending files were outside it, including `server.js`, `jobs.js`, `boot-restore.js`, `plugins/vnc/port-forward/opencode-serve`, `agentd.js`, `ws-handler/ws-create`). The set is now DERIVED by grep — every non-test file under `src/`, `data/bin/`, `scripts/` and the repo ROOT whose text sends or probes a signal, extension-less tools entering by SHEBANG so a GENERATED (untracked) agent CLI is reached at all — the suite PRINTS what it walked, the grep is deliberately over-inclusive (a false positive only widens enforcement; a false negative is the defect), the shipped sweep is parameterised by ROOT and re-run against a temp tree carrying each shape as its controls, and every exported shell BUILDER is swept on the text it EMITS (a source scan cannot see a runtime-composed probe). GENERATED files (gitignored — the esbuild daemon bundle carries cli-identity's `ps -p` lines verbatim, so a rebuilt tree went red on a CLEAN checkout) are a THIRD bucket with a stricter rule that needs no author: every `ps -p` line in them must be VERBATIM in an authored file the sweep walked. **A standing sweep is only as good as the set it walks: derive the set from the property, never enumerate it, and print what was walked.**

### discovery-facts.js

  discovery-facts.js   — **Shared discovery INTERPRETATION (2.278.0)** — deliberately tiny so the esbuild agentd bundle carries it (unlike the ssh one-file scanner, the daemon CAN share code). One naming rule (was DRIFTED: local first-line vs remote whole-message-collapsed = one session, two names; + unterminated-truncated-string fallback the old regex missed), one extractTailIds (was 3 implementations feeding claimJsonls with different mention windows; session-store delegates, null-on-unreadable contract kept), and pidLooksClaude — the PID-reuse guard the daemon snapshot never had (liveness-only = phantom running session on a recycled pid). **IDENTITY IS NO LONGER SPELLED HERE (B-3185 r3, the STANDING SWEEP): `pidLooksClaude` and the open-rollout scan both call `isCliProcess(pid, name)` from src/cli-identity.js — the SAME rule the writer sweep and the ssh discovery CO leg run in shell.** They used to be `comm/cmdline.includes('claude')` and a whole-argv codex regex, i.e. the rule B-3185 retired on the kill side and left standing here for two rounds because "it only labels a card RUNNING"; measured, the loose spelling said YES to 106 of 4277 processes against 16 real CLIs (30 vs 4 for codex), so a recycled lock pid on any dtach master, chat-wrapper or editor helper produced the exact phantom this guard exists to prevent. `isCliProcess` is re-exported from here for the same reason `claimJsonls` is injected: one import, one rule. Parity is asserted functionally on both sides (test-discovery-interpret §8 + test-writer-sweep §6/§12). **R5 steps 2-3 (2.290.0/2.291.0): + `interpretDiscoveryLines(out, {hostId, hostName, claimJsonls})` AND `synthesizeDiscoveryLines(snap)`** (snapshot→fact-lines, also extracted out of hosts) — with both halves shared, the daemon runs the WHOLE chain as the `discovery-claims` op — **SWITCHED LIVE in 2.292.0** (hosts.discoverSessions asks the device first; ladder: claims op → server-side snapshot+synthesize+interpret → legacy ssh script → stale cache) (capability-gated; device returns finished session cards, orchestrator's remaining job = cross-machine merge). Parity in test-usage-scan-op: device-computed claims BYTE-IDENTICAL to the server-side interpretation of the same snapshot. Original note: — the ~120-line lock-first CLAIM algorithm (exact-id → tail-id → mtime; keeper adoption; codex rollout cards; brand-new-lock case) extracted verbatim out of hosts.discoverSessions so the interpretation can run WHERE the facts live (the future dark `discovery.v2` op — device computes its own claims, orchestrator only merges). `claimJsonls` INJECTED (session-store must not be pulled into this tiny module). Golden-fixture parity: scripts/test-discovery-interpret.mjs (18 — the 9 tricky claim cases + §8, the process-identity twin: wiring pin, the PID-reuse guard on real processes with the retired rule as the negative control, the CO-line scan, and JS⇄shell parity over the same live pids). Fixture trap recorded in the m3m4 suite: /bin/sleep can be a uutils MULTI-CALL binary that dispatches on argv0 — a symlink named 'claude' dies; use a script. Test: scripts/test-discovery-facts.mjs (18).

### writer-sweep.js

  writer-sweep.js      — **THE pre-resume writer sweep, ONE implementation for every machine (CS separation, 2.276.0).** The invariant: before resuming, no OTHER process may still be writing that conversation's transcript (two writers on one JSONL = the B-4058 corruption class). It used to exist THREE times — ssh, dial, and NOT AT ALL for local; the local gap was never a decision, it is what happens when `hostId` is a BRANCH instead of a PARAMETER (whoever fixes the remote bug never exercises the local twin). `sweepWriters(hosts, hostId, rid, …)` — falsy hostId = this machine via device #0; ssh keeps its per-op channel as fallback, dial correctly has none, local throws rather than pretending. Every kill leg echoes `SWEPT:<pid>` and the created reply carries them (a destructive op must never be silent). **FORKS NEVER SWEEP (2.284.4, real incident: forking a LIVE conversation SIGTERMed the parent's claude mid-turn the day the local sweep went live)** — a fork resumes the PARENT's rid but only READS that transcript and writes a NEW id's JSONL, so a live parent writer is legitimate; the resume-already-live guard exempts forks by design, which voids the "live sessions were filtered upstream" assumption the sweeps were written under. All three ws-handler gates carry `!data.fork`; the test's drift guard fails any new call site without it. Test: scripts/test-writer-sweep.mjs. **CODEX LEGS (P1 codex double-writer, 2026-09).** `writerSweepScript(rid, shq, {backend, protectSids})` / `sweepWriters(…, {backend, protectSids})`: the claude script is byte-for-byte what it was (default backend); `backend:'codex'` swaps the holder legs for ① a /proc fd scan (lsof on macOS/BSD) for an open `rollout-*-<threadId>.jsonl` — the pattern also matches `.jsonl.zst` (codex ≥0.153 may compress) — and ② an argv leg for `codex resume <threadId>` (a TUI in an external terminal) / `CODEX_WEBUI_RESUME_ID=<threadId>` (an orphaned VibeSpace wrapper or its dtach master); the pipe-session-meta + keeper legs are shared. Both codex legs report `SWEPT:`; the fd leg's holder must pass `vs_is_cli <pid> codex` (the EXECUTABLE test below — the pre-B-3185 text said "kill only `*codex*` cmdlines", which is the substring rule the fix retired), and the argv leg is deliberately exempt because it is self-evidencing. **THE PROTECT LIST is load-bearing**: a codex app-server keeps EVERY rollout of its thread tree open for its whole lifetime — measured on the dev box, one app-server (`…/bin/codex app-server`) held the parent + three finished sub-agent rollouts two days later — so a plain "kill the holder" would SIGTERM a healthy LIVE session's app-server whenever a sub-agent or forked-away thread is resumed (the 2.284.4 class). ws-create's `sweepOpts(hostId)` passes the webui ids of the live codex sessions on the TARGET machine; the script resolves a candidate's `CLAUDE_WEBUI_SESSION_ID` from its argv (dtach/wrapper carry the env pairs as args) or `/proc/<pid>/environ` (`ps -E` on macOS) and skips protected holders — live sessions are the resume-already-live guard's business, the sweep reaches EXTERNAL/orphaned writers only. The argv leg scans `ps -eo pid=,args=`, and the sweep's own `sh -c <script>` carries RID in its argv: the literal `VS_WRITER_SWEEP` sentinel in the script text is what skips the sweep shell and its subshells — keep it. Protect ids are validated `^[\w-]+$` before they reach the shell. Parity: local and remote receive a byte-identical codex script (test §8); test §9 drives the real script against real holder processes (temp HOME, real rollout path shape, SIGTERM observed; protect-list positive/negative; argv leg; .zst; non-codex holder untouched; the claude script never touches a codex holder); test §10 pins the ws-create guard functionally (real `createWsCreateHandler`) + every sweep site passing `sweepOpts`. **IDENTITY IS THE EXECUTABLE, NOT A SUBSTRING OF THE COMMAND LINE (B-3185).** The holder test used to be `case "$(ps -p $pid -o args=)" in *claude*)` — a substring over the WHOLE command line — so every process whose argv merely NAMED a path under `~/.claude` read as the CLI and was SIGTERMed: `tail -f ~/.claude/projects/<id>.jsonl`, an editor with the transcript open, and (the first casualty, patched around IN THE SUITE for a while instead of fixed) a test process running from a git worktree under `~/.claude/worktrees/` — where the worktree-only-smokes law puts every agent — which matched its own guard and killed itself: exit 143, `npm run ci` red, on code that was fine. `vs_is_cli <pid> <claude|codex>` (cliIdentityShellFns — **which lives in src/cli-identity.js since r3 and is only RE-EXPORTED here, beside its JS twin `isCliProcess`; read that entry**) now decides by the EXECUTABLE, in three rungs covering the three shapes the CLIs actually ship in: argv[0]'s basename (`~/.local/bin/claude`; the codex vendor binary `…/@openai/codex-linux-x64/vendor/<triple>/bin/codex` that the npm `codex.js` shim spawns by path), the interpreter's FIRST NON-FLAG operand when argv[0] is node/nodejs/bun/deno (`node <prefix>/bin/claude` via the npm shebang shim, `node …/@anthropic-ai/claude-code/cli.js`, `node …/bin/codex.js`), and `/proc/<pid>/exe` (a native install execs `~/.local/share/claude/versions/<ver>` behind an argv[0] of `~/.local/bin/claude`). argv is read NUL-split out of `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` where that exists (exact) and falls back to `ps -o args=` word splitting elsewhere, and a trailing ` (deleted)` is stripped from `/proc/<pid>/exe` before either exe rung matches (**r3**: the kernel appends it when the image is replaced on disk — what auto-update does to a LIVE session — and without the strip the sweep missed exactly the mid-update CLI it exists to stop). Only argv[0] and the first non-flag operand are ever consulted — a later ARGUMENT never identifies a process, which is exactly what the old rule got wrong. THE FD EVIDENCE STAYS PRIMARY (the process holds THIS conversation's transcript open); vs_is_cli only answers writer-or-reader. THE ARGV LEG IS DELIBERATELY EXEMPT: `codex resume <threadId>` / `CODEX_WEBUI_RESUME_ID=<threadId>` is self-evidencing (it names the thread) and the shape it catches — an orphaned VibeSpace wrapper or its dtach master — is NOT the codex binary, so `vs_codex_kill` keeps only the PROTECT check and each caller supplies its own evidence. **THE FD SCAN IS BATCHED — AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE (B-3185).** `fdScanShellFns()` emits `vs_fd_scan <ere>` (one `<pid>\t<fd target>` line per matching open fd) + `vs_fd_pids`; both backend scripts embed it VERBATIM, and `src/server/boot-restore.js` builds its "which conversations does a live claude still hold?" probe from the same export (same scan, kill removed). Measured on the dev box (32 cores, loadavg 3.4–4.8, 3678–3713 processes, 405,735 open fds): the old per-process `ls` + `grep` fork PAIR = 7356 forks, 9.9–12.2 s wall — over half the 20 s budget spent on fork/exec alone, and past it entirely on a busier or bigger machine; the batched form (one `ls -l` per 400 fd directories + one awk) = ~20 forks, 3.0–3.5 s, so the UNCHANGED 20 s budget now covers ~24k processes. boot-restore's variant was worse and silently dead: a `readlink` fork PER FD (~6.4 minutes here) against a 6 s timeout it therefore ALWAYS blew, swallowed by a bare catch — every local conversation looked "not live" and the socket dedup fell back to createdAt; it now runs the shared scan and a failure LOGS. Two shell facts are load-bearing: `/proc/self/fd` rides in every chunk so `ls -l` always has more than one operand and therefore always prints the `<dir>:` headers the pid attribution reads (with a single operand it prints none), and the scan is CHUNKED because `vs_fd_chunk` EXECS `ls`: ITS argv is one operand per process and is what ARG_MAX/MAX_ARG_STRLEN bound, and an E2BIG there fails into a discarded stderr and a silently empty answer. **The old boot probe's `/proc/[0-9]*/fd/*` glob was NOT an ARG_MAX case** (r1's comment said it was, and the r2 review caught it): a shell `for` loop expands in the shell's own memory and never reaches execve — its problem was only the 405,735 `readlink` forks. Naming the wrong mechanism in a comment is how the next person removes the right guard, so both copies and both kb twins are pinned by the suite. Next rung if a machine ever needs it: GNU `find -lname` measures 0.76 s — not adopted, because it is not portable and 3 s is inside budget. **WHICH LEG ACTUALLY REACHES A LIVE CLAUDE (measured, and it changes how you read the fix):** the installed CLI (2.1.226, native binary) does NOT keep the transcript open — it appends and closes; a machine-wide scan of this box found ZERO processes holding any `~/.claude/projects/**.jsonl` while 16 claude CLIs were running. So the claude FD leg is a rung that fires for a holder that happens to exist — an older CLI, a mid-append race, or a READER — while the leg that really catches a live claude is the CLI's own `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` lock file. That makes the old substring guard worse than it looks: the leg that almost never finds a real writer was the one matching every process that merely named a path under `~/.claude`. It also bounds boot-restore's probe honestly — it now runs correctly and fast, but it still answers "nobody" for current claude; giving `claudeAlive` the CLI registry (with the `procStart` counter-capture against pid reuse) is a separate decision, because retiring is destructive (SIGTERM + unlink the dtach socket). The CODEX fd leg is the live one: real app-servers on this box do hold their rollouts open, which is why the PROTECT list exists. Field-validated read-only against 3700 real processes: of 70 whose argv merely contains 'claude', exactly the 16 real CLIs answer yes and the dtach masters and chat-wrappers that carry the whole `…/claude --resume …` command line as ARGUMENTS answer no (the old rule said yes to all of them — and SIGTERMing a dtach master destroys the session); both real codex shapes (`node …/bin/codex app-server` through the npm shim and the `…/vendor/<triple>/bin/codex` binary) answer yes. Test §6 is now the identity matrix on real processes: WRITERS that must be swept (a native `…/bin/claude`, `node …/@anthropic-ai/claude-code/cli.js`, `node <prefix>/bin/claude`) and READERS that must survive (`tail -f` on the transcript — proven non-vacuous by checking its fd first — a process running from a `.claude/worktrees/` path, a `cli.js` outside a claude package, a neutral `node -e` holder, and THE SUITE'S OWN PROCESS holding the same fd, under a SIGTERM trap so a regression is a red assertion instead of a mysterious exit 143). §1 pins the batching structurally (exactly one `/proc/[0-9]*` walk per script, no per-process `ls`, no whole-argv identity test) and asserts that every `kill -TERM` line carries a `SWEPT:` — intent, not the old count, which the dedup would have broken. §11 pins the boot-restore wiring. **R2 REVIEW ROUND (six defects, each with a negative control).** ① The §1 drift pin was UNSATISFIABLE — its regex matched neither the exact pre-fix line nor any realistic reintroduction, so the kb advertised a guard that could never go red; the pin is now asserted against the verbatim pre-B-3185 lines AND proven by splicing them back into the shipped script. ② The awk pid attribution never cleared `p` on an UNRECOGNISED directory header — and `/proc/self/fd`, appended to every chunk by design, is exactly such a header AND is the `ls` process's own fd table, which inherits the sweeping shell's fds: an inherited matching fd was attributed to whichever numeric pid came last in the chunk, and the sweep SIGTERMs on that attribution. `p` is now reset on EVERY header; §1b proves it functionally (the shell holds the target on fd 9, so `ls` inherits it in every chunk) with the sticky-`p` revert as the control — one bogus victim per chunk. ③ The ARG_MAX rationale above. ④ RUNG 3 WAS A WIDENING INSIDE A NARROWING FIX: `*/<name>/versions/*` on `/proc/<pid>/exe` classified every process running the CLI's binary IMAGE as the CLI — and the CLI RE-EXECS that image as its bundled helpers (measured live: 18–19 processes under `…/share/claude/versions/<ver>`, 2–3 of them `ugrep -G …` with argv[0] a bare `ugrep`), so a search helper that inherited its parent's transcript fd was a SIGTERM target. Rung 3 now also asks how the process PRESENTS itself and takes either honest answer — argv[0] basename starts with `<name>` (a launcher naming the CLI), or argv[0] basename EQUALS the exe basename (nothing renamed: a wrapper's `exec "$IMG"`, the shape that keeps the rung reachable at all). A re-exec'd helper fails both because it renames itself. The measured real CLI never needs rung 3 (argv[0] is `~/.local/bin/claude`, so rung 1 answers) — which is why widening it was pure downside. §6 runs ONE binary under THREE argv[0]s (same `/proc/<pid>/exe`, asserted) and reverts rung 3 to r1 against the LIVE helper pid as the control. ⑤ The "SUITE ITSELF" leg labelled its argv from `process.argv[1]`, which node ABSOLUTISES, while the guard reads the command line AS TYPED — so it announced "the real regression shape" for a run whose argv contained no `.claude` at all. A COPY of the suite (holder mode, top of file) now runs from a real `.claude/worktrees/<wt>/scripts/` checkout BOTH ways — absolute (the argv that SIGTERMed the suite) and relative (what the gate types) — with the two argvs read out of `/proc` and asserted. ⑥ THE STANDING-SWEEP TWIN: hosts.js's remote-discovery `CO` leg still carried all three retired shapes (a `tr`+`grep` fork PAIR per process over `/proc/[0-9]*`, a `readlink` fork per FD, and an argv-regex identity) — measured on this box 5.02 s / 8068 forks at 4034 processes inside a per-host discovery budget, versus 3.52 s / ~20 forks for the ported leg. `codexOpenRolloutsShell()` (hosts.js) now embeds `fdScanShellFns()` + `cliIdentityShellFns()` VERBATIM, so the sweep and discovery answer "is this the codex CLI" identically by construction; §12 pins that structurally with the pre-B-3185 leg as the control, and §9 runs it for real (a wrapper/dtach-master that merely NAMES `…/bin/codex resume <tid>` in its ARGUMENTS is no longer a RUNNING thread — the retired regex says yes to it, asserted). **THE PORT ALSO EXPOSED A LIVE HAZARD THE SUITE CAUGHT: this leg is the LAST command of the ssh discovery script and `_ssh` REJECTS a non-zero exit, and a `while read` loop exits with its LAST iteration's status — so the leg returned 1 whenever the last matching fd under `$HOME` belonged to a non-CLI holder, sending the whole host's discovery into the serve-stale-cache catch. It ends in an explicit `:`; never let a scan's FINDINGS decide a script's exit status.** ⑦ The kb's advertised assert count is now self-checked by §13. **THAT TWIN IS GONE (r3 — the standing sweep is a MEASUREMENT, and "recorded" is not "resolved"):** r2 left `src/discovery-facts.js` identifying processes by command line — `pidLooksClaude` = `cmdline.includes('claude')`, `isCodexCommandLine` = a whole-argv regex — for the DEVICE snapshot's liveness labels, on the grounds that it only decides whether a card reads RUNNING and never who receives a SIGTERM. The blast radius really is different, and it was still the wrong call: a rule with two spellings has two behaviours the moment either is touched, and the loose spelling was doing its own damage (measured: YES for 106 of 4277 processes against 16 real claude CLIs; 30 vs 4 for codex — so a RECYCLED lock pid on any dtach master, chat-wrapper or editor helper produced the exact phantom the PID-reuse guard exists to prevent). Both now call `isCliProcess` from src/cli-identity.js. §12 still pins the boundary that must never be crossed (no kill path in discovery-facts or cli-identity — they classify; only the sweep script kills), and parity is now FUNCTIONAL on both sides (test-writer-sweep §6/§12, test-discovery-interpret §8) instead of a promise.

### remote-shell.js

  remote-shell.js      — **ALSO buildRemoteExec (2.279.0): the five remote spawn command builders (ssh terminal/ssh keeper chat/ssh agentd pipe/dial pty/dial pipe) are ONE composition** — `cd → pre → resolve → AMBIENT_OAT_UNSET → tokenAssign/acctEnv → exec env parts/tail`, every difference a named parameter; the ambient-oat strip is STRUCTURAL (2.267.0 hand-added it to five sites) and provably ordered before the deliberate token assign; two builders' redundant double-REMOTE_PRELUDE gone. Drift guard in test-remote-shell (20 asserts) fails anyone hand-assembling an `exec env` line in ws-handler. **ONE definition of the remote shell prelude + POSIX node finder (2.274.0, CS unification).** Every remote command (spawn builders ×4, capability probes, agentd stdio bootstrap, tool install/uninstall, usage harvest) must import `REMOTE_PRELUDE`/`nodeFinder()`/`buildRemoteShellPrelude()` — the string was copy-pasted into 11 sites and the copies DRIFTED (spawn builders running a stale variant is exactly how a bug becomes remote-only; two node-finder copies were the weaker no-PATH-export variant, leaving `#!/usr/bin/env node` tools dead on dash hosts). scripts/test-remote-shell.mjs FAILS if any file re-inlines either string.

### hosts.js

  hosts.js             — HostManager (ssh host registry, connectivity test, keygen, remote discovery + first-user-message names, bootstrap, backend-status, dir-complete, fetchSessionJsonl = remote transcript → data/remote-jsonl cache; **add() stays SYNCHRONOUS (setDialToken calls it then findByDeviceId on the result — an async add returns a Promise there and silently breaks device pairing), so it can NEVER run the ssh-keygen unlock: the /api/hosts route decrypts first (src/ssh-key.js) and hands add() plaintext; add()'s own classifyPrivateKey check is the BELT for callers that skip the route and REFUSES a still-encrypted key (code 'key-encrypted') rather than storing one that only fails at connect time. 2.246.0 also moved the duplicate-NAME check BEFORE the key write (the old order wrote data/ssh/<id>.key then threw, orphaning a file no record referenced) and importBundle SKIPS a passphrase-protected key returning {warnings} — nothing can prompt during an unattended import, and persistence.js surfaces it in the `applied` array;** 2.129.0 remote-integration transparency: static AGENT_TOOLS list (ws-handler's per-spawn distribution reuses it), agentToolsStatus(id) = one read-only ssh round trip (per-tool sha256 — content compare, NOT mtime: hook/status are regenerated every local boot — + remote hook registration grep + node + keeper .sock count), installAgentTools (tar-over-stdin + hook-register), uninstallAgentTools (register --uninstall BEFORE rm; only our 6 files, .tok-* untouched). **`_hostShell(h, script)` (2.188.0) = the shared read-only probe channel: ssh hosts via _ssh, dial devices via dm.runCmd — backendStatus/accountsStatus/agentToolsStatus/cliPrimaryKey/readRemoteOAuth all route through it (they were ssh-only, so a selected dial device showed everything not-installed/unreachable; install/uninstall stay ssh-only — dial tools ship per-spawn). accountsStatus knows every claude auth shape (OAuth/primaryApiKey/apiKeyHelper — keep in sync with backendStatus's loginMethod chain, the 2.186.7/2.188.0 same-dialog-contradiction class) + returns host identity (claude config email; codex auth.json JWT email/plan decoded server-side). ⚠ CLI AUTH PRECEDENCE (2.191.0, verified by disassembling the 2.1.211 binary — CW-H200 incident): a configured `apiKeyHelper` in the MERGED settings UNCONDITIONALLY disables claude.ai OAuth (HS() gate), valid creds or not — so backendStatus/accountsStatus report `keyHelper` as an INDEPENDENT flag (never an elif rung) and Manage Agents warns "apiKeyHelper overrides this login" when both exist; the billing switcher explains why no pick can change it. `--settings '{"apiKeyHelper":null}'` is DROPPED by the CLI's field-level schema .catch (null fails string validation); **`""` VERIFIED working (2.236.0 controlled A/B): an inline empty-string helper overrides the file's helper — apiKeySource flips apiKeyHelper→none (subscription OAuth) and the turn succeeds; buildSessionArgs merges it (`neutralizeKeyHelper`) for every EXPLICIT subscription pick (the bare 'subscription' CLI-login sentinel deliberately keeps machine behavior)**; forceLoginMethod only affects the login FLOW, not runtime precedence. 2.191.0 also: `fetchWorkflowState` (remote View Workflow — one compound probe, nonce-delimited payload, GNU-else-BSD stat, 2s TTL, ≤1MB for dial's runCmd stdout slice), `fetchAgentJsonl` + the generalized `_fetchRemoteByFind` core (fetchSessionJsonl's cache/invariants, parameterized find), and discovery sessions carry `pid` (Terminate on remote EXTERNAL cards reaches killRemotePid — it silently no-op'd without it).**) **ONE CACHE SLOT, MANY REMOTE FILES (2.369.x (codex-zst review batch)):** a backend's `remoteFind` predicate may resolve DIFFERENT files across polls — codex matches `rollout-*<tid>.jsonl` OR `.jsonl.zst` and the host compresses a finished rollout — while `data/remote-jsonl/<host>/<backend>/<id>.jsonl` is keyed by conversation id alone. The `.meta` therefore records `remotePath` + `compressed` next to {size,mtime}: a changed path invalidates the slot (full refetch, no cache-valid short-circuit), and a compressed remote — or an already-compressed cache file (magic-byte check, so pre-fix metas are covered too) — is NEVER delta-appended, because recompression rewrites the file rather than appending plain bytes. Without it the append-only slab path concatenated the compressed twin's bytes onto the cached plain prefix and stamped it complete; a stopped thread never changes again, so the corrupt transcript was served forever. The locate pipeline is `find … | sort | head -1` so the pick is deterministic and prefers the plain twin. **AND THE ALREADY-CORRUPTED SLOTS HEAL THEMSELVES (review follow-up, same batch):** every slot in the field carries a PRE-provenance meta, and reading “no remotePath” as “same file” is vacuously true — the hybrid a stopped thread’s slot already held kept passing the short-circuit forever. So `sameRemote` is strict (`meta.remotePath === remotePath`), a meta without provenance is NOT valid (refetch whole ONCE, rewrite it, never delta onto it), and the cache FILE is checked independently of its meta: head magic must agree with the resolved remote, and a plain cache whose last 4KB carry a NUL byte or a zstd frame header is spliced (an append lands foreign bytes at the TAIL). Single exception: a remote already past `maxBytes` cannot be refetched whole at all (the delta path is how the slot grew there), so a byte-verified cache adopts the provenance instead of failing “too large”. **THE ADOPTION IS THE ONE DECISION THAT OUTLIVES THE TAIL WINDOW (B-7638):** the pre-fix delta path kept APPENDING after it spliced, so on a long-lived slot the marker sits far above the last 4 KB and a tail-only read called the hybrid verified. A cache slot therefore scans the WHOLE file (1 MiB chunks, 3-byte carry so a magic split across a boundary is still seen) before its bytes are trusted — gated on a meta SCHEMA MARKER (`v: 2`, written by every meta writer here), NOT on “no remotePath”: the shipped tail-only adoption stamped provenance onto the very hybrids it adopted, so `sameRemote` is true for exactly that corruption (round 2). The scan is paid ONCE per slot because the verification is WRITTEN BACK (`stampVerified`: remotePath + compressed + `v` + an `adopted` marker when the bytes were verified rather than fetched) on BOTH early-return rungs; a refused slot is left unstamped, and the “too large” error says the cache could not be verified AND that the remote is past the cap (the suffix is only reachable there, so the earlier “delete it to re-sync” was advice that could not work). **The legacy ssh rung also grows now** (round 2): stamping fixes only the repeated scan, while the harm — an over-cap slot hard-failing “too large” on its next byte — was a TWIN GAP against the slab rung's 2.187.0 delta; it now runs the same append-only delta via `tail -c +N` (1-based on GNU and BSD), same legality test, short read throws, a read that OVERTAKES the stat keeps the extra append-only bytes and stamps the real size. And a transcript SHORTER than the 4-byte magic is verifiable after all: it cannot BE a zstd frame nor hide a splice, so an empty/one-record transcript short-circuits on its cache instead of being fully re-pulled on every poll forever (a cache whose head is not a record, or a sub-magic cache under a COMPRESSED remote, is still refused). Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥/⑥b/⑥c (stub device switching remote files under one slot; a pre-written hybrid + old-shape meta healing with the remote standing still, negative-controlled against the pre-fix predicate)). **ROUND 3 — the round-2 delta's own three residuals:** ① the compression-legality clause (`!isZstPath(remotePath) && !cacheIsCompressed()`) had NO negative control on either rung — deleting it left the suite green, while the case it exists for (a host RE-compressing a finished rollout: same path, magic-verified .zst cache, cached archive shorter than the remote — every other precondition satisfied) would weld a foreign frame tail onto the old frames and serve the OLD transcript with the growth silently gone; both rungs now carry a both-compressed growth leg that dies when the clause is removed from its own copy. ② The round-2 ssh delta REPLACED the whole `cat` on the LAST rung, so a failing `tail` (remote rotated between stat and read; a host whose `tail` has no `-c +N`) hard-failed a fetch the pre-delta code completed — a delta failure now falls back to the whole `cat` when `size <= maxBytes` (failure logged VERBATIM) and stays a hard error only over the cap, where a whole re-pull is impossible by construction (so the fallback is never a cap bypass). ③ Both meta writers built a FRESH object, so the next delta erased `adopted`/`slab` — the durable provenance ① depends on, on the one slot class that can never re-earn it. ONE shared `nextMeta(fields, {whole})` spreads the prior meta on both rungs; only a WHOLE refetch clears those markers (they describe bytes no longer in the slot). Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥d/⑥e. **ROUND 4 — ONE WRITER PER SLOT:** both rungs compute the append offset from a stat taken BEFORE the remote read, so two overlapping fetches of one slot (poll + a user opening the window, two clients, goal-sync + an attach) both appended the same tail and the cache grew a DUPLICATED region — self-healing until the remote passes the doubled size, after which the next delta stamps the spliced file COMPLETE and a stopped thread serves duplicated + missing records forever (duplicated JSON trips neither byte marker). `_fetchRemoteByFind` is now a single-flight wrapper over the core `_fetchRemoteByFindOnce` (in-flight promise per cache path — the second caller awaits the first's verdict; caps come from one descriptor so coalescing cannot lose a cap), and every delta goes through `_appendDeltaAt(cachePath, offset, buf)`: open `r+`, re-read the size through that fd, write POSITIONALLY at `offset`, `false` when the slot has moved ⇒ whole refetch under the cap / loud failure over it, never a splice. Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥f (both rungs, pre-fix repro per leg). **ROUND 5 — A MOVED SLOT IS TERMINAL ON EVERY RUNG:** round 4's over-cap refusal was thrown INSIDE the data-plane `try`, whose catch degrades every failure to the ssh rung — so on a dial host with a reachable ssh fallback the refusal was swallowed silently and the ssh rung recomputed its offset from the MOVED file and spliced: right-size / wrong-bytes cache, meta stamped COMPLETE, served forever (reproduced with a foreign 203-byte append during `fsReadRange`). Three legs, each mutation-tested on its own. ① The verdict is TYPED (`slotMovedError`, code `ESLOTMOVED`) and the catch re-throws it through `_isTerminalFetchError` — a statement about the CACHE is true on every transport, only TRANSPORT failures may degrade. ② What a rung learned about the slot outlives it: `observed.moved` (read via `_slotMovedEarlier`) denies the ssh rung a delta even when the slab rung then failed for an unrelated reason (its own whole refetch dying). ③ The durable half, because the movement is invisible one poll later — the foreign bytes are simply IN the file, so `_appendDeltaAt`'s re-read offset MATCHES them and the append lands right behind them: `_prefixIsOurs(localSize, meta)` lets a delta extend only bytes this cache's own writers stamped (file ≤ `meta.size`; a SHORTER file is deliberately still growable — a positional append only writes at the end, so it is a genuine prefix after a crash or a truncation, and refusing it would strand every over-cap slot that ever crashed mid-write). Over the cap all of it is a loud failure that leaves cache+meta byte-identical, and the cap error names WHICH fault (spliced bytes vs "holds N bytes where the last fetch stamped M"). Two consequences: the whole `cat` now stamps `buf.length`, not the stat it was promised (a live transcript hands back more bytes than the probe said — the tail branch has always stamped the real length, and an under-claiming meta would make the invariant read our own fetch as foreign), and the data-plane catch LOGS every fault it swallows VERBATIM (it was silent — the 2.340.2 lost-binding class hides in exactly such a catch). Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥g (the verifier's dial+ssh scenario refused on both rungs and again on the next poll; leg isolation per guard; the pre-fix repro with all three neutered reproducing the sealed corrupt slot; the degrade log verbatim with a healthy-fetch negative control; the overtaking `cat` stamp with a pre-fix-meta control). **ROUND 6 — THE ONE-TIME OVER-STAMP HEAL (and the log that repeated itself):** round 5's "grow only what we stamped" also condemns an INNOCENT shape wearing the same signature — the pre-round-5 whole `cat` stamped the PROBE's stat while `cat` returned everything the live transcript had grown to since, so `meta.size` was SHORT on a perfectly healthy fetch. Under the cap that costs one whole re-pull; OVER it there is no whole re-pull, so every such slot (a remote stopped thread that opened yesterday) became a PERMANENT hard failure. No local scan can separate the two shapes — the round-5 corruption appends ordinary text — so the disputed bytes are checked against the source of truth: when a slot is over the cap AND its meta predates the `sizeExact` marker AND the file is longer than that meta claims, the gap `[stamp, file)` is read back (BOUNDED: `fsReadRange` on the slab rung, `tail -c +N | head -c LEN` on the ssh one) and compared byte-for-byte with the cache. Equal ⇒ genuine prefix ⇒ `meta.size = file size` + `sizeExact` + `healedStampAt`, once, and the delta rides again; not equal ⇒ round 5's refusal stands. `_overStampedLegacySlot(localSize, meta)` is the precondition (neuterable like its siblings), `sizeExact: true` is stamped by `nextMeta` and by `stampVerified` (whose early-return now also requires it, so the marker-less population shrinks by one slot per poll), and four bounds keep the heal from becoming a way around round 5: over the cap only, marker-less metas only, never on a slot `_slotMovedEarlier` says moved in this window, and the free local whole-file splice scan first (skipped when this poll's `cacheUsable` already paid for it). The re-stamp is written the moment the remote CONFIRMS the bytes — not on the fetch's own meta write, or a delta dying on the wire would throw the proof away and re-probe every poll on exactly the flaky over-cap slots. A REFUSAL, by contrast, is memoized in memory only (`_healRefusedAt`, keyed by cache path AND the size it was made on, 10 min, carrying the verdict) — stamping a slot we would not serve freezes the corruption in. Four verdicts ⇒ four different sentences appended to the cap error (`foreign` = checked mismatch, `spliced` = local marker, `moved`, `unverified` = retryable), because claiming a check that was skipped is "an error string is not a diagnosis" with the sign flipped. Second half: round 5's data-plane degrade line is rate-limited per HOST via `_warnDegradeOnce(id, msg, nowMs)` (≤1/60s, next line carries `(+N similar suppressed in the last minute)`; the heal's own probe-failure line shares that budget) — the fault it degrades on is usually persistent and every attach/poll/goal-sync tick comes through there. Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥h (pre-fix permanent failure reproduced first; heal + delta on both rungs; durability across a dead delta; a dirty same-length cache refused and memoized; a buried marker refused with no remote read; under-cap = whole refetch; a marked meta refused without asking; a probe dying on the wire = retryable; the log rate limit with a second host and a window-expiry leg — ten mutations each red on their own). **ROUND 7 — THE HEAL'S OWN COSTS, AND A COMPARISON THAT NEVER RE-ASKED:** ① the whole-file splice scan was "paid once per slot" only INSIDE one rung — `cacheUsable` runs on the slab rung and again on the ssh rung after the degradable "too large", and the slots that owe the scan are exactly the ones that REFUSE (a refused poll leaves the meta byte-identical by design, so nothing ever retires the legacy marker) ⇒ a 64 MB slot read whole, twice, on every session poll / attach / goal-sync tick, forever. The verdict is about BYTES, so it is memoized against the bytes it judged (`_deepScanMemo`, keyed inode:size:mtime, 10 min, dropped by every writer that touches the slot); `deepDone` is gone — the heal asks the question outright instead of inferring from a flag whether someone else already answered it; and `wholeCacheOk` answers in three states (clean / marker / unreadable) so only a VERDICT is remembered (a transient EMFILE must not freeze a healthy slot for the whole TTL). ② `_warnDegradeOnce` keyed its 60 s window on the HOST alone, so the first fault held the minute and every DIFFERENT fault inside it was dropped into a "+N similar" tally that names nothing (the rate limiter becoming the silent catch it replaced); the key is now host + message CLASS (`_degradeClassKey` — the same sentence with its offsets/sizes/ids blanked), so distinct faults each speak once a minute and only genuine repeats are counted. ③ the heal's gap comparison used a `localSize` stat'd BEFORE the remote round trip and cache bytes read AFTER it: a slot that shrank in between produced a mismatch that was reported as 'foreign' ("CHECKED against the remote: they are not its own") and MEMOIZED for ten minutes, condemning a healthy over-cap slot on every later poll; `_healGapIsStillOurs` re-stats before comparing and a slot that shifted under the probe is the MOVED class — terminal for this poll, memoized by nobody. Pins: test-codex-zst (M)/(N)/(O), each with its pre-fix repro and a negative control. THE REMOTE TERMINATE ASKS THE ONE IDENTITY (B-3185 r4, `killPidShell(pid)`, exported + driven for real by test-writer-sweep §14):** `killRemotePid` used to gate its SIGTERM with `case "$(ps -p N -o args=)" in *claude*|*codex*)` — the whole-argv substring B-3185 retired, still live on a KILL path, on a machine the user cannot look at (a remote `tail -f` on a transcript, an editor, a wrapper whose ARGUMENTS name `…/bin/codex`, a dtach master carrying `claude --resume …` — killing which destroys the session — all matched, and the route answered `{success:true}`). It now embeds `cliIdentityShellFns()` VERBATIM (a host has no checkout) and asks `vs_is_cli` for BOTH CLI names; the three outcomes `VS_GONE`/`VS_OK`/`VS_NOTAGENT` and the `ps`-based existence test are unchanged (`kill -0` reports another user's process as gone), only the ACT decides the exit status, and the pid is validated to be an integer AT the builder. The local twin of the same route (`/api/kill-pid` without `host`) asks `isCliProcess` for the same two names — `hostId` is a parameter here too. **ONE CACHE SLOT, MANY REMOTE FILES (2.369.x (codex-zst review batch)):** a backend's `remoteFind` predicate may resolve DIFFERENT files across polls — codex matches `rollout-*<tid>.jsonl` OR `.jsonl.zst` and the host compresses a finished rollout — while `data/remote-jsonl/<host>/<backend>/<id>.jsonl` is keyed by conversation id alone. This is (q) again: the text runs under the REMOTE USER'S shell, so busybox is a real floor. Existence is now a LADDER OF POSITIVE EVIDENCE (`vs_alive`): `kill -0` (POSIX, a builtin in every shell that can interpret this text — its SUCCESS is proof; its FAILURE is ambiguous between EPERM and ESRCH, which is why it is handed on rather than believed) → `[ -d /proc/N ]` (Linux incl. busybox, visible for processes we may not signal) → `ps -p N` (the no-/proc rung: BSD/macOS `ps` does have `-p`). And "I could not look" is no longer reported as "it is not an agent": `vs_known` asks whether argv or exe could be READ at all, so the outcomes are FOUR — `VS_GONE` / `VS_OK` / `VS_NOTAGENT` (alive, evidence read, not a CLI) / `VS_UNKNOWN` (alive, nothing readable — the busybox + no-/proc combination), and `killRemotePid` THROWS on the last two rather than returning a success. The enumerated sibling `src/server/sysinfo-wiring.js signalProc` keeps its `ps -p` on purpose: there it sits on the FAILURE branch of a kill that was already ATTEMPTED, so it can mislabel EPERM as ESRCH but can never report a kill that did not happen as a success (pinned in test-writer-sweep §16).
