Gate: scripts/test-collab-live-counter.mjs (node composers + wiring pins, then a REAL codex rollout opened read-only and a LIVE codex chat session driven by a stub app-server through the real wrapper → real normalizer → real ws push; the stub is a reminder that a faithful fixture matters twice over — its blobs must differ inside the normalizer's 32-char fernet discriminator or the dedup silently collapses the burst, and a `commandExecution` needs its `item/started` or the wrapper records only an OUTPUT and the card falls to the unknown-tool 'mcp' kind). **THE STEER CHORD'S VIEW HALF (2026-09-07 + round-2 verifier):** `_canSteerComposer()`/`steerComposerText()` are what the contributed command `chat.steerNow` runs (see contributions.js), and `_steerAfterSend(msgId)` is its second half — a steer NAMES A QUEUED ITEM, so the chord sends on the ordinary path and this parks the msgId in a MAP until `_setQueue` drains it into the SAME `queue-op` frame the strip and the chip send. Its 8s timeout is the no-silent-failure half, and the guard that keeps it from CRYING WOLF is a TURN IDENTITY, not a flag: `_showTyping` stamps `_turnEpoch` on the same null→armed transition that sets `_typingSince`, `_steerAfterSend` captures it, and the timeout speaks only if that SAME epoch is still running. Testing `_typingSince` for truthiness (the shipped first cut) was false exactly where it mattered — the only shape that lets the timer fire is a NOT-busy wrapper running the message as its own `turn/start`, which re-arms the flag — so the window apologised for a message the agent was visibly running. `_typingSince` is a TIME and a time is not an identity (two turns can arm in the same millisecond); the epoch advances only where the flag arms, so a relabelled turn is still the same turn. Gate: test-queue-steer ⑨c drives the real `_hideTyping()`→`_showTyping()` sequence (a hand-set `_typingSince: null` fixture cannot occur on that path) with the old predicate's value asserted as the negative control.

**PAGING INTENT — ONE PREDICATE (inc-mtq5bpjt-0o0n, after 2.369.42).** Every AUTOMATIC upward-paging trigger asks `_autoPagingBlocked()` and gets a REASON back (`disposed / suspended / resume-settle / pinned / settling / no-input / input-resize`) — the scroll handler's laws, in ONE place. Its callers: the gap sentinel's `IntersectionObserver` (`_observeHistoryGap` → `_loadEarlierGap(el, null, {via:'io'})`) and the scroll/wheel-driven `_maybeSeekEarlier` — the gate itself lives INSIDE `_loadEarlierGap` (chat-view-seek.js), i.e. on the path that is alive in the failure state, and traces `gapSkip {why, via}`; an explicit retry CLICK passes `{auto:false}` and bypasses it. Before this, `_loadEarlierGap`'s tail-mode branch (`!teleported && windowStart>0 ⇒ _extendTop()`) was the ONE upward-paging entry point with no gate at all, and an IntersectionObserver is PURE GEOMETRY — it needs no event, so a desktop resume's re-measure (scrollTop momentarily 0 while pinned) paged three pinned windows into history. **A new automatic paging trigger routes through this predicate; a new DIRECT `_extendTop()` call site must justify why it is user intent.**

**RESUME SETTLE + THE PINNED-TAIL INVARIANT (same incident).** `setSuspended(false)` arms `_resumeSettleUntil` (`RESUME_SETTLE_MS` 1200 ms) on top of the 2.369.1 `_lastStructuralAt` stamp: while it is open the scroll handler no-ops BEFORE it touches the pin (transitional geometry must not unpin either), `_autoPagingBlocked` says `resume-settle`, the loadHistory auto-fill DEFERS with bounded re-arms instead of deciding, and the pinned re-tail runs again when it expires (`_resumeSettleTimer`, cleared in `dispose()`). The window is armed as `RESUME_SETTLE_MS + RESUME_RETAIL_SLACK_MS` (40 ms) so it ENDS at the re-tail — the 40 ms gap between expiry and re-tail was itself a door (an input-less `scrollTop = 0` landing there unpinned the window and the re-tail, asserting on the live flag, then refused: stranded in history) — and the re-tail asserts on `_pinnedAtSuspend`, the pin SNAPSHOT taken by `setSuspended(true)` before the geometry starts lying, restoring `_pinned` itself when it acts off it. Real user input (wheel/touchmove/pointerdown/keydown) calls `_endResumeSettle()` — settle AND snapshot — so the settle suppresses input-LESS displacement only, never a reader. And the invariant the trim broke: **a PINNED view never loses its tail** — `_extendTop` skips `_trimBottom()` while `_pinned` (traced `trimSkipPinned`) and re-asserts the bottom after the prepend (`pinnedRetail`), because under transitional geometry the element anchor fails (`anchored:false`) and the delta fallback clamps scrollTop to 0. DOM growth stays bounded: the live-append path trims the TOP while pinned, and the next genuine (unpinned) page-up trims normally. Pins: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (source pins + a FUNCTIONAL hide→show unit: a transitional unpin still re-tails, a real reader is left alone) + the end-to-end scripts/test-desktop-resume-paging.mjs (real >34MB transcript, real desktop switch, a resume+1210 ms input-less injection, source-level negative control).

**ROUND 2 — THE TWO HOLES THE SETTLE LEFT (same incident).** ① *The re-tail was blind to readers who navigate.* Only the FOUR message-list listeners (wheel/touchmove/pointerdown/keydown) called `_endResumeSettle()`, and the surfaces a reader actually uses to go somewhere are not on the list: the minimap's pointer events live on the CONTAINER, the floating run bar on `this._container`, and `jumpToIndex` / a search reveal touch neither — so a reader who jumped 400 ms into a resume was silently dragged back to the live tail when the re-tail fired (measured: at +2600 ms pinned at the tail again). Now every off-list positioning surface calls `_noteUserNav(via)` (stamp `_lastNavAt` + end the settle): the two minimap callbacks, `jumpToIndex`, a USER `jumpToBottom({user:true})` (the re-tail passes `user:false` so it cannot cancel its own series), `_jumpToFileTime`, `_landOnHeader` (run bar + footer) and ChatSearch's reveal through the new `onNav` callback. The re-tail captures `resumeAt` and returns when `_navigatedSince(resumeAt)` — the chat-view-seek `userScrolled = _lastUserScrollAt > max(jumpAt, revealAt)` idiom generalised over every positioning stamp we own (`_lastNavAt`, `_lastUserScrollAt`, `_lastJumpAt`, `_search._lastRevealAt`). ② *The protection was a one-shot CLIFF.* The settle ended at 1240 ms and the re-tail ran there; an input-less displacement at +1400 ms unpinned the window and stranded it (3/3 sessions — +1240/+1280 survived only because `_forceScrollToBottom`'s 10-frame chain happened to still be running). Now the re-tail is a BOUNDED SERIES (`RESUME_RETAIL_AT_MS` = [1240, 2000], `_resumeRetailTimers`, `_clearResumeRetail()` on suspend/dispose/real input) **and the UNPIN ITSELF is gated on positive evidence** like the paging triggers: `_resumeDisplacement()` = inside `RESUME_DISPLACEMENT_MS` (2800 ms — past the last rung, because the displacement the rungs cannot catch is the one that arrives between them), pin SNAPSHOT still true, and nothing the reader did has landed since ⇒ keep `_pinned`, re-assert the bottom, trace `unpinSkipResume`. A real wheel/touch/key/pointer or any nav drops the snapshot, so a reader at +1400 ms unpins and pages normally. ③ The settle return in the scroll handler moved BELOW the destructure + `_updateRunBar(scrollTop)` (next to the `_programmaticScroll` return) — the bar is a READOUT, not a decision, and returning above it froze the 2.369.45 floating run bar for the whole settle; everything below that line is a decision. WHICH guard owns which part of the horizon was MEASURED, not assumed (the chrome sweep captures per-leg traces): 1240 ms is inside the settle (the scroll handler decides nothing, the rung re-tails), 1400 ms still traces `collapsedGeomSkip` — the 2.301.0 guard owns it, because its `settling` window runs 1500 ms from the resume's structural stamp — and only at 1900/2400 ms is `unpinSkipResume` the sole thing between an input-less displacement and a stranded window. Pins: the trim-guard suite's source ORDER pin restates its budgets per segment (suspend→runBar ≤400, runBar→programmatic ≤200, programmatic→settle ≤1400, settle→atBottom ≤2400) and the chrome suite adds a nav-during-settle leg, the displacement sweep (outcome for all four offsets + the gate's own trace where it is the only guard left), a real wheel-up inside the horizon (it must page and must NOT trip the gate — its final pin state belongs to the collapsed-geometry guard, not to this fix), and a PER-MECHANISM negative control for each, neutered on the instance in the same run (the source-level control rebuilds with `RESUME_SETTLE_MS = 0` and cannot host a timing-dependent repro; its cliff leg injects at the SAME 1900 ms the fixed build passes at, because at 1400 ms the 1240 rung's own `_forceScrollToBottom` chain re-pins even with the gate off — the very accident the finding named).

**ROUND 3 — A CLICK IS NOT A POSITIONING ACT (same incident, reproduced with TRUSTED CDP input).** Every message-list listener ran the same `_endResumeSettle()`, which zeroes the settle window AND drops `_pinnedAtSuspend` AND clears the bounded re-tail series — so a plain left-click/tap anywhere in the list during the 1.24 s settle disarmed the whole repair and the resume's own input-LESS displacement then reproduced the incident BEHIND the click (measured: click at +300 ms, `scrollTop = 0` at +1400 ms → unpinned, `fromBottom 1883`). THE RULE, by grade: `_noteUserInput()` — a pointerdown, or a key that is not in `NAV_KEYS` — stamps `_lastUserScrollAt` (the paging gates' `no-input` evidence, which scrollbar and keyboard readers need) and may end the settle WINDOW, and that is ALL it does; `_notePositioning(via)` — wheel, touchmove, a navigation key (PageUp/PageDown/Home/End/Arrow*/Space), or a scrollbar drag — additionally stamps `_lastPositionAt` and runs `_endResumeSettle()` (window + snapshot + series), exactly like `_noteUserNav`'s six off-list surfaces. `_navigatedSince()` reads the POSITIONING stamps only (`_lastNavAt`, `_lastPositionAt`, `_lastJumpAt`, `_search._lastRevealAt`) and never `_lastUserScrollAt`. A SCROLLBAR DRAG has no event of its own, so it is recognised by a signature: `_pointerDragScroll(scrollTop)`, stamped in the scroll handler ABOVE the settle return (the drag must be able to end the very settle it starts inside; the order pin's budgets are now suspend→runBar ≤400, runBar→programmatic ≤200, programmatic→drag ≤700, drag→settle ≤1400, settle→atBottom ≤2400). Round 3 wrote that signature in TIME — a scroll within 400 ms of a pointerdown that moved the view by more than `POINTER_DRAG_PX` (2 px) — see ROUND 4 below for why that had to become a POSITION. The chrome suite drives all three with real `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` — a click (the repair survives it), a `mouseWheel` up (unpins immediately and pages), and a native-scrollbar drag with `.chat-minimap-active` removed first (the semantic minimap sets `scrollbar-width: none`, so a native scrollbar only exists with the minimap off — with it ON the reader drags the minimap, which is already `_noteUserNav('minimap')`), each with its own instance-level control. KNOWN RESIDUAL (round-3 MINOR, backlog B-8e5e, not fixed here): a wheel-up at resume+1400 ms pages but is re-pinned ~1/3 of the time by the collapsed-geometry `atBottom` re-pin — that flag belongs to the 2.301.0 guard, and this fix does not assert it.

**ROUND 4 — THE DRAG IS WHERE THE PRESS LANDED, NOT WHEN A SCROLL FOLLOWS (same incident, two MAJORs against round 3's own signature).** ① The 400 ms window admitted the ARTIFACT: the incident's re-measure bounces run +366…+602 ms after the switch, so a plain CONTENT click with one of them landing behind it satisfied "a scroll soon after a pointerdown that moved the view" → `_notePositioning('scrollbar-drag')` → `_endResumeSettle()` (window + snapshot + series) → the incident, reproduced behind the click. Round 3's leg passed only because it put the click at +300 ms and the displacement at +1400 ms. ② …and it EXCLUDED a real reader: a drag whose first move comes later than 400 ms after the press (press, look, then drag) was never positioning, so the re-tail series yanked that reader back to the live tail — a NEW harm versus master. NOW: `_pointerOnScrollbar(e)` hit-tests the pointerdown against the list's scrollbar GUTTER — `offsetWidth - clientWidth` minus the borders, on the right in LTR and on the LEFT in RTL, plus the bottom strip for a horizontal scrollbar — with coordinates converted viewport→LAYOUT px first (the uiScale body `zoom` scales `getBoundingClientRect`/`clientX` but NOT `clientWidth`; mixing the two spaces is the 2.369.5 VNC-pointer bug). The verdict rides `_pointerDownOnScrollbar` for the LIFETIME of the press, cleared by WINDOW-scoped `pointerup`/`pointercancel` listeners bound to `winInfo._listenerCtl.signal` (a scrollbar drag routinely releases outside the list; `dispose()` also removes them, for a view torn down while its window lives on), and `_pointerDragScroll` is "the flag is set AND the view moved > `POINTER_DRAG_PX`". `POINTER_DRAG_MS` is DELETED — a content click never becomes positioning however close a displacement lands, and a press-and-hold-then-drag is positioning for the whole press. `_pointerOnScrollbar` is DOM-free (it reads injected geometry) and unit-tested in plain node; the chrome suite adds a click-at-+300 ms/displacement-at-+450 ms leg and a press-hold-900 ms-then-drag leg, each with an instance-level control that restores the pre-fix outcome (the old time-window signature strands the window; a false hit-test yanks the slow dragger back).


### chat-input.js
  chat-input.js — **THE Alt+Enter STEER CHORD + THE SEND-MODE HINT (2026-09-07, owner "顺便加入一个queue的快捷键, 不支持queue的就不显示").** `Enter` already sends as QUEUED while a turn runs; the chord is the OTHER mode. It is checked in the textarea's keydown BEFORE the plain-Enter branch — that branch tests only `!e.shiftKey`, so Alt+Enter would otherwise be swallowed as an ordinary send — and it is deliberately the ONLY new chord: `Tab` is the slash-command completion a few lines above and `Ctrl/Cmd+Enter` keeps meaning send/queue. `steerChordAllowed` (streaming ∧ the PURE `composerSendModes(this._queueCaps).allowSteerChord`) is the ONE capability answer, read by the chord, by both surfaces and by chat-view's `_canSteerComposer()`; the chord never degrades to a plain send, because a chord that silently does something else is worse than no chord. `steerNow()` sends on the ordinary `_send()` path (which now RETURNS the msgId — `null` for an empty composer, a disconnected socket, or a `/goal`, which is a control frame and never a queueable message) and hands that id to the host through `onSteerSend`, because a steer NAMES A QUEUED ITEM (`turn/steer` takes the app-server's queued-submission id) — there is no "send this text as a steer" verb to invent. `onSteerChord` routes the composer to the contributed command `chat.steerNow` so the keyboard and the button run the same verb; without a host it acts directly (standalone/test use). TWO SURFACES, ONE CAPABILITY: `_updateSendModes()` toggles `.hidden` on the hint (`ChatInput.sendHintHtml`, PURE + escaped, one segment per backed mode) and on the `.chat-steer-btn` bolt button, and is re-run wherever either input changes — `showTyping`/`hideTyping` (the streaming flag) AND `setQueue` (the caps, which arrive AFTER the composer is on screen, the same late-capability ordering that once shipped a permanently dead bubble chip). WHICH surface is visible is CSS's business, not JS's: the hint is `display:none` ≤768px and the button is `display:none` above it (`.chat-attach-btn`'s shape), so a phone gets the button, a desktop gets the line, and neither can contradict the capability. `_queueCaps` now carries `queue` too (the hint's queue segment needs it). ChatInput (textarea, send, attachments, drafts, slash commands, TODO display). **A TEXT CHANGE MUST NEVER REBUILD THE STOP BUTTON (2026-09-07 r2 — the live sub-agent counter's own regression, found by adversarial review):** `showTyping(label, kind)` renders `<spinner> <span class="chat-stream-label">…</span> [■ Stop]` and is re-asserted once a second while collab traffic ticks. The first cut guarded it with an “unchanged label” memo — which could never fire, because the label CHANGES on every tick (the age is in it). So `innerHTML` was rewritten every second and `.chat-interrupt-btn` was destroyed with it: a mousedown whose target leaves the DOM before mouseup fires `click` on the common ancestor (`.chat-stream-status`, no handler), so the interrupt is lost with NO toast and NO log — measured 6/10 (verifier) and 7/10 (this suite) trusted clicks delivered while ticking vs 10/10 with a stable node, and keyboard focus on Stop died within 1.4 s (100%). It bites in exactly the situation the readout exists for: the user believes the turn is wedged and reaches for Stop. Now: while `_isStreaming`, the button is still there and the KIND (which decides what the button DOES) is unchanged, the label is a `textContent` write into `.chat-stream-label` and NOTHING else — the button node, its focus and the two-step compaction ARM (`kind === 'compacting'` → “Cancel compaction?”) all survive. `_showPending`'s button-less “still working” line has no `.chat-interrupt-btn`, so it always falls through to the full render (it also clears `_typingLabel`). `hideTyping()` clears both memo fields; `typingLabel` exposes the current line. ChatView's read-only `_streamStatus` fallback has the same `.chat-stream-label` element and the same text-only update (`_roTypingLabel`). Which TEXT the line shows is ChatView's decision (`_applyStreamLabel`), never chat-input's — chat-input only owns the local “thinking…” / compaction label it sets on its own send. **`setSlashCommands(cmds, {terminal})` REPLACES (§2.6)**: the harness pushes the WHOLE list whenever it changes, so a command that disappeared upstream disappears here; the terminal-BOUND subset (`terminal_slash_commands`, upstream: “Phone/remote UIs should hide these from command menus”) is filtered out — `/doctor`, `/color` used to sit in the chat composer's completion and do nothing when picked. The prefixing + filtering live in ONE pure rule, `slashCompletionList` (agent-meta.js), used by the init side effect, the mid-session meta op and the attach payload alike.**

**THE QUEUE STRIP'S CLIENT LAWS (2026-09-07, the verb-table round-2 … round-8 reviews AND the MERGE review that put them on top of 2.369.60; chat-input.js + chat-view.js, gated by scripts/test-queue-steer.mjs ⑦ + ⑪).** Twelve rules the edit/reorder controls only look right without:
① **An edit's typed text is kept until the result proves it landed.** `_send()` moves the rewrite into `_pendingEdit {id, text, raw, original, draftBefore}` and leaves it IN the textarea — the pre-edit draft comes back only on `ok` (and only if the box still holds exactly what was sent, the `_pendingSend` guard). **THAT GUARD COMPARES `raw`, NEVER `text` (round-3 verifier).** `_send` TRIMS before it dispatches, so a pending edit that stored only the trimmed payload and compared it against the raw textarea took the "the user typed something else" early return for every rewrite ending in a space or a newline — i.e. every multi-line one — and every outcome below silently did nothing: the ok result left the rewrite in the box so the NEXT Send posted the queued message a second time as a new chat message (measured), a refusal never re-opened the editor, a gone row never reached the draft, and the dead-socket path left the row spinning forever (the round-1 finding-3 lie, resurrected). `text` is what goes ON THE WIRE; `raw` is what is in the BOX and is the only thing a textarea may be compared against. **And no exit from `_resolvePendingEdit` may leave a spinner**: the row's `pending` mark is cleared at the top, before any early return — `setQueueOpResult` has already written `refused`/cleared, but the two callers that answer WITHOUT a result (the dead socket, the 20 s fallback) write no row state at all. A refusal is the NORMAL race for this control (you rewrite the FRONT item, the turn ends while you type, the app-server drains the ORIGINAL and answers `gone`), and it now HANDS THE REWRITE BACK: still queued ⇒ straight back into edit mode with the reason on the row; row gone ⇒ the text becomes the draft plus a toast saying where it went. Every path answers — the wrapper's `queue-result`, a ws refusal, the republish that drops the row, `setDisconnected`, a 20 s fallback — because one edit at a time is enforced (`_beginQueueEdit` toasts while a save is unanswered).
② **Edit mode borrows the TEXTAREA, not the DRAFT CHANNEL.** The debounced `saveDraft` is skipped while `_editingQueueId || _pendingEdit` (otherwise the queued message's text was persisted as the session draft and synced to every other client), and an inbound `_draftSyncHandler` lands on the STASHED draft instead of clobbering the rewrite mid-edit.
③ **The reorder drag re-queries its rows every frame.** `strip.innerHTML` is rebuilt by every `queue_changed`, so rows captured at pointerdown are DETACHED — and a detached rect is all zeros, so the midpoint hit-test read "below every row" and the drop silently landed the item at the END of the queue. `liveRows()` inside `apply()`, `ids` recomputed from `this._queue` inside `finish()`, a dragged row that LEFT the queue sends nothing, and `_renderQueue` re-runs the running drag's `apply` so the drop indicator survives the republish.
④ **A dispatch that sends NOTHING undoes its own pending mark.** `_dispatchQueueOp` marks the row BEFORE calling out; `_sendQueueOp` returns `false` when the liveness choke point refuses, and the mark is undone right there (no result will ever come, and the republish does not clear a row that never left the queue). The other silent path — the ws layer's `queue-op-unsupported` — now ECHOES `op` and `id`, and `_onSessionError` feeds it to `setQueueOpResult` so the row ends with the reason on it.
⑤ **"No verb list" is UNKNOWN on the payload path and THE LEGACY THREE on the in-band path.** `create`/`attach` send `queueVerbs: null` when the server knows nothing (an empty ARRAY is a real answer the client intersects with, and sending `[]` for "unknown" hid the WHOLE strip); a `queue_changed` that names no verbs is mapped to `LEGACY_QUEUE_VERBS` — the SAME frozen array `src/server/wrapper-files.js` maps a verb-less sidecar onto, exported from the PURE `src/backend-caps.js` so the two ends cannot disagree about what an older wrapper serves.
⑥ **A row that leaves the queue while you are still TYPING is the same race — in a much larger window.** `setQueue`'s dropped-row branch used to call `_cancelQueueEdit({silent:true})`, which restores the pre-edit draft OVER the textarea: the rewrite in progress was destroyed with no save and no toast (round-3 verifier; the trigger is ordinary — you rewrite the FRONT item and the turn ends while you type). `_abandonEditOfDroppedRow()` now answers it exactly the way `_resolvePendingEdit`'s row-gone branch answers a rewrite already sent: the typed text becomes this session's draft plus the same toast, and `_editDraftBefore` is DISCARDED rather than written over the user's words. The discriminator is `_editOriginalText` — the queued message's own text as the editor opened it — so an UNTOUCHED (or emptied) editor is still the plain silent cancel, and `_cancelQueueEdit({silent:true})` survives only on the paths where the USER abandoned the edit (Esc, opening a different row). The SAME question — "where do these words live if this textarea disappears?" — closes the two other paths that had no answer, because law ② keeps the debounced autosave OFF for the whole edit, so "the draft is already saved" (what covers ordinary typing) is false here: `_resolvePendingEdit`'s **bail-out** now PERSISTS the text it protects (the user typed something else while the save was in flight; the edit is over, so the draft channel belongs to the box again — without it those keystrokes were in a volatile textarea and nowhere else, while the store still held the pre-edit draft), and **`dispose()`** (window closed, tab swapped, view replaced) stashes an UNSENT rewrite as the draft. `dispose()` deliberately does NOT stash a rewrite whose frame is already OUT **and unchanged** — that text is on its way into the queued message itself, and a copy of an edit that LANDED would leave the user their own queue item sitting in the input — but it DOES stash anything the box holds that differs from the sent payload (round-4 verifier): the `input` listener disarms the autosave for `_editingQueueId` **OR** `_pendingEdit`, so keystrokes typed AFTER Send are just as unprotected, for as long as the result takes (up to the 20 s fallback), and a `dispose()` that only looked at `_editingQueueId` let exactly those die with the window. The one predicate is `_unstashedEditText()`: the baseline is the pending edit's `raw` while a save is in flight (never the trimmed wire payload — the round-3 skew), `_editOriginalText` before Send. **The last path in this family — opening the pencil on ANOTHER row while a rewrite is unsent — used to discard it silently too** (it called `_cancelQueueEdit({silent:true})`, which restores the pre-edit draft over the box); it now shares `_rewriteInBox()`, the ONE discriminator ("is what is in the box the user's, or still the queued message's own text?"), and a carried rewrite becomes the session draft **and** `_editDraftBefore`, so cancelling the newly opened edit hands it straight back, with a toast. Refusing the switch was the other legal answer; keeping the switch working (a normal action) while never losing text was chosen for consistency with the two vanished-row twins. `_cancelQueueEdit` now survives only for Esc and the untouched-editor cases — i.e. only where the USER threw the edit away.
⑦ **Edit mode is its OWN state, never a value of the row's op state (round-4 verifier).** `_queueRowState` is TRANSIENT per-row OP state — `pending` while a frame is out, `refused` with the sentence — and every dispatch and every result WRITES it: a batch verb (`run-all`/`steer-all`) marks EVERY row pending, a refusal on the edited row marks it refused. While `'editing'` was a third value of that same map, the only on-screen sign that the textarea holds a queued message — the edit→cancel toggle, the row marker and the "Editing a queued message — send to save, Esc to cancel" hint — was erased by any of those, WITHOUT edit mode actually ending: the pencil came back over a live rewrite, and one click on it replaced the user's words with the queued message's own text (measured in the neutered control). Edit mode is now `_editingQueueId` (+ `_editOriginalText`), and `queueStripHtml(items, caps, rowState, editingId)` takes it as a SEPARATE argument, so a row renders both facts (`data-queue-state` and `data-queue-editing`, whose CSS rings compose). Consequences to keep: `setQueueOpResult(ok)` simply clears the op state (no "unless it says editing" guard), `_beginQueueEdit`/`_cancelQueueEdit` no longer touch the op state at all (a refusal that is on the row describes the op, and it outlives the edit like every other verb's refusal), and `_resolvePendingEdit`'s "still queued ⇒ back into edit mode" branch writes the reason as `refused` while re-arming `_editingQueueId`. GENERAL: a transient mechanism state and a user MODE are different lifetimes — rendering one through the other makes whichever is written last erase the other, and the loss is silent precisely because the mode itself is still on.
⑧ **While an editor is open, the textarea belongs to the QUEUED MESSAGE — every other writer of it must be audited (round-5 verifier).** `sendText()` is the programmatic send behind every in-chat action button (`Compact now`, the design request): it wrote its text into the box and called `_send()`, which in edit mode SAVES the box as an `edit` op. One click therefore did three wrong things at once — the queued message was silently rewritten to the action's text, the action never reached the agent, and the unsent rewrite was destroyed (law ② keeps it out of the draft store for the whole edit, so it lived nowhere else). It now REFUSES out loud and touches nothing, the same shape as `_beginQueueEdit`'s sibling guard, and returns `false` so the control that fired it survives the refusal: the Compact button re-enables itself, and the design dropdown — whose brief exists ONLY in its own textarea — stays open instead of closing before the send. The same audit closed the other half of `sendText`: `_send` clears the box, PINS the draft store to what it sent and folds any pending attachments into the frame, so an action fired over a half-typed prompt used to delete it from box AND store and hand the user's pasted screenshots to `/compact`; both are now put back around the send (the store explicitly, not via the 300 ms debounce `_send` just overwrote), **and the draft SLOT goes back with the text** — `_send` also armed `_pendingSend` for the action, and `confirmDelivery()` clears the store on the first inbound frame, so leaving it armed would have deleted the user's prompt from the store milliseconds after handing it back (an OLDER unconfirmed send keeps its own slot — identity compare). `_send` now ANSWERS whether it took the box, so a bail-out (dead socket, empty box) reaches `sendText`'s caller as a `false` too: a Compact button that disabled itself over a send that never happened is the same dead control. The remaining writers are safe for stated reasons, listed in the code next to them: the constructor's draft restore predates any editor; another client's draft sync lands on the stash (law ②); the slash-command dropdown is a USER action on the box it is attached to; input-history recall is gated on an EMPTY box (an empty editor is not a rewrite — `_rewriteInBox`/`_unstashedEditText` both answer null); the unconfirmed-send / unconfirmed-goal restores write only an empty box, dispatch nothing and announce themselves; attachments never touch the textarea and `_send` refuses an edit that carries them. GENERAL: when a control BORROWS a shared surface, the borrow is only safe once every other writer of that surface has been enumerated — the dangerous ones are the writers that belong to a different feature entirely.
⑨ **The writers that must respect the borrowed box include the one that lands LATE (round-6 verifier).** The round-5 audit asked "who else writes this textarea?" and enumerated them — and missed `_insertUploadedPaths`, because it is the only writer that does not run on the click that caused it: an upload writes the box after a network round-trip, so the editor it has to respect may have been opened AFTER the upload started, and because it writes through a LOCAL alias (`const ta = this._textarea`) grepping the field name could never find it. The damage came in two grades: with the editor merely OPEN the paths were inserted into the queued message and the next Send saved a rewrite the user never typed; with the save already IN FLIGHT they changed the box out from under `_resolvePendingEdit`’s `raw` compare, which reads any difference as "the user typed something else" and takes the bail-out — so the ok/refused/gone outcomes of law ① all silently did nothing again. The answer is the sibling one law ② already gives another client’s draft sync: `_stashUploadedPaths` puts them on the stash the edit will restore (`_pendingEdit.draftBefore` / `_editDraftBefore`) AND in the draft store (memory-only stash + a window that can close = the same nowhere-else problem; `_beginQueueEdit` already pins the store to exactly that value), with a toast naming where they went. GENERAL: an audit of "every writer of X" is only complete once it is done by BEHAVIOUR, not by spelling — the writers that hide from it are the ones that write through an alias and the ones that write after an await. **The same audit’s three follow-ups on `sendText`, all round-6:** ⓐ the slot release nulled `_pendingSend` although `_send` had OVERWRITTEN it with the action’s own slot — an OLDER unconfirmed send therefore lost its dead-socket protection to a `/compact` click, so it is handed back (`this._pendingSend = prevPendingSend || null`) and `confirmDelivery()`’s clear became TEXT-AWARE (it exists to remove THE SENT TEXT from the store, not to empty the draft channel: with an older slot legitimately armed the store already holds the half-typed prompt the action handed back, and clearing deletes words that were never sent). ⓑ the box restore was gated on `keptText.trim()`, so a bail-out from an EMPTY box (`_send` refuses a dead socket before it clears anything) left the ACTION’s own text sitting in the input, one Enter away from being posted as a chat message — the restore is unconditional now (writing `''` back is a no-op when `_send` took the box) and only autosize/slot/saveDraft stay behind the gate. ⓒ with an older slot now surviving, `setDisconnected`’s notice had to stop claiming a rescue it does not always perform: it only restores INTO A FREE box, so the sentence is chosen by whether the restore happened.
⑩ **A control that BORROWS the user's text must release it unconditionally, and may only announce what it actually did with it (round-7 verifier — four defects, one shape).** Law ⑨'s three `sendText` follow-ups were written INSIDE `if (keptText.trim())`, i.e. they ran only when there was typed text to hand back and never in the ORDINARY state of an in-chat action button: an EMPTY box. So the round-6 fix was live for the rare case and dead for the common one — an older unconfirmed send still lost its slot AND its pinned draft to a `Compact now` click, and with no older send the action armed `_pendingSend` for ITSELF, so a later disconnect typed `/compact` into the user's empty input under a toast telling them their message had been restored. `prevPendingSend` **and** `prevDraft` (`loadDraft`) are now captured beside each other before the send, and both go back outside the gate: `this._pendingSend = prevPendingSend || null` unconditionally, `saveDraft(keptText)` only when there was something to give back and `saveDraft(prevDraft)` otherwise (an action is not a user message and owns neither the slot nor the store). **The announcement is a THREE-state answer, not a boolean**, and there are THREE places that hold text for an unanswered frame — the unconfirmed send and the two `/goal` twins (`setDisconnected`'s `_pendingGoal` branch, `_markGoalPending`'s 10 s fallback). All three independently grew the same shape and then drifted: every one of them guards the RESTORE on a free box (the user keeps drafting while a frame is out, and those keystrokes are theirs), but two still said "your command was restored to the input" unconditionally, and the third conflated "nothing to restore" with "the box was occupied" — an attachments-only send carries `text: ''`, so a user with an EMPTY input was told it "already had text, so it was left alone". One `_announceUnsent(text, {restoredMsg, keptMsg, noneMsg})` now answers for all three and RETURNS the outcome (`none`/`restored`/`kept`); `noneMsg` is required of any caller whose text can be empty and the goal twins say at the call site why theirs cannot be. **And the deferred draft clear has to know what the send DISPLACED, not only what it pinned**: round-6 made `confirmDelivery()` compare the store against `pending.text`, which an attachments-only send never writes — so its clear became a permanent no-op and text the user DELETED inside the 300 ms debounce window (whose autosave `_send` cancels) stayed in the store forever and came back as the draft of the next window on that session. `_pendingSend = {text, storeBefore}` records `loadDraft` at send time and the echo clears on EITHER match, while words typed AFTER the send still match neither and survive. GENERAL: **a fix written inside an existing conditional inherits that conditional's blind spot** — ask which state the control is normally in (an action button's box is normally EMPTY) before deciding a branch is the safe place for it; and **when N places hold the same kind of text they must share the sentence-chooser, because a sentence is a claim about what happened and each copy drifts into claiming it unconditionally.** Pins: DOM-free (the release outside the gate, the helper's three returns, all three call sites, `storeBefore` on both ends, zh+ja for every new sentence) + chrome legs with a neutered pre-fix body per finding — the round-6 `sendText` verbatim, the two-way announcer, the `text`-only clear — each reproducing its defect, plus a reopened-window probe that shows the zombie draft coming back.
⑪ **A rule written for one caller is a rule about ALL of them: `sendText` has TWO, and only one of them authored its own text (round-8 verifier — the round-7 fix, applied one caller too wide).** Law ⑩'s release is right for an in-chat ACTION (`Compact now`): `/compact` is a string the PRODUCT wrote, so the pending-send slot and the draft pin `_send` arms for it belong to nobody and arming them types `/compact` into the user's empty box on the next disconnect. The other caller is the DESIGN REQUEST, whose payload is built around a brief **the user typed** — into a dropdown that CLOSES the moment the send is accepted, so `_send`'s slot is the only surviving copy. Releasing it unconditionally meant a brief that went into a half-open socket vanished with zero trace, which is exactly the loss `_pendingSend` exists to prevent — and the round-6 code it replaced had restored it with a notice (its release sat inside `if (keptText.trim())`, a gate the dropdown's empty box never enters, so the regression rode in on the fix for that gate). `sendText(text, { carriesUserText = false } = {})` now names the difference AT THE CALL SITE, where the payload is built: the action passes `false` explicitly, the design request `true`; a caller that forgets it gets the ACTION semantics, i.e. it loses a rescue rather than leaving somebody else's text armed in the user's box. An OLDER unconfirmed send still wins over both (`if (prevPendingSend) … else if (!carriesUserText) …`) — it is the user's too, it is older, and it is what this send displaced. **The same review scoped law ⑩'s other half**: the deferred clear accepts `storeBefore` — the value the send DISPLACED — only when the send has no text of its own (`!(pending.text === '' && stored === pending.storeBefore)`); for a text-carrying send the store holding its pre-send value again (re-typed, recalled with ArrowUp, re-pasted, or handed back by `sendText`) is words that were never sent sitting exactly where its pin demonstrably is NOT, so it may only ever clear its own pin. GENERAL: **before making a helper's behaviour unconditional, enumerate its callers and ask which of them AUTHORED the thing being spent** — "the caller owns nothing" was true of the caller in front of the reviewer and false of the one that had made this method worth having; and **a widening clause added to fix one shape (a pinless send) must be gated on that shape**, or it silently applies to every other. Pins: DOM-free (the option + its default, the two-armed release, the skipped store restore, the scoped clear, BOTH call sites and a count that keeps them the only two) + chrome legs where the two callers run over the SAME empty box one flag apart, the round-7 `sendText` and `confirmDelivery` bodies neutered in place to reproduce each defect, and positive controls that the action semantics and the pinless clear are untouched.
⑫ **A TRANSIENT MODE OWNS THE ROWS IT LIVES IN — no automatic render may take them away (the MERGE's own defect; neither side had it alone).** master's bounded strip (2.369.60) collapses past `QUEUE_COLLAPSE_AT` with NO user action, and the branch's drag/edit controls live only on `.chat-queue-item` rows: a 9th queued job notification arriving mid-drag emitted zero rows, the live drag's repaint hit-tested an EMPTY list, and its `let afterId = null` — which in this protocol MEANS the front of the queue, never "no answer" — went out as `reorder <id> afterId:null`, a silent move to the FRONT; the same republish during an open edit deleted the ✕ and the row's `data-queue-editing` marker while `_editingQueueId` stayed set and Send silently still meant "save". THREE rules: (a) `_renderQueue` suppresses the AUTO collapse while `_queueDrag || _editingQueueId` — the collapse nobody asked for, only, and the predicate is deliberately NARROW: an in-flight save (`_pendingEdit`, whose mode `_send` already closed) owns no control the user must reach, it answers by itself, and a refusal re-opens `_editingQueueId` and re-expands the strip — so holding the strip open for the 20 s of a save would be the same surprise pointing the other way; (b) the drag's `apply()` returns on a ROWLESS render, so `drag.afterId` keeps its last REAL value (and `undefined` — never any answer — which `finish()` already refuses); (c) the ONE collapse that is still honoured (the user's own chevron, visibly undoable) must leave the mode FINISHABLE — the edit's cancel moves onto the always-drawn `.chat-queue-editing` hint line, behind the SAME `canEdit()` predicate as the row's own control so the two cannot drift. The general law: **an initial value that is also a meaningful answer must never be reachable by "nothing was measured"**, and a feature that changes layout on a timer/arrival must ask whether a live mode is holding the thing it is about to remove. Legs: test-queue-steer ⑤ (DOM-free collapsed markup + peer/no-verb negative controls) and ⑪ (trusted CDP: 8→9 mid-drag republish, the 8→8 control, the explicit-collapse belt, the defect reproduced end-to-end with the belt neutered, and the SCOPE pinned from both sides — an in-flight save still auto-collapses, and the refusal that re-opens the mode re-expands the strip).
⑬ **A clean final tree does not make a chain bisectable (the merge review's history finding).** One intermediate pick commit shipped literal conflict markers in `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/kb-api.md` and `docs/kb-file-structure.md` — every suite was green because they all read the WORKING tree, and `git bisect` / `git show` landing on that commit saw an unresolved file. test-queue-steer ⑫ asks GIT instead: every commit in `origin/master..HEAD` is scanned for `^<<<<<<< ` / `^>>>>>>> ` (never bare `=======`, which is ordinary prose), with the detector itself negative-controlled on a synthetic conflicted buffer and an evidence-SKIP when there is no branch range.

### chat-run-summary.js (PURE)
  chat-run-summary.js — the run-fold classifier + summary composer (2.369.37; `messageKind`, `foldToggleFor`, `countKinds`, `runSummaryParts`, `runSummaryLabel`, `mcpParts`, `RUN_KINDS`/`SUMMARY_ORDER`/`SUMMARY_EXTRAS`). It imports NOTHING and test-fold-ux pins that. **`collabPart` (2026-09-07)** is how the live sub-agent traffic segment reaches the label without breaking that rule: the CALLER hands `runSummaryLabel` a pre-composed string (`collabRunPart` from the PURE src/collab-row.js — “3 sub-agents · 47 messages · last 4s ago”) and this module decides only WHERE it goes (appended to the kind parts, before the touched-files — segment). Words in one module, order in one module: the run header, the floating bar and the run footer can never read different orders, and no second kind table exists anywhere. `SUMMARY_EXTRAS` (`subAgentIn`) stays what it always was — a count the caller assigns into `byKind`, never produced by `messageKind`, never zero-filled, so an unset one renders nothing and can never go NaN.

### chat-renderers.js
  chat-renderers.js — Message rendering (renderUserMsg/renderAssistantMsg/renderToolMsg/renderToolResult/renderSystemMsg, linkify, diffs, permissions). **Tool-card headers**: `toolHeaderHtml(name)` = curated localized display name (toolDisplayName's map — claude built-ins AND codex raw names `web_search`/`web_fetch` → t('Web search')/t('Fetch page'), 2.369.43) or the MCP short name + server chip; the raw identifier rides the card's title tooltip. **SEARCH-CARD TITLE CHIP (2.369.43, owner ask "see the query without expanding"):** `searchQueryChipHtml(block, msg)` appends `<span class="chat-tool-query" title="<full>">…</span>` to the header of every search-kind card — claude WebSearch (input.query) / WebFetch (input.url) by tool name, codex web_search/web_fetch and ACP search tools by the normalizer's `collapseKind:'search'` hint — with the text from the PURE shared `searchQueryOf` (src/search-card.js: query → action.queries joined ' | ' → action.query → 'pattern' in url → url → pattern). Three header paths call it (pending/interrupted tool_call, error result, generic result); '' when nothing to show (a codex card is an empty stub until item/completed). XSS LAW: the query is model/web-controlled text that syncs to every client — escHtml on the chip text AND its title attribute, truncated at 90 chars with the full string in the tooltip; CSS `.chat-tool-query` (chat.css) is theme-var only. Non-search cards are untouched. Gate: scripts/test-search-card-title.mjs (35: the pure helper over real input shapes, the REAL renderer esbuild-bundled for node with a DOM shim — claude/codex/ACP chips, XSS escaping, truncation, no chip on Bash, pending stub — plus wiring pins). **COLLAB HEAD LIVENESS (2026-09-07):** `_renderCollabMsg` asks the injected `isCollabLive(msg)` (ChatView's `_liveCollabId`) and passes `{live, now}` into `collabRowsHtml`; absent (view-only windows, sub-agent viewers) means FROZEN, which is the truth for a stopped transcript. The renderer never keeps a timer — the view's single ticker rewrites the `.chat-collab-head` text in place. **THE SESSION-START CARD (§2.6, 2026-09-07)**: `renderSystemMsg`'s init branch used to return `{el:null}` — a FAILED MCP server was therefore invisible (its tools simply did not exist and nothing said why; this instance's own live sessions carry `status:'failed'` servers). `buildInitCard(frame, {repeat})` now returns a card when the frame **has facts to show**. **That gate is NOT a version test, and describing it as one was round 1's false claim** (round 2, verifier-reproduced): `tools` / `mcp_servers` / `skills` / `plugins` / `output_style` / `claude_code_version` are REQUIRED in the CLI's own zod schema (dumped from 2.1.238 / 2.1.239 / 2.1.257 — the suite pins required-vs-optional and negative-controls the reader on the keys that really are `.optional()`), so EVERY claude init renders. What renders nothing is a producer with no `frame` at all — codex (`codex-message-manager`) and ACP/OpenCode (`acp-message-manager`) both build `initData` from `{model, permissionMode, slashCommands}` — and those REAL normalizer outputs are the suite's negative control, replacing a hand-made 3-key claude record no CLI has ever emitted. **ONCE PER DISTINCT FRAME**: a conversation carries one init per spawn, and this instance's own (rotating) buffers held **62 init records in 13 conversations, 62 with a health issue, 33 byte-identical ones in ONE conversation** at 2026-09-07 05:34 — all 33 of which round 1 drew as cards, each with a warning strip; a re-measure six minutes later, after rotation, gave 30 / 14 distinct / 30 warned / 6 max, i.e. the same shape. The normalizer marks a frame equal to the previous init's as `frameRepeat` and this returns null for it; the side effects still apply, and a frame that CHANGED always draws. Shape: one collapsed `<details>` line; **what is WRONG lives in the ALWAYS-VISIBLE summary** (a health strip behind a click would not fix the invisibility it exists for) while the inventory (skills / plugins / MCP servers / sub-agents / tool count / output style / CLI version + betas) is one click away. The health rule is PURE and shared (`initHealthIssues`, agent-meta.js): any server whose status is not exactly `'connected'` (the vocabulary is an OPEN set — 'failed', 'needs-auth' observed — so the status is shown VERBATIM), plus `plugin_errors` and `mcp_server_errors`. The INVERSE is deliberately never rendered: an absent error key does NOT mean a clean load (upstream omits both on frame-persisting lanes), so the card never claims 'all healthy'. The side effect additionally carries `terminalSlashCommands` + `memoryPaths`. Every frame value is escaped (a plugin/skill name syncs to every client). CSS: `.chat-init-*` in chat.css — the closed-details rule is EXPLICIT (`display` on the child overrides the UA's slot hiding; headless chrome measured the 'collapsed' card at 202px before it was added).**

### search-card.js (PURE)
  src/search-card.js — ONE home for web-search cards on both sides of the wire (PURE tier; CJS pulled into the bundle like task-color-seq.js): `renderSearchOutput({query, action, results, error})` (server: the codex normalizer's only results renderer — the wrapper does NOT render, it relays codex's typed event), `searchQueryOf(input)` (client title chip + any server consumer), `searchActionKey(action, query)` (0.14x twin pairing: action-only key, the bare query stands in only when there is no action — an open_page end's query is its url and its call twin has no query at all). Caps: 20 results, 4096 bytes (TextEncoder, char-boundary cut — no Buffer, the module ships in the browser). actionType(action)`, `searchActionKey(action, query)`. **`action.type` is spelled TWO ways for the same thing and both reach here** — core protocol `search|open_page|find_in_page|other` (0.120-0.149 `web_search_end`/`web_search_call`) and v2 camelCase `search|openPage|findInPage|other` (the wrapper's live item AND the 0.153.4 rollout's Extension item); `actionType()` canonicalises, so every head/key/title path handles both. **`url`/`pattern` are NULLABLE in the v2 schema** (a real 0.153.4 `findInPage` carried `url:null`): the head is `found '<pattern>'` with no location rather than a fabricated one, and the title chip never trails a dangling ' in'. **'no results' is a CLAIM only an EMPTY ARRAY supports** (2026-09-06 refutation: 0.120/0.125/0.130 NEVER persist the key — 1698 ends, 0 with it — so absent means *nothing known*; 42 cards of one file claimed 'no results' about searches that had them). Absent key ⇒ the card states what was DONE: the `opened <url>` / `found '<p>'` head, else `searched: <query>`, else `status: completed`. 0.149.1 and 0.153.4 always persist the array. **Twin dedup (0.120-0.130 only)**: `searchActionKey` is ACTION-ONLY (the id-less `web_search_call` twin has no query; an open_page end's query is its url) and the bare query stands in only when there is no action at all — with `{type:'other'}` and NO-ACTION deliberately in ONE bucket, because an `{type:'other'}` end's twin carries no `action` key and keying them apart produced a second empty card per search (the owner's `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' shape; real rollout-2026-04-14T03-14-36 lines 6417-6421). Caps: 20 results, 4096 bytes (TextEncoder, char-boundary cut — no Buffer, the module ships in the browser).. actionType(action)`, `searchActionKey(action, query)`, `NO_SEARCH_DETAILS` (the label a record with NO query, NO action and NO results wears — head `web search (no details recorded)`, input `{note:…}`; the ONE 0.120.0 orphan in the local corpus used to render as the owner's `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' shape, and a card must never reproduce it byte for byte). **`action.type` is spelled TWO ways for the same thing and both reach here** — core protocol `search|open_page|find_in_page|other` (0.120-0.149 `web_search_end`/`web_search_call`) and v2 camelCase `search|openPage|findInPage|other` (the wrapper's live item AND the 0.153.4 rollout's Extension item); `actionType()` canonicalises, so every head/key/title path handles both. **`url`/`pattern` are NULLABLE in the v2 schema** (a real 0.153.4 `findInPage` carried `url:null`): the head is `found '<pattern>'` with no location rather than a fabricated one, and the title chip never trails a dangling ' in'. **'no results' is a CLAIM only an EMPTY ARRAY supports** (2026-09-06 refutation: 0.120/0.125/0.130 NEVER persist the key — 1698 ends, 0 with it — so absent means *nothing known*; 42 cards of one file claimed 'no results' about searches that had them). Absent key ⇒ the card states what was DONE: the `opened <url>` / `found '<p>'` head, else `searched: <query>`, else `status: completed`. 0.149.1 and 0.153.4 always persist the array. **Twin dedup (0.120-0.130 only)**: `searchActionKey` is ACTION-ONLY (the id-less `web_search_call` twin has no query; an open_page end's query is its url) and the bare query stands in only when there is no action at all — with `{type:'other'}` and NO-ACTION deliberately in ONE bucket, because an `{type:'other'}` end's twin carries no `action` key and keying them apart produced a second empty card per search (the owner's `{"query":"","action":null}` / 'status: completed' shape; real rollout-2026-04-14T03-14-36 lines 6417-6421). Caps: 20 results, 4096 bytes (TextEncoder, char-boundary cut — no Buffer, the module ships in the browser)..

### backend-caps.js — review / renameWriteback / forkAtMessage (§2.13, 2026-09-07)
  Three capabilities that were ALREADY shipped and gated on a **backend id** at four call sites, while the client carried a `caps.review` row the server did not have — so the mirror had nothing to be checked against and drifted for releases. Now: `review` (the harness can start a code review of a target — codex `review/start`: working tree / base branch / commit / custom, inline or detached), `renameWriteback` (renaming here also renames in the AGENT's own store — codex `set-thread-name`; claude's JSONL has no title field, so a rename stays ours), and **`forkAtMessage`, which is NOT `fork`**: claude's `--resume-session-at <uuid> --fork-session` branches from ONE message, codex's `thread/fork` branches the whole thread with no message boundary — reading `caps.fork` for the per-message button would put a button that cannot work on every codex card. The SIX call sites read caps: `src/ws-handler.js` `case 'review-start'` + the rename writeback, `chat-view.js` `_syncReviewAvailability` / `_startReadOnlyPolling` / **`_forkFromMessage`**, `chat-renderers.js` `addForkBtn`, and **`sidebar-state.js` `renameSession`** — the TRIGGER half of `renameWriteback`, which decides whether the `rename-session` frame is produced at all and read `sessionOrKey?.backend === 'codex'` until round 3 (the same latent shape as the fork defect with the halves swapped: a server ready to write the name back and a client that never asks). It reads the CLIENT mirror `backendFeatureCaps(...).renameWriteback` — the same row the deep compare pins — and dropping the gate is NOT an option: the ws case also writes `session.name` + session-meta and broadcasts, so sending it for claude would change behaviour, which is why that site is pinned POSITIVELY (reads the cap) as well as negatively (no backend id). **Both halves of a capability, always** (round 2): round 1 gated only the fork BUTTON and left the handler on `backend !== 'claude'`, so the first harness whose row said `forkAtMessage:true` would have shown a control whose click did nothing and said nothing; the handler now reads the same predicate, and a click blocked by a not-yet-known session id raises a toast instead of returning silently. GATE: `scripts/test-harness-contract.mjs` deep-compares every client caps key against the server row (a key that is neither mirrored nor a NAMED chrome-only flag fails: `effort`/`autoResume`/`accounts`/`quotaRefresh` are the four grandfathered ones) and pins each call site against a backend-id branch — with a NEGATIVE CONTROL that plants a gate and proves the checker catches it.

### chat-status-bar.js — the SESSION-HEALTH chip (§2.6 round 4, 2026-09-07)
  `setInitHealth(rows)` — rows in, rows out: the CALLER runs the one classifier (`initHealthIssues`), this only displays. `null` = never told (no chip), `[]` = told and nothing is reported broken (chip clears), non-empty = one warning pill `⚠ {n} not working` rendered right after the model/effort badges, tooltip listing every row through the SHARED `initHealthLabel`, click = a dropdown with one row each plus a provenance note. Deduped on a content key so a repeated status never re-renders the bar.
  · **Why a chip and not just the init card**: the card is a record at a position in the transcript and is suppressed for a `frameRepeat`; the chip is pinned, so a window that opens later sees the same fact as one that watched the session start (the full measurement is under agent-meta.js below).
  · **No pulse** — unlike `.chat-status-remote` (a transient the user waits out), this is a STANDING condition; an animation on it would read as "something is happening right now".
  · **Touch**: ≤768px the status bar is a single swipeable `nowrap` row whose children are `flex-shrink:0`, so the chip keeps its FULL label (measured 90px at 375×667, fully on screen, not clipped by its own box) and the click-dropdown is the only way to read the rows there — hover does not exist. It is deliberately placed at child index ≤2: a warning parked behind eight chips on a 375px row is a warning nobody swipes to.
  · CSS `.chat-status-health` / `.chat-status-health-row` in chat.css — theme vars only, `UI_ICONS.alert` inline SVG (never an emoji).
  · **MEASURE THE AFFORDANCE YOU ADDED, NOT THE ONE NEXT TO IT (round 5, verifier-reproduced).** Round 4 added the dropdown *because* touch has no hover, then measured only the CHIP at 375×667 and clicked the panel open at 1280×800 — so its mobile claim covered everything except the thing the round was for, and the panel was broken there twice:
    ① **a dead declaration.** `.chat-status-health-row { white-space: normal }` never applied: the row also carries `.chat-status-dropdown-item`, declared LATER in chat.css at the same 0,1,0 specificity with `white-space: nowrap`, so an unbounded upstream `message` (2.1.257 zod: `plugin_errors[].message` is free text) hard-clipped inside the panel's `overflow: hidden` — measured 610 vs 320 at BOTH viewports, while the sibling `.chat-status-dropdown-note`, which has no competing class, wrapped correctly all along. Fixed as the compound selector `.chat-status-dropdown-item.chat-status-health-row` (0,2,0) + `overflow-wrap: anywhere` (an upstream error *type* like `unsatisfied_dependency:` is one unbreakable token and can exceed a narrow row even when wrapping). **A rule that another rule can silently take away is not a rule — assert the COMPUTED value.**
    ② **a panel positioned out of the scroller it hangs off.** `showDropdown` set `left` from the anchor's offset with no bound: at 375×667 the health panel landed at right 443.7 while `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`, i.e. the 68.7px hanging off it was UNREACHABLE by any gesture (the ≤768px bar scrolls sideways; the absolutely-positioned panel does not live in that scroller). Now the placement is decided in `showDropdown` after the append (round 5 said "every status-bar dropdown is clamped ONCE" — see ③: three callers wrote their width after it returned, so that sentence was true of the clamp and false of the file): `left = clamp(anchorOffset, 0, containerW - minWidth - DROPDOWN_EDGE_PAD)` and `maxWidth = containerW - left - DROPDOWN_EDGE_PAD` — **cap the WIDTH, never the content** (the panel is `overflow: hidden`, so a row that cannot fit must WRAP). Both numbers are LAYOUT px: `offsetWidth`/`min-width` are unzoomed while `getBoundingClientRect` is not (the 2.369.5 uiScale class), and the cap never dips below the panel's own CSS `min-width`, which would win anyway. This was ALSO a pre-existing bug: at 375 the permission-mode panel landed 89px off screen (its own chip is already past the edge of the swipeable bar). Gate: test-init-frame's 375×667 dropdown legs — computed `white-space` on every row, `scrollWidth ≤ clientWidth`, `right ≤ innerWidth`, with same-run negative controls that restore the pre-fix state (nowrap ⇒ 84px clipped; raw offset + nowrap ⇒ right 470.5 on a 375px screen) and a positive control that the pre-existing panels keep their full 130px min-width.
    ③ **"clamped ONCE" was true of the clamp and false of the file (round 5 r2, verifier-reproduced).** THREE call sites wrote `dropdown.style.minWidth/maxWidth` AFTER `showDropdown` returned — design 300/440, active goal 240/400, set-a-goal 280/420 — and the clamp had already chosen `left` from the panel's *pre-content* 130px CSS min-width, so the caller's widening was invisible to it. Measured at 375×667: design right 537 (162px off), set-a-goal 517 (142px), active goal 477 (102px), all on a page that does not scroll sideways; the design chip is not an edge case (it renders for every editable chat window). The claim came from generalising the two panels in the belt loop, which happen to keep the default 130px. **Now the width is an ARGUMENT, not a later style write**: `showDropdown(anchor, {minWidth, maxWidth})` (constants `DESIGN_PANEL_W` / `GOAL_PANEL_W` / `GOAL_SET_PANEL_W`) decides width and placement from the same two numbers — a bigger intent moves the panel LEFT, and an intent bigger than the container caps the MIN-WIDTH too (`min(max(intent, cssMin), containerW − PAD)`), because a minimum nobody can see is not a minimum, it is a hidden panel; a container with NO measurable width binds nothing at all (clamping off a zero rect would size the panel to 0 — worse than the thing the clamp prevents). Desktop geometry is byte-identical (every panel's width and right edge unchanged at 1280×800). Same census found the twin harm: the squeezed panels still clipped their rows (`.chat-status-dropdown-item` was `nowrap`; response style −111px, a background task's description −143px), so the generic row now wraps like the health row did since round 4 — shrink-to-fit means it only wraps once the cap actually bites. Gates: a SOURCE pin (every `.style.min/maxWidth` write in the file must be inside `showDropdown`; a planted pre-fix line turns it red) + a 375×667 CENSUS of all TEN panel-opening chips (model, effort, permission, response style, health, design, set-a-goal, active goal, background tasks, workflows — a chip that does not render FAILS its entry) asserting placement AND clipping (rows and the panel's own content box, since the three width-intent panels carry no `.chat-status-dropdown-item` rows at all), with the whole pre-fix path replayed per panel as the negative control (537/517/477 reproduced in-run) and an oversized-intent control (300px asked inside a 200px view ⇒ min-width 192px and inside; the cap removed ⇒ 300px reaching past the view).

### agent-meta.js — the init-frame client rules (§2.6, 2026-09-07)
  Three PURE rules every surface shares, so the frame's facts never fork into per-view copies:
  · `slashCompletionList(commands, terminal)` — the composer's completion list: the terminal-bound subset removed, one leading slash each, whatever spelling the caller passes. An absent/empty terminal list filters nothing (old CLI, codex, ACP).
  · `initHealthIssues(frame)` — `{kind: 'mcp-server' | 'mcp-config' | 'plugin', name, detail}` rows: any MCP server whose status is not exactly `'connected'` (open vocabulary, reported verbatim), every `mcp_server_errors` entry and every `plugin_errors` entry. Never computes the inverse — an absent key is not a clean load.
  · `initHealthLabel(issue)` — the ONE spelling of a row ("MCP github — failed", "plugin old-helper — unsatisfied_dependency: …"), shared by the init card's strip and the status-bar chip. **TWO surfaces render these rows since round 4, and the second one exists precisely so a window that opens later AGREES with one that watched the session start — two spellings would be the disagreement it was built to remove.** `detail` is protocol text, shown verbatim, never translated.
  · **THE HEALTH FACTS HAVE AN ATTACH PATH (round 4, verifier-reproduced): the pinned status-bar chip, not only the card.** The card is a record at a POSITION — suppressed as a `frameRepeat`, and on an attach the init record usually sits hundreds of records before the tail-50 the window loads. Measured on this instance's own buffers: 9 conversations carry more than one init, and in 2 of them (the two LARGEST — i.e. exactly the long-running ones that accumulate MCP failures) the rendered tail holds an init record and ZERO drawable cards, so the "{n} not working" strip a live watcher saw was simply absent for a window opened later — while `chatStatus.initFrame` had been delivering `mcpServers`/`mcpServerErrors`/`pluginErrors` to that same client all along with no consumer (`initHealthIssues` had exactly ONE caller, `buildInitCard`). `ChatView._applyInitHealth(frame, {replay})` is the ONE application point, fed by BOTH paths — a LIVE init record and `applyStatus(status.initFrame)` — and hands the rows to `ChatStatusBar.setInitHealth`. **ABSENT ≠ CLEAN**: a falsy frame returns before touching the chip (codex/ACP/an older CLI must never make a window claim "nothing broken"), while a frame that reports everything connected yields `[]` and CLEARS it — a gauge that cannot fall is not a gauge. Gate: scripts/test-init-frame.mjs §5b (node: the harm reproduced deterministically — a 50-message slab with an init record and zero drawable cards; the attach twin still naming all 4; wiring pins) + the chrome legs (the SAME slab draws 0 cards AND shows a visible warning-coloured chip whose tooltip names every row verbatim; all-connected ⇒ no chip; never-told ⇒ no chip; a later frame-less status leaves it standing; click lists the rows; 375×667 = 90px, unclipped, `flex-shrink:0`, child index ≤2).
  · **A REPLAYED RECORD IS NOT NEWS (round 5, verifier-reproduced).** Round 4 wrote "the chip is NOT gated on the slab" into the method's doc-comment and then fed it from the RENDER path — which is a slab: `_onCreateMessage`'s system branch runs for every record the window renders, including ones paged in from OLDER spawns, so scrolling up past a previous spawn's init silently rewrote a present-tense readout. Reproduced through the REAL `ChatView.prototype._onCreateMessage` against REAL normalizer output, both directions: attach on a broken spawn → page up past a healthy older one ⇒ chip cleared (the dead MCP server went invisible again — the exact invisibility §2.6 exists to end), and attach healthy → page up past a broken older one ⇒ an invented warning. Nothing restored it: `setInitHealth` has one caller and `applyStatus` only runs on attach/HTTP/poll. THE RULE NOW: the chip has exactly TWO writers — (a) a record arriving through the LIVE feed and (b) `applyStatus`, whose frame the server picks as the NEWEST init across the whole record list (something no page-up can do). `replay` is the CALLER's statement about provenance (`{replay: this._loadingHistory}` at the feeder), not a window bound and not a global read, so the rule survives a new feeder. Three consequences, each pinned:
    – the application moved ABOVE the "viewing history" deferral in `_onCreateMessage`. Below it, a mid-session respawn's frame was dropped OUTRIGHT for a reader who happened to be scrolled back (the record never reaches the renderer at all — no card, no side effect, no chip), and only a later history rebuild accidentally recovered it. **What a session IS does not depend on where its reader is standing.**
    – `sideEffect.initFrame` is GONE from chat-renderers: rendering is where a record LANDS, and a renderer runs for replays. Where the frame lives on a record is now the ONE pure reader `initFrameOf(msg)` (this file), used by the renderer and by ChatView — two spellings of `content[0].initData.frame` is exactly the drift this module exists to prevent.
    – the same-epoch reconnect now applies the attach payload's `chatStatus`. It was the ONLY attach path that dropped it (loadHistory's rebuild, its identical-skip branch and the read-only poll all apply it), so the chip's only writer there had been the catch-up batch REPLAYING records — and once a replay is not an authority, that path needed its authority applied where it arrives. `chatStatus` is computed from the whole record list at attach time, i.e. it already covers everything the catch-up is about to render.
    KNOWN, DELIBERATE: a conversation whose newest init sits beyond `chatStatus`'s bounded 2000-record tail scan now stays silent instead of lighting up when a page-up happens to reach it. That bound is the server's and is the right place to widen it — "the chip speaks when you scroll far enough" is the coupling this round removed.
  · `noteMemoryPaths(paths)` / `isAgentMemoryPath(fp)` — **the frame FIRST, the regex as FALLBACK.** `memory_paths{auto,team}` exists upstream for exactly our use ("Lets SDK renderers classify Read/Write/Edit tool calls on these paths as memory operations without re-implementing CLI path detection"), and `memoryPathRe` IS that re-implementation: a user who moves the store made every memory write render as an ordinary Write on a long dotfile path. Declared dirs accumulate for the process (a memory dir named by ANY session identifies memory content in every view) and match as PATH prefixes (`…/memory/` never swallows `…/memory-backup/`); the per-backend regexes still cover old CLIs, codex and anything outside the declared dirs. chat-view.js and chat-renderers.js both call the ONE predicate — the two local unions are gone. **ORDER MATTERS (round 2, verifier-reproduced):** `loadHistory` learns the frame's dirs from the attach payload BEFORE its render loop, not from `applyStatus` after it. The attach tail almost never contains the init card (it sits hundreds of records earlier), and a rendered card is never re-rendered on a status change — so the late answer landed after every memory card in the window had already been drawn as an ordinary file card, permanently, on exactly the relocated-store case the feature exists for. The pin compares POSITIONS and is negative-controlled on the pre-fix source.
